# MONTH OF JULY

*Having 31 days.*

*July 1: The Holy and Wonderworking Unmercenaries Cosmas and Damian, Martyred in Rome:*

*Stichera on 6.*[^6]
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Celestial Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Troparion:*
By marvelous rays drive away every disease of our afflictions, freely extending grace, being enriched with the gifts of the Teacher, Who upon earth took the sicknesses of the earthborn upon His shoulders.

*Troparion:*
Having first been well instructed in the healing art, through faith you cleanse the illnesses of all; and secondly, having armed yourselves spiritually, you divinely drive away also the injuries of spiritual maladies.

*Troparion:*
As you freely received grace from Christ God, O unmercenaries, you freely heal the infirmities of all; not only do you cleanse our illnesses, but as merciful ones you also bestow restoration rationally upon cattle.

*Other Stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Troparion:*
With spiritual waters, overflowing like rivers and visibly filled, you water creation with divine signs and the most glorious gifts of healings. And you cast out soul-destroying passions, and heal infirmities, and drive away evil spirits, O God-bearing unmercenaries, intercessors for our souls.

*Troparion:*
Having subdued irrational passions by spiritual powers, O sacred ones, you bestow beneficence upon men and cattle, being enriched by Christ with the gift of healings. Therefore, as we celebrate your sacred and light-bearing solemnity, we ask for cleansing for our souls.

*Troparion:*
Your divine temple has been shown forth as a heaven, radiant and saving, having now acquired salvific wonders like stars, and like the bright sun the divine operation of healings: O most blessed Cosmas, O most glorious Damian, servants of the Lord, intercessors for our souls.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Endless is the grace of the saints which they received from Christ; therefore their relics also, by divine power, unceasingly work wonders, whose names also, when invoked with faith, heal incurable diseases. Through them, O Lord, deliver us also from spiritual and bodily passions, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Laid Aside All Earthly Hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
When of old the ewe-lamb, the undefiled and spotless Lady, beheld her Lamb upon the Tree of the Cross, she cried aloud as a mother, and marveling she exclaimed: "O sweetest Child, what is this sight, new and most glorious? How does the ungrateful assembly deliver Thee to Pilate's judgment seat, and condemn unto death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word."

*At the Aposticha, the stichera of the Octoechos. Glory... Tone 6:*
Ever having Christ working in you, O holy unmercenaries, you perform wonders in the world and heal the sick. For your medicine is an inexhaustible fountain: the more it is drawn, the more abundantly it flows forth; and as it is poured out, it abounds; day by day being depleted, it multiplies, giving unto all and remaining unfailing; and those who draw are filled with healing, while it remains unspent. What then shall we call you? Healing physicians of souls and bodies? Healers of incurable passions, who freely heal all, having received the gift from Christ the Savior, Who bestows upon us great mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Risen on the Third Day" (Тридне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who bare Thee cried aloud: "What is this strange mystery which I behold, [O my God]?[^7] How art Thou dying in the flesh, suspended upon the Tree, O Bestower of life?"

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Holy unmercenaries and wonderworkers, Cosmas and Damian, visit our infirmities: freely you have received, freely give unto us.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion twice.*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Both Kathismata from the Octoechos. And stichera And of the saints on 6, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the tripartite power of my soul in the depth of dispassion, even as Thou didst the mighty captains of old, that unto Thee as on a timbrel, through the mortification of the body, I may sing a triumphal hymn.

*Troparion:*
O radiant pair of wise unmercenaries, as you stand before the honored Trinity with all the chosen, pray that those who keep your light-bearing memory may be enlightened with the divine brightness of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as exalted in mind above material things, O wise and holy ones, you received the immaterial radiance of the Spirit. Therefore, by your divine visitations, O unmercenaries, you ever dispel the darkness of diseases.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired an unsleeping eye of the soul toward the divine fulfillment of God's commandments, O glorious, merciful, and God-bearing unmercenaries, by grace you awaken unto good health those sleeping in infirmities.

*Theotokion:*
From thy pure blood, O pure one, thou didst incarnate the Savior and God, Who through the Spirit showed forth unto us the honored unmercenaries as physicians for our souls, O most pure one, and as saving and fervent protectors.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ: for none is holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Your divine temple sacredly pours forth fragrant myrrh and spiritual showers of healings, ever washing away foul-smelling passions.

*Troparion:*
Ever taking up your abode in the heavenly mansions, O all-wise ones, by the grace of the Almighty you ever show forth your tabernacle to be a fountain of healings.

*Troparion:*
Having curbed the passions of the flesh with the bridle of temperance, you most richly received spiritual beams. Therefore with healings you enrich the world.

*Theotokion:*
God, Who rests in His saints, chose thee as the fairest among women, and was well pleased to be born of thee in the flesh, O pure one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Givers of healings and wonderworkers, you have appeared unto all as light-receiving lamps of wonders through spiritual grace; for by faith you cool the flame of passions and warm the understanding of the faithful therein. Therefore, having acquired your divine church as a medicine for souls, fleeing unto it we cry: O God-bearing unmercenaries, pray to Christ God, to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate your holy memory with love.

*Glory... Tone 8, Podoben: The same ("Of Wisdom"):*
Shedding the rays of healings upon those on earth, O divine wonderworkers, you manifestly drive away the ailments of men, having truly acquired within yourselves the unwaning Sun, Christ our God. Therefore, having worthily assembled, we honor your glorious and precious memory, O holy ones, God-bearing unmercenaries. Pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and the only one among women who without seed bare God in the flesh, all human generations call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou didst nourish the Creator and Lord with milk. Therefore we, both the angelic and human race, worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry unto thee: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who venerate thy most holy childbearing in faith.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding her Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting she exclaimed: "The world indeed rejoices, having received deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns within me as I behold Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest through the tender compassion of Thy mercy, O longsuffering Lord. O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto those who in faith hymn Thy divine passions!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, on a swift cloud, Jesus the most divine, is come with an incorruptible palm, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
By life-bearing operations and through every good work, you heal the mortal passions of men, O radiant pillars, immovable bulwarks of the true vineyard, divine branches.

*Troparion:*
Let the breasts of the Church, which pour forth the milk of healings, the enlightened unmercenaries who nourish all with divine foods, be praised with joyful hymns!

*Troparion:*
Your temple has been shown forth as a hospital, O holy ones, a tempest-free and saving haven for the storm-tossed, fleeing into which we all receive tranquility and deliverance from cruel afflictions.

*Theotokion:*

Vouchsafe me, O Virgin, the mercy that is in thee, for thou didst bear the Word, the only truly merciful One, Who showed forth the holy ones as merciful healers and wonderworkers unto the ends of the earth.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin unwedded, didst hold in thy womb the God of all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly radiant with divine splendors, you traverse the whole world, enlightening all men, taking away the darkness of passions, driving away demons, O God-bearing unmercenaries.

*Troparion:*
Like two lamps you illumine the world under heaven, traversing all creation by the movement of the Spirit, O God-bearers, visiting those upon their bed of sickness and delivering them from misfortunes.

*Troparion:*
Bestowing healing upon the sick and deliverance from passions freely, you have appeared unto all as great protectors, as helpers with God unto all, and as intercessors for all, O God-bearing unmercenaries.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to the hypostatic Wisdom of God, O most pure Virgin Mother of God, Who showed forth the saints as all-wise, through whom the raging insolence and cruel contrivances of the evil-minded one were brought low.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
Not by human art, but by divine grace do you cleanse the infirmities of men, O glorious ones. Therefore, having come together, we worthily call you blessed.

*Troparion:*
Bound by love unto Christ, O God-bearing ones, by divine grace you destroy all the malice of the demons. Therefore we celebrate your solemnity.

*Troparion:*
As shoots of the divine vineyard, pouring forth the wine of healings unto us who are in the mourning of diseases, you fill us with gladness, O unmercenaries.

*Theotokion:*
The most pure Word made thee a pure temple, O Mother of God, Who ever magnifies the divine temple of the unmercenaries in wonders and signs.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Having received the grace of healings, you extend health unto those in needs, O physicians, most glorious wonderworkers; but by your visitation cast down the boldness of the adversaries, healing the world with wonders!

*Ikos:*
The word of the wise physicians surpasses all understanding and wisdom, and they impart understanding unto all; for having received grace from the Most High, they invisibly bestow health upon all. Whence unto me also grace was granted to recount and hymn them as God-bearing pleased ones of Christ and His servants, who bestow a multitude of healings; for they deliver all from diseases, healing the world with wonders.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths, inflamed by the love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Opening the God-given fountain, O holy ones, you pour forth unto all rivers of pure healings, washing away the defilements of the passions and the all-destructive malice of the demons.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the grace of the Spirit of the Almighty, the pair of unmercenaries, marvelous warriors, ever grants health unto the sick, O Word of God, Wisdom and Power!

*Troparion:*
Thou alone art holy, Who glorifiest Thy saints; for for their sake Thou deliverest the world from misfortunes, and enlightenest those who cry: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bud forth as a rod from the root of Jesse, O Mary, having blossomed forth the Flower, Christ, Who as flowers adorned His pleasing servants, the unmercenaries, with wonders.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, stopped the open mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, having girded themselves with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
You have stood against all the snares of the enemy's malice, O holy ones, by the divine Spirit, having become for us a garment of salvation, and the cause of forgiveness, and the transformation of cruel afflictions, as we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having become sons of God by participation, O Damian and Cosmas, by faith you have now obtained the paternal lot: heavenly delight and truly the bright operation of wonders. And you cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
With your divinely-befitting drops wash away our soul's defilement, and drive away painful passions, O holy ones, and remove the assaults of demons, as our protectors and as our merciful healers.

*Troparion:*
Like lilies, like spiritual flowers, like roses adorned by the Spirit, you have appeared unto us, O holy ones, emitting fragrance and driving away the stench of passions from those who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Death is put to death through thee, for thou didst bear the hypostatic Life, Christ God, O most pure one, Who showed forth the unmercenary physicians as protectors of our life, as we cry: Blessed, O most pure one, is the fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the shoot of thy gestation, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
"Behold now, what is so good or what is so pleasant," as it is written, save for brethren now to dwell in oneness of mind in the radiant place, in the heavenly mansions, in unfading glory, whom we worthily call blessed!

*Troparion:*
How great is this temple, ever adorned with wonders, into which entering continually, O marvelous Cosmas and Damian, you grant health unto those in need: therefore you are worthily called blessed.

*Troparion:*
Today the angels and the divine apostles, the prophets, venerable ones, and all the righteous rejoice together with us in your memory, O blessed ones, with whom joyfully abiding, they pray for the whole world.

*Troparion:*
O divine and light-bearing pair, God-bearing and all-honored unmercenaries, ask remission of transgressions, amendment of life, and deliverance from all cruel afflictions for those who ever praise you.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the dwelling-place of the Light, O pure one, Who illumined all things and showed forth the saints as light-bearing, Who now takes away the darkness of soul-corrupting passions, O most pure and only Ever-Virgin!

*Exapostilarion.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Having Adorned Heaven with Stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
What word will suffice to declare the grace of healings of the unmercenaries? For after God, they are physicians and saviors unto the whole universe.

*Theotokion:*
By thy sovereign protection preserve us all unharmed from the plotting of enemies, O pure one, us thy servants; for we have acquired thee as our only refuge in tribulations.

*At the Praises, stichera on 4.*
*Tone 1. Automela.*

Having received the grace of healings from God, you freely and fervently heal the passions of our souls and bodies, O all-comely unmercenaries. Therefore Christ, bestowing given health through you unto the faithful, shows you forth to the universe as unfailing beacons. Entreat Him that our souls may be saved!

*Troparion:*
Having drawn an abyss of healings from the Providence on high, you pour forth healings unto all the faithful, O unmercenaries; for supernaturally, through mystical handiwork, offering saving remedies from the treasures of the Spirit for the sufferings of the sick, you heal those who suffer. Therefore, having become an honored temple of the Life-originating Trinity, for It manifestly made Its dwelling in you: pray to It that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 2. By Theophanes:*
Having lived actively in divine love and desire for the things to come, you set aright saving pathways. Therefore, having kept spiritual purity undefiled, you stood against material things unto the end; and being enlightened by the divine Spirit, you freely bestow healing upon the infirm, O sacred pair, light-bearing assembly, enlightened divine yoke of unmercenaries, visiting us in tribulations and sicknesses, and gratuitously healing the infirmities of our souls.

*By Germanus:*
Having been accounted worthy of great gifts, O all-praised ones, you lived in humble conduct upon the earth; and going about everywhere, freely healing the sufferings of the sick, you were revealed as companions of the angels, Cosmas with wise Damian, all-comely brethren: heal our passions also by your prayers!

*Verse: To the saints that are in His earth, the Lord has made wonderful all His desires in them. [Ps. 15:3 LXX]*
The pool of healing[^8] healed only one a year, but the temple of the unmercenaries heals the whole multitude of the sick; for unfailing and unspent is the riches of the saints. By their prayers, O Christ, have mercy on us!

*Verse: Behold now, what is so good or what is so pleasant, as for brethren to dwell together in unity? [Ps. 132:1 LXX]*
*By Germanus:*
The choir of the saints rejoices unto ages, for they have inherited the Heavenly Kingdom; the earth, having received their relics, poured forth fragrance: having been servants of Christ, they have taken up their dwelling in life eternal.

*Glory... Tone 4. By Theophanes:*

Having a fountain of healings, O holy unmercenaries, you bestow healing upon all who are in need, having been accounted worthy of great gifts from the ever-flowing fountain, the Savior Christ. For the Lord said unto you, as unto equal zealots of the apostles: "Behold, I have given you authority over unclean spirits, so as to cast them out, and to heal every disease and every infirmity."[^9] Therefore, having lived well in His commandments, freely you received, freely you give, healing the passions of our souls and bodies.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*
*If it be Wednesday or Friday, say the Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кw до́блѧ):*
Seeing Christ, the Lover of mankind, being crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the most pure one cried aloud, weeping: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee in return for the good things Thou didst do for them? And art Thou striving to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 8:*
Who will not marvel? Who will not glorify? Who will not faithfully sing of the wonders of the wise and most glorious unmercenaries? How even after their holy departure they richly bestow healings upon all who run unto them with faith, and their precious and holy relics pour forth the grace of healings! O holy pair! O honored heads! O wisdom and glory of the grace given unto you from God! Therefore with hymns we cry unto God the Benefactor, Who showed them forth for the healing of our souls and bodies.

*Now and ever... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагw чꙋдесѐ):*
The undefiled Heifer, seeing the Young Bull willingly nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously, cried aloud: Woe is me, most beloved Child! What has the ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O all-beloved One?

*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes of the Octoechos on 4; and from the canon of the saints, Ode 3, on 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: To the saints that are in His earth, the Lord has made wonderful all His desires in them. [Ps. 15:3 LXX]*
*Verse: I foresaw the Lord always before me, for He is at my right hand, that I may not be shaken. [Ps. 15:8 LXX]*
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 153 [1 Cor. 12:27–13:8].*

*Alleluia, Tone 4: Behold now, what is so good or what is so pleasant, as for brethren to dwell together in unity? [Ps. 132:1 LXX]*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 34 from the half [Mt. 10:1, 5–8]. Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous. [Ps. 32:1 LXX]*

*July 2: The Deposition of the Precious Robe of Our Most Holy Lady the Mother of God in Blachernae.*[^10]

*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 1.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Celestial Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Most glorious art thou in generations of generations, O Virgin Maiden Mary, Mother and Protectress of the world, who gavest birth in the flesh unto the Son of the unoriginate Father, co-eternal with the Spirit: pray Him that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*

Encompassed by hopeless afflictions, O pure one, having acquired thee as our only protectress, O Virgin, we cry aloud with thanksgiving: Save us, O good Bride of God, for thou art a refuge unto the world and the protection of our race.

The world was renewed in thy nativity, O Mother of God, salvation of the faithful and sleepless intercessor. Therefore, unto those who piously make supplications, O most pure one, cease not to pray fervently for all who faithfully celebrate the deposition of thy precious robe.

*Glory... now and ever... Tone 1:*
A divine solemnity has arrived today, brethren: let creation leap for joy, and let mankind dance! For the Mother of God summons us, the blameless treasure of virginity, the spiritual paradise of the Second Adam, the composition of the union of the two natures, the solemnity of saving reconciliation, in which the Word betrothed flesh unto Himself: truly a light cloud, bearing Him with the flesh Who sits upon the Cherubim. By her prayers, O Christ, save our souls!

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Rejoice, O Blachernae, divine treasury of the only Mother of God, wherein has been deposited the precious robe of the Queen of all!

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary. [Ps. 131:8 LXX]*
The Mother of God, the only Mother of God, most blessed one, having departed gloriously, left unto us her robe.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn the truth unto David, and will not set it aside. [Ps. 131:11 LXX]*
O ye church doors, open wide! For behold, the robe of the Mother of the Most High, the divinely-honored Virgin, is brought unto the church.

*Glory... now and ever... Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Glory in heaven and gladness upon earth! For behold, the robe of the Mother of the Creator of all has come to be placed in the church by hierarchal hands.

*After the Trisagion: Troparion, Tone 8:*
*O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin, protection of men:*
*(Seek it at Great Vespers.)*

*At Great Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the first Kathisma.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 8 verses; if there is no vigil, then 6. And we sing stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Thou, O Lover of mankind, hast given Thy Mother as help unto Thy servants, as Thou art compassionate, through whom Thou didst accomplish Thine ineffable and awesome dispensation, and didst set aright our primal divine dignity. Therefore, honoring her all-honorable solemnity, we hymn Thy dominion, O Jesus all-powerful, Savior of our souls! *(Twice)*

The city that honors thee and glorifies thee as is due, O all-hymned one, thou dost compass about with thy precious robe, O pure one, from godless foreigners, and from famine, earthquake, and civil strife at all times, O Virgin unwedded. And for this cause the city glorifies thee, O most pure Bride of God, helper of mankind! *(Twice)*

Thy precious robe, O Lady full of God's grace, thou hast given unto thy city as an inalienable riches, as a protection and glory, and an invincible wall, and a treasury of healings, an ever-flowing fountain of wonders, and a saving harbor for those who are ever tempest-tossed. Therefore we sing of thee, O all-hymned and pure one!

Draw forth, O men, health of souls and body! For the shrine lies before all, wherein was placed the precious robe of the Ever-Virgin, pouring forth streams of wonders unto us, and washing away the dark defilements of hearts and the pollutions of passions, and sanctifying the souls of the faithful with divine grace.

*Glory... now and ever... Tone 2:*
Having cleansed our understanding and mind, let us also celebrate with the angels, brightly beginning the Davidic song unto the Maiden-Bride of the King of all, Christ our God:

...saying: "Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary!"[^11] For having adorned her as a beauteous palace, He assigned her unto Thy city as a good thing, to govern and shelter it from the adversary pagans by Thy sovereign power, through her prayers.

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings.*
*A Reading from Genesis [Gen. 28:10–17]:*
Jacob went forth from the Well of the Oath,[^12] and went toward Haran. And he found a place, and slept there, for the sun had set; and he took of the stones of that place, and put it under his head, and slept in that place, and saw a dream. And behold, a ladder was set up on the earth, whose top reached unto heaven; and the angels of God were ascending and descending upon it. And the Lord leaned upon it, and said: "I am the God of Abraham thy father, and the God of Isaac; fear not: the land whereon thou sleepest, unto thee will I give it, and unto thy seed. And thy seed shall be as the sand of the earth, and shall spread abroad to the sea, and to the south wind, and to the north, and to the east; and in thee shall all the tribes of the earth be blessed, and in thy seed. And behold, I am with thee, keeping thee in all the way whithersoever thou goest; and I will bring thee back into this land; for I will not leave thee until I have done all that I have spoken unto thee." And Jacob arose from his sleep, and said: "Surely the Lord is in this place, and I knew it not." And he was afraid, and said: "How awesome is this place! This is none other than the house of God, and this is the gate of heaven."

*A Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel [Ezek. 43:27; 44:1–4]:*
Thus says the Lord: "It shall come to pass from the eighth day and forward, that the priests shall make upon the altar your burnt offerings and your peace offerings; and I will accept you," says Adonai the Lord. And He brought me back by the way of the outer gate of the sanctuary, which looks toward the east; and it was shut. And the Lord said unto me: "This gate shall be shut; it shall not be opened, and no man shall enter through it; for the Lord God of Israel shall enter through it, and it shall be shut. For the Prince, he shall sit in it to eat bread; by the way of the porch of the gate he shall enter, and by the way of the same he shall go out." And He brought me by the way of the north gate, toward the north, in front of the house; and I looked, and behold, the house of the Lord was full of glory.

*A Reading from Proverbs [Prov. 9:1–11]:*
Wisdom has built a house for herself, and set up seven pillars. She has slain her beasts of sacrifice, and mingled her wine in her bowl, and prepared her table. She has sent forth her servants, calling with a lofty proclamation unto the bowl, saying: "Whoso is simple, let him turn aside unto me." And unto those lacking understanding she said: "Come, eat of my bread, and drink the wine which I have mingled for you. Forsake folly, and you shall live; and seek understanding, that you may live, and set aright understanding in knowledge." He that rebukes evil men shall get dishonor to himself; and he that reproves the ungodly shall bring reproach upon himself. For reproofs unto the ungodly are wounds unto him. Reprove not evil men, lest they hate thee; reprove a wise man, and he will love thee. Give occasion to a wise man, and he will be yet wiser; instruct a just man, and he will continue to receive it. The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord, and the counsel of the saints is understanding; for to understand the law is the sign of a good mind. For in this manner thou shalt live a long time, and years of thy life shall be added unto thee.

*At the Litiya, stichera, Tone 2.*
Who will worthily praise and bless thee, O Maiden Bride of God, for the deliverance that came unto the world through thee? Giving thanks, therefore, we cry aloud unto thee, O Lady: Rejoice, thou who hast deified Adam and gathered together into one those who were scattered! Rejoice, thou who hast enlightened our race by the most bright Resurrection of thy Son and our God! For the Christian race incessantly calls thee blessed.

*Tone 3:*
A transformation and a second life of human nature we have come to know through thee, O most pure Mary, who didst contain in thy womb the Creator of creation, Who raised us from Hades and death and granted us eternal life. Therefore we cry unto thee, O Ever-Virgin: Rejoice, thou who hast united the earthly with the heavenly! Rejoice, hope, protection, and defense unto all the ends of the earth! Rejoice, thou who by thy most holy childbirth hast enlightened all things, and who dost grant great mercy unto the world!

*Tone 4:*
The Son Who is glorified on high with the Father and the Spirit by the Seraphim, desiring to renew the first-formed, emptied Himself wholly and ineffably in thy womb, O all-hymned Mother of God. And having shone forth from thee, He enlightened the whole world with Divinity delivering them from the madness of idols. And having renewed mankind in Himself, Christ God and the Savior of our souls brought it up unto the heavens.

Thou didst conceive without seed, and didst ineffably give birth unto Him Who casts down the mighty from thrones and exalts the humble, Who raises up the horn of His anointed ones that glorify Christ's Cross, burial, and Resurrection. Therefore, O Mother of God, as the mediatress of so many blessings, we call thee blessed with unceasing hymns, and we celebrate the deposition of thy precious robe.

*Tone 6:*
Come, all ye nations, with voices of rejoicing let us praise the all-holy Virgin and Mother of God, the furnace of human substance, the worker of ineffable mysteries; for in her a new and most glorious mystery has come to pass: the Unoriginate has arrived, the Word has taken on substance, God becomes man that He may make man god! Her therefore we fervently entreat, that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... now and ever... Tone 4:*
O all-hymned and all-glorious Queen and Mother of God, Mother of the Maker of all creation, Christian hope and protectress, consolation of the sorrowful, speedy expectation of the despairing and hopeless, we pray thee: be merciful unto us sinners; forsake not thy servants, and reject not the supplications of the unworthy! We pray thee as we gaze upon thine honored icon: with those who sail and those who travel by land, let thy mercy abide. O Lady, defend with thy precious robe and preserve thy cities and people in every place from the assault of barbarians; and pray unto Christ our God Who was born of thee, that He may save our souls from sins!

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
Thou alone wast the animate palace of God, and thy precious robe, concealed in thy holy house, thou hast granted as sanctification and an invincible wall unto the faithful; for whose sake thy city is saved, O Mother of God, as it hymns thy divine dominion.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary. [Ps. 131:8 LXX]*
Thy precious robe, O pure one, which covered thy precious and holy body, thou hast granted as a garment of glory unto all, and as a fountain pouring forth waters of ever-living grace, whose deposition we celebrate, honoring thee as the Mother of God, more honorable than all!

*Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face. [Ps. 44:13 LXX]*[^13] the people.

Thy temple, O Lady, bearing thy sacred robe as a treasury of sanctification, ever sanctifies all who run unto it with faith and who continually bless thee therein as is due: the hope of our souls, and a steadfast protection and dominion.

*Glory... now and ever... Tone 2:*
As with a most bright crown, O all-pure Mother of God, the Church of God has clothed herself with thy precious robe; and today she shines and rejoices, and mystically celebrates, O Lady, crying unto thee: Rejoice, honored diadem and crown of divine glory! Rejoice, only perfection of glory and eternal gladness! Rejoice, haven, deliverance, and our salvation for those who run unto thee!

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin, protection of men, thou hast granted unto thy city a sovereign defense: the robe and sash of thy most pure body, remaining incorrupt in thy seedless childbearing; for in thee both nature and time are renewed. Therefore we pray thee: grant peace unto thy city, and great mercy unto our souls!
*And at the Blessing of the Loaves, the same thrice.*

*At Matins, after the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the Beauty of Thy Virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
By the beauty of thy virginity having given birth unto the all-comely Word, and Him, O pure one, as an Infant thou didst swaddle with thy robe. This, therefore, thou hast granted unto thy servants, O Lady: a protection, help, and fountain of sanctification; whose precious deposition, O most pure Virgin, we all faithfully celebrate.
*Glory... now and ever... the same.*

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the Beauty of Thy Virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
Incorrupt childbirth and virginity thou alone didst possess, O blessed one; and as a saving garment unto men, thou hast granted thy holy robe, which even unto now remains incorrupt, O Bride of God; for whose sake we thy servants, O pure one, receive great mercy from God.
*Glory... now and ever... the same.*

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*
We magnify thee, O most holy Virgin, divinely-chosen Maiden, and we honor the deposition of thy sovereign robe in the Church of Blachernae.[^14]
*Then, the Selected Psalm: Remember David, O Lord, and all his meekness. [Ps. 131:1 LXX]*
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Today the universe celebrates the precious deposition of thy robe, O pure one, and with love cries unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, helper of the faithful!
*Glory... now and ever:*
Today thy people celebrate the deposition of thy precious robe, O all-hymned one, and fervently cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians!

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation. [Ps. 44:18 LXX]*
*Verse: Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear. [Ps. 44:11 LXX]*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 4 [Lk. 1:39–49, 56].*
*After Psalm 50:*
*Glory: Through the prayers of the most holy Mother of God...*
*Now and ever: the same.*
*Then, Have mercy on us, O God...*
*Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Marvelous are the celebrations of the Orthodox! Assembled today, let us faithfully sing with the psalms, saying: "Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary,"[^15] for Thou, O Word, hast granted us the robe of Thy most pure Mother as victory. Through her prayers, O All-Powerful One, make the world peaceful, and save our souls!

*Two canons: the first with the Heirmos on 8, whose acrostic is: "I honor the robe of the most pure Virgin," the work of Joseph.[^16] Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having been born of the Virgin, drown, I pray, the threefold part of my soul in the depth of dispassion, O Mighty Conqueror of captains, that unto Thee as on a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory.

*Troparion:*
Thy precious robe, O Mother of God and Virgin, thou hast given ever unto the people who honor thee as a sacred defense, always overcoming every assault of adversaries by spiritual power.

*Troparion:*
Having appeared upon earth in the flesh for our sake from thy pure blood, thou didst sanctify the sacred robe by wearing it and by the touch of thy body; for whose sake thou dost sanctify all thy servants who sing of thee, O Virgin.

*Troparion:*
Truly a precious treasure hast thou granted unto those who faithfully honor thee, O good Virgin: thy precious robe, enriching all with the gifts of the Divine Spirit and with many deeds of wonders.

*Troparion:*
O undefiled Ewe-lamb, who didst bear the Lamb of God beyond nature, thou hast given unto us thy precious robe, truly and ever cleansing the defilements and spots of those who venerate it, O all-blameless one.

*Another canon on 4, the work of the same; same Tone [Tone 4].*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Israel of old, traversing the deep of the Red Sea on dryshod feet, overcame the power of Amalek in the wilderness by the cross-shaped stretching forth of Moses' hands he conquered in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
As a light-manifesting and never-setting lamp, O most pure one, having found her precious robe as a bright heaven, this church today enlightens the universe with the rays of graces.

*Troparion:*
Truly having acquired thy most pure divine robe, O pure one, as strength and a bond of confirmation, thy city possesses an invincible dominion. Therefore it also boasts in thee.

*Troparion:*
Truly more honorable than the ancient Ark, O Mother of God, thy shrine has appeared upon earth: bearing signs thereof, yet preserving the faithful in the knowledge of the truth.

*Katavasia: I will open my mouth...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
As the barren church from the gentiles has brought forth, and the synagogue great in children has grown weak, let us cry aloud unto our marvelous God: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Let us sing of the beautiful sanctification, the heavenly door, the Mother of God, and her robe pouring forth divine gifts, which we kiss with love.

*Troparion:*
Unto those stripped by corruption of the garment of incorruption, thou hast clothed all by thy incorrupt childbirth, O pure one; unto whom thou hast granted thy precious robe as an inalienable riches.

*Troparion:*
Him Who clothes all heaven with clouds, O pure one, thou didst swaddle with thy robe, O most pure one. Venerating it with faith, we glorify thee as the protection of our souls.

*Troparion:*
A gratuitous healing unto the sick is this divine church; for it has acquired thy robe, O pure one, an ever-flowing fountain pouring forth healings, O all-blameless one.

*Another [canon]. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge and confirmation!

*Troparion:*
We honor, O faithful, as a bond of union with God, the robe of the pure one today, faithfully showing reverence.

*Troparion:*
Healings unto the faithful, most precious unto us today, the robe of the pure one pours forth most purely by grace.

*Troparion:*
Like morning dew, thy ever-flowing joy ever quenches the furnace of passions of those who hymn thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Rivers of wonders, O Mother of God, proceeding from thine all-honorable shrine, as from Eden water the face of the earth, pouring forth graces unto those who faithfully honor thee; therefore we hymn thee, faithfully praise thee, and ever cry aloud with thanksgiving saying: Rejoice, only hope of those who sing of thee!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and my praise.

*Troparion:*
Let us magnify the only blessed one, and let us touch her robe with faith, that we may draw spiritual grace.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired thy robe as a priceless treasure, O Maiden, the city that honors thee kisses it with faith and receives grace.

*Troparion:*
Thy robe, as pouring forth incorruption and having become a garment of glory unto all who hymn thee, we honor, O all-blameless one.

*Troparion:*
O all-blameless and only blessed one, who didst appear holier than the Cherubim, save the souls of us who faithfully honor thee from every adversity!

*Another [canon]. Heirmos:*
Seeing Thee raised upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
As an all-honored firmament, O Mother of God, the Creator and Maker having established thee, as with stars adorned thee with divine splendors, wherewith thou adornest the ends of the earth.

*Troparion:*
Thy divine and pure robe, O Mother of God, lifts us from earth unto heaven, inflamed warmly with love for it; therefore we glorify thee as the cause of the better things.

*Troparion:*
Behold inexhaustible grace! Come, all ye with contrite heart, draw forth the divine streams pouring forth unstintingly, O lovers of the feast, from thine honored shrine, O all-hymned one!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as light, a holy light turning those who faithfully hymn Thee from dark ignorance.

*Troparion:*
Thou, O pure Lady, hast granted unto us thy servants the robe for confirmation, and thine honored sash as a divine defense.

*Troparion:*
A beauteous paradise, pouring forth the fragrance of the Spirit, O most pure one, has the temple holding thy robe been shown to be.

*Troparion:*
Sanctify our souls and bodies, who with faith honor thy holy robe, O most pure one, as all-honorable.

*Troparion:*
Show us forth as temples of God Who dwelt in thee, O Mother of God, in thy divine temple, who faithfully call thee blessed.

*Another [canon]. The Heirmos the same.*
*Troparion:*
Thou, O Lord, hast magnified Thy Mother; Thou hast exalted her above all the noetic powers above all the noetic powers in comparison to her glory.

*Troparion:*
Thou, O most pure Mother of God, art pouring forth the grace of God unstintingly from thine honored shrine unto those who venerate thee with love.

*Troparion:*
By thy strength the faithful kings are girded; thy city possesses thee as a bond, O Mother of God, and is honored by thy robe.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah, praying in the whale, cried: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparion:*
Having given birth unto the King of all and Lord, thou hast shown forth thine honored and holy robe unto the reigning city of all as an indestructible wall.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, let us glorify the grace of the Virgin, and let us honor her all-holy robe, from which all health unceasingly proceeds unto the infirm.

*Troparion:*
We know thee, O Mother of God, as a fountain welling forth living waters, and we honor thy divine robe, from which we, the faithful, ever draw forth healing.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord, having been with thee, O Mother of God, made human nature His own, and expelled all harm of the stranger from it.

*Another [canon]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord," the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which, for mercy's sake, flowed from Thy side.[^17]

*Troparion:*
Beyond nature has the Lord glorified thee, having adorned thee entirely, O Maiden; beyond word has He honored thee entirely, O Mother of God, with sash, robe, and divine shrine.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired thee as strength and praise, the faithful are clothed with glory through thine honored robe, O Birthgiver of God, having acquired thee as a most radiant and honored adornment.

*Troparion:*
All the divine prophets of old longed in travail to see thine ineffable glory, O Mother of God; but in the last days He Who is above all years appeared unto us.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
As a garment of incorruption for all the faithful, O pure one full of God's grace, thou hast bestowed thy sacred robe, with which thou didst cover thy sacred body, O protection of all men. Its deposition we celebrate with love, and with fear we cry unto thee, O pure one: Rejoice, O Virgin, the boast of Christians!

*Ikos:*
The pure garment and tabernacle of the Word of God, the animate cloud and jar of manna, Mary the Mother of God, let all of us who have been saved by her childbearing bless in faith; and let us touch the honored robe, with which having wrapped the Master, as an Infant she bore Him Who was clothed in flesh, for Whose sake human nature was lifted up unto the most high life and the Kingdom. Therefore with joy we cry aloud with a loud voice: Rejoice, O Virgin, the boast of Christians!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamic children of old in Babylon trampled upon the flame of the furnace, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
With high understanding and a humble heart, let us glorify the most high throne of the King, the all-holy Maiden full of grace.

*Troparion:*
O chosen bridal chamber of God, in this thine honored bridal chamber we honor thy robe lying therein with faith, as a holy ark and a protection of piety.

*Troparion:*
Everyone is renewed who approaches with faith and kisses the holy reliquary holding thy radiant robe, O pure Virgin.

*Troparion:*
O pure Birthgiver of God, save the city that honors thee from famine, earthquake, and every affliction, and from the invasion of heathens, and from adverse harm.

*Another [canon]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic children, inflamed by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
As the Sun of righteousness, Thou Who alone art good, hast divinely adorned the temple of the pure one with Thy diverse gifts, wherein having shone forth, she emits rays from this robe.

*Troparion:*
Thy divine cups pour forth wonders, O most pure one, true grace unto the faithful; and unstintingly unto those who honor thee, noetic rivers pour forth from thy shrine as from another Eden.

*Troparion:*
A purifier of every soul's defilement is thy shrine, O all-blameless Virgin, which covers the robe that once covered thine honored body.

*Troparion:*
Now draw near with gladness, all ye who are on earth; come, the shrine mystically summons you, saying: Embrace her most glorious robe, which is treasured within me!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O Almighty Redeemer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew those who practiced piety, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and praise the Lord!

*Troparion:*
With thankful voices we sing of thee, the cause of all good things unto us, our mediating advocate; and kissing thine honored robe, O Maiden, we cry aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having the sacred garment of the most pure Virgin lying upon the lampstand of the altar as a noetic lamp, we ever enlighten the pupils of our eyes.

*Troparion:*
In no wise hast thou left thine unworthy servants without a share in thy sacred touch, O Virgin; for in place of thy life-bearing body, thou hast granted thy robe unto all.

*Theotokion:*
With divine hymns let us honor the spacious dwelling of God, the most pure among mothers, the heavenly gate, through which the door leading unto death was shut.

*Another [canon]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, shut the gaping mouths of the lions in the den; and the children, zealous lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord![^18]

*Troparion:*
The angels now celebrate in choir in thine honored temple, O pure one, and they surround thine honored and holy robe with love and joy, O Lady; and we, with great gladness, fall down in worship, singing of thee as the glory of our race.

*Troparion:*
Thou art the rod that budded the Flower of life, the much-honored joy of all, O most pure one; vessel of the Spirit's myrrh, treasury of good things, fountain of sweet fragrances, from which the divine shrine wells forth the myrrh of healings.

*Troparion:*
The noetic hosts sing of thy greatness, O most pure one; all the patriarchs and prophets clearly proclaim it, and the holy apostles, and the choir of martyrs, and the whole fullness of the venerable ones; with whom we worship thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the budding fruit of thy womb, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
As the Song of Songs of Solomon we honor thee with joyful hymns; and having surrounded thine honored robe as another ark of sanctification, we receive saving healings, O Ever-Virgin.

*Troparion:*
Thy divine shrine containing thine honored robe has now appeared, O Virgin, as a fountain of light, emitting rays of healings and driving away the darkness of diseases, which shrine we kiss with love.

Today all the heavenly hosts rejoice together with us, O all-blameless Lady, only Ever-Virgin, at the veneration of thy robe: the apostles, prophets, and martyrs, the venerable ones, and all the righteous.

*Troparion:*
All the earth is sanctified by the deposition of thy robe, the resting place of the all-holy Sovereign; and David of old rejoices at the placement of the ark, which prefigured thee, O Ever-Virgin.

*Troparion:*
As a radiant garment and a defense hast thou granted thy robe unto the reigning city of all cities which honors thee, as thou art the Queen of all creatures, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin Mother.

*Another [canon]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
This most pure bridal chamber, O Birthgiver of God, has thy shrine been shown to be, having thine honored robe, a virginal and bridal garment, and guarding it as a treasure of life.

*Troparion:*
Reaching today the temple which manifests all heavenly adornment of the Virgin, we, the faithful, shine with the beams of wonders as with stars, and are illumined with grace.

*Troparion:*
Thy city possesses thy holy sash and thine honored robe, O Birthgiver of God, as a wall, and a bond of divine teachings, and the boast of the Orthodox, and the victory of kings.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thine ineffable glory and thy boundless grace; for thou art the fountain of wisdom, from which the Word goes forth unto all who honor thee, O most pure one, and magnify thy childbearing.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "O Changeless Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нне):*
Come, ye earthborn, today let us honor with hymns the robe of the maiden of God and Mother of God Mary, which sprinkled Christ, that by its grace we may ever be blessed.

*Glory... now and ever...:*
Thou alone hast renewed both nature and time, O Mother of God; for incorrupt is thy childbearing, and incorrupt also is the robe wherewith thou coverest thy city, wherewith thou upholdest the scepters of piety.

*At the Praises, stichera on 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кw до́блѧ):*
The shrine containing thy robe, O most pure one, is known every day by thy servants as an ark of sanctification, and a sacred defense, and glory and praise, and a fountain of healings. Where having sacredly gathered together today, we sing of thy many great wonders and the abyss of miracles. *(Twice)*

Most glorious is the place, ever radiant with beams, wherein by grace has been placed the treasure, the honored robe of the Maiden of God. Draw near, O men, and draw forth abundantly enlightenment and cleansing therefrom, and cry aloud with a thankful heart: O all-holy Virgin, we bless thee, being saved by thy childbearing!

The sacred deposition of thy robe, O Lady, we have acquired as a feast, rejoicing that today thou hast accounted thy city worthy to receive it: a sacred vestment, an unstolen treasury, an honored gift, an inalienable riches, a river of healings, full of spiritual gifts.

*Glory... now and ever... Tone 2:*
Having cleansed our senses and mind, we also keep festival with the angels, radiantly beginning the Davidic song unto the Maiden, the Bride of Christ the King of all, our God: "Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary,"[^19] saying: for having adorned her as a beautiful palace, Thou didst reckon her unto Thy city, O Master, to guide and protect it from adversary heathens by Thy sovereign power, through her prayers.

*Great Doxology and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes, Ode 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 3 (Song of the Theotokos): My soul magnifies the Lord, and my spirit has rejoiced in God my Savior. [Lk. 1:46–47]*
*Verse: For He has looked upon the low estate of His handmaiden. [Lk. 1:48]*
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 320 [Heb. 9:1–7].*
*Alleluia, Tone 8: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest. [Ps. 131:8 LXX]*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 54 [Lk. 10:38–42; 11:27–28].*
*Communion Hymn: I will take the cup of salvation. [Ps. 115:4 LXX]*

*July 3: The Holy Martyr Hyacinth.*[^20]

*Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагw чꙋдесѐ):*
A cornerstone has been laid, chosen in Zion, and an immovable foundation, upon which the ranks of martyrs were built, with whom the victor Hyacinth shines with heavenly brightness. O Thine ineffable compassion, O Master, through which, O Christ, save our souls, as Thou alone art compassionate!

Running as a stone of jacinth[^21] through the radiant city of God, having become a stone dyed red with blood, with the purple of suffering. And now pray clearly, dancing and rejoicing. Save by thy prayers those who celebrate thy most glorious and sacred memory, O all-blessed one!

Having, as an unconquerable martyr, acceptable boldness unto Christ the Master, as a lawful athlete, an advantageous hearing from Him, cease not, O right-honored one, to pray for those who faithfully sing thy memory, delivering them from these misfortunes and afflictions.

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
Taking flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth, O pure one, as an intercessor for men. Therefore deliver thy servants from every trouble and affliction, and from the snares of the crafty enemy; and account all who glorify and worship thee worthy to partake of the radiance of the chosen.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Holding all creation, Thou art lifted up upon the Tree and dost die, Who givest life unto all!—the Mother of God cried weeping, when she saw God and man, Who shone forth from her ineffably, being lifted up upon the Cross.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord, Hyacinth, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers and crushed the powerless insolence of demons. By his prayers save our souls!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "The radiant martyr Hyacinth, I praise as a stone." Tone 8.*[^22]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!

*Troparion:*
Having become a precious stone of the divine Church through the radiance of martyrdom, I now call upon thee to be my helper in thy praises.

*Troparion:*
Having left earthly glory, thou hast inherited heavenly glory, ever being with the Master of all, O crowned and glorious martyr.

*Troparion:*
The youthfulness of age and the nobility of mind for Christ's sake didst thou show forth, O passion-bearing martyr, having acted manfully against deceit.

*Theotokion:*
The Creator of all that is, for the sake of man's salvation, took up His abode in thy womb, taking flesh taking flesh, O Mother of God, building up that which is according to our likeness.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparion:*
Though a youth, thou wast shown to possess the understanding of an elder, O wise one, martyr of Christ, and wast adorned with wisdom.

*Troparion:*
Having swiftly run the course as a martyr, thou didst attain unto the valor of the martyrs and the honorable crowning.

*Theotokion:*
Direct by thy prayers toward the heavenly ascent, O Maiden full of grace, those who piously confess thee as the Mother of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
An honored stone of the Church, preserved in the heavenly treasuries, hast thou become, O Hyacinth; thou didst rebuke those who worship stones, and didst drink the cup of martyrdom, O glorious martyr. Pray Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
Without parting from the divine nature, the only Lord became flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained such, Who after childbearing preserved thee, His Mother, a Virgin all-blameless even as before childbearing: pray Him fervently to grant us great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing Him Who budded from her without travail hanging upon the Cross, maternally sobbing, cried aloud: Woe is me, my Child! How art Thou suffering willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard the mystery of Thy dispensation, O Lord, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparion:*
A lawful athlete, invested with the sufferings of piety, wast thou shown to be, O most rich one, and thou wast not terrified by the cruelty of the torturers.

*Troparion:*
Having received from heaven a crown of the stone named jacinth, O divinely-wise one, thou wast accounted worthy to rejoice in choir with the heavenly hosts as a heavenly citizen.

*Troparion:*
With valiant mind thou didst proclaim the word of piety, and didst rebuke the torturer with unconquerable resolve, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Having alone received God the Almighty, O divinely-blessed one, deliver from every affliction and trouble those who hymn thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Early in the morning we cry unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst rebuke the raving torturer, O passion-bearer, having clothed thyself with unconquerable power from God.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast acquired immortal piety, O crowned one, having received voluntary death for Christ's sake.

*Theotokion:*
May we be delivered from the snares of the enemy by thy prayers, O most pure one, who honor thy childbearing beyond word.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Suffering the torments of the ungodly, the martyr rejoiced, strengthening his mind by the Passion of the Passionless One, when he was led unceasingly by senseless men unto the Rewarder of suffering.

*Troparion:*
As a radiant jacinth stone thou hast illumined the divine day; as an offering dyed with the chosen scarlet blood of suffering, thou hast become an offering of the Church of the firstborn, O most glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
May we be delivered from grievous transgressions by thy prayers, O pure Birthgiver of God, and may we obtain, O most pure one, the divine radiance of the Son of God Who was ineffably incarnate of thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Having acquired in the midst of his soul the Tree of life, even faith in Thee, O Christ, Thy martyr became more honorable than the paradise of Eden; having boldly destroyed the tree of the serpent's deceit by the Spirit, he was crowned with Thy glory, O greatly Merciful One!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The children who arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, trampled upon the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having a pious mind clearly sovereign over the passions, thou didst spit upon the food of the lawless, O all-blessed one, nourished by the divine word, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst offer thyself as a sacred slaughter, most near unto the Lord, O wise one, through purity of soul and brightness of mind, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
When the human race was falling by reason of mortal corruption, O Virgin, having conceived the Fountain of immortality and incorrupt Life, thou didst give rest unto the God of the fathers, O pure and blessed one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly kindle the furnace for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Redeemer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
From grievous distress and prison thou didst reach the spacious and beautiful breadth of paradise, beholding the radiant brightness of the saints and seeing the angelic choirs; standing before God, thou criest fervently: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst love God from thy soul, resisting sin even unto blood, O blessed athlete, and clearly slaying the enemies; and adorned with victory crowns, thou criest earnestly: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
As a comely offering, as a transparent stone, as a sacred purple robe hast thou adorned the heavenly temple, the Holy of Holies; having adorned it with the radiance of martyrdom, thou unceasingly criest: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Beyond word hast thou given birth unto the unoriginate Word of God for the good adornment of rational nature, through Whom we are delivered from corrupting death and have received the life-creating Spirit; therefore as the true and real Mother of God we glorify thee, O Virgin, unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, the unwedded Mother of the Most High God; Thee, who beyond understanding didst give birth by a word unto God in truth, higher than the most pure hosts, with unceasing doxologies we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Make the Master merciful by thy prayers unto all who faithfully hymn thine unconquerable suffering, through which thou didst abolish idolatrous deceit and didst make clear the word of piety.

*Troparion:*
Comely in appearance unto the angelic hosts, as with a heavenly sphere, O Hyacinth, dyed with the blood of confession and faith in Christ, and adorned with the crown of martyrdom.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast entirely an all-sacred receptacle of God; thou didst sanctify body and soul by unslackening resistance in the torturer's and lawless battle; therefore we all bless thee.

*Theotokion:*
Slay in me, O Lady, the sin that still lives; quicken the mortification of my soul by the operation of the true Life that was born of thy womb, for the sake of ineffable mercy, of those who piously magnify thee.

*July 4: Our Father among the Saints Andrew, Archbishop of Crete, the Hierosolymite;*[^23] *and the Venerable Martha, Mother of Saint Symeon the Stylite of the Wonderful Mountain.*[^24]

*Stichera of Saint Andrew, on 3.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Celestial Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Having hated the bodily pleasures of earth, with pure conduct and a simple heart, thou didst join thyself unto the Lord, O wise father; and thou wast shown to be an inspired instrument of the Comforter, having sung for us a saving song.[^25]

Having found the Mother of the Lord, the cause of great praises, with honey-flowing words thou didst sing of her, O Andrew, and of the assembly of the sacred apostles and blessed martyrs; together with them remember us who honor thee.

We know thee, O father, as a pillar of Orthodoxy, an image of chastity, and a pattern of meekness, and as a divine hymnographer who enlightens Christ's Church, O sacred initiate Andrew; therefore we celebrate thy festival.

*Other stichera of Saint Martha, on 3.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кw до́блѧ):*
Unto painful abstinence, unto manly struggles, unto warfare against the enemy who tripped up human nature, thou didst separate thyself, not sparing the flesh through fasts and tears for the sake of divine love; therefore thou didst accomplish divine ascetical struggles, having humbled the mind of the adversary, O honored and most glorious Martha! *(Twice)*

Thou didst loathe temporal things, and didst flee from the love of corruptible things; and thou didst desire the heavenly lot, eternal and worthy of the righteous, incorrupt, O pure one, and unceasing life. Therefore in faith performing thy radiant and light-bearing memory, we honor thee with divine hymns, O glorious Martha!

*Glory... the same Tone, Idiomelon:*
By spiritual grace thou takest away diseases, thou drivest away evil spirits, and thou healest passions, O all-glorious Martha, and thou enlightenest the faithful with the flashings of thy wonders. Therefore today with unceasing voices we sing of thy radiant, light-bearing, and right-marvelous memory.

*Now and ever... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
Seeing Thee upon the Tree, her Lamb and Shepherd, the Ewe-lamb that bare Thee wept and maternally cried aloud: O my beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How were Thy hands and feet, O Word, pierced with nails by the lawless, and how hast Thou poured out Thy blood, O Master?

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 8:*
The multitudes of monastics honor thee as an instructor, O our father Andrew; for by thy path we have truly learned to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst refute the power of the enemy, O converser with the angels, companion of the venerable and the righteous; with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved!

*Now and ever... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагw чꙋдесѐ):*
The undefiled heifer, seeing the Calf nailed willingly upon the Tree, weeping piteously, cried aloud: Woe is me, most beloved Child! What hath the thankless Jewish council rendered unto Thee, desiring to bereave me of Thee, O All-beloved?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
The truth of things revealed thee unto thy flock as a rule of faith, an image of meekness, and a teacher of temperance; therefore through humility thou hast acquired the high things, and through poverty the rich things: O father Andrew, pray Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Another Troparion, Tone 8:*
In thee, O mother, was preserved that which is according to the image; for taking up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thine deeds didst teach us to overlook the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, a thing immortal: therefore thy spirit rejoices together with the angels, O venerable Martha!

*One canon of the Octoechos, and two of the saints.*
*Canon of Saint Andrew, whose acrostic is: "With manly songs let us praise Andrew." Tone 5.*[^26]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto the Red Sea, He Who shatters battles with a lofty arm, Christ shook horse and rider, and saved Israel as they sang a song of victory.[^27]

*Troparion:*
Having shaken off sleep from thine eyelids, by virtue thou didst prepare thyself as a radiant dwelling for the Good One, O divinely-manifest, venerable father, and a divine resting place.

*Troparion:*
Moving thy lyre in all-night singing, Christ's Church thou didst clarify the hymnody of Christ's Church, O marvelous Andrew, instructed by the divine grace of the Holy Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Wisely obeying the divinely-written law, thou didst mortify the passions of the flesh in an ascetical manner; and thou didst elevate thy mind through thy firm desire, singing divine songs, O all-wise one.

*Theotokion:*
All desire and most pure delight is Thy Son and God, the fullness of good things, O all-blameless one; pray Him to deliver those who now flee unto thee in faith.

*Canon of the Venerable Martha, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who shook the tyranny of Pharaoh into the sea, and guided Israel through the dry land, let us sing unto Christ, for He is glorified unto ages.[^28]

*Troparion:*
Wounded by the divine desire for eternal good things, thou didst despise corruptible glory and food; therefore also an angelic life, O venerable mother, didst thou live in a godly manner.

*Troparion:*
Inflamed with divine zeal, having lived in a godly and valiant manner, thou didst give birth unto a luminary for the faithful and a bulwark, a second Samuel,[^29] and the fair fosterling of the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
An offspring of abstinence wast thou shown to be, having shone forth upon us a sun of many lights and a great healer, the all-wise Symeon, and for the eastern land a protector and a wall.

*Theotokion:*
Offspring of the earth, O Mother of God, having become children of God through divine grace and through thine ineffable childbearing, we diligently hymn thee, the only cause of our salvation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and didst suspend its weight unrestrained, on the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, establish Thy Church, O only Good and Lover of mankind![^30]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst guide thy life by virtue, O venerable one; and united unto God by divine desire, thou wast shown to be an articulate instrument, enlightening the whole inhabited earth with hymns, drawing from divine treasures.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast filled with heavenly wisdom; for having opened wide the mouth of thy soul, O venerable father, thou didst openly receive the light-bearing radiance of the thrice-radiant brightness, as a sacred priest.

*Troparion:*
Having strengthened the powers of thy soul, O venerable one, and restrained the flesh by temperance, thou wast an all-radiant star unto all, adorning the Church with thine Orthodox teachings and hymnic songs.

*Theotokion:*
To deliver me from the captivity of passions, I now pray thee, O all-blameless Mother of God, and to cleanse all the wounds of sins, O thou who didst gloriously give birth unto Him Who takes away the sin of the world!

*Another Heirmos:*
There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, of Whom all creation sings: there is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^31]

*Troparion:*
Having offered unto the Master an all-honored fruit from thy womb, thou didst offer thyself as a perfect sacrifice by the fire of temperance, O Martha, hymning Him.

*Troparion:*
An acceptable and ever-living sacrifice didst thou offer unto thy Christ, him who budded forth from thy womb, O God-bearing and venerable mother, confirmation of the faithful, and a wall and a bulwark.

*Troparion:*
Unto heavenly height thou didst wing thy soul together with the body, and thou wast a fellow-dweller with the wise virgins, and a divine image unto those who flee unto God in faith.

*Theotokion:*
Upon the rock of my hope, O most pure one, establish the ascents of my soul; that remaining ever unshaken, I may praise and glorify thee, my only protectress.

*Kontakion of Saint Martha, Tone 2, Podoben: "In Prayers" (Въ моли́твахъ):*
Standing in prayers before the Lord, offering songs and praises unto the most pure Virgin Mother of God, O honored Martha, thou didst bear a sacred child, the most marvelous Symeon, a universal luminary: with him pray ever for all of us!

*Then the Sessional Hymn of Saint Andrew, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рw предварѝ):*
As with precious stones, with thy words, O all-wise one, having adorned the crown of the Church, thou wast a sacred priest, O Andrew; therefore after many labors in this life, thou hast received an ageless kingdom and glory: therein remember, O father, those who remember thee!

*Glory... Sessional Hymn of Saint Martha, Tone 4:*
Unceasingly watching in prayers, and pleasing God through almsgiving, often visiting divine temples, O most praised Martha, praying with tears and fasting and faith, thou didst receive a sacred child: with him pray that we who honor thee with love may be saved from perils!

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*

O all-blameless Virgin, who didst give birth unto the transcendent God, unceasingly pray Him together with the bodiless hosts, that remission of transgressions and correction of life before the end be granted unto us who hymn thee with faith and love as is meet, O only all-hymned one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
O all-blameless Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced through thy most holy soul when thou didst see Thy Son and God being willingly crucified:[^32] cease not, O blessed one, to pray Him to grant us remission of transgressions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Understanding Thy divine emptying, Habakkuk farsightedly cried aloud with trembling unto Thee, O Christ: Unto the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed ones, art Thou come![^33]

*Troparion:*
Thy word adorned by thy life, thou wast shown to be a sure rule of theology, O marvelous one, clearly proclaiming the glory of the all-hymned Trinity.

*Troparion:*
Having by action shown the ascent to contemplation, O wise one, and by contemplation the image of the common life, O Andrew of godly voice, thou wast made like unto them.

*Troparion:*
As a divine hierarch, O most wealthy one, contending pastorally, thou didst drive away from the Church the onslaughts of the lions, O Andrew, whose name is synonymous with manly courage![^34]

*Troparion:*
Having found the Mother of God to be a cause of great praises, thou didst show forth twofold zeal, O wise one, honoring her who is higher than praises with manifold praises.

*Theotokion:*
Without intercourse thou didst conceive incorruptibly in thy womb, and without travail didst thou give birth; and after childbearing, having given birth in the flesh unto God, thou wast preserved a virgin.

*Another Heirmos:*
From the shaded mountain, O Word, the prophet foresaw in godly vision that Thou wast to become incarnate of the only Mother of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power.[^35]

*Troparion:*
Having Christ ever in thy mind, thou didst loathe the love and desire of corruptible things; and therefore, O Martha, having glorified Him, thou wast accounted worthy of gifts.

*Troparion:*
Having burned up the raging surge of passions, O Martha, thou didst extinguish the onslaughts of wild beasts; and thou didst put to shame the intricate snares of the spiritual Amalek.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast an outer wall and bulwark of the faithful, unto those who honor thee with pure love and celebrate thy holy memory, O divinely-blessed helper of the faithful!

*Theotokion:*

...thou wast shown forth as an intelligible cloud, O Mother of God, bearing in the flesh the very Sun of glory, Who illumines all creation with the light of divine grace.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, unto Thee I rise early, and unto Thee I cry: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou alone art compassionate![^36]

*Troparion:*
Now adorning ourselves with thy teachings and divinely-inspired hymns, sacredly delighting in them, O all-blessed Andrew, we honor thy memory.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly enlightened by spiritual radiance, thou didst most sacredly praise the choirs of the saints; with them thou now rejoicest, O all-blessed Andrew.

*Troparion:*
Now no longer through the senses of the corruptible body, nor through imaginings dost thou behold divine things, but noetically, O all-wise one, through spiritual operations hast thou been united unto the higher things.

*Theotokion:*
With tears I fall down before thee, earnestly praying to be delivered from my transgressions now through thee, O all-blameless one, and to be accounted worthy of eternal joy.

*Another Heirmos:*
O Lord Who hast enlightened the ends of the earth through the knowledge of God from the night of ignorance, enlighten me with the morning of Thy love for mankind![^37]

*Troparion:*
Beholding thine offspring lifted up unto heaven upon a pillar, through his divine deeds, O Martha, thou didst sing of Christ the Most High.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly receiving a promise from heaven, O divinely-blessed Martha, from divine revelation thou didst conceive a great minister.

*Troparion:*
The city of Antioch offers thine offspring, O venerable mother, as a fervent protector, a wall, and a refuge for those who are in afflictions.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as our power against wounds, O Bride of God, we who hope in thee conquer the ranks of adversaries.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Tame the sea of passions raging with a soul-corrupting tempest, O Master Christ, and lead me up from corruption, as Thou art compassionate!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst refute the godless speakers with radiant lips; for thou didst truly explain the veneration of the honorable icons, O sacred initiate.[^38]

*Troparion:*
Sacredly didst thou adorn thy life, O glorious father; for having extinguished the passionate turmoil, thou hast fled unto the life of dispassion.

*Troparion:*
In the beauties of thy words and teachings, all the faithful piously delighting, rejoice, overcoming the idle talk of heretics.

*Theotokion:*
He Who shone forth unoriginately from the Father unites Himself entirely unto me, through the incarnation from thee, O all-blameless Bride of God.

*Another Heirmos:*
Grant me a radiant robe, O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God![^39]

*Troparion:*
Desiring from thy soul the Queen of all, thou didst adorn thyself with purity; and for the sake of her gifts, O Martha, thou didst obtain them.

*Troparion:*
At the mature wisdom of thine age, O honored mother, the angels marveled; and men hymn the Creator of all Who glorified thee.

*Troparion:*
As a fair-growing and beautiful tree thou didst adorn thyself, and wast an undefiled mirror through the manifestation of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Foreseeing thee ever as a calm haven in the storm of passions and afflictions, O Lady, I flee unto the haven of salvation.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having clearly sounded the trumpet of divine sweet melody, thou wast shown to be the brightest luminary of the world, shining with the light of the Trinity, O venerable Andrew: therefore we all cry unto thee: Cease not to pray for all of us!

*Ikos:*
Wretched as I am, I desire to praise thy life, O father; grant me, for the sake of thy righteous deeds, to seek from thence as gifts the praises of thine adornments, and to weave a wreath for thy venerable head, O Andrew, which the angels revere, crying: Cease not to pray for all of us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
