The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers extinguished the flame, and dewed the children who sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God![^40]

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest behold the brightness of the saints, O venerable one, with the brightness of good deeds thou didst enlighten thy life, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Troparion:*
Clothed in the body, thou didst emulate the ranks of the bodiless hosts, O venerable father; with them rejoicing in unison, thou criest aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Troparion:*
Jerusalem now brightly rejoices, having shone thee forth unto the world as a luminary of many lights, O blessed one, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Theotokion:*
He Who was formerly bodiless is incarnate of thy holy womb, O most pure one, through His countless compassion; unto Whom we all sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
He Who in the beginning established the earth, and Who by a word didst establish the heavens: Blessed art Thou unto ages, O Lord God of our fathers![^41]

*Troparion:*
Having put off the passionate garment, thou didst clothe thyself in the garment of dispassion; and having purely betrothed thyself unto Christ the Bridegroom, O venerable mother, thou didst please Him.

*Troparion:*
A pillar of fire was thine offspring shown to be, O honored Martha, enlightening the world, and cleansing those in the world from dark ignorance and every other wrath.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst quench the fleshly haughtiness, and didst abhor the craftiness of spiritual enemies, O venerable Martha; and their entanglements didst thou cast down unto the ground.

*Theotokion:*
That we may behold the glory of Thy Son, and be delighted with its honor, we who desire thee and hymn thee pray thee fervently, O thou who art full of divine grace!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto Thee, the Creator, the children in the furnace, having woven a universal choir, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The Church of the new ark, O father, thou didst clearly gladden, assembling choirs, crying: Hymn the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
A new song in the Church, O blessed one, thou didst reverently cry aloud unto the Almighty: Hymn the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Reverently accomplishing works by virtue, thou didst sing of the sufferings of all the saints of old who were admired and perfected in Christ unto all ages, O all-wise Andrew.

*Theotokion:*
Come, let us hymn the pure Maiden of God, saying in divine songs: Rejoice, O Virgin, through whom joy was given unto human nature, O all-blessed one!

*Another Heirmos:*
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush through fire revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin unto Moses, hymn ye the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^42]

*Troparion:*
Through poverty of temporal things thou hast acquired eternal riches and glory, O Martha; and therefore with love thou didst cry aloud: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
God, knowing thy heart and desire, from infancy divinely crowned with cordial faith him whom thou didst bear, O venerable Martha, who cries unto Him in faith unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The radiant day of thy feast illumines the minds of the faithful, and illumines all the earth, like the morning star running even unto the ends of the earth.

*Theotokion:*
He Who formed our nature, having clothed Himself in it from thee, O Mother of God, shapes and renews it as the Lover of mankind, unto those who hymn and truly glorify thee as the Mother of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin had in her womb, and bare a Son, Emmanuel, both God and man; the Orient is His name: Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.[^43]

*Troparion:*
The proclamation of thy works has traversed all the earth, and the beauty and brightness of thy teachings, O blessed Andrew; therefore Christ, the King of all, has crowned thee with a crown of comeliness.[^44]

*Troparion:*
Delighting in the transcendent three-sunned radiance through a higher union, O Andrew, initiate of sacred things, now by thy prayers preserve from perils those who reverently celebrate thy holy memory in love!

*Troparion:*
With the bodiless ranks thou now rejoicest in the heavens; for having lived unswervingly on earth as they live, thou wast shown to be an eminent luminary of the rule of faith, O divinely-speaking, right-marvelous one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst stop the unceasing onset of death brought upon us, having truly born in the flesh beyond understanding the Eternal Life; colliding with Which, bitter hades was abolished with its mouth, O all-holy Virgin Mother!

*Another Heirmos:*
We magnify thee, the Mother of God, and we glorify thee, O Virgin Mother of God, as having given birth unto the Savior of our souls.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing together with thy son in one choir, O Martha, pray Christ our God for those who honor thee!

*Troparion:*
Having lived piously in labor, thou didst strive manfully; therefore thou hast stored up heavenly riches.

*Troparion:*
We kiss the shrine of thy relics, O all-honored Martha, from which we draw the waters of healings.

*Theotokion:*
Shining forth brighter than the rays of the sun, thy prayer, O most pure one, illumines those who magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "The Heaven with Stars" (Нб҃о ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Desiring the angelic life, O venerable one, thou didst therefore manfully subdue the fleshly passions, having become equal to the angels, O God-bearing father!

*Theotokion:*
Thee do all of us sinners have as an intercessor, O all-holy Virgin: do thou make Thy Son propitious through thy maternal prayers, O most pure one!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 6:*
O venerable father Andrew, into all the earth has the proclamation of thy righteous deeds gone forth;[^45] therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors, thou didst destroy the demonic hosts, and didst attain unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate. Having boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for our souls!

*Now and ever... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "On the Third Day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Pondering Thy seedless conception and ineffable childbearing, I marveled greatly: how hast Thou thus, like an evildoer, willed to die, O my Son?—the all-pure one cried weeping.

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^46] *Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song.*[^47] *Epistle to the Hebrews, section 334 from the midpoint.*[^48] *Alleluia, Tone 2: Thy priests shall clothe themselves in righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.*[^49] *Gospel of Matthew, section 21.*[^50] *Communion Verse: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be.*[^51]

*July 5: Our Venerable Father Athanasius of Athos.*[^52]
*And where there is a temple of Athanasius, we celebrate a Vigil.*
*At Small Vespers, stichera, on 4. Tone 1, Podoben: "O Most Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́льні́и мꙋ́ченицы):*
Having become an ardent lover of divine peace, by this thou didst cast down every power of demons, and didst torment their craftiness; and thou didst keep every virtue unharmed by those deceptive wiles, O father Athanasius!

Setting forth the fear of the Lord as the best beginning of wisdom,[^53] as an unshakable foundation and confirmed by Him, thou didst keep the divine commandments, and being cleansed by them, thou didst enlighten thy soul; and thou wast accounted worthy of ineffable mysteries in thy heart, O father Athanasius!

...Athanasius.

*Troparion:*
In the earth thou didst not hide the talent given thee from above from divine grace,[^54] but thou didst hand it over, and unto many others generously, richly of divine gifts; of which thou didst partake, as a faithful steward, and wast shown to be an heir of God, O Athanasius!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Let us all praise him whose name is synonymous with immortality,[^55] who on Athos manfully and valiantly fasted, who also gathered this flock, and in love raised a temple unto the Lord, and dedicated all things unto the Mother of God; whom he also prays for us who celebrate his memory in faith.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the vine...

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Let us, the choirs of fathers who are on Athos, assemble in memory of the divine and wise Athanasius, glorifying the Lord!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^56]
From thy soul save those who flee unto thy divine temple, O all-blessed one; save them from perils, O divinely-wise Athanasius!

*Verse: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord, who shall greatly delight in His commandments.*[^57]
The venerable assembly of fathers having gathered, together with thy disciples, we all touch thine honorable shrine.

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
The choirs on high, O Virgin Mother of God, sing of thee together with those below, unceasingly glorifying thy most pure childbearing.

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
At thy life in the flesh the angelic ranks marveled, how with the body thou didst go forth unto invisible wrestling, O ever-glorious one, and didst wound the demonic hosts: whence, O Athanasius, Christ rewarded thee with rich gifts; therefore, O father, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*

*At Great Vespers we sing "Blessed is the man", the first antiphon.*[^58]
*At "Lord, I Have Cried", stichera on 8. Tone 6, Podoben: "With What Praiseworthy Crowns" (Кі́ими похва́лными):*
With what praiseworthy honors shall we crown Athanasius?—him who excelled in deeds and abounded in graces; him who with the sword of God-illumined brightness cut down the assaults of passions; a pillar shining with divine visions; a luminary of temperance, shedding radiance upon the candlestick of the soul; preaching the commandments of God Who gives life unto all the King Who gives life unto all.[^59]

*Tone 6, Podoben: "With What Praiseworthy Crowns" (Кі́ими похва́лными):*
With what praiseworthy crowns of words shall we crown Athanasius?—the lover of the sacred course; him who was taken up by heavenly teachings on the divine wings of the vision of God; the unshakable pillar of humility; the unswerving wall of discernment; the venerable beauty of moral adornment; who prays unto Christ God, the only compassionate One, for his children!

An ever-radiant dawn didst thou appear, O venerable one, with rays of grace; those who were blinded by the gloom of passions hast thou brought unto the unwaning light, O divinely-wise father Athanasius. Therefore, as one who increased the talent,[^60] Christ presents thee as a physician unto souls, cutting down with the sword of words those that were rotted by incurable sores, and ever granting health and salvation unto all!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
As thy life was divine, so was thine all-sacred end, O father Athanasius; for the multitude of the whole mountain,[^61] having gathered together when they saw thee breathless upon the bier, cried out with loud voices: Give a final word unto thy servants, O holy one! Teach us where thou leavest thy children, O father, whom thou didst pity as a father, truly merciful and beloved! Yet even if the tomb covers thee here, on high we all possess thee, who love and honor thee, as a rich intercessor and petitioner before God!

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*
Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?...[^62]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*
*The Three Readings:*

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 3):*[^63]
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was accounted to be affliction, and their going from us destruction, but they are in peace. For though in the sight of men they be punished, their hope is full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly rewarded, for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace did He prove them, and as a whole burnt-offering sacrifice did He accept them. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and like sparks through stubble shall they run. They shall judge nations, and rule over peoples, and the Lord shall reign in them unto the ages. Those who trust in Him shall understand the truth, and the faithful in love shall abide with Him. For grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation is among His elect.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 5):*[^64]
The righteous live unto ages, and their reward is in the Lord, and the care for them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive the kingdom of majesty, and the crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord, for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He protect them. He shall take His zeal for a panoply, and shall arm creation for vengeance upon enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall take unfeigned judgment as a helmet. He shall take invulnerable holiness as a shield; and He shall sharpen severe wrath into a weapon; and the world shall fight together with Him against the senseless. Right-aiming arrows of lightning shall go forth, and as from a well-drawn bow of the clouds they shall fly to the mark. And from a stone-casting wrath full hailstones shall fall. The water of the sea shall rage against them, and rivers shall violently drown them. The spirit of power shall stand against them, and like a whirlwind shall sift them. And lawlessness shall lay waste the whole earth, and evildoing shall overturn the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O kings, and understand; learn, ye judges of the ends of the earth! Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and that pride yourselves over mobs of nations! For dominion is given you from the Lord, and strength from the Most High.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 4):*[^65]
Though a righteous man happen to die early, he shall be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is the stature of old age. Pleasing God, he was beloved. And living among sinners he was translated, he was caught away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of wickedness darkens that which is good, and the wandering of lust perverts the innocent mind. Being made perfect in a short time, he fulfilled long years; for his soul was pleasing unto the Lord. Therefore He hastened to take him out of the midst of malice. But the people saw it, and understood it not, neither did they lay such a thing in their mind: that grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation is among His elect.

*At the Litiya, stichera. Tone 1. Automela:*
The unwaning luminary of the universe, let us all hymn Athanasius for brighter than the sun did he shine forth in the stronghold of faith, and guided the members of the faithful unto virtue: on the one hand by God-inspired teachings and the saving entrance of words, which like unstinting and abundant streams he poured forth from his lips; and on the other hand by marvelous deeds and an angel-equal life, drawing them unto zeal. And both in life and in word most radiant, as an excellent guide, he showed himself in a godlike manner. By his prayers, O Christ our God, preserve this flock, and confirm the faith of the Orthodox, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Tone 2:*
The lover of Christ who shares His name with immortality,[^66] the true initiate of the Savior's mysteries, the beauty and boast of all monastics and teachers, sanctified from infancy unto the Lord, let us, the choirs of fathers, assemble and praise! For having received divine desire within himself, he left the worldly deceit, and took the yoke of Christ upon His shoulder,[^67] and manfully vanquished the hosts of demons, having shown us in deed the excellent path of divine love. For having run through this in diligence, he attained the true Light of the all-creating Trinity. O awesome wonder and godlike deed! that being earthly by nature, he became equal to the noetic essences, receiving the glory and honor of God; and he is an intercessor for us, that we may obtain eternal good things in the Day of Judgment!

*Tone 4:*
The exceedingly marvelous labors of the temperance of all thy life amazed the noetic hosts and struck the earthly with awe; for like one completely bodiless, even in aged flesh, thou didst vanquish the invisible enemies. All generations of the pious hymn thee, and most of all thine honorable flock, the radiance of thy labors, an inscription on a pillar which thou didst show forth as a fair city in the wilderness,[^68] having godlikely fashioned an abode for monastic assemblies, which as with precious vestments is adorned by thy miracles and valiant sufferings; and through thy supplications and prayers, O Athanasius, it asks to be preserved unto Christ, Who possesses great mercy!

*Glory... Tone 5:*
Let us sound the trumpet of songs; for the grace of the Spirit, more splendid than any trumpet, calls all unto the praising of the God-bearing father! Let kings and princes marvel at the true servant of the King of all, who the world-ruler, the principalities and powers having conquered them with all the armor of the divine Spirit! Let pastors and teachers praise him who in truth was an excellent rule and pattern for the flock; who was radiant in commandments, and valiant in faith; who was high in visions, and above the clouds in deeds; in teachings a torrent of delight; a guide to the wandering; a support to the bowed down; showing mercy unto all who are afflicted; a great adornment to Athos; and praising him let us all say: O supreme among fathers, Athanasius, ever stand before thy servants, O our father, and save thy flock by thy prayers!

*Now and ever... Theotokion, in the same Tone:*
Let us sound the trumpet of songs! For having bowed down from on high, the Queen of all, the Virgin Mother, crowns with blessings those who hymn her. Let kings and princes assemble, and let them clap their hands in hymns unto the Queen, who gave birth unto the King Who was pleased in His love for mankind to loose those that were formerly held fast by death. Ye pastors and teachers, having gathered together, let us praise the most pure Mother of the Good Shepherd: the gold-gleaming candlestick, the light-bearing cloud, more spacious than the heavens, the ensouled ark, the fire-formed throne of the Master, the manna-receiving golden urn, the closed door of the Word, the refuge of all Christians; and praising her with God-proclaiming hymns, let us say thus: O palace of the Word, account us humble ones worthy of the Heavenly Kingdom, for nothing is impossible unto thy mediation!

*At the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ):*
Rejoice, conversing with the angelic choirs, O venerable Athanasius; for having truly kept virtue, thou didst live this life on earth, O father! Thou wast shown to be an undefiled mirror of purity, receiving the radiant lightnings of the Holy Spirit, O all-glorious one. Whence, being illumined, thou didst foresee the things to come, foretelling all things, being taught by the divine shining of the light of Christ: Whom do thou pray to grant great mercy unto our souls!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^69]
Rejoice, having been the leader of fasters and an invincible champion! For having cut off the root of passions and the assaults of demons, having manfully stood firm, O Athanasius, thou didst put their soul-corrupting deception to shame. And thou didst reveal the operation and invincible power of the Savior's Cross, wherewith also having girded thyself, thou didst conquer all who reject the divine appearance of Christ in the flesh: Whom do thou pray to grant unto our souls great mercy unto our souls!

*Verse: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord, who shall greatly delight in His commandments.*[^70]
A radiant pillar confirmed in virtue, and a cloud overshadowing those on Athos didst thou appear, O father! Leading those who behold God from earth unto heaven, by the rod of the Cross thou didst dry up the sea of passions. And having repulsed the noetic Amalek,[^71] thou didst find the stumblingless heavenly ascent, O divinely-blessed one, and an unchanging portion, rejoicing as thou standest with the bodiless hosts before the throne of Christ: Whom do thou pray to grant great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
The fatherly beauty, the adornment of fasters, the fountain of miracles, and an unashamed petitioner before God, having gathered together, O ye lovers of the feast, let us hymn with hymnic praises, saying: Rejoice, rule and pattern of monastic life, and sure seal! Rejoice, most radiant luminary, enlightening every virtue with brightnesses! Rejoice, great comforter in afflictions, and fervent protector of the sorrowful! Therefore, O Athanasius, cease not to pray unto Christ God for this thy flock, and for all the faithful who honor thine all-venerable falling asleep!

*Now and ever... in the same Tone:*
O Mother of God, thou art the true vine...[^72]

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
At thy life in the flesh...[^73] *(Written at Small Vespers).*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the Troparion twice.*
*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thou who wast our intercessor...[^74]

*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
An all-radiant star of monastics didst thou appear, and a sure step, the limit of temperance, and a most radiant mirror of discernment, O venerable one! For those who celebrate thy memory with joy, ask remission of transgressions, O all-blessed one, as thou standest before God! *(Twice)*

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
Guide us into the way of repentance, who have ever turned aside unto the pathless ways of evil, and have provoked the all-good Lord unto anger, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of desperate men, and dwelling-place of God!

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Held fast by divine desire of immortality, O Athanasius, and having taken the Cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst of old with desire settle on Athos, where, O venerable one, thou didst gather a flock unto the Lord therefore also we have acquired thee as a fervent petitioner unto the Lord! *(Twice)*

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
A divine tabernacle of the Word wast thou, O only most pure Virgin Mother, having surpassed the angels in purity! Me who more than all am dust, defiled by fleshly transgressions, cleanse by the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having received contemplation and action, adorning thy senses, O venerable one, thou didst direct thy life unto the divine commandments; for thou didst appear as a bright luminary unto monastics, guiding and enlightening us unto understanding. Therefore, praising, we magnify with love the memory of thy departure, O God-bearing Athanasius! Therefore we cry unto thee: Pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
The heavenly door and ark, the all-holy mountain, the bright cloud let us hymn, the unburnt bush, the rational paradise, the recall of Eve, the great treasure of the whole universe; for in her was salvation wrought for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions. Therefore we cry unto her: Pray thy Son to grant remission of transgressions unto those who piously worship thine all-holy childbearing!

*Then the Gradual Antiphons, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^75]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*[^76]

*The Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 43.*[^77]

*After Psalm 50, sticheron, Tone 6:*
Let us all praise him whose name is synonymous with immortality, who on Athos manfully and valiantly fasted; who also gathered this flock, and in love raised a temple unto the Lord, and dedicated all things unto the Mother of God; whom he also prays for us who celebrate his memory in faith.

*Canon of the Mother of God, on 6; and of the Saint, on 8. Canon of the Mother of God, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having crossed the water as if it were dry land, and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried: Unto our Redeemer and God let us sing![^78]

*Troparion:*
Held fast by many temptations, unto thee do I flee seeking salvation: O Mother of the Word and Virgin, save me from grave and cruel dangers!

*Troparion:*
The assaults of passions agitate me, seeking to fill my soul with much despondency; quiet them, O Maiden, by the tranquility of Thy Son and God, O all-immaculate one!

*Troparion:*
Unto thee who didst give birth unto the Savior and God, I pray, O Virgin, that I may be delivered from cruel dangers; for unto thee now fleeing, I stretch forth both soul and understanding.

*Troparion:*
Account me who am ailing in body and soul worthy of divine visitation and providence from thee, O only Mother of God, as thou art good, and the Mother of the Good One!

*Canon of the Saint, in the same Tone (Tone 8).*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The miracle-working rod of Moses, of old striking the sea in the form of a cross and dividing it, drowned Pharaoh who rode in chariots; but it saved fugitive Israel who fled on foot, singing a song unto God.[^79]

*Troparion:*
Having been named after immortality,[^80] ending thy life, thou wast shown to be the receptacle of true immortality, O father Athanasius, having passed from temporal things; but standing before Christ, remember us who commemorate thee!

*Troparion:*
Having been pious from thine earliest infancy,[^81] O venerable one, the deprivation of thy parents did in no wise hinder thee from the acquisition of virtue; but with all thy might thou didst turn all desire and diligence unto the only Creator!

*Troparion:*
From defiled lips, having grown bold, I sing praise unto thee, having become the recipient of desire for thee, and a partner of love; but, O father, be thou an intercessor, and grant that I may worthily offer this praise unto thee!

*Theotokion:*
Beyond word didst thou give birth unto a young Child, Who is the Ancient of Days,[^82] Who showed a new path of virtue upon the earth; whose faithful servant, Athanasius, O Maiden, consumed with love, built this temple unto thee!

*Katavasia:*
I shall open my mouth...[^83]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Fashioner of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church, do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind![^84]

*Troparion:*
A protection and shelter of my life do I set thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: guide me unto thy haven, thou cause of good things, confirmation of the faithful, O only all-hymned one!

*Troparion:*
I pray thee, O Virgin, to disperse the agitation of my soul and the storm of my grief; for thou, O Bride of God, didst give birth unto Christ, the Prince of peace, O only most pure one!

*Troparion:*
Having given birth unto the Benefactor, the Cause of good things, pour forth the wealth of beneficence unto all; for thou canst do all things, having given birth unto Christ Who is mighty in strength, O divinely-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Assailed by cruel sicknesses and painful passions, do thou help me, O Virgin; for I know thee to be an inexhaustible, unfailing treasury of healings, O all-immaculate one!

*Another Heirmos:*
O Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens in understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters, establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments, for there is none holy besides Thee, O only Lover of mankind![^85]

*Troparion:*
Already from childhood taken up with love toward God, gathering those of thine own age among children, and forming a choir, thou didst spiritually lead them, removing them from childish ways, O all-wise one!

*Troparion:*
Drawn by desire of wisdom, thou didst come unto the prudent city of Constantine,[^86] O divinely-wise one; and like a wise bee, having gathered with diligent care what was pure from words, thou didst leave aside what was unprofitable!

*Troparion:*
Having steered the ship of thy soul by the sail of the Cross, O father, comfortably and easily didst thou escape the cruel drowning of worldly life, and rejoicing didst draw it unto the calm of divine havens!

*Theotokion:*
He Who was ineffably begotten of the Father before the ages, in the last days came forth from thy womb and deified our nature, O Virgin Mother, clearly bringing forth the choirs of the venerable!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having left all worldly things from thy youth, both the fair and the pleasant, thou didst zealously follow Him Who called thee, O venerable one; and having taken the Cross of thy Lord upon thy shoulder, in fastings, O father, thou didst exhaust thy body. Therefore also the all-good Lord excellently appoints thee a shepherd unto His sheep, O blessed Athanasius: pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
As a virgin and only one among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all human generations call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt within thee, and like an infant thou dost nourish with milk the Creator and Lord! Therefore the angelic and human race worthily glorifies thine all-holy childbearing, and with one accord we cry unto thee: Pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who in faith worship thine all-holy childbearing!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead![^87]

*Troparion:*
O thou who didst give birth unto the Helmsman, the Lord, calm the tumult of my passions and the storm of my transgressions, O Bride of God!

*Troparion:*
Grant unto me who call upon the abyss of thy tender mercy, O thou who didst give birth unto the Compassionate One, and the Savior of all who hymn Thee!

*Troparion:*
Delighting in thy gifts, O most pure one, we sing a hymn of thanksgiving, knowing thee to be the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Having thee as hope and confirmation, and an immovable wall of salvation, O all-hymned one, we are delivered from every distress.

*Another Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the bosom of the Father hast visited our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^88]

*Troparion:*
Making thy mind master over passions, and being guided by divine laws, and confirmed by the commandments of God, thou didst piously repulse the conversations of youths, and assemblies of the defiled, and dance-gatherings of demons, choosing what was better like one all-wise!

*Troparion:*
Having received the light yoke of the Savior,[^89] and being sustained by divine fear, and purifying body and soul, thou wast the cause of purity and chastity, the boundary of temperance, the seal of dispassion, and the image of love, O Athanasius!

*Troparion:*
Having passed through our learning and the external education of philosophy,[^90] the budding and the dialectical, each didst thou traverse, fenced about by righteousness, by wisdom and discernment, by humility and understanding, and by other fair gifts!

*Theotokion:*
Borne upon the awesome shoulders of the cherubim, the Master, as upon a fiery throne, dwelt within thy womb, O pure one; and by the assumption of flesh He deified human nature, as the excellent one among the venerable, Athanasius, teaches, O only all-hymned one!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thine arm on high grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind![^91]

*Troparion:*
Fill my heart with gladness, O pure one, giving thine incorruptible joy, O thou who didst give birth unto the Cause of gladness!

*Troparion:*
Deliver us from dangers, O pure Mother of God, who didst bear eternal redemption, and the peace that passes all understanding![^92]

*Troparion:*
Dispel the gloom of my transgressions, O Bride of God, by the shining of thy brightness, O thou who didst bear the divine and pre-eternal Light!

*Troparion:*
Heal, O pure one, the infirmity of my soul, having accounted me worthy of thy visitation, and grant me health by thy prayers!

*Another Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^93]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear like a magnet, drawing by the sweetness of thy words, O father, unto instruction and obedience to teachings those who were commanded unceasingly to gaze upon and behold thine honorable and joyful face!

*Troparion:*
Following the divine laws and obeying the commandments of the Creator, thou wast shown to be a lawgiver unto monastics, and a most sure rule, an instructor to the senseless, and a guide to the wandering, and a luminary in darkness, O all-glorious one!

*Troparion:*
Being an instrument of virtues and a receptacle of the gifts of grace of the Holy Spirit, Thou didst build a sacred and fair dwelling-place, fruitfully proposing unto monastics every kind of ascetical conversation and life!

*Theotokion:*
Fleshly lips cannot worthily hymn thee, O all-hymned one, who art higher than the cherubim and all creation; therefore, together with the divine Athanasius, pray the Master for all of us!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows, for my soul is full of evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades; and like Jonah I pray: From corruption, O God, lead me up![^94]

*Troparion:*
As He has saved my nature from death and corruption by giving Himself up to death—my nature which was held fast by corruption and death—O Virgin, pray the Lord and thy Son to deliver me from the evildoing of enemies!

*Troparion:*
I know thee as the protectress of my life and a steadfast guardian, O Virgin, who dispersest the tumults of temptations and drivest away the assaults of demons; and I ever pray to be delivered from the corruption of my passions!

*Troparion:*
We have acquired thee as a wall of refuge, and the all-perfect salvation of souls, and an enlargement in afflictions, O Maiden; and in thy light we ever rejoice, O Lady: and now save us from passions and perils!

*Troparion:*
Upon a bed now do I lie in infirmity, and there is no healing for my flesh; but O thou who didst bear God and the Savior of the world, and the Deliverer from diseases, I pray thee, who art good: raise me up from the corruption of diseases!

*Another Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation![^95]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst mortify fleshly lusts through much temperance; thou didst crucify thyself unto the world and passions through fastings and tears, through prayers and unceasing hymns, cleaving unto God.[^96]

*Troparion:*
Being an instructor of the monastics who are on Mount Athos, and an image and pattern of virtuous deeds, thou didst adorn all the dwellings of the blameless that are there.

*Troparion:*
Sweet, upright, and good, meek and humble, approachable unto those who draw near, thou wast merciful, bountiful, and tenderhearted, quiet in demeanor, an imitator of Christ in character.

*Theotokion:*
Having appeared like a new Moses, thou didst establish an enclosure like another tabernacle, O all-honored one,[^97] which also thou didst magnify by labors and sweats, and didst dedicate unto the Mother of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To Thee, the Champion Leader" (Възбра́нной):*
As an eminent beholder of immaterial essences and an all-true practical teacher, thy flock cries unto thee, O speaker of divine things: fail not to pray for thy servants, that they may be delivered from perils and assaults, as they cry unto thee: Rejoice, O father Athanasius!

*Ikos:*
Having received thy calling from on high in a most glorious manner, thou didst inherit immortal life; for having passed through the conversation of the bodiless with the flesh upon earth, thou wast unassailable by passions. Therefore we praise thee, O father: Rejoice, radiant glory of monastics! Rejoice, clear pillar of chastity! Rejoice, manifestly demonstrative knowledge of courage! Rejoice, revelation of all-wise understanding! Rejoice, rule of uprightness, an evenly-weighed assurance of justice! Rejoice, thou who by word didst accomplish through deed the rush of endeavors! Rejoice, O mind delighting in ineffable thoughts! Rejoice, having piously amazed all creation! Rejoice, through whom demons were put to shame! Rejoice, through whom every passion was mortified! Rejoice, O spring of life-flowing waters! Rejoice, O deliverer of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O father Athanasius!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths who had arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trod down the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^98]

*Troparion:*
When Thou didst desire to accomplish our salvation, O Savior, Thou didst dwell in the womb of the Virgin, whom Thou didst show forth unto the world as an intercessor: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Pray the Bestower of mercy, Whom thou didst bear, O pure Mother, that those may be delivered from transgressions and defilements of soul who cry in faith: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast shown forth her that bare Thee as a treasure of salvation, and a fountain of incorruption, and a pillar of confirmation, and a door of repentance unto those who cry: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Deign, O Mother of God, to heal the bodily weaknesses and soul-diseases of those who with love approach thy protection, O Virgin, who didst bear Christ the Savior for us!

*Another Heirmos:*
The fire was put to shame before God's condescension in Babylon of old; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^99]

*Troparion:*
The proclamation of the righteous deeds of thy life has traversed all creation, O father; and thou didst attain unto a most glorious height, for God glorified thee and made thee renowned unto all.

*Troparion:*
A provider for the poor, a defender of widows, a protector of orphans, a speedy comfort of the sorrowful, a haven of the shipwrecked, a defense of the wronged wast thou, O father, imitating thy Master!

*Troparion:*
Knowing thee as a universal star and an unwaning luminary of monastics, a helper in afflictions, a great refuge of sinners, I set thee forth before the Master Christ as an intercessor and man of prayer, O all-honored one!

*Theotokion:*
Desiring to see the ineffable glory of thy Son and God, O Mother of God, thy pleasing servant, taking up His precious Cross upon his shoulder, followed His life-bearing and divine steps.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Hymn the King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts sing of, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^100]

*Troparion:*
Despise not those who require the help that comes from thee, O Virgin, who sing of thee and supremely exalt thee unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou healest the weakness of my soul and the illnesses of the body, O Virgin, that I may glorify thee, O pure one, unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou pourest forth a wealth of healings unto those who faithfully sing of thee, O Virgin, and who supremely exalt thine ineffable childbearing!

*Troparion:*
Thou drivest away the assaults of temptations and the onsets of passions, O Virgin; therefore we sing of thee unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^101]

*Troparion:*
Youthfully conquering principalities and the powers of darkness, O Athanasius,[^102] thou wast a great teacher and a saving guide, exposing their plots, barkings, and deceits, and preserving thy flock unharmed from all the evildoing of demons.

*Troparion:*
Walking upon earth as a man, thou didst truly acquire a life in the heavens, appearing as a converser with angels, accomplishing their conversation and life; with them thou now singest: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Having assembled today, we worthily praise thee and honor the shrine of thy relics, asking by thy prayers deliverance from passions, remission of transgressions, and release from every temptation and circumstance, celebrating thy holy memory faithfully and joyfully unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Even as Elijah settled in Carmel of old,[^103] so also in Mount Athos, practicing stillness, thou didst desire to be alone with God; and illumined by divinely-sovereign visions, thou didst appear as a pleasing servant unto the pure Mother of God, crying unto her with the Archangel: Rejoice![^104]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly do we confess thee to be the Mother of God, who through thee have been saved, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee together with the bodiless choirs.[^105]

*Troparion:*
Turn not away from the stream of my tears, O Virgin, who didst bear Christ Who takes away every tear from every face![^106]

*Troparion:*
Fill my heart with joy, O Virgin, who didst receive the fullness of joy, consuming sinful sorrow!

*Troparion:*
With the rays of thy light enlighten, O Virgin, driving away the gloom of ignorance from those who piously confess thee to be the Mother of God!

*Troparion:*
Heal him who has been brought low in the place of affliction of infirmity, O Virgin, transforming him from sickness unto health!

*Another Heirmos:*
At this the heaven was struck with awe, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee as the Mother of God.[^107]

*Troparion:*
In purity and chastity, in a pure abode and an undefiled life, having lived upon earth as one bodiless, thou didst ascend unto the manifest paths; and thou rejoicest with all the venerable and righteous, praying Christ for all!

*Troparion:*
Exceedingly didst thou magnify thy Master upon earth, and with thy members through contemplation and action didst gloriously glorify Him; thou didst appear conspicuous everywhere, and wast glorious, O God-bearer, for virtue truly knows how to put the adversary to shame!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast joined unto the bodiless hosts, and united unto the venerable choirs, and thou dancest with all the elect, partaking of true deification and immortal life, O father; with them unceasingly pray thy Master for thy flock!

*Theotokion:*
Having become a consecrated temple containing the Son, One of the Trinity, thine honored servant Athanasius erected thy temple, O Lady, unto thy glory and honor; in which unceasingly, cease not to grant help by thy prayers!

*Exapostilarion:*
As from afar didst thou shine forth unto those in darkness, being light-bearing, and the dark regions didst thou enlighten, O Athanasius, with the brightness of thy virtues; yet cease not to pray unto the Lord for the world. *Twice.*

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having been entreated, O Queen of all, by the supplications of thine honored Athanasius, keep thy flock inviolate from every opposition, which ever prays unto thee, the protectress of the world.

*At the Praises, stichera on 4. Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагw чꙋдесѐ):*
Precious indeed in Thy sight, O Christ, was the death of Thy venerable one;[^108] for lo, from his foot Thy pleasing servant poured forth a fountain of blood,[^109] driving away every disease even after death, and driving out the spirits of wickedness against which, while yet alive, he stood arrayed in battle even unto blood.[^110] *Twice.*

*Sticheron:*
As a fountain of wonders and a river of gifts did the Lord show forth the shrine of thy relics unto men, O all-wise Athanasius; unto the blind is sight granted through them, and unto lepers cleansing; those possessed by unclean spirits it delivers from their dominion, and causes to be of sound mind!

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, luminary of fasters, star of monastics, boast of pastors, O father Athanasius, companion of the venerable! Rejoice, dwelling of the Trinity! Rejoice, fountain of love and chastity! Rejoice, all-radiant lamp of discernment! Rejoice, upright rule of virtues, and ensouled pillar!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
The multitudes of monastics honor thee as an instructor, O our father Athanasius; for by thy path we have truly learned to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst expose the power of the enemy, O converser with the angels, companion of the venerable and the righteous; with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy!

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*
*O Lady, accept the prayers of thy servants...*[^111]

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Saint, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^112] *Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^113]
*Epistle to the Galatians, section 213.*[^114]
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord.*[^115] *Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.*[^116]
*Gospel of Luke, section 24.*[^117]
*Communion Verse: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be.*[^118]

*July 6: Our Venerable Father Sisoes the Great.*[^119]
*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Radiantly did thine all-festal memory shine forth like the sun, O venerable one, flashing with the rays of thy virtuous deeds, O all-blessed one, and illumining the minds of the faithful with the shining of thy wonders; celebrating this, joyfully we honor thee and faithfully call thee blessed, O boast of monastics!

*Sticheron:*
As another earthly angel didst thou live, having withered thy body through temperance, O most rich one, and ever exercising thyself in vigils and the remembrance of death in a godly manner, O father, and growing through great ascents unto the utmost desire; therefore thou didst truly draw near unto perfection itself!

*Sticheron:*
A wonder surpassing the mind took place at thy falling asleep, O venerable-minded, wonderful one, when a choir of holy ones suddenly appeared, O father; for like the sun, O wise one, did thy face shine forth, revealing in its flashing appearance the purity of thy soul, and assuring all of what an end thou didst obtain![^120]

*Glory... Tone 8:*
The multitudes of monastics honor thee as an instructor, O our father Sisoes; for by thy path we have truly learned to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst expose the power of the enemy, O converser with the angels, companion of the venerable and the righteous; with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy!

*Now and ever... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled heifer, seeing the Calf willingly nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously, cried aloud: Woe is me, most beloved Child! What hath the thankless Jewish council rendered unto Thee, desiring to bereave me of Thee, O All-beloved?

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
A dweller of the wilderness, and in the body an angel, and a wonderworker wast thou shown to be, O our God-bearing father Sisoes; having received heavenly gifts through fasting, vigil, and prayer, thou healest the diseased and the souls of those who run unto thee in faith: Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who operates healing for all through thee!

*Canon, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.[^121]

*Troparion:*
Quicken me who am slain by passions, O father, by thy prayers, as thou now standest with great boldness before the Life of the living!

*Troparion:*
Having cast off the desire of the flesh through divine desire, O father, thou didst follow Him Who called thee in love, O blessed Sisoes!

*Troparion:*
Having received the immaterial radiance within thy heart, O father, thou wast enlightened, and didst consume the all-passionate gloom.

*Theotokion:*
Save me who am tempest-tossed in the storm of all pleasures, O thou who didst bear the Savior, the Calm of all, O all-undefiled one!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee![^122]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst firmly subdue the flesh unto the mind, O venerable one, and didst show thy soul free from the slavery of passions.

*Troparion:*
By thy labors, O wise and venerable one, well tilling the furrow of thy soul, thou didst produce an ear of corn of dispassion and wonders.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst clothe thyself in the dying of the life-giving Christ, O venerable one, following His steps; therefore grace grants thee to raise up even the dead.[^123]

*Theotokion:*
As the place of sanctification and the noetic table, O pure one, who didst receive the Bread, Christ, the Life of all, we sing of thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
By temperance thou didst adorn thy life, and having mortified thy members, thou didst conquer the surges of passions, O blessed Sisoes; and thou didst pass over unto God, into eternal life, as a worthy heir: therefore cease not to pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
Fervent protectress and invincible, sure and unashamed hope, wall and covering and haven of those who flee unto thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: pray thy Son and God together with the angels to grant peace unto the world, and salvation, and great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
By the Cross of thy Son, O thou who art full of God's grace, all the deceit of idols was abolished, and the demonic power was trodden down; therefore, we the faithful ever duly sing of thee and bless thee, and truly confessing thee to be the Mother of God, we magnify thee!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard the mystery of Thy dispensation, O Lord; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.[^124]

*Troparion:*
By the raining down of grace, O father, thou didst pour forth rivers of wonders, cleansing from defilements those who flee unto thee in faith, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
By all-night supplications and all-day standings, O venerable one, having pre-purified thy soul, thou didst show it forth as a dwelling for the Trinity!

*Troparion:*
Fleeing, thou didst remove thyself into the wildernesses, O father, and didst wait for Him Who saves thee from faint-heartedness and malice, O blessed one![^125]

*Theotokion:*
As the mountain unhewn did Habakkuk foresee thee of old, O all-undefiled one,[^126] from which God appeared and renewed us who were corrupted.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early at dawn we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God, and we know no other besides Thee.[^127]

*Troparion:*
Having become a temple of the thrice-radiant Godhead, thou didst destroy the idols of passions from thy soul.

*Troparion:*
Having mortified the wisdom of thy flesh, O God-bearer, thou didst raise up the dead by the operation of the Spirit.[^128]

*Troparion:*
An expeller of unclean spirits wast thou shown to be, O father, for thou wast a pure dwelling of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the unwaning Sun, O Mother of God, wholly enlighten me who am darkened by passions!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation![^129]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst escape the all-destroying ruination of the noetic whale, having acquired temperance, prayer, unclouded love, mighty humility, and a steadfast affection toward Christ, O father!

*Troparion:*
God directed thy steps unto Himself, and the Mighty One manifestly made thee mighty, treading upon the heads of serpents and scorpions, O all-blessed God-bearer![^130]

*Troparion:*
By thy nodding ascent unto God, thou wast manifestly made a god by grace; having become light unto the Light through all love, an earthly angel, O God-bearing father, and a heavenly man!

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, pure vessel of virginity, cleanse my mind from passionate darkening, and fill my heart with grace and righteousness, O all-undefiled one!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Having striven ascetically, thou wast seen as an angel upon earth, ever illumining the thoughts of the faithful with divine signs, O venerable one; therefore in faith do we honor thee, O Sisoes!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trod down the flame and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages![^131]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst receive all the radiance of the Spirit with a pure mind, and wast a luminary of those who faithfully cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages! *Twice.*

*Troparion:*
With the divine fire of temperance thou didst burn up the thorns of sin, and didst pass over unto the noetic light, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O Mother of the Savior, who am held fast by the depth of transgressions; and snatch me from every malice of the enemy, that being saved, I may magnify thy protection!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^132]

*Troparion:*
Persevering in unceasing prayers and supplications, thou didst drive away the gloom of passions from thy soul, and didst purify the contemplative faculty of thy mind; therefore wast thou accounted worthy prophetically to foresee the things to come, O Sisoes, piously singing: Ye people, supremely exalt the Lord unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Angelically didst thou live in the body, O venerable one, and didst conquer the hostile ranks; therefore into the immaterial life, where are the angelic ranks, didst thou settle with gladness, O father; with them thou criest aloud: Ye priests, sing unto the Lord; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Cleanse my soul, which is dominated by passions, by thy sacred prayers unto God, and deliver me from the captivity that holds me, O God-bearing father, that I may purely sing: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Having been purified by the Spirit, thou didst receive into thy womb the Creator of human nature, and didst ineffably give birth unto Him, O pure Maiden; unceasingly singing of Him, we sing: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God: that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, becoming man from the virginal womb; therefore we the faithful magnify the most pure Mother of God.[^133]

*Troparion:*
An awesome wonder appeared at the very time of thy falling asleep, O father: thy face shone brighter than the sun when the angelic choirs arrived, receiving thy spirit and bringing it unto the thrice-radiant Light![^134]

*Troparion:*
Delighting in divine beauties, O blessed one, deified by participation, and enlightened by radiant rays from thence: by thy supplications, O wise one, deliver from the dark ruin of passions those who faithfully honor thy light-bearing memory!

*Troparion:*
Righteous, blameless, abstaining from every evil wast thou, O father, venerable and guileless, having served God in holiness; therefore did the Trinity rest in thy heart, of Which thou now manifestly delightest in radiance, O wondrously-worthy one![^135]

*Theotokion:*
From works there is no salvation in me at all, O all-undefiled one; for I walk the opposite path and am filled with darkness: but O thou who didst make the Light from Light shine forth upon us, enlighten and save me, and despise me not who am bitterly perishing!

*July 7: Our Venerable Fathers Thomas of Maleon and Acacius of the Ladder.*[^136]
*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
We know thee as a divinely-radiant luminary, O father, illumining the world with noetic rays, and driving away the gloom of the evil one and the turmoil of demons; therefore do we faithfully celebrate thy light-bearing memory, O blessed one!

*Sticheron:*
Having been illustrious of old in worldly life with wealth and power, O venerable father Thomas, thou didst clothe thyself in the monastic habit,[^137] imitating the poverty of Him Who became incarnate for our sake; therefore did He enrich thee with many gifts!

*Sticheron:*
By a pillar of fire visibly did God guide thee unto salvation, O father,[^138] unto the calm haven and unto the mountain of dispassion; and by thy prayer thou didst pour forth water, and granted sight unto the blind, and swift running unto the lame!

*Other Stichera of Saint Acacius. Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кw до́блѧ):*
By temperance thou didst mortify the wisdom of the flesh, and didst quicken thy soul through fasting and the perfection of virtues; whence also thou wast revealed as truly alive after thy death: for death was accounted as sleep unto thee when thou didst pass over unto God, and didst commit thy soul into His hands, O glorious Acacius![^139]

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst put to sleep the desires of passions, O Acacius, having acquired all-night vigil, obedience, and humility, and meditation upon the divine Scriptures, and a heart pure, and a mind untroubled; therefore thou becamest also a receptacle of the Holy Spirit, flashing rays of wonders unto the ends of the earth.

*Sticheron:*
Having utterly loathed worldly attachment, thou becamest a vessel of dispassion; and in love of wisdom, O glorious one, through active ascent thou didst ascend unto the ultimate desire itself, and by mystical contemplation didst behold the incomprehensible and divine beauties of invisible things, O all-wise and blessed Acacius!

*Glory... now and ever... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who in love for mankind became man, taking our mixture from thee and manifestly deifying it: despise me not who now sorrow, O all-pure one, but quickly show compassion, and deliver me from diverse enmities and the harm of the evil one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the Ewe-lamb that bare Thee saw Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she wept and maternally said unto Thee: O longed-for Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How have Thy hands and feet, O Word, been pierced by the lawless, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, take not Thy mercy away from us, but by their prayers guide our life in peace!

*Canon of the Venerable Thomas. Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He is gloriously glorified.[^140]

*Troparion:*
Having received the illumination of divine grace within thy soul, O glorious one, thou didst leave behind the gloom of passions as another Egypt, and didst receive the heavenly life.

*Troparion:*
Having been a luminary in the world, O father, thou didst win most honorable victories over the aliens;[^141] and having lived as a monk in holiness, thou didst conquer the legions of demons.

*Troparion:*
Wrestling steadfastly with the enemy, by the aid of the Holy Spirit, O venerable one, thou didst bring him low, and didst receive the crown of victory from heaven.

*Theotokion:*
Alone hast thou given birth, O Maiden, unto God of all, Who is uncontainable, yet contained in thee by reason of goodness; pray Him to save those who sing of thee.

*Another Canon of the Venerable Acacius, the acrostic whereof is: I praise Acacius the excellent obedient one in songs. Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The people of Israel of old, traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dry feet, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^142]

*Troparion:*
Enfolding thyself in a guileless character, thou didst acquire the fair beauty of virtues, imitating the voluntary obedience of Christ, even unto death, O all-blessed one![^143]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst put to shame the inventor of malice by thy guilelessness, and by endurance of temptations, O father, thou didst bring low unto the earth his proud and exalted hostility, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Having pre-purified thyself by patient temperance, O venerable one, and cut off the principles of passions, thou didst afterwards adorn thyself with the garment of dispassion, being covered by divine grace.

*Troparion:*
Strengthening the disposition of thy soul with firm endurance and a valiant mind, O all-blessed one, day by day thou didst endure a martyric struggle, and wast shown forth as a crown-bearer.[^144]

*Theotokion:*
The creating strength and song, God and Lord, Who for our sake was incarnate and born of thee, as Isaiah prophetically proclaimed of good tidings, O all-pure one.[^145]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst mortify sin, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing of Thee.[^146]

*Troparion:*
Inclined by thy supplications, God delivered thee from bonds by the hand of the Prophet,[^147] and was well-pleased that thou shouldest walk unto the painful labor of temperance.

*Troparion:*
Having left behind the bitter surge of life, thou didst draw near unto the haven of temperance and unto divine stillness, O father Thomas, being filled with the grace of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
He Who guided Israel of old with a pillar as they traversed the wilderness, O all-blessed one, guided thee with a pillar of fire,[^148] foreseeing the nobility of thy soul.

*Theotokion:*
As truly the golden censer and the jar of manna, the divine mountain, and the fair palace of God do we name thee, O Virgin!

*Another Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge, and my firm foundation![^149]

*Troparion:*
Having committed thyself wholly unto the Creator of heaven, thy God, O venerable one, thou didst endure tormenting humiliation.

*Troparion:*
Lawfully didst thou show forth martyric endurance on earth, O ascetic; therefore heavenly joy was given unto thee.

*Troparion:*
True humility and longsuffering didst thou strictly maintain, with patience, temperance, and obedience, O all-blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
The Creator of all is fashioned, having taken an ensouled flesh from thee for our sake, O most holy Bride of God!

*Kontakion of Saint Acacius, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having left the world, thou didst follow Christ from infancy, O all-wise, venerable Acacius; imitating His voluntary humility, thou didst cast down the proud tormentor: pray unceasingly for us all!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Having well-pleased God by divine deeds and a pure life, O venerable one, thou wast accounted worthy of the inheritance of the bodiless: pray that we may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
By the brightness of faith and by active light, thou wast revealed as an equal of the angels upon earth, truly, O all-wise Acacius. Thou didst manfully show the strength of thy mind, and didst fiercely crush the assaults of passions; therefore even after thy falling asleep thou wast revealed as alive, O unconquered one![^150]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou didst renew the mortal nature of the earthborn which had decayed in passions, and didst raise all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore, as is due, we all call thee blessed, O most glorified Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.[^151]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thy most pure Mother saw Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she said: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, desiring to quicken the dead, as Thou art merciful?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, Who alone lovest mankind.[^152]

*Troparion:*
With the divine fire of temperance thou didst burn up the thorns of sin, and with the radiance of wonders, O father, thou didst shine forth.

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed thine ears, O father, from passionate mire, thou didst open the beholding pupils of thy soul, O father.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst estrange thyself from worldly turmoils, O God-bearer, and didst obtain transcendent stillness.

*Theotokion:*
O unwedded Sovereign Lady, who didst conceive God without seed, deliver us from all temptations and sorrows!

*Another Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^153]

*Troparion:*
Having mortified the wisdom of thy flesh, O father, thou didst quicken thy soul, and didst receive the crown of righteousness, crying, O Acacius: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^154]

*Troparion:*
Receiving prolonged struggles day by day, thou didst show patient resistance, O father; therefore after death, as one alive, thou didst cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
With a steadfast mind thou didst endure torments for Christ, O blessed Acacius, contemplating Him nailed upon the Cross for the salvation of our race.

*Theotokion:*
Purer art thou than the heavens and the noetic powers, having given birth unto their Creator, O all-pure one; therefore we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O all-hymned Mother of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for we know no other God besides Thee.[^155]

*Troparion:*
Piously didst thou have Elijah the Tishbite as thy guide unto the wilderness, O father, appearing by night and mystically instructing thee in divine things, O venerable one.[^156]

*Troparion:*
As unto another Carmel, thou didst settle upon Mount Maleon, O wise one, delighting in divine visions, and receiving the grace of wonderworking in a most glorious manner, O blessed one![^157]

*Troparion:*
Preferring the narrow way rather than the broad, thou didst reach the breadth of Paradise with joy; and of the Tree of Life, O father, thou partakest unforbidden, rejoicing.[^158]

*Theotokion:*
Heal my heart, O pure one, which has been wounded by the assaults of the enemy, as thou art merciful, having ineffably given birth unto Him Who in the flesh was nailed upon the Cross!

*Another Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a Light, a holy Light turning from the gloomy darkness of ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith.[^159]

*Troparion:*
Being wounded with wounds like a martyr, O venerable one, and enduring beatings day by day with fortitude, thou didst fall asleep peacefully.

*Troparion:*
Having finished the good course, O guileless Acacius,[^160] thou didst receive rest free from labors and sicknesses.

*Troparion:*
Thy mind being steered by pure thought, O God-bearing Acacius, happily hastened unto the Cause of all.

*Theotokion:*
Neither an angelic mind nor a human one can recount thy childbearing, for it surpasses every comprehension of reason.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the depth of sin, I call upon the unfathomable depth of Thy compassion: from corruption lead me up, O God![^161]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst settle in a waterless wilderness, and by the raining down of thy prayers, O venerable one, thou didst beseech the Master to pour forth all-rich water.[^162]

*Troparion:*
Even as righteous Abraham of old, thou didst remove thyself unto another land,[^163] and didst draw near unto God, and settled in the land of the meek.

*Troparion:*
A true monk wast thou, and even after burial thou pourest forth the gifts of healings unto those in need by spiritual power, O father Thomas.

*Theotokion:*
He Who by His will created all things, having desired, dwelt within the unwedded womb, wishing to honor with incorruption those who were diseased with corruption, as He is tenderhearted.

*Another Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries out unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that flowed through mercy from Thy side.[^164]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst fall asleep with the sleep of the blessed, O blessed one, and after death wast shown forth obeying those who questioned thee, O father: that he who has obedience does not die![^165]

*Troparion:*
Having finished the good struggles, O namesake of innocence,[^166] thou wast accounted worthy of truly incorrupt immortality, O godly-wise one, where rejoicing thou didst receive the crowns of victory.

*Troparion:*
The divine, good, and God-pleasing temperance of thy life, O blessed Acacius, confirms many in divine fear and in the desire of future good things.

*Theotokion:*
Truly do we glorify thee in faith as the Mother of God, as having conceived God and unweddedly given birth unto Him, Who was united with the holy temple taken from thee.

*Kontakion of Saint Thomas, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Thou didst manfully show forth a great victory; whence, inflamed with divine love, thou didst despise the corruptible king and all beautiful things; having accomplished thy dwelling upon Mount Maleon, from whence thou didst ascend unto the heavenly realms unto the King of kings, O Thomas: pray unceasingly for us all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the golden image was worshipped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the ungodly command; and cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^167]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst quench the fire of passions with the dew of temperance, O father, and shining forth like the sun with the brightness of virtues, thou enlightenest the hearts of all who cry, O blessed one: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Granting sight unto the blind, and swift running unto the lame, O father Thomas, deliverance unto the sorrowful, and healing unto the sick by the power of Christ, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
In stillness didst thou accomplish thy life, O blessed father, being enlightened by poverty and adorned by temperance, singing with compunction of heart unto the Master, O all-rich one: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, unwedded and blessed, correction of the fallen, deliverance of sinners: save me, a prodigal, who cry unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite youths, burned rather by love of piety than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^168]

*Troparion:*
Walking with all desire unto the Master, enduring reproaches, dishonor, and wounds, O venerable one, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thy thought, contemplating the Master's passions and crucifixion, and meditating upon death while being wounded, sang: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Lying dead in the tomb without breath according to the law of those who die, thou saidst supernatural things unto him who spoke: that I have not died; and thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^169]

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the true Life, thou didst raise up the dead who were held fast by the law, as they cry: Blessed art thou among women, O all-undefiled Sovereign Lady![^170]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended unto the Hebrew youths in the fiery furnace, and turned the flame into dew, sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^171]

*Troparion:*
Having subdued the unrestrained passions of the soul with the bridle of temperance, thou didst assimilate thyself unto the immaterial angels, crying aloud: Sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Following the holy life of Elijah the Tishbite, O all-blessed one, thou didst ascend the mountain to converse with God, having cleansed thy mind through fasting, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^172]

*Troparion:*
Meek and guileless wast thou revealed to be; thou dwellest in the land of the meek, O godly-wise one, with all the venerable and elect, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^173]

*Theotokion:*
As a living, sacred fountain that bare the Water of Life, give drink, O Virgin Mother of God, unto my soul which is melted by the flame of sin, that I may glorify thee unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the open mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^174]

*Troparion:*
By strong temperance it was granted thee to be joined unto God, O venerable one, when the bonds of the body were loosed and the flesh was worn away; and through active contemplation crying, O father: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Being joined and numbered with the martyrs of Christ, unceasingly sing unto the Lover of mankind, O venerable one, praying for us who praise thee and cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having suffered lawfully upon earth, thou didst find heavenly rest, rejoicing with the saints, O venerable one, and becoming a partaker of their gladness and joy, and thou didst cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
He Who was formerly bodiless is incarnate; the Creator is fashioned of thee, O all-undefiled one; for entering into thy most pure womb, the Incomprehensible One was pleased to be comprehended by fleshly circumscription; therefore we all bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, do we the faithful with one mind magnify in hymns.[^175]

*Troparion:*
Given wings by the Spirit, O father, thou didst receive the Heavenly Kingdom with all the elect, being filled with ineffable glory, and receiving the recompense of the honor of thy pains, O Thomas; therefore in faith and love do we call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
The shrine of thy relics pours forth rivers of healings unto those who draw near in faith, drowning the passions of souls, and taking away the illnesses of bodies, O blessed Thomas, intercessor for those who ever call thee blessed in faith!

*Troparion:*
Being joined with the radiant choirs of the venerable, and noetically illumined with unwaning brightness, O father Thomas, enlighten those who faithfully celebrate thy divine memory, and account them worthy to obtain salvation by thy prayers!

*Theotokion:*
The shining ray of all thy wonders, O pure one full of God's grace, takes away the gloom of passions and sorrows, and extends the noetic light of gladness unto those who magnify thee as the Mother of God!

*Another Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, without hands of man, even Christ, Who united the natures that were sundered; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God![^176]

*Troparion:*
In prayer didst thou draw near unto the Benefactor of all, O father, adorned with the beauty of guilelessness, and shining with the loveliness of virtues; therefore we all call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
Obeying the law, thou didst bend thy good neck unto the struggle of monastics, and having become a victor, O father, thou didst attain unto perfect guilelessness, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast accounted worthy to send up hymns unto God with the bodiless, as having kept their life and purity; and by endurance of deeds thou didst receive the glory of a martyr!

*Troparion:*
Receive from us, O God-bearer, the hymn offered unto thee from a pure heart; and grant by thy supplications remission of transgressions and salvation, O all-honored one!

*Theotokion:*
As being truly the root and confirmation of incorruption, O Virgin, do all we the faithful honor thee with praises; for thou hast given birth unto Hypostatic Immortality for us!

*July 8: The Holy Great-Martyr Procopius.*[^177]
*Stichera on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кw до́блѧ):*
A calling came unto thee from heaven, even as unto the preacher of the Gentiles,[^178] O all-glorious Procopius; and enlightened in mind, thou didst leave behind the darkness of idols, and becamest a luminary of the faithful, illuminating the ends of the world with the divine brightness of thine honored struggles: the glory and praise of martyrs!

*Sticheron:*
Having first bridled the leaps of the flesh by temperance, afterwards thou didst manfully array thyself for suffering, O all-blessed Procopius, sparing not the flesh by the power of the Spirit; therefore thou didst endure every kind of torture, and as a crown-bearer didst ascend unto the celestial choirs!

*Sticheron:*
Arming thyself with the Cross as an invincible military commander,[^179] thou didst submerge all the power of the adversaries in streams of blood, and didst pour forth showers of healings richly, pouring from saving fountains, O wonderfully-worthy one, giving drink unto all who are held fast by the flame of passions, O divinely-inspired one!

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Even as Paul of old, Jesus the Truth called thee from heaven, O all-glorious Procopius;[^180] believing in Him with courage of soul, O blessed one, thou didst forsake the falsehood handed down by thy fathers and ungodly delusion; and rejoicing, enlightened in mind, thou didst set thyself unto the painful labors of consummate suffering!

*Sticheron:*
