Scraped with three-pronged iron claws, worn away by fire, and shut up in prison, thou didst remain immutable, with enlightened thought looking toward the divine rewards that lay before thee, O much-suffering martyr; therefore did Christ the Crown-bestower crown thee, O Procopius, who didst finish the course and didst keep the faith![^181]

*Sticheron:*
By the pattern of suffering which He received in the flesh, Jesus captured thee, having cultivated the beauties of thy divine soul with uprightness, O Procopius, at whose patience and brightness the enemies were amazed; therefore do we call thee blessed together with those who suffered with thee,[^182] O glorious one, with whom ask remission of grievous sins for those who sing of thee!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Today thine illustrious memory has blossomed forth, O much-suffering Procopius, summoning us who celebrate unto good praise and the glory of Christ our God. Therefore, drawing near unto the shrine of thy relics, we receive the gifts of healings, and sing of the Son, Christ, Who crowned thee, unceasingly glorifying Him unto ages!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дрѣ́ва):*
When of old the undefiled Ewe-lamb and spotless Sovereign Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Tree of the Cross, maternally exclaiming and marveling, she cried aloud: O sweetest Child, what sight is this, new and most marvelous? How hath the thankless assembly delivered Thee unto the judgment-seat of Pilate, and condemned Thee unto death, Who art the Life of all? Yet I sing of Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 8. By Leontios the Chartophylax:*
Enlightened rather by mystical rays, shining with wounds, O valiant-minded martyr Procopius, by adoption thou becamest a god, through the deification of participation;[^183] therefore save from manifold temptations by thy prayers those who celebrate thy light-bearing feast in faith!

*Both now... Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "Thine Honored Passion" (Страда́лческꙋю чⷭ҇тнꙋ́ю):*
Seeing Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and willingly accepting passions, the Virgin and Thy Mother cried aloud: How, woe is me, O sweet Child? How dost Thou endure unjust wounds, O Physician Who healed human weakness and delivered all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Procopius, O Lord, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors, and crushed the feeble insolence of demons: by his prayers save our souls!

*At Matins, both canons of the Octoechos without the martyr-troparia, and of the Saint on 6, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The people of Israel of old, traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dry feet, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^184]

*Troparion:*
Shining with the rich brightness of the Spirit, this light-bearing and honored feast of them that honor it, deliver from the gloom of passions and misfortunes, O martyr Procopius!

*Troparion:*
Thy calling was not from men, O Procopius, but from heaven, even as of old Christ captured Paul,[^185] having made thee a most true witness of His passions.

*Troparion:*
Having wisely exchanged corruptible warfare for things eternal, O Procopius, and a mortal king for the Immortal One, Who bestows upon thee an incorruptible kingdom.

*Theotokion:*
By nature hast thou given birth unto the Creator, Who deified us beyond understanding through a true union, O all-hymned Mother of God; Whom do thou earnestly beseech to enlighten those who sing of thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind![^186]

*Troparion:*
In the midst of the Cross Christ gloriously appeared unto Thee, clearly teaching Thee of His descent unto us, and summoning Thee unto suffering.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst raise up thyself as a temple and pillar unto the Divine Spirit, breathing with divine zeal; and the temples and lifeless idols of the demons thou didst cast down, O martyr Procopius.

*Troparion:*
By scraping wast thou stripped of the garments of skins,[^187] and thou didst clothe thyself with the vesture of incorruption, O Procopius; and by suffering thou didst strip the crafty one.

*Theotokion:*
Deliver me from every assault of the adversary serpent, O pure Virgin Mother, and enlighten my heart as I glorify thee in faith, O all-undefiled one!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Even as Paul of old, Christ from heaven called thee unto the faith, O wise Procopius, having foreknown the divine beauty of thy heart. Therefore thou didst suffer manfully, proclaiming His passions and His descent unto men, O all-blessed one. *(Twice)*

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
O unwedded, pure Virgin Mother of God, sole intercessor and protection of the faithful: deliver all from perils, afflictions, and grievous circumstances, who have their hope in thee, O Maiden; and save our souls by thy divine prayers!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, the all-spotless Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Merciful One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^188]

*Troparion:*
Looking with divine gaze unto the Sovereign Master, and crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord! Thou didst shatter the proud tormentor, who was exalted by the dominion of impiety.

*Troparion:*
The Deliverer appeared unto thee when shut up in prison, O Procopius, filling thee with courage, and illuminating thee with brightness, and granting a twofold calling unto thy progression.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thy face enlightened by divine grace, they that were covered with the gloom of unbelief received the light of faith by a divine beckoning.

*Theotokion:*
Weighed down with the sleep of despondency, raise me up unto the morning of repentance by thy sleepless prayer, O Sovereign Lady, and save me, who didst alone give birth unto the Savior!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The ungodly shall not behold Thy glory, O Christ, but we, rising early from the night, hymn Thee, O Only-begotten One, the Radiance of the Father's glory of Godhead, O Lover of mankind![^189]

*Troparion:*
By the light of thy words thou didst bring unto Christ a God-obedient army,[^190] which through the blood of martyrdom inherited with thee, O Procopius, the unshakable Kingdom.

*Troparion:*
Senatorial women were revealed as partakers of the senate on high,[^191] renouncing the nobility of their lineage, and choosing to die for Thee, O Immortal Word!

*Troparion:*
Their souls being inflamed with divine fire, they disregarded the body that was scorched and unsparingly burned, having desired Thy Kingdom, O Many-merciful One!

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived, thou didst give birth unto God Emmanuel, Who became flesh for the renewal of men, O all-undefiled, pure one; Whom do thou ever beseech to save our souls!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries out unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that flowed through mercy from Thy side.[^192]

*Troparion:*
Thy honey-flowing tongue, pouring forth honeyed teachings, truly delivered from the bitterness of godlessness those who obediently fled unto thee, O martyr Procopius.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired piety as a mother, O martyr, thou didst deliver thy venerable mother according to the flesh from impiety,[^193] and didst bring her unto the Incorruptible Father through suffering.

*Troparion:*
Scraping thy cheeks with iron, the tormentor did not shake the firmness of thy mind, O martyr, through love of the Desired One, Who endured passions in the flesh.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst ineffably give birth unto the Holy Light, O all-pure Virgin, Who ever illumines the faithful, and unites the choirs of martyrs with the angels.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Inflamed with divine zeal for Christ, and protected by the strength of the Cross, thou didst cast down the insolence and arrogance of the enemies, O Procopius; and thou didst exalt the honored Church, advancing in faith and enlightening us.

*Ikos:*
Grant me mouth of understanding, O pre-unoriginate Word, as I desire to sing of Thy champion Procopius; for Thou hast an unsearchable wealth of tender mercy, O my Christ, the abyss of Thy judgments: that having cleansed myself from the gloom of soul, and washed away every defilement of mind, I may become a temple by Thy divine works, and worthily sing of the struggle: advancing in faith and enlightening us.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite youths, burned rather by love of piety than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^194]

*Troparion:*
When heavy lead was crushing thy neck, O martyr, thou didst not feel it; for thou didst earnestly desire to bear upon thy neck the yoke of Him Who out of compassion united Himself unto us.

*Troparion:*
Having received from on high the grace of healings, thou takest away illnesses, O martyr, and drivest away evil spirits, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Receiving manifold wounds from the enemies, O blessed one, and scorched with fire, thou didst steadfastly consume ungodliness, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
By an incorrupt birth beyond nature is the Word born, Who by a word created all things, wishing to deliver from irrationality those who glorify His voluntary condescension.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the open mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^195]

*Troparion:*
Fiercely scorched with torches, pierced with sharp points, and receiving scraping upon grievous wounds, and every pain of torments, thou didst cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
With thy right hand, O blessed one, thou didst choke the dark faces of demons; for defying the lawless tormentor, O glorious one, thou didst place it upon burning coals to be burned for the Savior, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having Christ as an unshakable Rock for a foundation, O all-blessed, glorious one, thou didst rejoice, O Procopius, when harshly weighed down with stones, which lightened thee toward unfading joy as thou criedst: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
All generations bless thee, O Maiden, even as thou didst foretell,[^196] who barest the blessed God, Who makes blessed those that faithfully follow His commandments and cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, without hands of man, even Christ, Who united the natures that were sundered; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God![^197]

*Troparion:*
Having attained the end desired by thee, with magnanimous mind thou didst bow thy neck unto God; and being struck with the sword, thou didst mount as upon a chariot by thy blood, and didst ascend unto Him, O martyr!

*Troparion:*
Thou pourest forth rivers of healings, having become a fountain of wonders; and thou dost quench the flame of passions, and drownest the demonic hosts by the all-powerful divine strength of the Spirit, O wonderfully-honored one!

*Troparion:*
Save from every contention, from every disease, and from every distress those who joyfully celebrate thy holy memory, O Procopius, through thy sacred supplications unto God the Creator.

*Theotokion:*
A Light has shone forth unto us from thy light-bearing womb, O Maiden: the Creator of the sun and stars, and of all creation; Whom do thou earnestly beseech to enlighten those who sing of thee.

*Exapostilarion:*
From heaven, even as unto Paul, came a calling unto thee; progressing in accordance with thy name,[^198] O Procopius, thou didst lead two choirs of martyrs,[^199] with whom thou prayest for those who praise thee.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin who barest the Light, begotten of the Light, even the Unwaning Light: by thy sleepless prayers cease not to guide those who hymn thee, O Mother, Ever-virgin!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2. By Byzantius:*
As one who progresses in God, pray for all, O passion-bearer that we may progress in Him, O great Procopius, boast of martyrs; for having boldness toward Him, and ever standing before His throne, pray for those who are well-pleasing unto Him in excellent paths of God and divine deeds, and who call thee blessed!

*Tone 3. By Germanus:*
Struggling in youthful age, even as the most divine Paul thou didst receive from on high a divine gift; and by the whole armor of the Cross thou didst cast down the audacity of the fierce adversary, O boast of martyrs, passion-bearer Procopius: pray unto the Lord for us, that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 4. By Cyprian:*
Praise is offered unto God from earthen lips at thy divine memory, O much-suffering Procopius; for thou wast revealed as an excellent warrior of the word of faith, having conquered the idol forms by thy labors; and now thou art enlightened with the light of the Father, illuminating our thoughts by thy prayers.

*Tone 6:*
Clothed today with thy strength, O martyr of Christ, the Church rejoices, and worthily glorifying thy most excellent memory, she honors thee, crying aloud: Rejoice, thou that didst emulate Paul, and taking up the Cross of Christ, didst break the snares of the alien! Rejoice, boast of martyrs and strength of kings, O much-suffering Procopius: cease not to pray unto the Lord for us, as thou hast boldness, O adornment of sufferers!

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Adorned with the purple robe of the blood of thy martyrdom, the Church of God sends forth solar rays, O much-suffering Procopius; for arming thyself with the Cross, and desiring with valiant mind to die for Christ, thou didst stand against the host of the alien; and having put to shame the plots of the lawless, thou wast revealed as a victorious warrior of Christ. Therefore we beseech thee, as thou hast boldness toward Him, O adornment of martyrs: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кw до́блѧ):*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one weeping cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the thankless people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Tenderhearted One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Then we say the refrain:*
*Wondrous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*
*And we sing the Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Come, all ye ends of the earth, let us form a spiritual choir, taking up the sound of trumpets in song, and let us offer thanksgiving hymns, saying: Rejoice, warrior of Christ, O martyr Procopius, for through love of Him thou didst suffer, O blessed one! Rejoice, thou who didst surrender thy body unto cruel wounds for the sake of truly eternal and blessed life! Rejoice, thou that with insatiable soul unceasingly delightest as a partaker of Christ our God! Therefore pray that they who celebrate thy glorious memory in faith may be delivered from all wrath, corruption, and torment!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Even as Paul, having received a calling from God, O martyr Procopius, and progressing in God through desire of the Cross, thou didst manfully put to shame the impiety of the tormentors and all torments. Therefore, even unto blood didst thou resist against sin,[^200] and didst contend against invisible enemies; and thou prayest unceasingly unto the Deliverer and God to grant peace unto the universe, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Martyrs of the Lord" (Мꙋ́нцы гдⷭ҇ни):*
I endure not, O Child, seeing Thee asleep upon the Tree, Who bestowest wakefulness upon all, that Thou mayest grant divine and saving wakefulness unto those who of old fell asleep in the sleep of perdition through the fruit of transgression: the Virgin weeping said, whom we magnify!

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos, and of the Saint's Canon, Ode 3.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Wondrous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.
*Verse:* In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.[^201]
*Epistle to Timothy, composite pericope 292.*[^202]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree.[^203]
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 34.*[^204]
*Communion Verse:* In everlasting memory.[^205]

*July 9: The Holy Hieromartyr Pancratius, Bishop of Taormina.*[^206]

*Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дрѣ́ва):*
When the supreme one among the disciples, traversing the whole world, confirmed it, then he found thee as an honored stone, and laid thee as a foundation of the Church, O blessed one,[^207] casting down idol pillars and temples by the divine power of the Word, Who was pleased to unite Himself in the flesh unto men.

*Sticheron:*
Driving away by a word the evil spirits of wickedness, thou didst make a spiritual people by the grace of the Spirit, O martyr Pancratius; cultivating the field of their hearts, and the good divine seed, which thou didst offer unto the true Cultivator, praying for those who praise thee in faith.

*Sticheron:*
By noetic rays thou didst make the West an East,[^208] bearing the Sun of the knowledge of God, Who shone forth unto us beyond understanding from the Virgin; and having set through suffering, O father, thou didst shine forth unto the Unwaning Light, where, the mirrors being dissolved,[^209] thou beholdest the desired beauty of thy Contest-master, O Pancratius!

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
Thou alone, O all-pure Bride of God, didst contain in thy narrow womb the Uncontainable God, Who became man through goodness; therefore I pray thee: deliver me from the passions that constrain me, that walking by the narrow and upright path, I may attain unto that which leads unto life, O Virgin![^210]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Jesus, nailed unto the Tree of the Cross, she that knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who gave birth unto Thee, O Unapproachable Light of the Pre-unoriginate Father? Make haste and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
And as a sharer in their manner of life, and a successor upon their thrones didst thou become unto the apostles; thou didst find practice, O divinely-inspired one, as an ascent unto contemplation; therefore, rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst also suffer for the faith even unto blood, O hieromartyr Pancratius: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
A triumphal song let us all sing unto God, Who wrought marvelous wonders with an exalted arm, and saved Israel, for He is glorified![^211]

*Troparion:*
The Church acknowledges thee as a most honored stone, O Pancratius, ever established by the founding of thy words; and therefore today do we honor thy memory.

*Troparion:*
With the plow of thy divine words thou didst renew the hearts that were formerly possessed by the gloom of evil faith, O Pancratius, and through faith clearly revealed them to be fruitful.

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming the Monad numbered in three, united in the same essence, thou didst extinguish the gloom of godlessness, and by light-beaming teachings didst enlighten the people.

*Theotokion:*
From thee has the Giver of light shone forth, enlightening the ends of the earth, Jesus the Lord, O pure Mother of God; through Whom they that were in darkness and shadow saw the light.[^212]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who didst establish the second heaven above the waters, and didst found the earth upon the waters, O All-powerful One![^213]

*Troparion:*
Being confirmed by the Spirit, thou didst drive away evil spirits, and by the labor of prayers didst tear down idol temples, erecting churches, O wonderfully-honored one.

*Troparion:*
By the cleansing words of thy mouth thou didst cut down the thorny briers of impiety, and didst plant in souls the saving teachings, abounding in fruits of virtues, O sacred one.

*Troparion:*
Finding thee a vessel of the Divine Spirit, receiving pure rays, the supreme disciple sent thee unto the West,[^214] to drive away the gloom of godlessness, O all-radiant one.

*Theotokion:*
As an all-radiant cloud, an ark, and an all-golden jar,[^215] more spacious than the heavens, and an exalted ladder,[^216] O Virgin that knewest not wedlock, do we the faithful bless thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Shown forth as a champion of piety and a victor, O sacred Pancratius, thou now standest before the Lord in the heavens with the bodiless hosts: pray that we may be saved!

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
A fervent protection for those in afflictions, and our helper, and reconciliation unto God,[^217] through whom we are delivered from corruption, do we the faithful bless thee as Mother of God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn in maternal heart, mournfully thou didst cry aloud: Woe is me, how hast Thou set, O my Timeless Light!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Foreseeing in the Spirit the Incarnation of the Word, O Prophet Habakkuk, thou didst proclaim, crying aloud: When the years draw nigh, Thou shalt be known; when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown forth: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^218]

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as fire-breathing by the fire of the Comforter, thou didst consume deception, O wise Pancratius; and shining forth as a beacon unto those drowned in the sea of ignorance, from the waves thou leadest unto divine havens.

*Troparion:*
Peter, being satisfied and filled with drink from the unhewn Rock,[^219] sent thee as another river, O godly-wise one, to give drink unto souls that were held fast, and to dry up the rivers of impiety by the torrent of divine preaching.

*Troparion:*
The sound of thy words poured forth, O wise one, illuminated by the brightness of the understanding of Christ; therefore Hellenic wisdom also became foolish,[^220] and the contriver of malice was burned, O martyr and hierarch Pancratius!

*Troparion:*
Thy life, shining with divine beauties, darkened all the assaults of demons; and having destroyed the darkness of godlessness, it revealed as sons of the day[^221] those who through love submitted unto thy teachings.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth, O pure one, unto the Word incarnate in two essences and wills, O Maiden, Who gloriously made known the ascents of salvation unto those enslaved by deceit, O pure, all-hymned Virgin![^222]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Grant Thy peace unto us, O Son of God, for other than Thee we know no God; we name Thy Name, for Thou art the God of the living and the dead![^223]

*Troparion:*
By the manifestations of wonders thou didst capture the people, and by a word didst destroy pagan altars; and thou didst build the beauties of churches, O sacred one, for the renewal of men.

*Troparion:*
With blood thou didst dye thy sacred vestment, and didst dry up the demonic blood; and as a victor thou didst ascend unto heaven, receiving the crown of victory.

*Troparion:*
The idol temple was sundered before thy face, and the door was opened unto the Gentiles; and divine grace was given into the hearts of the faithful, O sacred initiate, father!

*Theotokion:*
As rain came down upon thy womb, O pure one, Christ; and He dried up the cloud of polytheism, and brought forth the water of the knowledge of God unto those who were in the deceitful flame.[^224]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Emulating the Prophet Jonah, I cry aloud: From corruption free my life, O Good One, and save me O Savior of the world, who cry: Glory to Thee![^225]

*Troparion:*
Having bestowed grace upon Thy mind, and richly enlightened it, Jesus, the Enlightenment of all and God, by Thy word delivered the people from irrationality.

*Troparion:*
Ministering as a priest in the divine Gospel, O wise one,[^226] thou didst radiantly seal the divine teachings with blood, O sacred initiate and martyr Pancratius!

*Troparion:*
From idol sprinklings and defiled blood thou didst deliver the people, and wast sacrificed even as a lamb, O Pancratius, offering thyself as a living sacrifice unto God.[^227]

*Theotokion:*
Weakened by the assaults of barren thoughts, lead me up unto the light of salvation and life, O all-spotless one, who barest Christ the Savior!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
A radiant star unto the people of Taormina wast thou revealed to be, O Pancratius, and wast shown forth as a hiero-sufferer for Christ; standing before Whom now, pray for those who honor thee, O blessed one!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When Thy youths were in the furnace, O Savior, the fire neither touched nor troubled them; then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^228]

*Troparion:*
With thine own blood wast thou baptized, O Pancratius, having first baptized the people in water; and rejoicing, thou didst pass over unto Christ, with whom taking up thy dwelling, through participation thou art ever radiantly deified, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Those drowned in the sorrow of cruel evils thou didst draw up with the hook of thy words, O sacred one; and by the pure rain of prayers thou didst dry up the turbid abyss of false belief, O sacred initiate of Christ!

*Troparion:*
The Church's sacred Builder laid thee truly as an indivisible rock, a foundation and an immovable step, in which every malice of the childish enemy is shattered, O wise one![^229]

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee alone most pure, O all-spotless, most pure one, the Word of God, being born of thy womb, cleanses the faithful from defilement, O Bride of God, which adhered unto us from the intemperance of evils.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him at Whom the angels and all the hosts tremble as Creator and Lord: sing, ye priests; glorify, ye youths; bless, ye people, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^230]

*Troparion:*
Consumed by the fire of diverse temptations, thou didst remain unharmed; having sharpened thy mind with radiant contemplations, thou wast revealed, O wise one, as a sword cutting down the matter of polytheism.

*Troparion:*
Leading the people unto the knowledge of Christ, thou didst openly work signs and wonders, foretelling things to come as a prophet of God by the inspirations of the Comforter, O godly-wise one.

*Troparion:*
Showing forth unto the people the image of the Creator, Who of His own will was united unto us in reproach,[^231] through it thou workest signs and powers, driving away the audacity of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*
As rain Christ came down into Thy womb, and truly gave drink unto all creation, drying up the turbid rivers of idol frenzy, as the only Benefactor.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The life-receiving, ever-flowing fountain; the light-bearing lampstand of grace; the animate temple; the all-pure tabernacle, more spacious than heaven and earth: as the Mother of God do we the faithful magnify thee![^232]

*Troparion:*
Enlightened in soul and mind with the light-beaming rays of Peter, like a many-rayed star thou didst reach the West, enlightening with teachings those who had sunk into the depth of ignorance, O Pancratius!

*Troparion:*
Knowing that the honor of an icon passes to the prototype,[^233] O glorious one, thou didst everywhere set up the precious icon of Jesus and our God, unto the destruction of demonic temples.

*Troparion:*
Celebrating thy light-bearing feast with joy, the city and the congregation faithfully honor thee, O marvelous Pancratius; cease not as a shepherd to bless them, making prayer for all!

*Theotokion:*
Spare us who sing unto Thee, O Lord, Who wast ineffably born of the Virgin: delivering Thy servants from misfortunes, passions, and afflictions through her prayers, as the Benefactor and only Lover of mankind!

*July 10: The Holy Forty-Five Martyrs of Nicopolis in Armenia; and the Memory of Our Venerable Father Anthony of the Caves in Kyiv, Founder of All Monastics of Rus'.*[^234]

*His service is written after this service.*

*Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
O all-praised martyrs, broken with stones, ye did not deny Christ, the indestructible Rock; but having become victorious by grace, ye rejoice with the angelic choirs; with whom pray ye, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

*Sticheron:*
O victoriously triumphant martyrs, locked together in prisons as keepers of God's commandments, and wasting away with thirst, ye received dew from heaven spiritually cooling you; therefore pray ye, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

*Sticheron:*
O invincible martyrs, desiring the true and ever-abiding life, ye joyfully surrendered yourselves unto an unjust death by the lawless sword; and now ye make merry with the martyrs; with whom pray ye, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Mother of God, the hope and confirmation of the faithful, refuge and help: we pray thee, preserve thy servants from every affliction, who worship thy birthgiving in faith; and pray Him, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the ewe-lamb and spotless Sovereign Lady beheld her own Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor comeliness,[^235] sobbing she said: Woe is me! Whither has Thy beauty set, O Sweetest One? Whither Thy splendor? Whither the radiant grace of Thine image, O my most beloved Son?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors, and shattered the powerless audacity of demons: by their prayers save our souls!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing the God-crowned company of martyrs." Tone 5.*[^236]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people through the sea with dry feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his host: unto Him alone let us sing, for He is glorified![^237]

*Troparion:*
In godly wisdom, ye faithful, let us hymn the soldiers of Christ and passion-bearers, as destroyers of deceit and radiant victors, singing a triumphal song unto God: for He is glorified!

*Troparion:*
Having radiantly contended upon earth, O sufferers, ye endured torments, and received crowns in the heavens, in harmony singing a triumphal song: for He is glorified!

*Troparion:*
Faithfully bound together in oneness of soul, ye turned away from deceit, and were shown forth as crowned victors, in harmony singing a triumphal song: for He is glorified!

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure Mother of God, unceasingly pray unto God Who was incarnate of thee and not separated from the paternal bosom, to save from every distress those whom He created!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind, that I may hymn and glorify Thy saving ascension![^238]

*Troparion:*
Adorned as soldiers with the knowledge of Christ, ye drowned the impious enemy in the streams of your blood.

*Troparion:*
Having surrendered your body unto bitter and cruel torments, O praiseworthy martyrs, ye received the divine inheritance through faith.

*Troparion:*
Being crushed with pelting of stones at the command of the tormentor, O passion-bearers, ye firmly preserved the realm of true piety.

*Theotokion:*
Unceasingly pray with the martyrs, O pure one, unto Him Who came forth from thy loins, to deliver from the deceit of the devil those who sing unto thee, O all-spotless one!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
The valiant soldiers of Christ, having contended well, utterly drowned the subtle enemy in streams of blood; crushed with stones, and cut with swords, burned with fire, and cast into water, they were manifestly revealed as crowned victors; therefore they are honored and praised in faith.

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*
He Who sits upon the throne of the Cherubim, and in the paternal bosom, sits upon a holy throne in thy womb, O Sovereign Lady; for God, having truly become incarnate, has reigned over all the nations, and with understanding we now sing unto Him; Whom pray thou that thy servants may be saved!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, she who in the end gave birth unto Thee in the flesh, Who wast begotten of the Unoriginate Father, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard the report of the power of the Cross, how Paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^239]

*Troparion:*
Encompassed by abundantly fruitful ways, the company of sufferers conquered the God-opposing enemy, being strengthened by the grace of the Savior.

*Troparion:*
O divinely called company of Christ's passion-bearers, ye conquered the multitude of hostile, impious enemies, singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having beheld the Unapproachable Light, the passion-bearers sitting in prison destroyed the gloom of polytheism and idol deceit, being strengthened by the divine power.

*Theotokion:*
The power of the Only One made thee to dwell, and made thee a paradise, having the Tree of Life in the midst, the Mediatress and Lord.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early at dawn we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us, for Thou art our God; for other than Thee we know none else![^240]

*Troparion:*
Nurtured by the words of the Holy Spirit, the martyrs brought to nought the irrationality of idols.

*Troparion:*
The passion-bearers became light-bearing stars and flowers of faith, giving forth a sweet fragrance.

*Troparion:*
Ye were revealed as the field of the Most High, O all-praised holy ones, reaped by the sickles of martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
Cease not, O Mother of God, praying unto Him Whom thou didst bear, to save our souls, who diligently hymn thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The abyss encompassed me, the sea-monster became my grave; but I cried aloud unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord![^241]

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, the martyrs cried aloud: Into Thy hands, O Master and Lover of mankind, receive our spirits, and give them rest; for Thee alone do we love, the Plenteous in mercy!

*Troparion:*
The company of Thy martyrs became fellow-dwellers with the angels, O Lover of mankind; for having finished their course, they now pray that our souls may be saved!

*Troparion:*
O glory of martyrs, beauty of martyrs, God-chosen choir of sufferers: diligently pray that all who flee unto you may be saved!

*Theotokion:*
What word can declare the wonder of thy seedless conception, O all-spotless one? For thou didst conceive God, Who came unto us through tender mercy.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The First-Fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кw нача́тки):*
For the sake of Christ, O martyrs, ye endured many torments, and cast down all the polytheism of idols, and destroyed the godlessness of every deceit, having trampled these down by the power of Christ; ye teach us all faithfully to cry aloud: Alleluia![^242]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the fiery furnace didst save the hymn-singing youths: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^243]

*Troparion:*
In the furnace, O Christ, Thy passion-bearers cried aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the Trinitarian light, the sufferers joyfully surrendered their souls, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Standing before God, O crowned sufferers, with joy pray unto Him for us!

*Theotokion:*
As our salvation we beseech thee, O Mother of God: pray for us unto Him Who was incarnate of thee!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who was begotten of the Father before the ages, the Son and God, and in these latter times was incarnate of the Virgin Mother: sing, ye priests; supremely exalt Him, ye people, unto all ages![^244]

*Troparion:*
With the martyrs, may our supplication be accepted, of us the faithful, and may we become partakers of their inheritance, singing unto Christ and supremely exalting Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
With gladness of heart the company of patient sufferers divinely receives crowns from Christ, and radiantly sings and supremely exalts Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having dyed yourselves with the streams of your blood, O praiseworthy ones, ye reign in the heavens with Christ unto all ages, piously singing and supremely exalting Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast revealed as higher than the Cherubim, O pure Mother of God, having carried in thy womb Him Who sits upon them; Whom with the bodiless hosts we mortals glorify and supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, who beyond mind and word didst ineffably give birth in time unto the Timeless One, the Mother of God: we the faithful in oneness of mind magnify![^245]

*Troparion:*
Pray for common salvation, O most glorious martyrs, standing before Christ, for Whose sake ye also endured torments!

*Troparion:*
By mighty power, O invincible ones, ye cast down the fraudulent stronghold; and now in the heavenly habitations ye are accounted worthy to dwell with the angels.

*Troparion:*
In the lawful manner of martyrs, having conquered the proud tormentor, ye received righteous crowns, O all-honored ones![^246]

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of God, Mother of Christ God, Whom thou didst bear: pray with the martyrs that forgiveness of sins be granted unto those who hymn thee in faith!

*Service of Our Venerable Father Anthony of the Caves in Kyiv, Founder of All Monastics of Rus'.*[^247]

*At Small Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 verses, and we sing Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Hosts" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Following in the steps of the great fathers of the Holy Mountain, O father Anthony, and contending in the flesh as though bodiless, thou wast an example of virtue unto many; whence also thou wast accounted worthy of gifts of wonders from Christ, to heal the diverse infirmities of men: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron:*
Thou wast the founder of monastics, O father Anthony, offering thyself as a bloodless first-fruit unto God by the temperance of philosophy of wisdom; by humility, exalted manners, and immeasurable fasting, thou didst put the enemy to shame, O venerable one: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron:*
Thy light-bearing memory, O venerable father Anthony, is revealed full of joy and gladness; therefore thy holy children, having assembled together, spiritually celebrating, glorify the Lord Who sincerely glorified thee!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Today Mount Athos, having most gloriously received thee as a new immigrant Abraham,[^248] rejoices, O father! And thy fatherland, O blessed one, having thee again as a co-dweller, greatly makes merry; and the cave in which thou didst contend is adorned concerning thee; the most radiant city of Kyiv, celebrating thy memory, sings festal hymns unto the Lord, Who granted thee as an undestroyed wall unto it; while we, thy children, guided by thy teaching, now celebrate thy dormition, we pray: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the vine...[^249]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
From earthly unto heavenly mansions thou didst most gloriously pass, O father, and wast there accounted worthy of the honor of the venerable ones; with whom pray thou, that our souls may be saved!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^250]

*Sticheron:*
The bodily senses thou didst put to death through temperance, O father; therefore also into a dark cave, as into a most radiant bridal chamber, thou didst enter; wherein also thou didst bring a multitude of disciples unto Christ.

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he will have great delight.*[^251]

*Sticheron:*
Thy flock, which honors thee, O venerable one, and celebrates thy dormition: delivering it from barbarian invasion, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... both now... Tone 2:*
A most precious temple of the Mother of God, even as she herself desired, thou didst erect; and there thou didst gather a multitude of disciples within it, to hymn the Sovereign Lady and Mother of God; whom pray thou, we beseech thee, O venerable Anthony, not to forsake, even as thou didst promise, thy sacred flock, delivering and saving them from the invasion of adversaries; that we all, honoring thee with hymns unceasingly as a child-loving father, may ask to receive great mercy from Christ through thee![^252]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having departed from worldly turmoil, in evangelical renunciation of the world thou didst follow after Christ; and having lived an angelic life, thou didst reach the quiet haven of the Holy Mountain of Athos; whence by the blessing of the fathers having come unto the mountain of Kyiv, and there laboriously having finished thy life, thou didst enlighten thy fatherland; and having shown unto a multitude of monastics the path leading unto the heavenly Kingdom, thou didst bring them unto Christ: Whom pray thou, O venerable Anthony, that He may save our souls![^253]

*At Great Vespers, Kathisma: "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon.*[^254]

*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 8 verses, and we sing Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
When divine yearning came upon thee, O venerable one, then worldly passion thou didst account as vile; therefore also thou becamest an immigrant from thy homeland unto the Holy Mountain; and there in the midst of those fathers thou didst shine forth like a luminary by virtue, O Anthony: pray Christ, Whom thou didst serve from thy youth, to save and enlighten our souls!

*Sticheron, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
When thou wast kindled with the love of Christ, O venerable one, then the wisdom and glory of this world, as passing away, thou didst hate; therefore also thou didst make a cave for thyself, and therein didst contend as though bodiless; therefore also from on high thou wast accounted worthy of the honors of the bodiless ones; with whom standing before Christ, pray that He may save and enlighten our souls!

*Sticheron, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
When thou didst receive divine love into thy heart, O father, then into the dark cave, as into a most radiant bridal chamber, thou didst enter; and there, enriched with the understanding of ineffable things, to behold things to come as though present, and clearly to speak unto all of Christ, Whom thou didst serve from thy youth: pray Him, O venerable one, to save and enlighten our souls!

*Other Stichera of the Venerable One, Tone 6, Podoben: "O Venerable Father" (Преподо́бне ѻ҆́тче):*
O venerable father Anthony, having illuminated thy mind by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, therefore as clairvoyant thou didst prophetically foretell the wrath of God about to come upon Orthodoxy; whence also thou didst pledge unto Shimon salvation from enemies; and before the church building, thou didst foretell that he would be laid therein; therefore, as our advocate, we ever hymn thee in all good things, asking through thee to receive great mercy from Christ![^255]

*Sticheron, Tone 6, Podoben: "O Venerable Father" (Преподо́бне ѻ҆́тче):*
O venerable father Anthony, fulfilling the commandments of Christ, the true Shepherd and Teacher, showing thy love even after thy falling asleep, by thy prayers and apparition thou didst alter the deadly disease of Erasmus who had lived slothfully after repenting of his possessions,[^256] and granting him time for repentance, thou didst guide others unto chastity; therefore pray Christ that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron, Tone 6, Podoben: "O Venerable Father" (Преподо́бне ѻ҆́тче):*
O venerable father Anthony, having with God chastely guided the ship of thy mind, thou didst untroubled sail across the impassioned deep of the sea of this world; guided by the sail of the All-Holy Spirit, thou didst reach the quiet harbor of pure generation, where is the dwelling of the saints and the rest of the righteous; with whom pray thou, we beseech thee, that we also may obtain entrance there!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
When thy austere life and labors according to God became known unto the fathers of the Holy Mountain, then by divine providence thou wast sent again unto thy homeland, saying: Go, child, that God may be known through thee unto those who know Him not! Whence having arrived, O venerable one, thou didst enlighten thy fatherland, and becamest the leader of monks, bringing multitudes of them unto Christ; therefore also pray thou to preserve us from unassailed enemies, who celebrate thy all-hymned dormition, and that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Who will not call thee blessed...[^257]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*

*Readings, 3:*
Written on the 5th day of this month, for Athanasius of Athos.[^258]

*At the Litiya, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 4:*
For the sake of thy namesake life,[^259] by divine providence thou wast called Anthony, receiving the name from thy deeds; for having gathered a multitude of disciples who followed thy teaching, these thou didst bring unto God, as a fellow-dweller with the venerable and the righteous; with whom standing before the Trinity, pray for all who glorify thy most glorious dormition with hymns!

*Sticheron, Tone 4:*
Having fled from the impassioned gloom of this world, O venerable Anthony, then thou didst settle in a cave, O most marvelous one, and there thou didst look into the depths of divine understanding; therefore also thou wast accounted worthy, as clairvoyant, to foresee distant things and clearly to speak of them as though near at hand; and grieving over the sins of the people, thou didst prophetically foretell the barbarian invasion that was to come for the sins of men;[^260] but pray, O father, we beseech thee, that we may be delivered from such a threat, and that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 2:*
What human mind or tongue can declare, or worthily praise thy life? For the very angels marveled at thy life in the flesh upon earth, and praised Christ, Who granted thee such victory over passions, O venerable one: Whom pray, O all-praised one, that he who weaves praise for thee as much as is possible may be saved, O most marvelous Anthony!

*Sticheron, Tone 2:*
Blessed is the cave that has within itself thy precious relics, O venerable one, from which healings pour forth unto the faithful; and blessed are thy disciples, who acquired such a teacher for themselves, and were called children of such a father, O most marvelous Anthony, intercessor for our souls!

*Tone 4. Both now* thy all-honored and sacred body, O venerable Anthony, lying in the cave in which while yet in this life thou didst labor much, abundantly gushes forth healings unto those who come in faith; therefore we pray thee: through thy prayers ever bestow also health of soul upon those who call thee blessed!

*Glory... both now... Tone 2:*
How great is the boldness and faith which thou didst acquire toward God, O venerable Anthony! For emulating the Prophet Gideon, who tested victory by a fleece,[^261] likewise thou also, O father, didst desire to know by dew where the place was pleasing unto the Sovereign Lady and Queen; therefore also thou didst say: If I have found grace before thee, let there be dew upon all the earth, but on the place where thou willest a church to be, let there be dryness; and again: Let there be dew upon the place! And both of these didst thou obtain, O father; therefore also thou didst erect a most precious church of the Mother of God;[^262] with whom pray Christ, we beseech thee, that our souls may be saved!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
In the steps of the great Anthony didst thou follow, O venerable one; for he settled in the desert for the sake of silence, and was accounted worthy to be a converser with angels; while thou didst shut thyself up in the subterranean places of a cave: thou wast accounted worthy to behold the ineffable light; therefore, having emulated his life, receiving a name from thy deed, with him standing before the Holy Trinity, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^263]

*Sticheron, Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
Like a most radiant luminary thou didst shine forth in a dark place; and like a palm tree in the house of God thou didst prophetically flourish,[^264] and offering thyself unto the Master as a living, bloodless sacrifice,[^265] thou didst bring fruit, O father; therefore, assembling in love, we all worthily and ever call thee blessed!

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he will have great delight.*[^266]

*Sticheron, Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
The cave in which thy sacred body lies, O venerable Anthony, by the power of the All-Holy Spirit heals diverse infirmities of those who approach in faith; demons are driven away from men, and the paralytic depart receiving health; while we, praising the Lord, say: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His holy ones!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
A marvelous petition and exceedingly great faith didst thou acquire, O venerable O venerable Anthony: for thou didst resemble the Prophet Elijah; for he brought down fire from heaven to consume the sacrifice; likewise thou also by prayer didst bring down fire from heaven, unto the consumption and purification of the place where the all-glorious church of the Sovereign Lady Mother of God was to be erected.[^267] Therefore, blessing thee as a companion of the venerable and an emulator of the prophets, we pray: Intercede with Christ, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Creator and Deliverer:*[^268]

*At the Blessing of Loaves:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
*Having departed from worldly turmoil:* (Twice)[^269]
*Written at Small Vespers.*
*And Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, once.*

*At Matins, After the First Kathisma Verse-gathering, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Most radiant and light-bearing, O God-wise Anthony, is thy venerable feast, calling together multitudes of monastics to glorify and sing of thy venerable falling-asleep, asking to receive great mercy from Christ through thine intercession. (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou didst appear higher than the celestial hosts, having given birth to the Sovereign Master, O Mother of God, and hast exalted the nature of earthborn mortals, O Bride of God; therefore, with soul and tongue we, the faithful, glorify thee as the true Birthgiver of God.

*After the Second Kathisma Verse-gathering, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*
A converse companion and fellow-servant of the fathers of the Holy Mountain wast thou, O father; and having followed their steps, thou didst live on earth as one bodiless; therefore thou didst appear as a fellow-citizen of the venerable; with whom also, rejoicing in choruses on high, pray that our souls may be saved, who unceasingly glorify thee.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Joseph was amazed, beholding that which was beyond nature, and he pondered in his mind the rain upon the fleece, in thy seedless conception, O Mother of God, the bush unburned by fire, Aaron’s rod that budded; and thy betrothed and guardian, bearing witness, cried unto the priests: A Virgin gives birth, and after giving birth remains again a Virgin!

*After the Polyeleos: Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Into the subterranean caves didst thou enter, O venerable one; and from thence, as a conqueror over passions, having mounted the chariot of virtues, flying up unto the heavens as another Elijah, there wast thou rightly crowned with a most radiant crown, O blessed one; but even as when thou wast with us in this life, caring for and providing what is profitable for us: so now also, O father Anthony, pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as unwedded Mother of the Deliverer, as receptacle of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: hasten to deliver me from the malice of the demons, seeing I have become a foul dwelling of lawlessness and a conscious plaything of devils; and make me a bright abode of virtues, O light-bearing, incorrupt one; drive away the cloud of passions, and make me worthy of communion on high and of the never-setting light, by thy prayers.

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], First Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^270]*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Conception 43 [Mt. 11:27–30].[^271]*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
*Thy austere life:*
*Written at Great Vespers.*
*Canon of the Mother of God with the Heirmos to 6, Tone 8; and two Canons of the Venerable One to 8, Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word to the Queen Mother; and I shall appear radiantly celebrating, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.*

*Troparion:*
By severe abstinence and labors, O father Anthony, and by all-night standing, thou didst establish thy soul as a king over bodily passions; therefore, rejoicing with the saints, we sing unto Christ, Who granted thee such victory over passions.

*Troparion:*
Arrived today is the all-honorable day of thy falling-asleep, O father Anthony, richly gladdening thy children, O all-honored one; therefore, remembering thine ancient way of life and radiantly celebrating thine angelic life, we sing to thee, O father.

*Troparion:*
Taken up from dark places, thou didst pass all-gloriously unto the highest splendors, O venerable one; and there with the saints, as a victor over passions, O father, wast thou crowned.

*Theotokion:*
My soul is grievously submerged in the abyss of passions, by the tempest of immeasurable tribulations: stretching forth unto me the hand of salvation, lead me up, O Sovereign Lady, from the depth of my evils.

*Second Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Of old, the all-powerful army of Pharaoh was laid low in the deep* the overwhelmingly armed might was overthrown; but the incarnate Word destroyed all-malicious sin, the exceedingly glorified Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified.

*Troparion:*
From tender age, O venerable Anthony, thou didst acquire great love toward God; and for this cause thou didst utterly mortify the carnal stirrings and passions; and having gloriously taken wing with purity as with wings, thou didst fly up unto the heavens, O father.

*Troparion:*
Emulating ancient love of pilgrimage, O venerable one, thou thyself didst choose to sojourn as a stranger, O Anthony;[^272] therefore the All-seeing Eye, beholding thy good resolve, enriched thee with the gift of wonders as an all-glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee adorned with quietness of soul and love toward God, and with good character and habits, thy spiritual superior, O venerable one, prophetically proclaimed unto all the all-glorious things that were to come to pass through thee in the end, O Anthony.[^273]

*Theotokion:*
Break the bonds of my transgressions, O Sovereign Lady, praying to thy Son and God, and cast down once and for all the sin that torments me, that, being saved through thee, I may ever praise thee, O all-immaculate one.

*Katavasia:*
*I shall open my mouth...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Mother of God, living and bountiful fountain: spiritually establish those who are united in a choir of singers unto thee; and in thy divine glory, vouchsafe unto them crowns of glory.*

*Troparion:*
Thy sacred Great Lavra, O father, which received its beginning through thee, proclaims all-gloriously and cries aloud louder than a trumpet; and the house of the Mother of God, though not with a voice, yet in faith boasts, saying: I have been established through thee, O Lord![^274]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst love the one Lord, and taking His Cross upon thy shoulder, O venerable Anthony, thou didst live in marvelous manner in fastings, vigils, and prayers, becoming an example unto thy disciples; therefore thou wast also glorified by Christ.

*Troparion:*
Having fled carnal desire, O venerable one, thou didst hate pride; and being enriched with humility and poverty, thou didst reach the highest dwellings of the saints.

*Theotokion:*
Have mercy, O sole Mother of God, have mercy and show compassion unto my wretched soul, overwhelmed by demons and wicked passions; and before the time of my departure, take pity to cleanse it.

*Second Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth over mine enemies; for my spirit hath rejoiced when singing: There is none* holy as our God, and there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.[^275]

*Troparion:*
Foreseeing the recompenses to come, O father, thou didst forsake the transitory things here as though non-existent; and for this reason thou hast received two-fold recompense, O venerable one; therefore with the angels thou dost unceasingly sing unto thy Sovereign Master.

*Troparion:*
Cease not, O father, to pray for thy flock, which thou didst acquire through many sweats and labors, that it may be delivered from all tribulations, dangers, and sorrows, so that we all may hymn thee as a child-loving father.

*Troparion:*
With gladness, O venerable one, thou dost stand before God with the ranks of the saints; and there, being sated with joy and gladness, make us also worthy to obtain the gladness there, that rejoicing we may magnify thee, O father Anthony.

*Theotokion:*
Stretching forth the hand of tender-heartedness, O Birthgiver of God, deliver me from every condemnation, who flee unto thee, and fall down before thee, and invoke thy help.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Thy spiritual superior was amazed, beholding the great abstinence in thee, and he pondered in his mind the things that were to come to pass concerning thee in the end, O Anthony; for beholding thy great life according to God, and how it was by no means shaken by the enemy's assaults, he also bore witness, as the father and guardian of thy life, crying unto the fathers of the Holy Mountain: This man, who appears the least among us, shall afterwards be a great luminary in Rus'![^276] (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Joseph was amazed, beholding that which was beyond nature, and he pondered in his mind the rain upon the fleece, in thy seedless conception, O Mother of God, the bush unburned by fire, Aaron’s rod that budded; and thy betrothed and guardian, bearing witness, cried unto the priests: A Virgin gives birth, and after giving birth remains again a Virgin!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*The unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk perceived and cried: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
The place wherein lies thy honorable and sacred body, O venerable father Anthony: from thence the faithful receive many healings, crying unto Christ from the soul: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
The length of the austere road did not dismay thee, O venerable one, nor the bandits' assault there of assaults; therefore, having reached the Holy Mountain, with the fathers who were there thou didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Beholding thy steadfast and good resolve, the all-good God made thee glorious there in holy places amid the fathers; with whom make us worthy to cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Desiring thee, who art of a royal root, the King of all, having made thee higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O Mother of God, took up His abode in thee.

*Second Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I sing to Thee, for I have heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who had strayed; therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Plenteous in mercy!*

*Troparion:*
By the providence of God, O blessed one, thou wast sent back unto thy homeland, that there also thou mightest guide many unto the saving path, to sing unto the Creator: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the hope of future good things, O Anthony, thou didst bridle carnal urge, and wast an unmerciful enemy unto thy flesh, O father; therefore thou didst also acquire help from on high.

*Troparion:*
Having spent thy life in chastity, O venerable one, thou didst exchange earthly things for heavenly, and didst purchase incorruptible things with corruptible; therefore thou dost sing unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
I am stripped of all good things, and lie in malice: O thou who didst clothe all who fell of old, adorn me now with the former things, and deliver me from the second evils, O Bride of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*All creation was amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst hold in thy womb the God over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.*

*Troparion:*
An exemplar and instructor of monastics wast thou, O father; therefore thou dost also rebuke the powerful, not to desire plunder, but rather to show mercy to the powerless; and by this wast thou glorified, O Anthony, and didst appear as a teacher called great.[^277]

*Troparion:*
A life above man didst thou acquire, O Anthony, having lived on earth equal to the angels; therefore thou wast vouchsafed to receive an honor equal unto the angels, and with them dost sing unto thy Sovereign Master.

*Troparion:*
Thy graced gifts, O father Anthony, astonished every human ear and taught men to despise all things that here seem great, as small and wretched, and to follow thy footsteps; therefore, O venerable one, pray that we may be saved.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art my hope, O all-pure one, and salvation, and praise; therefore I flee unto thy protection: abhor me not, who am now swallowed up by many sins, sicknesses, and sorrows, but anticipate and save me.

*Second Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou wast a mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Sovereign Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Source of Light.*

*Troparion:*
Marvelous was thy life, O venerable one, together with the humility that thou didst acquire, O Anthony: so that thou didst not deem thyself worthy to behold even that light which is in the world, nor the shining of the sun; but in a dark place thou didst enclose thyself alone.[^278]

*Troparion:*
A church and house of the All-Holy Spirit wast thou, O father Anthony, being adorned with diverse virtues: having inherited the height on high through humility, through poverty thou didst receive the heavenly wealth, O venerable one.

*Troparion:*
Deliver from all transgressions, sorrows, and tribulations those who keep thy sacred memory; grant thy divine intercession, standing before the King of all, O father Anthony.

*Theotokion:*
As the never-setting Light shone forth from thy womb, O pure Sovereign Lady: illumine my darkened soul, and drive away all gloom from my heart.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye God-wise, let us clap our hands, glorifying God Who was born of her.*

*Troparion:*
By thine ever-present longing toward God, thou wast outside the flesh and the world, O father Anthony; therefore thou didst show thyself a worthy dwelling of the All-Holy Spirit, even as Christ Himself said: Having come with the Father and the Spirit, I will dwell there.[^279]

*Troparion:*
As a certain most sweet vineyard, the Holy Mountain, having nurtured thee, O father, is all-gloriously adorned with thee; and rejoicing it glorifies Christ, Who gifted thee unto them.

*Troparion:*
Night was accounted unto thee as day, and day as night, O Anthony: for according to the prophet, thou didst not give sleep to thine eyes, nor slumber to thine eyelids, nor rest to thy body, until[^280] until thou didst present it pure, together with thy soul, unto God.

*Theotokion:*
Grant me thy help, and deliver me from dangers and sorrows and my transgressions, O thou who hast borne the everlasting Deliverance of all.

*Second Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: lead me up from corruption, O God!*

*Troparion:*
As thy blessed soul was set free from carnal heaviness and from the bitter Pharaoh, the tormentor, having gone forth from this world, it passed untroubled into the land of promised paradise, and abides rejoicing with the saints.

*Troparion:*
Now thy sacred body, O venerable one, lying in the cave, pours forth many healings unto those who approach in faith; therefore we pray thee: grant health of soul also by thy supplications unto those who glorify thee.

*Troparion:*
Brighter than the sun hath shone forth thy most honorable memory, O Anthony, wherein coming together we glorify thy falling-asleep and the labors that thou didst endure in this life, asking to receive through thee from Christ the remission of sins.

*Theotokion:*
Vouchsafe that I may behold the beauty of thy glory when I am about to depart from my flesh, for thou art my firm foundation, O Sovereign Lady, that thence I may receive deliverance.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):*
Having dedicated thyself unto God, Whom thou didst love above all from thy youth, O venerable one, thou didst follow Him with love from thy whole soul; and accounting the corruptible things of the world as nothing, thou didst fashion a cave in the earth, and in it didst fight well against the wiles of the invisible enemy, shining forth like a radiant sun unto all ends of the earth; therefore rejoicing thou didst pass unto the heavenly bridal chambers; and now, standing with the angels before the Sovereign Master's throne, remember us who honor thy memory, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Anthony our father![^281]

*Ikos:*
Desiring to enjoy the ever-existing, never-waning Light of the Holy Trinity, and for this cause having enclosed thyself for the space of forty years in a single dark cave, thou didst remain therein without going forth, O father, until thou didst present thy body pure, together with thy soul, unto God; therefore even after thy falling-asleep, as one living, thou didst receive from the Lord gifts of wonders, to heal diverse sicknesses of those suffering grievously, and to drive away demons from people who come[^282] to drive out; therefore we pray thee: pray, O father, that thy homeland, city, and people be preserved unharmed, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Anthony our father!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The God-wise ones served not the creature rather than the Creator, but valiantly trampling underfoot the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: O all-hymned Lord and God of the fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
By the command of thy spiritual superior, O blessed Anthony, thou wast sent again unto thy homeland; therefore what was told thee by the father, thou didst perform as commanded by God; and coming thither, O father, thou didst bring the souls of many unto the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having reached the most holy place, O venerable Anthony, thou didst astonish many by thy life; for having taken upon thyself a life above man, thereby thou didst cause many to despise worldly things.

*Troparion:*
Finding the cave of Archbishop Hilarion, thou didst laboriously dwell therein, O venerable one, in prayers and fastings, until thou didst make another cave for thyself, wherein thou wast vouchsafed the calling from on high.[^283]

*Theotokion:*
Destroy human tribulations and plots, O pure one, by thy divine prayers, and deliver thy servants from painful transgressions, that we may ever bless thee, O Ever-Virgin.

*Second Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread spiritual dew over the pious youth, He Who is blessed and exceedingly glorified.*

*Troparion:*
A great fellow-laborer and emulator of thy life, O venerable Anthony, didst thou acquire in the blessed Theodosius, who followed thine angelic manner of life; with whom taking up thy dwelling on high, remember us.[^284]

*Troparion:*
A new Moses did we know thee to be, O father, a conqueror over passions; for as he of old, having conquered Amalek, led out the people, so thou also, O father, hast brought a multitude of monastics into the promised land of paradise.

*Troparion:*
A sojourner from thy city and homeland wast thou, O father; but again by the divine providence thou dost return unto thy homeland, gloriously testified of, where thou didst receive grace from the Lord, O father.[^285]

*Theotokion:*
The furnace prefigured thy childbearing, O all-immaculate one: for it burned not the youth, even as neither did the unendurable fire burn thy womb the unendurable fire. Therefore we pray thee: deliver thy servants from the eternal fire.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The childbearing of the Mother of God saved the pious youth in the furnace; prefigured then, but now realized, it moves all the universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst emulate the humility of Christ thy Sovereign Master, O venerable Anthony; and for this cause thou didst love a humble and austere life, renouncing earthly attachment; therefore thou wast vouchsafed the honors of the saints, and with them dost sing unto the Lord unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Unto those who celebrate thy most glorious falling-asleep, O blessed one: pray that all may be delivered from diverse tribulations, that we all may glorify thee, O Anthony, and supremely exalt the Lord unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Stretch forth a merciful eye toward the Sovereign Master concerning us, O Anthony, showing thy love for thy children; and abide with us unceasingly in spirit, O venerable one, even as thou didst promise when thou wast with us,[^286] that we all may sing with thee unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Show compassion now upon my supplication, and grant me joy instead of sorrow, that I may sing to thee, O Sovereign Lady, chanting unto thy Son: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Second Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The fiery furnace in Babylon of old divided its action by divine command, burning the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Grant abundant healings, O venerable one, unto those who approach thy church in faith, and consolation unto those who are in sorrows, that we all may cry with thee unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Look down from on high with mercy upon those who offer thee this thanksgiving singing, O father, and pray to Christ the Deliverer to save all who cry unto Him: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
A multitude of the saved was brought as a gift unto the Almighty through thee, following thy life, O Anthony; therefore pray that all may be saved who cry with thee unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Who will not be amazed at the great mystery of thy childbearing, O Mother of God O Mother of God, or what earthly tongue or bodiless mind will be able to utter it? For beyond nature and understanding, O Mother of God, didst thou give birth to the Creator.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened in spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry: Rejoice, O all-blessed Birthgiver of God, pure Ever-Virgin!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endeavor in every way to emulate the life of Anthony, who shone forth of old; for he settled alone in the desert, but thou didst enclose thyself alone in a cave, and for many years, O father, didst remain without coming forth.[^287]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast the founder and guide of the cave-dwelling life, O father, and didst labor there as one bodiless; therefore He also enriched thee with the gift of wonders, O venerable one.[^288]

*Troparion:*
Receive the lifting up of my hands, O venerable one, emulating the Master's goodness, overlooking transgressions; for thou didst wound thy soul with the love of Christ and of His all-holy Mother of God, whom do thou implore to save our souls.

*Theotokion:*
Through the lifting up of thy hands unto God, O venerable Anthony, wonder-working was all-gloriously wrought; for by thy prayers thou didst bring down fire from heaven unto the purification of the site for the church of the All-Pure Lady, whom we all magnify with thee.[^289]

*Second Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened and to gather together the dispersed; therefore the all-hymned Mother of God do we magnify.*

*Troparion:*
Having emulated the life of Pachomius the Great, who was vouchsafed the angelic vision, thou didst live in the cave equal unto the angels, O father; therefore thou wast vouchsafed to receive an honor equal unto the angels, O Anthony.[^290]

*Troparion:*
By the path leading from the Holy Mountain, O father, thou didst reach the place of Kyiv, where it was pleasing that the church of the Sovereign Lady Mother of God should be erected; and there, together with the blessed Theodosius, thou didst erect a most honorable church unto the glory of the Mother of God; therefore we all magnify thee with him.[^291]

*Theotokion:*
Light- bearing Virgin, drive away the cloud of my soul, and grant me purely to behold, O Sovereign Lady, the saving beauty that shone forth ineffably from thy most pure womb as a light unto the nations, O all-hymned one!

*Exapostilarion:*
*Having put to death worldly voluptuousness, O Anthony; and having recognized short-lived glory, thou didst quickly depart from it, choosing to walk the narrow path leading to the spaciousness of Paradise. Therefore pray, O Anthony, that they who honor thee may be saved. (Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Truly confessing thee as the Birthgiver of God, O Sovereign Lady, we who are saved through thee: for thou didst bear God, Who destroyed death by the Cross and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable; with whom we praise thee, O Virgin.

*At the Praises, Stichera to 4, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Desiring to see the ineffable joy of the saints and the gladness of the venerable who pleased God, thou didst hate all worldly joy; but passing thy days in fasting and tears, until thou didst also put to death the bodily passions; therefore thou wast also accounted worthy of the rejoicing of the venerable; with whom pray thou, that our souls may be saved. (Twice)

*Sticheron:*
Today accept those who bring a hymn of praise unto thee on thy memory, O Anthony, and anticipate us before God by thy prayers, and deliver us from the standing on the left hand, and pray that we be joined to the portion of the elect: that we all may worthily bless thee as a fervent intercessor.

*Sticheron:*
In a dark place thou didst shine forth as a star, O venerable one; and thou didst put to shame the gloomy faces of the demons, O all-wise one. Whence having ascended unto the heavens, standing with boldness before the throne of Christ, remember those who celebrate thy memory, O venerable one, that we may find mercy on the Day of Judgment.

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having left thy homeland, O venerable father Anthony, and having accepted voluntary wandering for the Lord's sake: thou didst endure for Christ's sake the labors of long journeying, thirst and hunger, cold and heat. Therefore thou hearest: Good and faithful servant, enter into the joy of thy Lord![^292] Pray, O father, that we also may hear this voice: that we may always bless thy memory with love.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Accept, O Sovereign Lady:*[^293]
*Great Doxology. And Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^294]*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213 [Gal. 5:22–6:2].*
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord:*
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon the earth.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 10 [Mt. 4:25–5:12].*
*Communion Verse: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be.*

*July 11: The Holy and All-Praised Great-Martyr Euphemia, that is, the Definition of the Fathers.*[^295]

*At Lord, I have cried, Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*
O marvelous wonder! The ewe-lamb of the Lord, emulating His voluntary death by the pains of suffering, lying in the tomb, pours forth an outpouring of blood by the power of the Spirit; drawing which for the cleansing of souls, we ever offer praise of all things unto God.

*Sticheron:*
O marvelous wonder! How indeed in mortal nature the all-praised passion-bearer stood today, holding the divine scroll, not yielding it to the adversaries, but entrusting it unto the Fathers! O glorious martyr, boast of the universe, save now the unshakable Church of Christ by thy prayers.

*Sticheron:*
The assembly of the divine Fathers places the definition of faith upon thy breast: which having received, O most glorious one, preserving the divine faith unshaken, vanquishing every heresy, and putting to shame the false-glorying defenders: therefore we honor and bless thee.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
At the right hand of the Savior stood the virgin, passion-bearer and martyr Euphemia, robed in victorious virtues, and adorned with the oil of purity and the blood of suffering; and crying aloud unto Him, joyfully holding her lamp: I ran after the fragrance of Thy myrrh, O Christ God, for I am wounded with Thy love; do not separate me, O Heavenly Bridegroom![^296] By her prayers send down Thy mercies upon us, O All-Powerful Savior.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Laid Aside All" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
