A sword, as Simeon said, shall pierce through thy heart, O all-pure Sovereign Lady,[^297] when thou didst behold Him Who was lifted up upon the Tree by the ineffable Word, being lifted up upon the Cross as one condemned by the lawless, given vinegar and gall to drink, His side pierced, and His hands and feet nailed; and sobbing thou didst cry out, motherly exclaiming: What is this new mystery, O sweetest Child?

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos:*
*Then we say the Verse:*
*Wondrous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.[^298]*
*And we sing the Sticheron, Tone 2:*
With hymns let the martyr of Christ be honored, for truly harmony of praises is fitting for her, so as to weave a crown for her head; for she, truly unswerving and firm, cast down the cruelty of many tormentors. Therefore she cried out, joyfully suffering: Be Thou my help, O Lord, and forsake me not!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Robed in virtues and enlightened in mind, pouring out myrrh into the hearts of the faithful, having shone forth from the East as a radiant star, and having convened the council of the divine Fathers by the descent of the Holy Spirit, cease not to pray unto Christ for us, O all-praised Euphemia, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the Third Day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding the most lawless people unjustly nailing Thee upon the Tree, the Virgin and Thine immaculate Mother, as Simeon foretold, was wounded in maternal heart, O Savior.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy ewe-lamb Euphemia, O Jesus, cries with a loud voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I suffer, and I am crucified and buried with Thee by Thy baptism;[^299] and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; but as a blameless sacrifice accept me who with love have sacrificed myself unto Thee. By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls.

*Or this Troparion, in the same Tone:*
Having loved Christ thy Bridegroom, and having prepared thy lamp radiant, shining forth with virtues, O all-praised Euphemia: therefore thou didst enter with Him into the marriage, having received from Him the crown of suffering;

...but deliver from afflictions us who honor thy memory in faith.

*At Matins, one Canon of the Octoechos; and of the Holy Euphemia to 4, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking in the form of a cross and dividing the sea, submerged Pharaoh the chariot-driver of old; and it saved Israel fleeing on foot, chanting a hymn unto God.[^300]*

*Troparion:*
Shining with the pure rays of the Spirit and dispelling the gloomy darkness of deceit, disperse the night of my boundless evils, and grant me enlightenment, O all-praised martyr and passion-bearer.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst valiantly endure bodily pains, and hast joyfully passed over unto painless delight, O martyr; therefore I pray thee: transform the sickness of my soul by thy prayers, and guide me unto salvation.

*Troparion:*
O honorable, pure, and undefiled bride of Christ the King: cleanse the evil defilements of my soul, heal my sickness, cure the incurable passions of my heart, that being saved I may praise thee.

*Theotokion:*
O heavenly ladder reaching from earth unto heaven, by which God the Word came down unto men, O blessed and pure one; ineffable wonder, incomprehensible vision: save those who flee unto thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Supreme Fashioner of the celestial circle and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Confirmation of the faithful, only Lover of mankind.[^301]*

*Troparion:*
Seeing thee, O virgin, robed with wounds of suffering as with golden divine garments, the all-rich Word brought thee into the heavenly dwellings of the bodiless ranks to rejoice.

*Troparion:*
Into the fragrance of the myrrh that shone forth from the pure one didst thou eagerly run, O martyr, and thou didst pour forth unto the world the myrrh of divine healings. Therefore I cry aloud: drive away now the foul passions of my heart!

*Troparion:*
My soul, ailing with corrupting passions and having slipped toward the precipices of deceit, O all-praised martyr, heal by divine overshadowings, and strengthen it not to be overcome.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, thou who alone hast given birth unto the Lord of all; rejoice, thou who hast mediated life; rejoice, mountain shadowed and unhewn, confirmation of the faithful, only all-immaculate one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Прему́дрости):*

By the streams of thy blood thou ever workest a flood upon the impious, O all-praised one; and watering always with showers of grace the spiritual fields, in them thou hast grown the ear of grain of faith. Therefore most gloriously even after death thou didst appear as a cloud shedding forth a living testimony.[^302] O all-praised passion-bearer, pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As a virgin and the only one among women who without seed hast given birth unto God in the flesh, all we generations of men bless thee: for the fire of the Godhead made its abode within thee, and as an infant thou feedest with milk the Creator and Lord. Therefore, angelic and human race, we worthily glorify thy most holy nativity, and in harmony cry aloud unto thee: Intercede with Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who worship thy most holy nativity in faith.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb cried weeping, and bitterly lamenting exclaiming: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my maternal heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O Long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain: have compassion and grant remission of sins unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.[^303]*

*Troparion:*
I weep for myself, having ever an uncorrected life: O glorious martyr, help me, and deliver me from the future judgment!

*Troparion:*
Having withered by divine rains the fire that is our fellow-servant: deliver me from the fire and from eternal condemnation!

*Troparion:*
Vessel of virginity, dwelling of the Holy Spirit, O Euphemia: deliver me from every harmful adversity through thine intercession.

*Theotokion:*
Grant unto me who call upon Thee the abyss of thy tender mercy, O thou who hast given birth unto the Compassionate One, the Savior of all who sing of Thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou rejected me from Thy presence, O Never-setting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray.[^304]*

*Troparion:*
As a most radiant sun in the midst of stars, O martyr, thou didst shine forth among the martyrs who were with thee; and by thy suffering thou didst illumine the world. But I pray thee: enlighten my heart, which is darkened by the gloom of passions.

*Troparion:*
By unslumbering prayer, O all-praised one, put to sleep the soul-corrupting passions of my heart, and grant me divine vigilance, who lie upon the bed of despondency, enslaved to the sleep of slothfulness.

*Troparion:*
The snares of the crafty hunters have caught me like a bird, and I am grievously wounded, and am now held by evil hands: O chosen dove of the Lord, help me and snatch me from their malice!

*Theotokion:*
Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-immaculate one, we pray: despise not the kindred providence concerning us, for we Christians mercifully present thee, and thee alone, as our cleansing before the Sovereign Master.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.[^305]*

*Troparion:*
Having steadfastly endured the assaults of wild beasts, by thy prayers deliver me from the noetic beasts that grievously afflict me and mercilessly fall upon my weakness.

*Troparion:*
Having submitted to the divine yoke, thou didst subdue the proud enemy and crush him under thy feet. Snatch therefore from him, O martyr and passion-bearer, him who flees unto thy protection.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst pass through the grievous tempest of tortures, O martyr, and hast reached the haven of true life; therefore save me who am tempest-tossed in the abyss of worldly pleasures.

*Theotokion:*
May we be delivered from grievous transgressions by thy prayers, O pure Mother of God, and may we obtain the divine radiance, O all-pure one, of the Son of God Who was ineffably incarnate of thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Fulfilled the Dispensation" (Е҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ):*
Thou didst fervently undertake struggles in martyrdom, struggles in faith for Christ thy Bridegroom; but even now, as thou didst receive and preservest the definition from the six hundred and thirty God-bearing Fathers, O all-praised one: pray through the Birthgiver of God to subdue heresies and the insolence of enemies under the feet of Orthodox rulers.[^306]

*Ikos:*
What of thy sufferings, or what of thy right deeds, or what of thy virginity, or of thine immaculate life? For thou didst gladden the Father, having betrothed thyself unto the Son, and adorned thyself for the Holy Spirit. Who is sufficient for these things? Who can speak now? How many never-setting virtues overshadow thee: for having shone forth from the East as from a tomb, thou shinest unto all, and sendest forth rays upon earth and sea, and into all under the sun, enlightening and perfuming the ends of the earth. Therefore also, having received the scroll entrusted by the six hundred and thirty God-bearing Fathers, thou hast preserved the definition, O all-praised one!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages of ages![^307]*

*Troparion:*
With the medicinal streams of thy blood thou didst extinguish the flame of deceit, O God-wise passion-bearer; therefore extinguish the furnace of my humble body by the rain of thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Woe is me, my all-wretched soul, sinning and not repenting! How hopest thou to stand before the dread Judge? Hasten to cry aloud unto the Lord: O God, cleanse me!

*Troparion:*
Having emulated the three youths, thou didst trample upon the fire, O God-wise maiden; therefore deliver me from the unbearable fire and eternal flame, who flee unto thy protection.

*Theotokion:*
Having clothed Himself in all of man except sin, the All-Immaculate One came forth from thy womb bearing flesh, O pure one; Whom pray thou to save those who honor thee in faith.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tormentor furiously stoked the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages![^308]*

*Troparion:*
Adorned, O glorious one, with bodily torments, thou wast purely betrothed unto the fair Word, Who preserved thee incorrupt. Therefore, who am corrupted by all manner of passions, show me forth fair through sincere repentance: that I may ever bless thee as a good intercessor.

*Troparion:*
Thy heart was a divine scroll, bearing the law of God written by the Spirit, and thou didst entrust the scroll of Orthodoxy, O venerable martyr. Therefore I pray: tearing up the crafty document of mine evil deeds, pray that I may be written in the book of the living pray, O pure one, that I may be written in the book of the saved!

*Troparion:*
Having died according to the law of nature, beyond nature thou pourest forth blood from thy dead body, having thereby put to death the adversary of our life, O venerable one, and granting life-bearing health unto the faithful. For this cause I cry aloud unto thee: raise up my deadened soul!

*Theotokion:*
Desiring thee, the pure and spotless Virgin, the maiden preserved both soul and body blameless: having quenched the burning of passions through patience, and having endured the trial of many torments. And now in heavenly habitations she dances, rejoicing with thee unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Every ear trembled at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from the virginal womb. Therefore, the all-pure Birthgiver of God, we the faithful magnify![^309]*

*Troparion:*
That the all-praised one might behold Thy radiant beauty, O Master, she despised the beauty of the body and of the world, crying aloud with love: O Bridegroom, Father's Word, show me Thy most beloved countenance, with Whose divine beauties I am wounded!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst rise at dawn unto Him Who before the morning star shone forth from the womb of the Father,[^310] and thou wast light, shining more than the sun. Therefore enlighten me, I cry aloud, O glorious Euphemia, who am darkened by demonic crafty assaults!

*Troparion:*
I tremble, receiving in mind the dread judgment seat: for I have acquired deeds that lead me into grievous torments. O martyr who didst endure bitter torments for the sake of thy Beloved: deliver me from bitter condemnation!

*Troparion:*
A divine nightingale in truth, reaching the heavenly spring, a pure swallow, a fair turtle-dove, all-praised and adorned dove: save us from grievous perils, and account us worthy to obtain divine glory!

*Theotokion:*
Enlighten the eyes of my heart, O Door of Light, I pray, that I may not sleep unto the death of ungodly deeds; that in faith I may sing of thee, that I may magnify thee, boast of angels and venerable company of the holy martyrs!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
A strange and marvelous, and great miracle, let all the people glorify: how after death all-radiant Euphemia, having received the scroll, preserved it, and showed forth as victorious the Orthodox Fathers in the city of Chalcedon.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto Hypostatic Wisdom, the Supersubstantial Word, and Physician of souls and bodies: heal the festering sores and ulcers of my soul, grievous and of many years, and stay the sicknesses of my heart, O Virgin!

*At the Praises, Stichera to 4, Tone 3. Idiomela:*
Beholding the triumphant festival of the passion-bearer godly-wisely celebrated, ye faithful: let us sing a thanksgiving hymn unto our God Who is marvelous in counsels; for He conquered the invisible dominion of the opposing power in a woman's nature, and having accomplished His divine power in the weakness of the noble martyr, through her prayers He saves our souls! (Twice)

*Sticheron:*
Having drawn the cup of truth from her suffering blood, the all-praised martyr of Christ ever offers it unto the Church; wherein the Nourisher of the Church, with the voice of wisdom, convokes us, saying: Draw the drink that testifies of the Resurrection, that drives away the unbelieving, that cleanses passions, and preserves pious souls, crying aloud unto the Savior: Thou Who hast watered us with the stream of spiritual food, save our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Ye who have marked your soul with the blood of Christ on the day of redemption,[^311] let us prophetically draw with spiritual gladness the holy blood bubbling up for us from the martyr's spring, figuring forth the life-giving passions of the Savior and eternal glory. Therefore let us cry aloud unto Him: O Lord Who art glorified in Thy saints, by the prayers of Thine all-praised passion-bearer, save our souls!

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Let every tongue be moved unto the praising of all-praised Euphemia; let every tribe and every age, young men and virgins, crown with praises the virgin and martyr of Christ. For having lawfully contended with courage and cast away womanly weakness, through suffering pains she cast down the tormentor enemy. And adorned with a heavenly and divine crown, she prays unto the Bridegroom and God to grant us great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and willingly accepting passions, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, sobbing cried aloud: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human weakness and from corruption didst deliver all didst deliver all by Thy tender mercy?

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Then we say the Verse:*
*In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.[^312]*
*And we sing the Sticheron, Tone 8:*
Rejoice, definition of the faith! Rejoice, statute of the holy Fathers, O Bride of God! Rejoice, firm safeguard for us the faithful! Rejoice, glory of the Church of Christ! Rejoice, animate pillar of virgins! Rejoice, glorious and peaceful repository of myrrh, for whose sake thou pourest forth healings unto the faithful, O all-praised martyr!

*Glory... Tone 1:*
Today the choirs of the Fathers, O all-praised one, having gathered together for Christ's sake, bring unto thee the scroll of the Orthodox faith: which having received into thy venerable hands, thou preservest unto the end. Therefore also the assemblies of men, having come together, honor thy suffering, crying aloud in piety: Rejoice, all-praised one, who didst turn womanly nature into manly battle-array! Rejoice, all-praised one, who hast preserved uninjured the Orthodox faith handed down by the Fathers! Rejoice, thou who prayest for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́льніи му́чнцы):*
When the ewe-lamb and spotless Sovereign Lady beheld her own Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor comeliness, sobbing she cried aloud: Woe is me! Whither has Thy beauty set, O Sweetest One? Whither Thy splendor? Whither the radiant grace of Thine image, O my most beloved Son?

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos to 4; and of Euphemia, Ode 3 to 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Wondrous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*
*Verse: In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.*
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 181 [2 Cor. 6:1–10].[^313]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: With patience I waited for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 33 [Lk. 7:36–50].*
*Communion Verse: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be.*

*Month of the same [July], on the 12th Day: The Holy Martyrs Proclus and Hilarion; and our Venerable Father Michael Maleinos.*[^314]

*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Having endured manifold torments, ye received radiant crowns together, O Hilarion and Proclus; therefore, as we faithfully celebrate your all-festal memory, we pray fervently that ye intercede for us all.

*Sticheron 2:* Thou didst endure the tearing of thy flesh, O Proclus, foreseeing eternal glory, O blessed one, and dwelling in paradise, and the unwaning Light; wherein abiding, pray that we also who hymn thee may be enlightened.

*Sticheron 3:* Like two beacons ye enlighten the world with the ray of miracles, O passion-bearers of the Lord, Hilarion and Proclus, driving away the gloom of passions; therefore we joyfully hymn you, celebrating your memory.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable Father, Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне ѻ҆́тче):*

*Sticheron 1:* O venerable father, from youth thou didst love the life equal to the angels, counting the fair things of this world as refuse; and as grass the passing glory, not sparing, thou didst stir, forgetful not of divine glory,[^315] whereof thou now partakest, having shaken off the bonds of the flesh below, and standing before the Thrice-radiant Light, and being filled with the light-shedding of Christ. Pray to Him, pray to Him always, to grant to the Church oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy.

*Sticheron 2:* O venerable father, radiance of compunction, lamp to those in the darkness of despair, unwaning morning star, most precious ember of repentance, divine pearl of virtues, and candlestick of almsgiving; great in prayers, protector of orphans and widows, canonical rule of the Church, and active lamp of temperance! Pray to Christ, pray to Christ always, to grant to the Church oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy.

*Sticheron 3:* O venerable father, thou wast sanctified before thy birth, even as Jeremiah most sacred; for the Mother of the Lord foretold to her who bore thee that she would give thee as fruit, who wast indeed given from swaddling-bands, and manifestly proclaimed. And straightway with upright mind, fleeing the turmoil of life, O divinely-wise one, and loving the life of stillness, which thou didst also traverse. Like sand hast thou planted the assemblies of monastics by the outflows of the waters of God's Spirit.

*Glory... Tone 5:* O venerable father Michael, into all the earth hath the sound of thy right deeds gone forth. Therefore in the heavens hast thou found the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the demonic legions and attain unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate. Having boldness before Christ God, entreat peace for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6,[^316] Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*

Beholding our Life hanging upon the Tree, the all-immaculate Birthgiver of God wailed maternally, crying aloud: "O my Son and my God, save those who hymn Thee with love!"

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 8:* The multitudes of monastics honor thee as instructor, O our father Michael; for by thy path we have learned truly to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou, having labored for Christ, and having exposed the power of the enemy, companion of angels, fellow-partaker with the venerable and the righteous; with them pray to the Lord, that our souls may find mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8:[^317]*

The undefiled Heifer, seeing her Calf voluntarily nailed to the Tree, weeping piteously, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What hath the graceless Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O All-beloved?"

*Troparion, Tone 8:* In thee, O father, was preserved that which was after the likeness; for taking up the Cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thy deeds didst teach us to overlook the flesh, for it passes away, and to attend unto the soul, an immortal thing. Therefore, O venerable Michael, thy spirit rejoices together with the angels.

*At Matins, one canon from the Octoechos, and two for the Saints, to 8.*

*Canon of the Martyrs. A Work of Joseph. Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the threefold part of my soul in the depth of dispassion, as of old the mighty captains; that unto Thee, as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory.

*Troparion:* Having united yourselves with God, and enlightened ever by divine rays, O valiant sufferers, enlighten my soul, as with compunction of heart I sing of your light-bearing and honorable suffering, O blessed ones.

*Troparion 2:* Heavenly desire reached you; therefore, O sufferers, ye counted all the fair things of life as refuse, deified by gestures toward God, and humbling all the might of the godless.

*Troparion 3:* With the God-originating fire of the Divine Trinity, ye quenched the kindle-fires of impiety with streams of your blood, O wise ones. Therefore, with pure showers of healings wash away the defilement of our passions.

*Troparion 4:* Seeking after sorrowless life, heavenly glory, the delight of paradise, spiritual light, and fair joy, O martyrs, ye endured grievous surges of tempest with valiant mind.

*Troparion 5:* The wrathful faces marveled at your endurance, O wise ones; for the beatings of the flesh and woven torments ye endured valiantly, O martyrs, rejoicing and crushing the malice of the enemy.

*Theotokion:* The poison in the ears of foremother Eve did Gabriel truly shake off when proclaiming to thee: "Rejoice, O pious one!"[^318] for thou alone, beyond mind and word, hast given birth to the Severer of the serpent's malice.

*Another Canon, of the Venerable Father, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* The wonder-working rod of Moses, striking the sea in the likeness of the Cross and dividing it, drowned of old the chariot-driving Pharaoh; but it saved Israel as a fugitive fleeing on foot, as he sang a hymn unto God.

*Troparion 1:* Enlightened by the light-beaming lightnings of the Spirit, and filled with His breathing, being sounded as an instrument, O most blessed one, drive away the gloom of my soul, and grant me a word worthily to hymn thee.

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst prefer the love of the Creator to that of parents; and to passing life, the life equal to the angels; and to worldly beauty, the truly incorruptible adornment of the King of all; wherewith thou delightest thyself, rejoicing.

*Troparion 3:* Thou wast the all-radiant ray of the Thrice-radiant Dawn; by the thrice-shining light of thy life the whole face of the earth was richly illumined, and the darkness of sin, O father, didst thou dispel by thy words.

*Theotokion:* Thou hast been revealed from afar as the tongs which Isaiah saw in the Spirit, bearing the Live Coal; for having borne the Live Coal of the Godhead, Christ, in thy womb without being consumed, O most pure one, and carried Him in thine embraces, bearing all things by His gesture.

*Canon of the Martyrs, Ode 3. Heirmos:* We boast not in wisdom, nor in might, nor in riches, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 1:* With the wisdom of divine words, thou didst refute the foolish; and the tearing of the flesh thou didst endure by the strength of the Spirit, O most blessed Proclus, God-inspired martyr.

*Troparion 2:* When thou wast pitilessly stretched out upon the rack, the mindless tyrant commanded that thou be torn, while thou didst stretch forth the eyes of thy soul to God, being adorned by thy patience.

*Troparion 3:* The crushing of thy limbs thou didst endure manfully, O most staunch one; and by thy blood thou didst quench the kindle-fires of godlessness, O Proclus, strengthened by divine grace.

*Theotokion:* Untie, O Virgin, the bond of my sins, binding me to the love of the Word born of thee, O all-immaculate one; and save me, O Lady, by thy prayers.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Lord Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters, establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 1:* Having hated from the soul the bitterness of pleasures, and abhorred earthly glory, having voluntarily cast off the belt[^319] of worldly rank of the kings on earth, thou didst prefer the King of all.

*Troparion 2:* Sanctified from swaddling-bands, chosen from the womb wast thou revealed; for the Mother who bore Christ promised to thy honorable mother to give thee, and straightway accepted thee as a sanctified gift.

*Troparion 3:* By spiritual understanding, having purified soul and mind, and truly thy body by fasting, thou didst make Christ to dwell within thee; having Him speaking within thee and walking, and breathing forth fragrance to the world.

*Theotokion:* Within thy womb, O pure one, the Word enthroned together with the Father took up His abode, incarnate of thy pure blood, and being born, He cleansed creation from idol frenzy by the sprinkling of His blood.

*Kontakion of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

As the morning star, the honorable suffering of the martyrs Proclus and Hilarion hath shone forth, pre-illuminating us with the splendors of miracles. Therefore we celebrate your memory: entreat Christ God, that He may save our souls.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*

Hilarion, ye faithful, and glorious Proclus, who suffered firmly and cast down the enemy, let us with one accord worthily honor, magnifying Christ; for they by miracles drive away the mist of passions, enlightening those who run to them in faith.

*Glory... of the Venerable Father, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^320]

The rational lamb of Christ, who venerable-like pastured the Lord's sheep upon the grass of virtues, is marveled at by angels, having put demons to shame by his courage; he lived among men as one bodiless. Therefore he hath also worthily stood before the Trinity, and ceases not to guide his flock by prayers; unto whom let us cry aloud: Intercede with Christ God, to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Both now... Theotokion, Tone 8:*

Having fallen into manifold temptations from visible and invisible enemies, held fast by the tempest of mine innumerable sins, and fleeing unto thee, O pure one, as to my fervent protection and shelter, I run unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O all-immaculate Lady, entreat Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed, for all thy servants who unceasingly pray to thee, O most pure Birthgiver of God; entreating Him ever to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8:*

The Ewe, seeing the Lamb, Shepherd, and Redeemer upon the Cross, wailed weeping, and bitterly lamenting cried aloud: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, seeing Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of loving-kindness, O long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Wellspring, have compassion and grant remission of sins unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Seated in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus most divine came upon a light cloud, with His incorruptible palm, and saved those who cry: "Glory, O Christ, to Thy power!"

*Troparion 1:* The firmness of the matter followed thy words; for the bloodthirsty one forbade thee to go forward, until thou didst confess Christ, the one Lord and God Most High over all, O Proclus.

*Troparion 2:* Unconquerable in boldness and valiant in mind, O passion-bearer Proclus, thou didst stand before the judgment seat, loudly proclaiming the Incarnation of our Redeemer, Who poured Himself out even unto the form of a servant.

*Troparion 3:* Shamelessly indeed the persecutor displayed to the wise ones the kinds of torments and a most violent death; but seeing them unconquered, he marveled; and beholding his own defeat, he was confounded.

*Theotokion:* Having compassion on the shattered transgression, the work of Thy hands, O Word, Thou didst dwell in the unwedded womb, and camest in two natures, renewing the paths of incorruption for those who know Thee.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who left not the bosom of the Father, and visited our poverty. Therefore with the prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: "Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!"

*Troparion 1:* A stream of life watered thy mind by the operation of the Divine Spirit, whence casting away the bitterness of passions, it was filled with divine sweetness; receiving unceasing satiety therefrom, it sings: "Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!"

*Troparion 2:* The fire of temptations and the furnace of passions, by the streams of thy tears, O father, and by rich spiritual dew hast thou quenched, remaining unburned; for consumed with love for the King of all, thou didst wither material desires.

*Troparion 3:* Having raised thy mind unto heavenly life, O father, thou didst lighten the flesh which weighed it down with material passions; therefore thou didst serve the Trinity unswervingly. And emulating the character of Moses and the life of Elijah, thou didst behold the Lord.

*Theotokion:* All-fair art thou, O pure one, my neighbor, Thy Son, O Lady, calls thee; having found thee holier than angelic nature, He ineffably wove the purple of His body from thy pure blood, and glorifies those who glorify Him.

*Canon of the Martyrs, Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou, O my Lord, didst come as Light into the world, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.

*Troparion 1:* Thou, O Lord, art the strength and establishment of the martyrs; Thou art the unshakable wall of the holy ones who suffer zealously.

*Troparion 2:* Burning thy soul with desire for God, thou didst endure being scorched with candles, O Proclus, most blessed and marvelous.

*Troparion 3:* Fearing only the eternal fire, thou didst cry aloud when scorched by the command of the tormentor, O martyr Proclus, being voluntarily put to death.

*Theotokion:* Who can tell thy mystery, O Lady Birthgiver of God? For the Creator of all hast thou ineffably incarnate.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparion 1:* The slumber of despondency covered not the eyes of thy heart, but being wounded in thy mind by the sting of divine desire, by all-night vigilance and endurance in prayer thou didst not close the eyelids[^321] of the flesh.

*Troparion 2:* Having humility as thy companion from youth, enlightening thy mind with divine visions and teachings, thou wast not stolen away by the thought of the deceitful one, but keptest a humble mindset.

*Theotokion:* Having found thee fair in the beauty of virtues, O palace, the fair Word took up His abode in thy incorruptible womb, and was well-pleased to be born of thee changelessly, adorning the assemblies of the faithful.

*Canon of the Martyrs, Ode 6. Heirmos:* I came into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of my many sins hath drowned me; but as God lead up my life from the abyss, O greatly merciful One.

*Troparion 1:* Neither fire, nor wounds, nor beasts, nor the trial of death, O valiant ones, were able to separate you from the pure and blameless love of God.

*Troparion 2:* The laws of praise cannot encompass[^322] the martyrs' courage truly unto blood; the only Lord Who glorifies magnifies them as His pleasing servants.

*Troparion 3:* Having sailed favorably across the surge of cruel torments, ye hastened unto the divine haven which is in Christ, O passion-bearing martyrs, of everlasting delight.

*Theotokion:* "Behold, the Virgin," cried Isaiah of old, "bears the Angel of Great Counsel, Emmanuel, God and Lord, and Savior of our souls."

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 6. Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparion 1:* Having mortified the passions, thou didst come alive in the Spirit; and having bound the strong man, thou didst despoil his goods; and for useful virtues thou didst expand the receptacles by thy teachings.

*Troparion 2:* Having driven away the slothful brevity[^323] of the mind, dispensing with thy hand and tongue the Bread of Life and what pertains to abundance, O father, thou wast twofold unto those who desired.

*Troparion 3:* Having received a talent from divine instruction, as a son thou didst multiply it beyond measure by divine progress; thou hearest the joyful voice of thy Master calling thee unto higher things.

*Theotokion:* Beyond the laws of nature, in thee the Most High became man, and was revealed changelessly in the flesh, and lived among men, and showed the path to those desiring to ascend to heavenly ways.

*Kontakion of the Venerable Father, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Тве́рдыѧ):*[^324]

The heaviness of the flesh thou didst thin down by labors, and the swiftness of the soul thou didst wing with enlightenment, O Michael, and wast revealed as a dwelling of the Trinity; which beholding clearly, O blessed one, pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:* The immovable pillar of prayers, completion of the love of heavenly wisdom, abyss of compunction! Not unto the third part of the height wast thou taken up, but where the cherubim clearly behold the unwaning Godhead; and in these visions thou dost worship the Holy One, and receivest the pre-divine glory, being deified therefrom. Therefore, unable worthily to hymn thee, we ask forgiveness, crying together: Pray unceasingly for us all!

*Canon of the Martyrs, Ode 7. Heirmos:* The Abrahamic children of old in Babylon trampled down the furnace flame, crying in hymns: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion 1:* Having yielded your bodies to wounds for love of the incarnate Word, O martyrs, ye received from Him the crown of righteousness in beauty.

*Troparion 2:* Having enlightened your minds with divine light, by faith ye ran through the darkness of martyrdom, crying, O all-praised ones: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion 3:* By the stream of thy honey-flowing words from thy lips, O most blessed Proclus, as from divine healings dost thou water those who sing: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion 4:* Having strengthened his mind with divine power, he endured the bitter forms of torments, crying, O Hilarion: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Theotokion:* As higher than all creation, thou bearest the Creator of all, remaining an undefiled Virgin, only blessed one, ever all-glorified.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 7. Heirmos:* God's condescension of old put the fire to shame in Babylon; therefore the children in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow, sang: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!" Having received in thy heart the seed of God's commandments, more than an hundredfold, even a thousandfold fruit didst thou yield, a divine ear of corn, with the monks crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Troparion 2:* Thy lips were revealed as a vessel of the All-Holy Spirit, and thy tongue as a flood of honorable teachings unto those crying with one accord to the Trinity: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Theotokion:* Without seed He Who fashioned me took up His abode within thy womb, and from thee came forth God and Man in the flesh; not divided into two hypostases, nor introducing mixture, the blessed God of our fathers.

*Canon of the Martyrs, Ode 8. Heirmos:* O almighty Redeemer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew those who acted piously, and taughtest them to sing: "All ye works, bless and hymn the Lord!"

*Troparion 1:* After many torments, the fierce enemy gave thee over to be shot with arrows; pierced by which, O all-praised Proclus, rejoicing into the hands of God didst thou commend thy spirit.

*Troparion 2:* Thy honorable body was wounded, O holy one, by unceasing arrows, and incurably wounded the bodiless enemies who sing not: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 3:* Enlightened by the ray of grace, like a stone rolling upon the earth, thou didst crush the stronghold of deceit, O Hilarion, a company in strength: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 4:* The drops of thy blood having flowed, they quenched the fire of ungodliness, and gave drink to the faithful who unceasingly sing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Theotokion:* Incarnate of thy pure blood, God Who made and fashioned me, O Birthgiver of God, Lady, renewed the world that cries: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 8. Heirmos:* Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly kindle the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Redeemer: "Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion 1:* Beholding in the flesh, by brightness of mind, the incomprehensible beauty of the Lord as much as was desired, now having left the dust, and wholly migrated unto it, thou standest before the face of the Trinity, crying: "Ye people, supreme-exalt the Lord unto all ages!"

*Troparion 2:* A precious crown did the Master bestow upon thee, having finished thy contest against demons, brighter than gold and fairer than pearls, of the Kingdom and immortality, O father, shining more than the sun, thou criest: "Ye people, supreme-exalt the Lord unto all ages!"

*Troparion 3:* O the sweet tongue of thine, wherewith thou didst wisely catch those swallowed by the deceitful enemy! And unto a divine transformation didst thou change them, establishing hermitages[^325] uniformly like the sand, and crying unceasingly: "Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme-exalt the Lord unto all ages!"

*Theotokion:* Deadened by the all-destructive sting of the serpent, O Virgin, thou alone hast brought to life, having given birth to the Life-giving God. Whom entreat fervently to quiet the uprisings of passions in those who hymn with love: "Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Canon of the Martyrs, Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, through the burgeoning of childbearing didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion 1:* This radiant, sacred, and auspicious memory of the martyrs hath shone forth, enlightening the whole earth, taking away the gloom of malice, driving away the cloud from souls, and pouring forth the grace of healings.

*Troparion 2:* Like two spiritual breasts pouring forth the milk of healings, O martyrs, ye nourish every heart, driving away the bitterness of diseases, and healing grievous passions. Therefore worthily are ye blessed.

*Troparion 3:* Having dipped your purple robe in drops of sacred blood, O most glorious ones, and wearing it gorgeously, ye reign ever with Christ, our only King and God, praying for the world.

*Troparion 4:* The earth hath been blessed by your blood, and by the laying down of your relics, the Church of the firstborn in the Spirit shines forth divinely; wherein with the martyrs, as martyrs pray ye for the world.

*Theotokion:* A dwelling of the Light, O pure Lady, did thy all-pure womb become, for the sake of Him Who appeared through thee in visible bodily form, and enlightened all that were in darkness. Therefore with faith we call thee blessed.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 9. Heirmos:* Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High voluntarily came down even unto the flesh, becoming man from the virginal womb. Therefore, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, we faithful magnify thee.

*Troparion 1:* Today all the earth hath come, proclaiming the divine name of Christ, the most glorious and divine memory, celebrating; for thou hast made it heavenly by monk-like teachings, and hast emulated the noetic angelic ranks.

*Troparion 2:* The lightnings of thy wonders have enlightened all the ends of the earth; unto those who pray in faith, thy shrine pours forth healings. And heaven indeed possesses thee, while the earth possesses the earthly tabernacle of thy body, which even before the grave was worn thin by thy temperance.

*Troparion 3:* An earthly mind cannot comprehend that Light, the thrice-radiant, most bright, and pre-ineffable, which thou hast now received, whereby also thou art manifestly deified. Standing before the Holy Trinity, remember those who remember thee, O most blessed one.

*Theotokion:* We were deprived of divine life by falling away, on account of the tree of knowledge; but thou didst give birth to the Tree of Life, Christ God, Who hath led out from the dark abodes of Hades and brought into un-deprivable life those who hymn thee.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "The heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^326]

Desiring the angelic life, thou didst distance thyself from all the fair things of life, didst take up thine abode in the courts of the Lord's commandments, and rejoicest with the angels.

*Theotokion:* Of the good things granted by God unto the world, thou art the cause, O Birthgiver of God; but even now make the easily reconciled God merciful toward the common salvation.

*Month of the same [July], on the 13th Day: The Synaxis of the Holy Archangel Gabriel; and our Venerable Father Stephen the Sabaite.*[^327]

*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera of the Archangel, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron 1:* The Pre-eternal Mind, having established a second light, Gabriel, through divine partakings, enlightening the whole universe, and revealing unto us the divine and truly great mystery hidden from the ages: that He Who is bodiless is incarnate in a virginal womb, and hath become man in order to save man.

*Sticheron 2:* Standing before the throne of the Thrice-radiant Godhead, and richly shining with divine splendors emitted unceasingly therefrom: unto those upon earth joyfully celebrating and praising thee, deliver from the gloom of passions, and illumine with enlightenment, O Gabriel arch-strategist, intercessor for our souls.

*Sticheron 3:* Cast down the haughty strivings of the Hagarenes, which often come upon thy flock; cease the discords of the Church; calm the tempest of measureless temptations; deliver from perils and distresses those who honor thee with love and flee under thy protection, O Gabriel arch-strategist, intercessor for our souls.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable Father, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:* O God-bearing father Stephen, having brightly strengthened thy mind with divine wisdom, thou didst philosophically govern anger with courage, desire with chastity, and by righteousness all the power of the soul. Thou didst fashion a fair chariot of virtues, whereon having mounted, rejoicing thou didst ascend unto the height, O glorious one.

*Sticheron 2:* O God-voiced father Stephen, by theology thou didst enlighten thy mind, O venerable one. Bearing anger like a spear stretched out against the blaspheming heretics, O blessed one, with desire for heavenly sustenance, whereof thou hast been accounted worthy to partake, O marvelous one, standing before the throne of the King and Creator of all.

*Sticheron 3:* O God-speaking father Stephen, having hedged about thy mind on every side with temperance, thou didst accomplish an ascent unto the First Cause;[^328] and thou didst quiet worldly tumult, and shaking off turmoil,[^329] by purity of mind thou wast held fast, O most wise one, with truly utmost desire, O divinely-wise, venerable one.

*Glory... Tone 6. By Byzantios:*

Let all the angelic ranks rejoice with us; for your champion and our defender, the great arch-strategist, on this present day, appearing most gloriously in his honorable temple, sanctifies it. Therefore, singing worthily, let us cry aloud unto him: Cover us with the shelter of thy wings, O great Gabriel, archangel!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*

When the pure Lady beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross, weeping maternally, she cried aloud: "O my Son and my God, my sweetest Child, how dost Thou endure a reproachful passion?"

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*

*Glory... both now... Tone 8. By John the Monk:*

*Glory... both now... (continued):* As leader of the ranks and defender, and first captain of the angels, O arch-strategist, from every necessity and affliction, disease, and grievous transgressions set free those who faithfully hymn and entreat thee, O glorious one; manifestly as immaterial beholding the Immaterial One, and illumined with the unapproachable light of eternal glory. For He out of love for mankind took flesh for our sake from the Virgin, desiring to save mankind.

*Troparion, Tone 4:* Arch-strategist of the heavenly hosts, we the unworthy do ever entreat thee, that by thy prayers thou mayest surround us with the shelter of the wings of thine immaterial glory, preserving us who fall down diligently and cry aloud: Deliver us from perils, as leader of the ranks of the hosts on high!

*At Matins, one canon from the Octoechos, with the heirmos to 4; and of the Archangel to 6. A Work of Joseph, whereof the acrostic is: "Gabriel the all-honorable I praise."[^330] Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen and Mother, and I shall appear brightly celebrating, and sing rejoicing of her wonders.

*Troparion 1:* Being of light, through immaterial partakings of the divine and immaterial light, I pray thee, O archangel of the Lord: enlighten my mind by thy prayers, that I may hymn thee.

*Troparion 2:* Let us form a divine choir today, rejoicing, honoring the first of the bodiless minds, who announced the ineffable joy that came into the world through goodness.

*Troparion 3:* Having thee as a great protector and defender before God, and a wall and confirmation, O Gabriel, we who love thee are delivered from perils and the serpent's harm, as we praise thee.

*Theotokion:* When Gabriel beheld thee pure and all-undefiled, he brightly cried aloud to thee, O Lady: "Rejoice, O unwedded one, salvation of men, praise of angels, and veneration!"

*Another Canon, of the Venerable Father, to 4. Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Of old, the overwhelmingly armed power laid Pharaoh's whole army in the deep; and the Word incarnate hath blotted out all-wicked sin, the most glorified Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.

*Troparion 1:* Bringing to thee, O most blessed one, the debt of thy teaching, we extend recompense, weaving a crown of praises; but do thou show thyself merciful, O exceedingly rich one, by thy by thy supplications grant me spiritual grace.

*Troparion 2:* The destructive rush of passions, O father, thou didst cause to wither, embraced by words of divine wisdom; for thou wast well-disposed, sweet-voiced, meek, and humble, being crowned with the word of wisdom and understanding, O Stephen.

*Troparion 3:* Truly didst thou adorn thyself, O father, with understanding and the practical activity of grace, O God-speaking Stephen; desiring the crown of Christ's glory, of thy hope especially, O most blessed one, thou didst not miss the mark.

*Troparion 4:* Manifestly didst thou hold fast chastity, utmost meekness, and exalted humility, O God-bearer; whereby being exalted, through the activity of thy labors thou didst joyfully bring vision also,[^331] receiving an unfading crown.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast revealed, O pure one, higher than all creatures visible and invisible, O all-undefiled; for thou didst give birth to the Creator, as He was well-pleased to become incarnate in thy womb. Whom entreat with boldness to save those who hymn thee.

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish thy hymnographers, O Birthgiver of God, living and bountiful fountain, who have formed a spiritual choir, and in thy divine glory account them worthy of crowns of glory.

*Troparion 1:* By partaking of the immaterial light, being truly immaterial, O Gabriel, immaterially enlightened, thou wast seen as a second light, ever enlightening material men who hymn thee.

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast accounted worthy of a great calling, revealing unto us the great mystery, O great one among the angels; whereby we were taken up from the earth unto the greatest height, who greatly honor thee.

*Troparion 3:* Appear from heaven unto all who ever seek thee with love, and calm the tempest of misfortunes and afflictions moved against us, O Gabriel arch-strategist!

*Theotokion:* Telling the mystery hidden of old, of old Gabriel cried aloud unto thee, O Maiden: "Rejoice, O palace of God, wherein having dwelt, as merciful He hath deified all men!"

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 3. Heirmos:* The wilderness hath blossomed like a lily, O Lord, the barren church of the nations, by Thy coming, wherein my heart hath been established.

*Troparion 1:* Thy life shone forth, O God-bearing Stephen, all-brightly like the sun in the world, and enlightened those who come unto thee with faith and love.

*Troparion 2:* The ends of the earth are enlightened by thy teachings, O venerable one; for like a beacon didst thou shine forth, O God-bearer, in Christ's Church, wherein my heart hath been established.

*Troparion 3:* Thou didst keep thy soul unenslaved by passions and bodily pleasures, O God-bearing Stephen, honorable one. Therefore now worthily do we praise thee.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast revealed in purity like a lily, O Lady, blossoming in the midst of thorns by the rays of thy virginity, O all-pure Birthgiver of God.

*Kontakion of the Archangel, Tone 2:*

Beholding the glory of God in the heavens, and bestowing grace upon earth from the height, O leader of the angels, wise Gabriel, minister of God's glory and divine defender of the world: save and preserve those who cry unto thee: Be thou our helper, and none shall be against us![^332]

*Sessional Hymn of the Archangel, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

Holding the primacy among the bodiless ministers, the pre-eternal decreed fearful mystery is entrusted unto thee alone, O glorious Gabriel, the ineffable birthgiving of the holy Virgin. Crying aloud unto her: "Rejoice, thou full of grace!" Therefore worthily do we the faithful ever bless thee with gladness.

*Glory... of the Venerable Father, Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*

The uprightness of thy life enlightens the steps of all, O father Stephen, truly, of those who imitate thee with faith. Therefore unto those who ever bless thee with love, save by thy prayers from every distress, entreating Christ the Good, O venerable, blessed, and most marvelous one.

*Both now... Theotokion, Tone 3:*

As an uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou didst sprout the fairest Cluster, pouring forth for us the wine of salvation, gladdening the souls and bodies of all. Therefore, ever blessing thee as the cause of good things, with the angels we cry aloud unto thee: "Rejoice, thou full of grace!"

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Thy unwedded pure Mother, O Christ, seeing Thee dead hanging upon the Cross, weeping maternally, said: "What hath the Jewish lawless and ungrateful assembly rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I hymn Thy divine condescension."

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 4. Heirmos:* Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst grow radiant by partaking of the Source of Godhead of old, O Gabriel worthy of marvel, enlightening the Prophet Daniel, and expounding the vision of things uncomprehended by the Spirit.

*Troparion 2:* With earthly lips joyfully do we praise thee who art of fiery nature: deliver us from the fire that burns ever, O Gabriel, by thy divine intercessions.

*Troparion 3:* Clothed above the sun in a divine garment glittering with unapproachable glory, O arch-strategist of the ministers, rejoicing thou standest before the heavenly King.

*Theotokion:* Perceiving thee by the Spirit wholly pre-purified, O all-undefiled, Gabriel cried aloud unto thee effectively: "Rejoice, deliverance from the curse and restoration of the forefathers!"

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou didst come from the Virgin, neither an intercessor nor an angel, but Thyself incarnate, O Lord, and hast saved me, whole man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion 1:* Thou, O Stephen, wast a crown of monastics, set about with virtues as with precious stones; for thou wast revealed, O blessed one, truly as a God-gladdened adornment.

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst honor divine wisdom, O God-bearer; therefore did she illuminate thee with these crowns with gifts, mediating eternal glory for thee, O all-honorable father.

*Troparion 3:* By the brightness of thy manners and the patterns of goodness, O venerable Stephen, thou hast likewise reaped; therefore thou wast accessible unto all, O all-blessed and most marvelous one.

*Theotokion:* Eve brought unto me the fruit of deadening; but thou, O all-pure one, having given birth to the hypostatic Life, hast set it right again. Therefore I cry unto thee: "Rejoice ever, O Virgin!"

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 5. Heirmos:* All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst hold in thy womb God Who is over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, bestowing peace upon all who hymn thee.

*Troparion 1:* Enlightened by partaking of the First Mind, thou wast seen as a second light, crying with the countless ranks of angels: "Holy is the Father, the All-Doer; the Son, co-beginningless; and the Spirit, enthroned together!"

*Troparion 2:* Thy form is fiery, thy beauty most marvelous, striking every mind with awe. Great is thy glory, O Gabriel, great leader of the divine bodiless hosts, adornment of all who faithfully hymn thee!

*Troparion 3:* When divine Zechariah of old beheld thee standing at the time of censing, he became dumb; for he believed not thy fearful announcement, which thou didst proclaim, O Gabriel arch-strategist.

*Theotokion:* Being a temple of radiance, O all-undefiled one, at the voice of Gabriel the archangel thou didst give birth to the All-Holy God Who rests in the saints, Who sanctifies all and delivers from grievous things.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou hast become a mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Source of Light.[^333]

*Troparion 1:* By desire for dispassion thou didst quench the rushes of passions, O God-bearing Stephen; and by contemplation and action thou didst enlighten the disposition of thy soul, O father.

*Troparion 2:* With pure mind thou didst converse through spiritual Scriptures, O God-speaking Stephen; and through contemplation and action thou didst gather divine wealth, O venerable father.

*Troparion 3:* Having searched the depth of wisdom, O Stephen, thou didst attain unto finding that which was profitable; and through labors thou didst acquire the pearl of great price, O most blessed one, of understanding.

*Theotokion:* Behold, in thy womb, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, thou didst have Christ God beyond word, even as Isaiah proclaimed; above nature, O Birthgiver of God, hast thou given birth to Him.

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye divinely-wise, let us clap our hands and glorify God Who was born of her.

*Troparion 1:* Earthly tongues cannot honor thee, the radiant and clear mind, most brightly enlightened, above mind and word, by divine splendors.

*Troparion 2:* O much-bright ray of the sun, leader of the fiery ministers: by thy light-bearing supplications unto the Master, snatch away from the darkness of passions those who hymn thee.

*Troparion 3:* Destroy the counsels of the nations, establish the Orthodox faith, and cease the torn divisions of the Church, O archangel, by thy prayers unto the Creator of all.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast obedient, O pure one, unto the voice of Gabriel's divine words, and in the flesh didst give birth to the Pre-beginningless Word, Who hath delivered the world from irrationality.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy tender mercy: lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion 1:* A most bright beacon unto the universe wast thou revealed, O father Stephen, enlightening by the brightness of thy words those who flee unto thee with faith and love.

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst traverse a blessed life, putting to sleep thy senses, being outside of worldly turmoil, and didst draw near unto God, O Stephen.

*Troparion 3:* How good, how fair is the crown wherewith thou art now crowned, O most wise Stephen! Crowned with virtues, thou didst reign eminently over the passions.

*Theotokion:* Beyond nature, O Virgin, hast thou given birth, and abidest eternally a Virgin, having shown forth the images of the true Godhead of thy Son and God.

*Kontakion of the Archangel, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Тве́рдыѧ):*

Arch-strategist of God, minister of divine glory, leader of angels, and guide of men: ask that which is profitable for us, and great mercy, as arch-strategist of the bodiless hosts.

*Ikos:* Thou hast said, O Lover of mankind, in Thy Scriptures, that a multitude of angels in heaven rejoice over one man that repents, O immortal One.[^334] Therefore we who are in iniquities, O only sinless Knower of hearts, ever dare to entreat Thee as tenderhearted, to have compassion, and to send down compunction upon us unworthy ones, O Master, granting us forgiveness; for on behalf of us all, the arch-strategist of the bodiless hosts prays unto Thee.

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 7. Heirmos:* The divinely-wise ones worshipped not the creature rather than the Creator, but valiantly trampling down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: "O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion 1:* Gloriously didst thou announce the birth of John of old unto Zechariah within the divine temple, as he stood and sang unto the Redeemer and God: "O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion 2:* The all-glorious beauty of thy temple, O Gabriel, most brightly illumines the souls of the faithful, and commands them loudly to cry: "O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion 3:* Mystically enlightened by partaking of the First Light, O arch-strategist, thou wast seen truly as a second light, ever enlightening those who sing: "O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Theotokion:* Gabriel the arch-strategist was sent, announcing joy unto thee, O Virgin, pure Mother; through whom grief ceased, and the curse truly became unfruitful, and blessing blossomed forth for the faithful unto the ages.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 7. Heirmos:* The God-opposing command of the lawless tyrant raised up a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew unto the pious children, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.

*Troparion 1:* The words of thy teachings, O venerable one, are sweet-smelling, shining with the grace of the Spirit; for thou didst honor one Godhead in three Persons; and singing the incarnation of the Word, thou makest us fragrant, O exceedingly rich one.

*Troparion 2:* By purity of mind and brightness of understanding, O divinely-wise one, thou didst traverse life; and by purity of body and the radiance of virginity, thou wast revealed as an exceptional offering unto the All-Seeing and Almighty.

*Troparion 3:* Thy word was seasoned with salt; thy life, O most blessed one, shining with grace, was revealed as most marvelous. Therefore thou didst take up thine abode in the heavenly mansions, rejoicing and now standing with boldness before Christ.

*Theotokion:* Guiding my life, O all-pure one, direct it unto thy all-tranquil haven, having given birth to God, the Fountain of good things, Who bestows upon all the faithful an abundance of goodness.

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 8. Heirmos:* The birthgiving of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious children in the furnace: then indeed prefigured, but now in actuality, it moves the whole universe to sing unto Thee: "Hymn the Lord, ye works, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion 1:* With sacred words, sacred people hymn thee rejoicing; for thou, O archangel, dost announce to the Virgin the divine Word incarnate after our nature, which is for our sake, above mind and word. Therefore do we honor thee unto all ages.

*Troparion 2:* Immaterially uniting thyself unto the great and First Mind, O archangel, with thy fiery lips thou singest the sacred hymn which all the angelic choirs sing: "Hymn the Lord, ye works, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion 3:* Manifestly adorned with divine beauty, thou traversest heavenly things, and likewise accomplishest upon earth the sacred desires of Christ, the God of all, O Gabriel, leader of the angels, beauty of those who ever praise thee with faith.

*Troparion 4:* When according to hypostasis the Word in the flesh was pleased to unite Himself with man, He had thee running before, and preparing the sacred palace, O sacred Gabriel, crying: "Hymn the Lord, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Theotokion:* As the fair throne of the King, as excelling all creatures, as having alone given substance to the Pre-essential One Who hath deified men by a better union of the ineffable and strange birthgiving, let us hymn the blessed Virgin.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 8. Heirmos:* The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its actions by God's command, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 1:* Christ the Life-Giver hath placed a goodly crown upon thy head, O namesake Stephen;[^335] for by actions and contemplations thou didst shine forth, and wast revealed crying: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 2:* Having passed beyond all sense perception, O most blessed one, thou didst draw near unto the First Mind by purity of soul and brightness of life; thou bearest a crown, O Stephen, crying: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 3:* With intense desire, O most blessed Stephen, thou didst examine the divine life of the God-bearing Sabas; and by the brightness of thy life thou didst emulate him, O all-praised one, having become truly an exceptional disciple of his.

*Theotokion:* The dispassionate Mind in thy womb is united unto the human mind, O all-pure one; and by the grossness of the flesh He came to be under time, Who is above time. Therefore, O Birthgiver of God, in faith and love do we glorify thee.

*Canon of the Archangel, Ode 9. Heirmos:* Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: "Rejoice, O all-blessed Birthgiver of God, pure Ever-Virgin!"

*Troparion 1:* Awesomely dost thou stand before the throne of grace, ministering and irradiated by the outpourings of light which are beyond the mind, and holily deified; and seen as a light, enlightening those who honor thee with faith, O sacred Gabriel, arch-strategist.

*Troparion 2:* Like the heaven adorned with stars art thou seen with divine splendors; and as a commander thou holdest in thy hands a most bright scepter, and traversest all the earth, ever fulfilling the Master's will, and delivering the faithful from cruel things.

*Troparion 3:* Calm the fierce barbarian tempest ever stirred up against thy servants; cease the discords of the Church. Unto those who hymn thee grant deliverance from transgressions; and unto the King grant victories,[^336] O Gabriel, by thy fervent intercession!

*Troparion 4:* O most excellent and all-glorious twain, Michael and Gabriel, who stand before the throne of divine glory ask for all of us forgiveness of sins and turning away from evils, as intercessors, and as imitating in all things the goodness of the Master.

*Theotokion:* The light of grace shone forth through thy birthgiving and enlightened the whole universe, and destroyed the prince of darkness, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, boast of angels and salvation of all men who praise thee with unceasing voices.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable Father], Ode 9. Heirmos:* The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin hath appeared unto us, to enlighten those in darkness and to gather together the scattered. Therefore do we magnify the all-hymned Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion 1:* O most honorable Stephen, now honorably delighting and receiving nourishment that is above the mind, where the assemblies of the saints dance for joy in God, O blessed one: draw unto thyself by thine intercession those who now honor thee.

*Troparion 2:* Rejoicing, thou hast passed unto the ultimate desire, unto the one truly existing beatitude, unto surpassing beauty, unto life that truly waxes not old, unto the unwaning Light, O God-bearing father Stephen.

*Troparion 3:* Like the sun didst thou shine forth in the assemblies of monastics, sanctified from earliest infancy;[^337] emitting as a radiant dawn the virtues of thy life, O sweetest Stephen, God-bearing venerable father.

*Theotokion:* Discerning from afar with farsighted eyes, the prophets clearly foretold that thou wast to be the Mother of the Creator and Master of all. Therefore, O all-hymned one, do we confess thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны̀):*

O arch-strategist of God, encamp beneath the shelter of Thy wings; protect me who flee unto thee, and at my departure deliver me from the bitter exactors,[^338] and save me by Thy supplications.

*Theotokion:* God hath given thee unto us, O pure one, as a refuge and strength and help in our afflictions and tribulations; therefore deliver all of us from our perils.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 5:*

Where thy grace overshadows, O archangel, from thence is the power of the devil driven away; for the fallen Lucifer cannot bear to look upon thy light.[^339] Therefore we pray thee: quench by thine intercession his fiery darts hurled against us, delivering us from his snares, O praiseworthy Gabriel, arch-strategist!

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding of old her Lamb and Son lifted up upon the Cross, the Virgin Mother and all-blessed Maiden cried out weeping: "Woe is me, O my Son! How dost Thou die, Who by nature art immortal God?"

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos, on 4; and of the Archangel, Ode 3, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: He makes His angels spirits, [and His ministers a flame of fire] (Ps. 103:4 LXX). Verse: Bless the Lord, O my soul (Ps. 103:1 LXX). Epistle to the Hebrews, section 305 [Heb. 2:2–10]. Alleluia, Tone 5: Praise the Lord, all ye His angels (Ps. 148:2 LXX). Verse: For He spake, and they came to be (Ps. 148:5 LXX). Gospel according to Luke, section 51 [Lk. 10:16–21]. Communion Hymn: He makes His angels [spirits] (Ps. 103:4 LXX).*

*Month of the same, on the 14th day. Holy Apostle Aquila.*

*On this same day is sung the service of Cyricus and Julitta together with the Apostle.*

*Stichera of the Apostle, 3, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron 1:* The great Paul, the sun of the universe, O divinely-wise one, did send thee, O blessed Aquila, like a bright ray into the inhabited world, to enlighten by the bright splendors of thy words those who were perishing of old in the night of ignorance.

*Sticheron 2:* Thy pure heart, richly illumined by the bright beams of the divine Spirit, O divinely-speaking Aquila, was shown forth truly as light-bearing; and by divine grace it destroyed the fierce darkness of the pagans.

*Sticheron 3:* Those who flee unto thy divine temple and pray unto thee in it with faith, O Aquila, are delivered from all manner of diseases, and perils, and tribulations, through thy sacred supplications and intercessions before God, O consecrated one.

*Other Stichera of the Martyrs, 3, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron 1:* With mature mind, though immature in body, O martyr Cyricus, thou didst manly-mindedly put to shame the childish-minded tyrant;[^340] therefore, examining thee severely at the tribunal, he inflicted death upon thee, who wast mindful of the life that waxes not old, into which thou hast entered, bedewed with blood unfading unto ages.

*Sticheron 2:* Like a fruitful vine watered by spiritual irrigations, O glorious Julitta, bringing forth Cyricus who sprouted from thy side thou dost bring him to be slaughtered, and truly pressed in the winepresses of martyrdom; with whom having poured forth the wine of compunction, ye gladden the hearts of those who celebrate your memory in faith.

*Sticheron 3:* Drawing near unto wounds, thou didst truly endure the scraping of thy body, O all-marvelous one; and beholding with thine eyes the end of thy son, thou didst pass through a twofold martyrdom, O Julitta. Therefore the Umpire of the contest grants thee also twofold crowns, mightily bestowing victory upon those who suffer.

*Glory... Tone 6:*

Come and see, all ye people, a strange and all-glorious sight: who hath seen a child of three years putting the tyrant to shame? O the wonder! The mother shone, and he cried aloud unto his nurse: "Fear not, O my mother, the torments of the fierce ruler of this world; for Christ is the strength of those who believe in Him!"

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*

When the most lawless people unjustly nailed Thee to the Tree, O Savior, Thy Virgin and pure Mother, beholding it, was wounded in her womb, even as Simeon foretold.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 8:*

Being three years old, he proclaimed the Trinity; and sucking milk, he strengthened his mother: "Cease, O my mother, from weeping tears; the Creator looks down from on high, and He will save our souls!"

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что́ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*

"I cannot bear, O Child, to see Thee asleep upon the Tree, Who bestowest wakefulness upon all, that Thou mightest grant divine and saving wakefulness unto those who of old fell asleep with the sleep of perdition through the fruit of transgression," cried the Virgin weeping; whom we magnify.

*Troparion of the Apostle, Tone 3:*

O holy Apostle Aquila, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Glory... Of the Martyrs, Tone 4:*

Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received imperishable crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they put down the tyrants and shattered the powerless audacity of demons. By their supplications save our souls!

*At Matins, one Canon of the Octoechos; and of the Apostle, on 4. Its acrostic: "I hymn glorious Aquila with love." A composition of Joseph. Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Come, ye peoples, let us sing a hymn unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people *Ode 1. Heirmos (continued):* whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt; for He is glorified.

*Troparion 1:* Let us hymn the sacred Aquila with sacred songs, and let us cry aloud: By his prayers, O Compassionate One, save all, as Thou art supremely good.

*Troparion 2:* O blessed one, thou hast made thyself a light-bearing vessel of the Spirit, being illuminated by His radiant beams; therefore hast thou enlightened those who were in darkness.

*Troparion 3:* Having fenced thy mind with the law of Christ, thou didst put down all the raging of the lawless; and thou didst destroy all the idol temples, having suffered, O divinely-blessed one.

*Theotokion:* By the word, O pure one, thou didst conceive in thy womb the Word, Who created all things by His word. Therefore with divine words do we hymn thee, O full of God's grace!

*Another Canon of the Martyrs, on 4. Its acrostic: "I sing Cyricus with her who bore him martyr-like." A composition of Joseph. Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having traversed the water as dry land, and escaped from the malice of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!

*Troparion 1:* Having proclaimed the Word, manifestly born of the Father and incarnate of the Mother, ye became witnesses of His passions by passions and death.

*Troparion 2:* The glorious martyr, like a radiant moon, united with the sun that was born of her, Cyricus: they enlighten all creation with marvelous rays.

*Troparion 3:* By paradoxical deeds[^341] hast thou exchanged those things which are in nowise mutable, O martyr; and carrying thy son in thine arms, with him thou didst darken the deceit of the lawless and of the devil.

*Troparion 4:* Being of glorious lineage, yet by martyrdom and manifold sufferings, O martyr Julitta, thou didst become a daughter of the immortal King.

*Theotokion:* Death is abolished because of thee, and the sting of hades is destroyed, O pure Mother; for thou hast given birth in the flesh to the immortal Master Who was slain.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst slay sin; and plant the fear of Thee in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst intensely desire God, the only Master and King, O glorious one, as an adherent; and His divine will hast thou fulfilled with an unwavering heart.

*Troparion 2:* By the power of the precious and divine Spirit, O all-praised Aquila, thou didst cast down all the power of the deceitful one, having valiantly suffered, O most consecrated one. With thy pure mind contemplating the glory of the Lord, thou didst partake of it perfectly, O blessed one, having departed from temporal things unto those that abide.

*Theotokion:* The one Lord, having known thee alone as purer than the saints, purer than all creation, O all-undefiled one, took flesh from thy pure blood.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 1:* Ye were accounted worthy of heavenly bridal chambers, of radiant rest, and of eternal glory, O glorious martyrs, having destroyed the stings of the devil by the pains of suffering.

*Troparion 2:* Like a fair nestling, thou wast likened unto this chaste dove, O glorious Cyricus, opposing the deceitful one when he deceitfully approached, striving to steal thee who art invincible.

*Troparion 3:* The proud serpent, who set his mouth against heaven and boasted to destroy the earth, did an innocent child utterly cast down and destroy by the panoply of the Cross.

*Theotokion:* Not emptying the Father's bosom, O all-pure one, the Word Who gave substance to all things took up His abode in thy most undefiled womb; and He became flesh without change, and being pre-essential, He deified men.

*Kontakion of the Apostle, Tone 4:*

Having acquired thee as a great sun, O glorious Aquila, the Church enlightens by the splendors of thy teaching those who faithfully honor thee, O apostle of the Lord.

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

Having illumined thy soul with the voices of Paul, thou didst traverse as a sun with the light of the knowledge of God, O blessed Aquila; and thou didst lawfully weave the crown of martyrdom.
