Therefore dost thou pour forth rivers of healings unto those who celebrate thy memory in faith, O blessed one.

*Glory... Of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*

Truly a threefold-named martyr and preacher wast thou revealed, having suffered with thy mother, O glorious Cyricus of Christ;[^342] having finished the course of piety, thou wast revealed unto us as the courage of faith. Having trampled down all the power of the enemy, thou didst receive from heaven a crown together with thy mother, O glorious one. Pray ye unto Christ that all who celebrate your memory may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

By tempestuous surges of passions am I tossed about, who am without conscience, O pure one; I call upon thee fervently: despise me not, an accursed one, that I perish not, for thou hast given birth to the abyss of tender mercy; for besides thee I have no hope, lest I, having trusted in thee, should be seen as a laughingstock and mockery unto enemies; for thou canst do whatsoever thou wilt, as being Mother of the God of all.

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding upon the Cross the Lamb born of her without seed, pierced with a spear, the Virgin and Ewe-lamb, wounded with the darts of grief, cried aloud in sorrow: "What is this new mystery? How dost Thou die, Who alone art the Lord of life? Therefore arise, raising up our fallen forefather!"

*Canon of the Apostle, Ode 4. Heirmos:* I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 1:* Those who slept with the slumber of the deceitful one's impiety, O Aquila, hast thou raised up unto the light of piety.

*Troparion 2:* The proud rage of the enemy hast thou cast down, O Aquila, having armed thyself with divine humility.

*Troparion 3:* Blessed-mindedly didst thou suffer, having shed thy blood for Christ, a priest and witness of His passions.

*Theotokion:* We name thee spiritual palace and lofty throne of the King, O Birthgiver of God, Sovereign Lady!

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 4. Heirmos:* I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion 1:* An infant in body, yet of mature mind, O martyr, thou didst cast down the originator of evil, who worked wicked deceit.

*Troparion 2:* Like a fruitful vine, O martyr Julitta, hast thou sprouted a cluster, the truly noble Cyricus, dropping the sweetness of martyrdom.

*Troparion 3:* Looking tenderly, like an ewe-lamb upon her lamb the martyr, the all-marvelous Julitta, as he was slaughtered with a fervent heart.

*Theotokion:* The laws of nature, O all-pure one, are altered beyond nature in thy birthgiving; for beyond mind and word hast thou brought forth the Creator.

*Canon of the Apostle, Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord: direct us in the light of Thy commandments, for besides Thee we know no other God.

*Troparion 1:* Ever pouring forth the sweet word of piety, O all-wise one, thou didst sweeten the souls coarsened by bitter deceit, as a consecrated initiate of mysteries, O all-praised Aquila.

*Troparion 2:* The divinely-speaking Luke truly wrote thy praises; for having been a disciple of the sacred Paul, and a divine guide of strangers,[^343] he was manifestly instructed in divine things.

*Troparion 3:* Having found the sacred Paul like a bright star emitting radiant beams, the precious preaching: from his ray didst thou receive words, and thou becamest light.

*Theotokion:* Mother and handmaid wast thou of Christ Who was incarnate for our sake; Him do thou ever fervently entreat, that He save us who call thee the pure Birthgiver of God.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? Nay, turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee!

*Troparion 1:* While thy body was torn and dismembered by wounds, O divinely-wise one, thou didst put off the grossness of corruption; and thou didst clothe thyself in the garment wherewith the Only-begotten, having borne flesh of the Virgin, clothed men, having deified them.

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast stripped naked in body, O all-glorious martyr, having surpassed bodily shame; and having patiently and manfully contended, O Julitta, thou didst clothe in eternal shame the enemy who of old stripped Eve naked.

*Troparion 3:* Having been beheaded, O martyr, thou didst crush the head of the serpent. Deprived of wealth, thou didst inherit heavenly wealth, having received an unshakable kingdom with all the martyrs, O all-praised one.

*Theotokion:* God the divine Word took intelligent and animate flesh from thee, O all-pure one; and in His tender mercy He became man, and deified me who was cast off through transgression. Pray Him to save all.

*Canon of the Apostle, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy tender mercy: lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion 1:* Grace was poured out upon thy lips, O blessed and consecrated one; therefore the Lord of all hath blessed thee unto the ages, O divinely-inspired Aquila.

*Troparion 2:* Thy prayer became an alteration of diverse diseases and a purification for sinning souls, O God-bearing blessed Aquila.

*Troparion 3:* Thy goings, divinely directed and guided unto the ways of God, were shown forth as guidance and a saving path for the wandering.

*Theotokion:* O all-undefiled Virgin named after the Lord,[^344] who didst give birth to God, the Bestower of good things: save from manifold perils those who ever hymn thee.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my griefs; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and I pray like Jonah: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion 1:* Having navigated the turbulent water of immeasurable torments by the piloting of the Cross, O all-praised ones, ye manifestly reached the tranquil haven, having drowned the invisible Pharaoh in the streams of your blood.

*Troparion 2:* The city of Iconium that reared thee leaps for joy, and Tarsus is adorned by thy precious memory, O Julitta; for in it didst thou finish thy martyrdom with thy son, and wast accounted worthy of the crowns of victory.

*Troparion 3:* Thou didst despise the intricate torments, and wast revealed as a most fair turtledove, having flown upward with thy nestling and soared above all the snares of the serpent, O all-praised one, and hast most wisely rested in the heavenly mansions.

*Theotokion:* With the root-cleaving sickle of thy supplications, O Maiden, cut down the wicked thoughts of my soul, and show it forth fruitful, I pray thee; for thou hast given birth to God, the Creator and Savior of all.

*Kontakion of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*

Bearing Cyricus in her embrace, the martyr of Christ Julitta manfully rejoiced in the contest, crying aloud: "Christ is the boast of martyrs!"

*Ikos:* Enlighten my mind, O Christ, with the light of Thy commandments, that I may be able to hymn the saints and recount their contests: for what tongue can tell the sufferings which they endured? Therefore I fall down before Thee, O Lover of mankind: grant remission unto my wretched soul; grant me time for repentance: for unto this end didst Thou willingly take flesh, that Thou mightest lead all unto life; which having attained, the sufferers unceasingly sing: "Christ is the boast of martyrs!"

*Canon of the Apostle, Ode 7. Heirmos:* When the golden image was worshipped in the plain of Dura, Thy three children took no heed of the godless command; but cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers! O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Having woven for thyself a garment of incorruption by the divine Spirit, thou wast all-comely; and thou didst strip the enemy naked, and didst clothe in a radiant garment and saving light those who had been stripped naked by him, O Aquila.

*Troparion 2:* People held fast by spiritual hunger and in distress didst thou nourish with the bread of words, O all-marvelous and divinely-wise one, and madest them partakers of the heavenly banquet, having become a God-beholding sacred initiate of mysteries.

*Troparion 3:* Those who flee unto thy holy temple receive health, and are released from passions, and are cleansed from diseases, hymning thee with love and crying aloud, O all-praised one: "Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!"

*Theotokion:* Adam of old was slain by the corrupting food, O all-undefiled one; but thou, having given birth to our Life, hast restored him to life, O Virgin. Therefore, hymning thee as the cause of good things, we cry aloud: "Blessed art thou who hast borne God in the flesh!"

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 7. Heirmos:* The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion 1:* With clubs did the lawless one shatter thy body, O divinely-wise Julitta; yet he shook not the firmness of thy resolve: for thou didst hold the Cross of Christ as a rod of strength.

*Troparion 2:* By downpours of bloody floods did the sufferers of Christ quench the fire of godlessness, crying fervently: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!"

*Troparion 3:* Bearing in thine arms the one whom thou didst bear, O martyr, thou didst stand before the judgment seat, and didst finish a twofold good contest, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!"

*Theotokion:* Following thy words, all generations call thee blessed, O Maiden, who wast shown forth as Mother of the blessed God, Who hath made blessed those who believe in Him.

*Canon of the Apostle, Ode 8. Heirmos:* Hymn God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and turned the flame into dew, as Lord, ye works, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* Joyfully living in the heavens with the divine disciples and servants and eyewitnesses of the Word as a disciple, O Aquila, and divinely deified, remember those who hymn thee with faith and love.

*Troparion 2:* The streams of thy sweats pour forth unto all of us rivers of divine miracles, O blessed one: and ever dry up the streams of demonic passions, and drown the hosts of the deceitful one.

*Troparion 3:* Ever deified by approaching, as drawing near unto God with immaterial mind, thou art all light, having been released from the flesh, and thou seest what the angels see, O glorious Aquila, hieromartyr of Christ.

*Theotokion:* Having conceived in thy womb God, the Cause of all, Who is truly above every cause, O pure one, and having given birth unto Him Who appeared for our sake according to our nature, O all-undefiled one: pray Him on behalf of those who hymn thee.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 8. Heirmos:* Having become victors over the tyrant and the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the children cried: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion 1:* The bright stars, which were unfailingly set in the firmament of the Church and have enlightened the earth with miracles, are piously honored today by all who hymn Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion 2:* Like a young branch of a divinely-planted root, O divinely-wise one, carrying thy precious son, thou didst cause the gardens of impiety to wither, crying out with him and saying: "We supreme-exalt Thee, O Christ, unto the ages!"

*Troparion 3:* Like a young heifer, a sacredly-fair young maiden,[^345] O Julitta, bearing the glorious Cyricus, didst thou offer a living sacrifice unto the Lamb Who arose from the Virgin and was slaughtered for His surpassing tender mercy.

*Theotokion:* The Word, incarnate from thy most pure blood, O Maiden, by divine knowledge hath deified those who were bent down unto irrational fleshly passions, O pure one; Whom we supreme-exalt unto all ages.

*Canon of the Apostle, Ode 9. Heirmos:* God the Word from God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, and Who was ineffably incarnate of the holy Virgin for our sake: Him with one accord do we the faithful magnify in hymns.

*Troparion 1:* Having assembled, O Aquila, with sacred hymns do we with one voice bless thee, who becamest a sacred and precious vessel of the Word, and His all-wise preacher, and a light of the world, and the confirmation of our faith.

*Troparion 2:* As a destroyer of deceit, and a teacher of the nations, and a divine sufferer of the Savior, and a fellow citizen and companion of the holy angels, O divinely-speaking Aquila: with them entreat the Master to save our souls!

*Troparion 3:* Having known thee to be a disciple of Paul, O Aquila, and an unerring beacon to the world, and a sacred martyr, a destroyer of idols, and an introducer of the knowledge of God: with pious mind do we call thee blessed, O divinely-wise one.

*Troparion 4:* Thy radiant memory, having shone forth upon the world, like the sun enlightens the souls of those who piously hymn thee; in which entreat, O blessed one, that forgiveness of sins be granted, and profound peace, and great mercy unto all.

*Theotokion:* Foreseeing thee as a radiant lampstand, the prophet saw thee, O Virgin Mother, bearing the Light that shone forth from Light,[^346] Who richly enlightens those who were formerly darkened, with the beams of His ineffable Godhead, O all-undefiled one.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 9. Heirmos:* Every ear was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, from the virginal womb, having become man; therefore do we the faithful magnify the most pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion 1:* The shrine of the glorious sufferers offers healing, being watered by the grace of the divine Spirit: come, draw forth, and sanctify your souls, and be cleansed from diseases, ye lovers of the martyrs, magnifying the Giver of good things.

*Troparion 2:* Like a sweet-briar did ye sprout forth in the valleys of martyrdom, O all-blessed ones, like a fragrant lily, as a divine growth of an orchard, truly dripping with myrrh as an adornment; and ye gladden the souls of the faithful with mystical fragrance.[^347]

*Troparion 3:* With martyric wounds did ye adorn yourselves, O noble-victorious martyrs, and having clearly become like unto the angels, ye stand before God, radiantly encircled with the crown of victory, asking forgiveness of sins for those who honor you.

*Troparion 4:* The martyress like a ewe, bearing her son like a lamb, passed unharmed through the midst of wolves, and settled in the heavenly fold. Continually by their supplications, O Lord, save us who honor their sacred memory!

*Theotokion:* O portal of the Light, enlighten my soul, blinded by passions, and darkened by evil thoughts, and imperiled; and deliver me from tribulations, perils, and afflictions, that I may glorify thee, the hope and confirmation of the faithful.

*Exapostilarion of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Of the Martyrs, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*

Rejoice, be adorned and dance for joy, radiantly celebrating, O city of Iconium! For from thee shone forth two all-wise beacons: the glorious Julitta, and the divinely-wise Cyricus; for having suffered lawfully, they received crowns of glory.

*Both now... Theotokion:* We hymn thee as Mother and Virgin, O all-undefiled one; for thou didst bear in thy womb the God of all, Whom we preach as co-unoriginate, without division in hypostases,[^348] confessing thee truly as the Birthgiver of God, we who are saved for thy sake.

*At the Praises, Stichera, on 4. Tone 1. Idiomela. By Germanus:*

*Sticheron 1:* Let us worthily praise the good sufferer and preacher of the faith, together with his divinely-wise mother; for having patiently suffered, they manly-mindedly cast down the author of evil[^349] by the power of the Cross. Therefore they received crowns from God, the Umpire of the contest, and unceasingly entreat Him with boldness, to save those who celebrate their sacred suffering with faith. *(Twice)*

*Tone 6:*

*Sticheron 2:* O martyr Cyricus, filled with grace from swaddling bands, possessing a mature understanding in the victory of thy contests:[^350] pray with thy mother unto Christ God to grant peace unto the world, as a great confessor of the Trinity!

*Sticheron 3:* An infant didst thou appear among the martyrs, yet wast shown forth as mature in understanding; therefore having received the unoriginate Word, O praised one, thou didst not fear the fire of the lawless. With thy mother entreat the Creator, that as God He may save our souls!

*Glory... Same Tone [Tone 6]. By Byzantios:*

Come, ye faithful, having assembled, with praises let us crown the holy pair who uphold honor unto the Trinity; for they trampled underfoot with their feet the deceit of idols and the device of the torturers. Praising them, let us the faithful leap for joy, saying: Rejoice, O all-honorable Julitta, who didst cast off womanly weakness and manly-mindedly contest! Rejoice, O all-blessed Cyricus, three years old in body, who didst cast down the wily enemy! Rejoice, our boast and glory, of us who celebrate in faith your sacred suffering; whom we beseech ever to entreat the Lord of all, to grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "O when from the Tree" (Ѽ гдⷭ҇а ѿ дре́ва):*

Beholding Thee nailed unto the Tree of the Cross, O Jesus, she who knew not wedlock weeping said: "O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who bare Thee, O unapproachable Light of the pre-unoriginate Father? Make haste and be glorified, and be glorified, that those who glorify Thy divine Passion may receive divine glory!"

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 4. By Anatolius:*

Rejoice, be adorned and be glad, O city of Iconium! For from thee sprouted forth glorious fruit: Julitta the all-fair and noble-victorious martyress, and from her the honorable child Cyricus, named after the Lord. For having manly-mindedly trampled underfoot all the craft of the torturing enemy, they worthily received crowns of victory, preaching unto the peoples to honor and worship the all-holy Trinity. Therefore let us also cry aloud with boldness unto Christ our God Who glorified them, to pacify the world and save our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

When she who bare Thee, as a ewe, beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she lamented and maternally cried out: "O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How were Thy hands and Thy feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?"

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos; and of the Martyrs, Ode 3, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel (Ps. 67:36 LXX). Verse: In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel (Ps. 67:27 LXX). Epistle to the Corinthians, section 154 from the half [2 Cor. 4:6–15]. Alleluia, Tone 4: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise (Ps. 8:3 LXX). Gospel according to Luke, section 51 from the half [Lk. 10:19–21]. Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous (Ps. 32:1 LXX).*

*Month of the same, on the 15th day. Holy Martyrs Cyricus and Julitta. Their service was written before on the yesterday's day. And of the holy Equal-to-the-Apostles, Great Prince Volodymyr, named in holy baptism Basil.*

*At Vespers, Blessed is the man, the 1st antiphony. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron 1:* A second Constantine wast thou in word and deed; for he was born in a Christian time, and for many years in pagan- ism he dwelt; but thou wast born of the Varangians,[^351] yet didst love Christ Who loved thee, unto Whom thou hast ascended rejoicing: Whom cease not to entreat for those who honor thy memory.

*Sticheron 2:* Mount Sinai rejoices eternally, radiantly shining, having been overshadowed by the Mosaic law,[^352] having seen the Invisible One; and radiantly shining, thy great city rejoices and is glad, O Basil, not as in darkness, but as in the day beholding the Son glorified in its midst with the Father: Whom entreat to save and enlighten our souls!

*Sticheron 3:* As a father spiritually and a ruler physically,[^353] wast thou unto the Rus’ people, O Basil; and a true preacher, as an apostle of Christ, thou didst proclaim saving baptism, and having radiantly enlightened them thereby, thou didst sanctify thy people in all the regions of thy dominion, O blessed one!

*Sticheron 4:* A root of the true faith wast thou, O Basil, watered by the Holy Spirit, who didst bring forth for us divinely-planted branches that pour forth the flower of sweet fragrance: Boris the marvelous, and Gleb the zealous for piety, who bubble forth miracles abundantly for all the faithful; with whom standing before Christ, pray to save and enlighten our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*

Come, let us all assemble in the honorable memory of the Rus’ father and our instructor, Volodymyr; for he was born of the Varangians, and loved Christ Who loved him; unto Whom he ascended rejoicing, with his foremother Helen.[^354] For he taught all his people to believe and worship the one God in Trinity, and having destroyed the idols, he trampled them underfoot; and he sprouted forth for us his honorable shoots, Roman and David.[^355] Therefore we also, now radiantly honoring their memory with hymns in faith, celebrate with love: that they may entreat the Lord for us, and to grant unto our king victories over heathen enemies,[^356] and to pacify the whole world, and save our souls!

*Both now... Dogmatic Theotokion: The King of heaven (Tone 8). Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the Readings.*

*Reading from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 8: 1 Kings 8:22–23, 27–30 LXX):*

Solomon stood before the face of the altar of the Lord, before all the assembly of Israel, and having lifted up his hands toward heaven, he said: "O Lord God of Israel, there is no God like Thee in heaven above, and upon the earth beneath. For if heaven, and the heaven of heavens, do not suffice Thee, how much less this house which I have built to Thy name? Yet look down upon my prayer, O Lord God of Israel, to give ear unto the supplications and the prayer which Thy servant prays before Thee toward Thee today: that Thine eyes may be open toward this house day and night, toward this place of which Thou hast said: 'My name shall be there,' to give ear unto the prayer which Thy servant prays in this place by day and night; and to give ear unto the supplication of Thy servant and of Thy people Israel. And whatsoever they shall pray toward Thee in this place, do Thou also hear in the place of Thy dwelling in heaven, and do Thou act, and be merciful unto them."

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 61: Isa. 61:10–62:5 LXX):*

Let my soul rejoice in the Lord, for He hath clothed me with the garment of salvation, and with the robe of gladness hath He clothed me; as a bridegroom He hath placed a crown upon me, and as a bride hath He adorned me with beauty. And as the earth causing its flower to sprout, and as a garden causing its seeds to grow, so shall the Lord cause righteousness and joy to shine forth before all nations. For Zion’s sake I will not hold my peace, and for Jerusalem’s sake I will not give rest, until my righteousness go forth as a flower, and my salvation burn as a torch. And the nations shall see thy righteousness, and all the earthly kings thy glory; and they shall be called by thy new name which the Lord shall name. And thou shalt be a crown of beauty in the hand of the Lord, and a royal diadem in the hand of thy God. And thou shalt no more be called Forsaken, and thy land shall no more be called Desolate; for thy name shall be called My Pleasure, and thy land Inhabited; for the Lord hath been well pleased in thee, and thy land shall be inhabited. And as a young man dwelling with a virgin, so shall thy sons dwell with thee; and it shall come to pass, in like manner as a bridegroom rejoices over a bride, so shall the Lord rejoice over thee.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 60: Isa. 60:1–16 LXX):*

Shine, shine, O Jerusalem, for thy light is come, and the glory of the Lord hath risen upon thee! For behold, darkness shall cover the earth, and thick darkness upon the nations; but upon thee the Lord shall appear, and His glory shall be seen upon thee. And kings shall walk by thy light, and nations by the brightness of thy rising. Lift up thine eyes round about, and see thy gathered children: all thy sons shall come from afar, and thy daughters shall be borne upon shoulders. Then shalt thou see, and rejoice, and fear, and be astonished in heart, when the wealth of the sea, and of nations and peoples, shall turn unto thee. And herds of camels shall come unto thee, and the camels of Midian and Ephah shall cover thee; all from Sheba shall come bringing thee gold and frankincense, and they shall bring thee precious stone, and proclaim the salvation of the Lord. And all the sheep of Kedar shall be gathered unto thee, and the rams of Nebaioth shall come unto thee, and acceptable sacrifices shall be offered upon Mine altar, and the house of My prayer shall be glorified. Who are these that fly as clouds, and as doves with their nestlings? Zion is with Me; the isles waited for Me, and the ships of Tarshish among the first, to bring thy children from afar, and their silver and their gold with them, for the holy name of the Lord, and because the Holy One of Israel is glorified. And stranger sons shall build thy walls, and their kings shall stand before thee; for in My wrath I struck thee, and in My mercy I loved thee. And thy gates shall be opened continually, O Jerusalem, day and night, and they shall not be shut, to bring unto thee the power of the nations, and their kings led as captives. For the nations and kings that will not serve thee shall perish, and the nations shall be utterly desolated. And the glory of Lebanon shall come unto thee, in the cypress, and the pine, and the cedar together, to glorify My holy place; and I will glorify the place of My feet. And the sons of those who humbled thee and provoked thee shall come trembling unto thee, and all who provoked thee shall bow down to the tracks of thy feet; and thou shalt be called the city of the Lord, Zion of the Holy One of Israel. Whereas thou hast been forsaken and hated, and there was no one to help thee, I will make thee an eternal joy, a gladness unto generations of generations. And thou shalt suck the milk of the nations, and shalt eat the wealth of kings, and thou shalt know that I am the Lord Who saves thee, and the God of Israel Who delivers thee.

*At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple; then of the Saint, Tone 1:*

Today the memory of the pious prince Volodymyr shone forth like myrrh poured out; for having loved Christ and despised the idols, he raised up on earth churches unto Him Who was crucified for our sake: and in the heavens he received the kingdom and the crown.

*Glory... Tone 6:*

Come, let us all faithfully assemble at the honorable memory of the Rus’ father, our instructor Basil; for from the time he was born, he loved Christ Who loved him, unto Whom he also ascended rejoicing, with his foremother Helen. Therefore we also affectionately celebrate, praying that he entreat the Lord for us, to pacify the world, and save our souls!

*Both now... Dogmatic Theotokion: The Creator and Deliverer (Tone 6).*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*

*Sticheron 1:* O most marvelous wonder! A proud understanding is destroyed today, and all the deceitful demons lament, seeing the wild branches cut off by the power of God,[^357] and the great Basil, our faithful leader, planted by God and glorified, and radiantly crowned by God!

*Verse: I have exalted one chosen out of My people (Ps. 88:20 LXX).*

*Sticheron 2:* A marvelous deep of miracles! For by understanding, those of hard heart who formerly raged in vain, today before the face of Basil rejoice in the honorable Church; and Christ God reigns, having found him as Paul of old, and set him as a faithful prince in His land, who enlightened his people with honorable baptism!

*Verse: With My holy oil have I anointed him (Ps. 88:21 LXX).*

*Sticheron 3:* Rejoice, O boast of Rus’! Rejoice, O ruler of the faithful! Rejoice, O divine Volodymyr, our leader! Rejoice, rampart of the faith! Rejoice, most glorious wonder of wonders, and calm haven for those who run unto thee! Rejoice, all-holy rock of the faith, and suppliant for those who hymn thee and faithfully magnify thee!

*Glory... Tone 8:*

Come, ye Rus’ assemblies, having gathered together today, let us praise the great Volodymyr, the leader of piety, the preacher of the faith, and the supreme among the Rus’ princes, equal to the apostles; let us crown him with praises and spiritual songs, saying: Rejoice, most valiant soldier of Christ, who didst utterly destroy the tormenting enemy, and delivering us from his deceit, didst bring us unto Christ God the King! But, O all-blessed and all-praised one, pray that peace, and health, and firm dominion be granted unto our king, and victories over the heathen,[^358] and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion: O Lady, accept the supplications of thy servants (Tone 8).*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Thou wast like unto a merchant seeking good pearls, O gloriously-ruling Volodymyr, seated upon the height of the throne of the mother of cities, divinely-saved Kyiv; inquiring and sending unto the Imperial City to learn the Orthodox faith; and thou didst find the priceless Pearl, Christ, Who chose thee as a second Paul, and shook off blindness, both spiritual and bodily, in the holy font; therefore we celebrate thy falling asleep, being thy people. Pray that salvation be granted unto the leaders of thy Rus’ realm, the Christ-loving king, and the multitude of his subjects![^359]

*Both now... Theotokion: The mystery hidden from the ages (Tone 4).*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion twice; and the Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

As the leader and root of the faith, and the destroyer of idols, O blessed Prince Volodymyr, equal to the apostles, we cry aloud unto thee: Pray Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Guide us into the way of repentance, who have continually turned aside unto paths of evil, and have provoked the all-good Lord, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of despairing men, dwelling place of God!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*

Having been taught by the ineffable heavenly wisdom from God, thou didst richly attain unto the knowledge of His faith, and didst swiftly run unto His beauty. Therefore thou didst illumine the eyes of thy mind, and didst lift them up to the understanding of the pious faith. For this cause celebrating we say: Blessed is God Who hath enlightened thy heart with spiritual light, O radiant beacon Basil: Pray Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

The darkness of sin covers me, and my shameful deeds without restraint ever torment me, and the scourging of conscience unceasingly tortures me: Woe is me! Where now shall I hide? Unto whom shall I flee to find help again? Only unto thee, O Mother of God, do I fall down, and I pray thee: Pray continually that our souls may be saved!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*

A swift and firm zeal didst thou acquire toward the true faith of Christ; and having found the divinely-contemplative and spiritual font, thou didst swiftly run in love, and having cleansed the defilement of transgressions, thou didst sanctify thy people: Pray Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 4:*

Anticipate quickly, O prince, instructor of the faithful; for the enemies boast and threaten us, O pleasure of Christ: Destroy by thy faith those who battle against us, that they also may learn to glorify thy memory; and save us who hymn thee from every wrath!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

We have known the Father's Word, Christ our God, Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, only pure one, only blessed one; therefore unceasingly hymning thee do we magnify thee!

*Then the Hymns of Ascent, the 1st antiphony of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: I have exalted one chosen out of My people (Ps. 88:20 LXX). Verse: For My hand shall support him (Ps. 88:22 LXX). Let every breath. Gospel according to John, section 36 [Jn. 10:9–16].*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 8:*

Having assembled, let us praise the emulator of the apostles, Volodymyr, the all-blessed father and teacher, through whom we have known Christ God; and drawing near from darkness unto light, we enlighten our eyes and hearts, dancing for joy at his memory and rejoicing together, having found him as an Orthodox guide into the heavenly kingdom!

*Canon of the Birthgiver of God with the Heirmos, on 6 (written on the 5th day of this month); and of the Saint two Canons, on 8.*

*Canon of the Saint, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:* The cleaved severed the unsevered, and the sun saw the land which it had not seen; the water drowned the fierce enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable; and the hymn was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion 1:* O co-unoriginate Word of God, Who of old wast the Creator of the four elements, and by them didst make the whole world: loose my soul bound by passions, that I may sing dancing for joy of the glorious Prince Basil!

*Troparion 2:* O Master, Who didst descend in fiery form immaterially upon the spiritual mountain: send down from above an abundant radiance upon the souls of those who ask of Thee, worthily to praise the marvelous Prince Basil!

*Theotokion:* Thou art the boast of mothers and virgins, O Lady, who didst give birth unto the Life and Creator of the world, yet didst not experience motherly pangs; but thou didst remain both Mother and Virgin; therefore praising thee, we cry unto thee: Rejoice!

*Another Canon [of the Saint], Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Israel, having traversed the deep on foot as upon dry land, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion 1:* Come, ye faithful, let us cry aloud a spiritual song, and let us glorify Christ, Who hath radiantly glorified the honorable Volodymyr, the great prince!

*Troparion 2:* O Thou Who givest seed to the sower, do Thou also grant a word unto me, O Giver of good things; loose for me the bond of my tongue, and renew me, O Savior, unto faithful doxology!

*Troparion 3:* O Thou Who callest all unto Thyself willingly and not by constraint, even as of old Thou didst call Moses and Elijah: so also now hast Thou called in the heart of the faithful and all-praised prince!

*Theotokion:* Adorned with the beauty of virtues, O pure Mother of God, thou didst conceive the true God, Who hath enlightened us with divine virtues.

*Katavasia: I will open my mouth (Tone 4).*

*Canon 1, Ode 3. Heirmos:* My heart is established in the Lord, my horn is exalted in my God; my mouth is enlarged over mine enemies, I have rejoiced in Thy salvation!

*Troparion 1:* Like a divinely-sounding trumpet did thy spiritual tongue sound forth, O blessed one, proclaiming unto all the ends of the Rus’ land the Orthodox baptism of Christ; by which having been enlightened, thou hast enlightened the whole world!

*Troparion 2:* Today thy most glorious memory shines forth, O Basil, which the Christ-named king celebrates, praising thee as his forefather, and likewise the people whom thou didst lead unto the spiritual Light![^360]

*Theotokion:* The shadow of the law passed away by thy birthgiving, O Birthgiver of God, when grace came, even the Word, Christ our God; Whom having known, the glorious prince radiantly adorned thy church!

*Another Canon [of the Saint, Canon 2], Ode 3. Heirmos:* There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession!

*Troparion 1:* O Thou Who didst enlighten Paul and make him chosen: so also now hast Thou cast off the disease of the eyes from Basil, the Rus’ father, at prayer, through Thy baptism![^361]

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast shown forth as an emulator of faithful Constantine, having received Christ into thy heart; and like a father thou didst teach the whole Rus’ land His commandments!

*Troparion 3:* Being strengthened by divine power, thou didst shatter godless Perun, the demonic idol, and tying him to a horse's tail, thou didst command the soldiers to break the idol to pieces![^362]

*Theotokion:* Having found the holy tabernacle, the Virgin Mary, the great Prince Volodymyr, the vessel of God and emulator of the Apostle Paul, raised up a divine church in her name![^363]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*

Continually watchful in prayers with a joyful soul, O Basil: for this cause thou didst draw forth the Spirit of wisdom from the Fountain that descends from on high. Therefore having shone forth in faith like the sun, praying to Christ unceasingly: Make haste, O Compassionate One, and hasten to save the world from transgressions! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O unwedded, pure Birthgiver of God, who without seed didst bear the Master of all: with the angels entreat Him, that we may be delivered from every perplexity! and to grant compunction and light to our souls, and cleansing of transgressions, O thou who alone art a swift protectress.

*Canon 1, Ode 4. Heirmos:* The Prophet Habakkuk foresaw with spiritual eyes Thy coming, O Lord; therefore he also cried aloud: God shall come from the south! Glory to Thy power, glory to Thy condescension!

*Troparion 1:* Bright and sweet was the heaven, the sun, after the cloud by which it was covered; joyful and glad is the spring after the sorrow of winter: and after the cloud of idol darkness, through baptism thou hast shone forth upon us like a radiant sun, O Basil.

*Troparion 2:* Paul the Pharisee, journeying unto Damascus, was blinded by a small flashing of the great light, but was enlightened through baptism; whereof thou becamest an emulator, O glorious one: having reached Cherson,[^364] thou didst drive away the darkness of the eyes.

*Theotokion:* Thou hast appeared, O Virgin, as an unwatered root that sprouted forth forgiveness, having borne the light-bearing Fruit, Christ our God: enlighten my soul languishing with passions, and entreat forgiveness of sins.

*Another Canon [Canon 2], Ode 4. Heirmos:* "Christ is my power, God and Lord," the honorable Church sings in godly manner, crying out from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion 1:* A fearful and most glorious and ineffable wonder does the Master Christ accomplish: for by divine baptism He renews the whole Rus' land, and enlightens Prince Basil.

*Troparion 2:* Come radiantly, and cry aloud in godly-fitting manner, ye Rus' sons, unto our father Volodymyr, and let us all faithfully celebrate his memory in radiant manner!

*Troparion 3:* The ancestral impiety didst thou cast off; and having cleansed the pious faith of thy foremother,[^365] thou didst love it, O great prince Volodymyr, equal to the apostles of Christ.

*Theotokion:* As the portal that leads unto the divine entrance of radiant paradise, as the spiritual place of sanctification, as the beauty of Jacob, we call the Virgin blessed.

*Canon 1, Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Lord our God, grant us peace; O Lord our God, possess us; O Lord, beside Thee we know no other, we name Thy name!

*Troparion:* Rejoice and dance for joy in spirit, O Rus' king Basil:[^366] for noble branches planted by God hast thou brought unto Christ, thy fruit nurtured by God, Boris the glorious, and Gleb the pious: with whom standing before Christ, pray for us!

*Troparion 2:* The marvelous Isaiah foretold in truth: In the last days the mountain of the Lord shall be manifest, and the house upon the tops of the mountains;[^367] and righteously upon thee have we understood the grace of the Spirit: for thou didst build a house unto the Birthgiver of God upon the tops of the mountains.[^368]

*Theotokion:* The marvelous Isaiah preaches of thee in the Spirit, that being a virgin thou shouldest bear Christ without seed; and the Virgin, having marvelously foreannounced thy greatness unto thy forefather: Save us, for He is merciful![^369]

*Another Canon [Canon 2], Ode 5. Heirmos:* By Thy divine light, O Good One, do Thou lovingly illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as true God, calling them from the darkness of sins.

*Troparion 1:* Today the angels of God radiantly rejoice in the heavens at Thy holy memory: for thou hast saved us from every malice, and from demonic sacrifices; many people hast thou delivered, and the whole Rus' land.

*Troparion 2:* The demonic multitude and dark assembly laments, seeing the idols trampled upon the earth, which were raised up for the destruction of men, which by God's command thou, O Basil, hast shattered.

*Troparion 3:* Rejoice and be glad, O servant of Christ, great and wise prince, suppliant for our souls: for through thee have we all been delivered from demonic deceit; therefore crying aloud, we say unto thee: Rejoice!

*Theotokion:* Shining from afar with purity, having become the divine, all-honorable preparation of the Master: for thou alone wast shown forth as the sacred Mother unto God, bearing Him in Thine arms as an infant.

*Canon 1, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Like the waters of the sea, O Lover of mankind, am I tempest-tossed by the waves of this life; therefore like Jonah do I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Compassionate Lord!

*Troparion 1:* A zealot of piety, of glorious Emperor Constantine, wast thou, O Basil, who enlightened the Hellenic race through baptism; and by the spiritual laver thou hast most radiantly renewed thy people.

*Troparion 2:* Having sweetened thy soul with the love of Christ, and having taken intellectual wings, thou didst soar above idol darkness, and didst scatter the darkness of false belief, O blessed one, settling in the bridal chamber of the Savior, the God of all.

*Theotokion:* The Orthodox assembly of Rus' is of good courage today: and the assemblies of the orthodox, at the most radiant memory of Basil magnifying thy seedless childbearing, O Virgin: and diligently praying unto thy Son, they cry aloud: Thee do we magnify!

*Another Canon [Canon 2], Ode 6. Heirmos:* Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion 1:* By the power of God, Satan the destroyer of souls and his deceitful sacrifices are trampled underfoot: for Christ hath shown forth unto us a conqueror, Basil the faithful prince, to shatter him and cast him beneath our feet.

*Troparion 2:* Of old the Lord saved Israel from bondage by the hand of Moses; and the same Lord now by the hand of Basil the faithful prince hath saved all of us from idol deceit.

*Troparion 3:* Worthily do we cry aloud unto thee, and all exclaim: Rejoice, all-honorable and most blessed one! For under God we have thee as a deliverer, O Basil: despise not thy servants, but ever preserve us!

*Theotokion:* Through thee have we found salvation, O most pure and all-hymned Virgin; He Whom the angels desire to see in heaven is now seen in thy bosom as an infant, our God!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:* Having emulated the great Apostle Paul in gray hairs, O all-glorious Volodymyr, having left behind all strivings concerning idols like childish reasonings, as a mature man thou didst adorn thyself with the purple robe of divine baptism; and now standing in gladness before Christ the Savior, pray that the rulers of the Rus' realm and the multitude of their subjects may be saved!

*Ikos:* O Christ God, Who of old didst enlighten Israel by the Law of Moses, and didst enlighten the world by the revelation of Thy coming: Thou didst send Thy divinely-speaking disciples to preach throughout the nations Thy seedless birth from the Virgin, and didst command them to baptize in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. And seeing the Rus' land grown old in sin, Thou didst send Thy Spirit into the soul of stout-minded, glorious Volodymyr, to know Thee, Christ God, as the One of the Trinity; and by Thy baptism to enlighten Thine elect, even the people entrusted unto him by Thee, and to lead unto Thee those who cry aloud in faith: Deliver Thy heritage from adversary heathens, the Christ-loving king,[^370] and the multitude of his subjects!

*Canon 1, Ode 7. Heirmos:* Having quenched the burning power of the Chaldean furnace, through the image of the angel that descended, the youths cried aloud unto the Creator: Blessed art Thou and praised, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* The soul-destroying enemy stripped our forefather of the divinely-woven robe; but Jesus the unoriginate, having appeared in the flesh and cleansed it with the water of the Spirit, gave it unto me again: Whom having known, O glorious Volodymyr, thou art glad, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* Having kept the Mosaic Law, Daniel was counted worthy of the vision of God; and having trampled underfoot the ancestral idols, not as in darkness, but in greater glory, thou didst intellectually behold Christ with the Father and the Spirit; and enlightened by baptism, thou art glad, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* The enemy, having deceived thy first-created forefather, stripped him of the divine garment, O Virgin; but having come forth from thy pure and unwedded womb, my sweet Savior Jesus: upon Whom he rushed as upon Adam, but was broken against God, and terribly shattered. And unto thy Son do we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another Canon [Canon 2], Ode 7. Heirmos:* The angel made the furnace a dew-bearing one for the venerable youths, but the burning command of God persuaded the Chaldeans and the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Thou wast certified by the love of the new Helen, being the grandson of all-blessed Olga; and thou wast shown forth as a new Constantine the Great, crying unto Christ, O Basil: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* The fierce deceiver moved to wage war against Christians; but the most great Lord enlightens thee in Cherson,[^371] and counts thee worthy of divine baptism, and hath granted unto thee the heavenly kingdom.

*Troparion 3:* Thou didst drive away the outworn demonic deceit, as a great disciple of Christ; having renewed us, thou hast enlightened all of us to cry unceasingly unto the Lord: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* Out of all generations the eternal God chose thee before the ages; and in the last days having received flesh from thee, He appeared together as perfect God and man, O Ever-virgin.

*Canon 1, Ode 8. Heirmos:* O Thou Who coverest Thy chambers with waters, Who settest the sand as a boundary to the sea, and upholdest all things: the sun sings of Thee, the moon glorifies Thee, all creation brings a hymn unto Thee as the Creator of all, unto ages of ages.

*Troparion 1:* "The generation of the upright shall be blessed,"[^372] the divine lips proclaimed, which was fulfilled in thee, O blessed one: for a hundredfold and divinely-chosen seed hast thou brought unto Christ, the invincible martyrs,[^373] by whom thou didst enlighten the Rus' land; whom praising together with thee, we highly exalt Christ unto ages of ages.

*Troparion 2:* Truly the mother of all cities was shown forth as Kyiv, the city of thy realm, in which Christ was formerly glorified with the Father and the Spirit by thy foremother, and by thee, O blessed one; where thy valiant body lies radiantly in the church of the Lady:[^374] and we thy people, praising, sing of Christ unto ages of ages.

*Theotokion:* The unoriginate Radiance and the Father's Word did prophetic voices truly proclaim, that Emanuel, both God and man, the strong hope of the nations, was to be born incorruptibly of thee: Whom entreat as His Mother that we may be saved.

*Another Canon [Canon 2], Ode 8. Heirmos:* From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and didst consume with water the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* From godless idols hast thou delivered all of us; and unto Christ God hast thou brought us, O father of faithful princes, instructor of thy flocks that sing of Christ unto ages of ages.

*Troparion 2:* A new Constantine wast thou certified to be in all the Rus' land, O blessed Basil: for thou didst clarify the name of Christ, Whom we highly exalt unto ages of ages.

*Troparion 3:* Unto the eternal King dost thou now stand, and art crowned by His right hand: pray unto Him for thine unworthy servants, that we may sing of Him and highly exalt Him unto ages of ages.

*Theotokion:* A cloud of the Word art thou, O all-hymned one, and a sun-like lamp, a priceless chariot, virginal exaltation; thou abidest as a fertile and curdled mountain,[^375] O most pure Virgin, named of the Lord.

*Canon 1, Ode 9. Heirmos:* Heaven was struck with awe at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God!

*Troparion:* David was found of old as a mighty king for Israel, and he saved the people, casting down the foreign gods, and in the Spirit he preached the Son of God preached; and thou in the Trinity hast known God, O blessed Basil, Whom we magnify.

*Troparion 2:* Let the divinely-chosen people today joyfully celebrate thy memory in a hundredfold manner in the church of the Mother of God,[^376] which thou didst lovingly adorn in eternal remembrance of thy falling asleep, an earthly heaven: resting in which, thou awaitest the all-dread trumpet of the archangel, O blessed one; therefore with songs do we magnify thee!

*Theotokion:* Inflamed by the divine Spirit, all generations lovingly call thee blessed, O Birthgiver of God: for thou didst loose the curse of our foremother; and diligently do we sing of the Word of God: for having received Him without burning in thy womb, thou didst bear Him in the flesh. Unto Whom pray that thy faithful flock may be delivered from every necessity and sorrow.

*Another Canon [Canon 2], Ode 9. Heirmos:* It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts do we call thee blessed!

*Troparion 1:* Sweet like a palm tree, grown up high, and thine olive flower, fruitful branches: thou art a growth of the vine, bringing forth two ripe clusters of martyrs, honorable Roman and David.[^377]

*Troparion 2:* Choirs of noble princes stand today, bearing piety from thee, praising thee with the faithful people: O instructor and swift protector, remember all of us, that through thee we may find life in peace!

*Troparion 3:* Ye Rus' people, come, assemble in the honorable church of holy Volodymyr, named Basil, the all-blessed great prince, the most glorious pleaser of Christ; and receive life and salvation, grace and great mercy!

*Theotokion:* O Virgin, thou didst bear God upon earth; in the flesh there was an incorporeal union, in one hypostasis, and in two natures: that He may save all of us who faithfully bow down unto thee as the most pure Birthgiver of God.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Having enlightened the whole universe, ye were truly shown forth as beacons by piety and faith, O divinely-crowned Volodymyr, with thy glorious foremother Helen; and we also glorify Christ Who loved you, Who is marvelous in the saints.

*(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
In two wills and natures, O all-immaculate one, yet in one hypostasis dost thou ineffably give birth unto God, Who for our sake voluntarily became poor even unto crucifixion willingly, and Who hath granted unto us divine wealth, O Virgin Mother of God!

*At the Praises, Stichera, on 4. Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron 1:* A mighty weapon hast Thou given unto our prince, O Lord, even Thy precious Cross, by which he lived righteously upon the earth, having shone forth in piety; and he was counted worthy of the heavenly kingdom through Thy mercy: therefore do we glorify Thy loving dispensation, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls! *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:* O Lover of mankind, Thou hast given unto Thy pious pleaser Solomon's wisdom, David's meekness, and the apostles' Orthodoxy, as the King of kings, and the Lord of all that rule: therefore do we glorify Thy loving dispensation, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls!

*Sticheron 3:* Thou wast the first voluntarily to subject the purple robe unto Christ, O prince ever to be remembered, having known Him to be God, and as King and Benefactor of all, imposing thy victories upon every principality and power: hence for thee, O lover of Christ, has Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls, prepared a kingdom.

*Glory... Tone 2:* Not from men didst thou receive the calling, but like marvelous Paul; rather, O glorious one, Volodymyr, apostle from Christ God, thou didst receive it from on high: having protected thyself with the sign of the Cross, and being captured by it as a most good prey, by this thou wast shown forth as an invincible conqueror over visible and invisible enemies. Therefore we who are born of earth beseech thee, as a fervent suppliant, worthily honoring thy memory, with boldness to ask for us enlightenment, and cleansing, and great mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion.*

*Great Doxology; and the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes, from both Canons, Ode 3 of the 1st Canon, and Ode 6 of the 2nd Canon. Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Sing praises unto our God, sing praises (Ps. 46:7 LXX). Verse: All ye nations clap your hands, shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing (Ps. 46:2 LXX). Epistle to the Galatians, section 200 [Gal. 3:23–29]. Alleluia, Tone 6: I have exalted one chosen out of My people (Ps. 88:20 LXX). Verse: For My hand shall support him, and Mine arm shall strengthen him (Ps. 88:22 LXX). Gospel according to John, section 35 from the half [Jn. 10:9–16]. Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be (Ps. 111:6b LXX).*

*The same month, on the 16th day. The Holy Hieromartyr Athenogenes and his ten disciples.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Athenogenes the glorious, clad in the radiant garment of the priesthood, showed it forth as most pure through the staining of his blood; with which having entered into the holy church, appearing before the All-seeing One, and ministering unto Him with the immaterial ranks, being enlightened by divine communion: whom we call blessed.

*Sticheron 2:* Athenogenes the all-wise brought unto Christ through faith a choir of sufferers, who had suffered firmly and finished the course; with whom having conquered the multi-scheming enemy, he is received into the choirs of martyrs, deified by communion: and they ever pray for us who continually celebrate their sacred memory.

*Sticheron 3:* The divinely-chosen ten disciples, having cast down the swelling of passions by fasting contests, suffered and put the serpent to death, having gained the sacred instructor Athenogenes through divine power: by their supplications, O Compassionate One, save us who ever place our hope in Thee, the All-good!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Same Tone and Podoben:* Save me, O all-pure Lady, who ineffably didst give birth unto the Savior Christ: for thee alone have I acquired as a protectress, and an unassailable rampart, and a shelter and joy, and divine consolation of my soul; do thou therefore deliver me from the unsleeping worm, and from the everlasting fire, O Mother of Christ God!

*Stavrotheotokion, Same Podoben:* "What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? O Thou Who upholdest all creation, Thou art lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest, Who givest life unto all!" said the weeping Birthgiver of God, when she saw Him Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and man, lifted up upon the Cross.

*At Matins, the Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing the glory of Thy sufferers, O Christ. Joseph." Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion 1:* Now dancing for joy with the heavenly ranks, O worthy of glory, sacred one, save those who hymn thee in faith!

*Troparion 2:* Instructed by the Master's laws, O honorable assembly of Christ's sufferers: ye destroyed the counsels of the lawless!

*Troparion 3:* As an immaculate slaughter, O divinely-blessed one of whole burnt offering: by the fire of torments wast thou brought unto God for the sake of faith.

*Theotokion:* O unwedded one, above mind and word didst thou bear the pre-eternal Word, Who became flesh from thy blood.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish our mind and hearts, O Thou Who didst establish the heavens by a word, that we may hymn and glorify Thee unto the salvation of our souls!

*Troparion 1:* Having destroyed the fierce crafts of the enemy by indestructible love toward God, Athenogenes the martyr and priest, with his disciples, was counted worthy of glory.

*Troparion 2:* The divine ones blunted the arrows and stings of the enemy by patient resistance, and they enlighten the minds of all with their light-beaming rays.

*Troparion 3:* The senseless tormentor, having stretched them out with hard thongs, scraped them unsparingly, wounding them unceasingly; yet he shook not their thinking, which possessed a firmness beyond words.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast shown forth as higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O Birthgiver of God: for thou alone didst receive the uncontainable God into thy undefiled womb; therefore with hymns do all the faithful call thee blessed, O pure one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Having first pre-cleansed thy soul by abstinence, and secondly by suffering, and with a perfect mind, thou didst please God, having become a hierarch and martyr, O God-bearer: and thou didst bring an assembly of disciples unto the Master, with whom entreat Him diligently for us, O glorious one!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure, all-immaculate, and unwedded one, who alone didst bear the timeless Son and Word of God in time: entreat Him with the holy and honorable patriarchs, and martyrs, and prophets, and venerable ones, to grant us cleansing and great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword passed through thine all-holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God voluntarily crucified:[^378] Whom praying unceasingly, O blessed one, cease not to grant us forgiveness of transgressions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion 1:* Being above all mind by divine contemplation of the ascent unto God, the divinely-wise ones feared not bitter bodily wounds.

*Troparion 2:* Having become an acceptable preacher of Christ and a blameless hieromartyr, Athenogenes was counted worthy of divine grace.

*Troparion 3:* O martyrs, bearing witness to the incarnation of the Word, ye endured wounds and death, having passed over unto immortality.

*Theotokion:* Thou didst incarnate the transcendent One, equal in power and sharing the throne with the Father, O all-immaculate one, Who partook of earthly things through mercy.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Waking early we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us: for Thou art our God, beside Thee we know no other!

*Troparion 1:* Having fenced the wisdom of thy flesh with dispassion, O wonder-worthy one, thou didst depict the sufferings of the Dispassionate One.

*Troparion 2:* By divine pre-ordinances, glorious Athenogenes brought the choir of his disciples unto the Master.

*Troparion 3:* The Giver of light made thy memory radiant, O martyr, fulfilling thy requests for those who run unto thee in faith.

*Theotokion:* Knowing thee as Mother and Virgin, O all-holy one, in unison do we honor thee, and piously call thee blessed.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion 1:* Athenogenes received the martyric noble calling, and with him the choir of disciples was crowned: having fasted beforehand, and conquered the wiles of demons.

*Troparion 2:* The Master Christ makes thy precious memory radiant, fulfilling thy supplications unto Him, O blessed one. For behold, the hind brings her offspring unto thee by divine nodding.[^379]

*Troparion 3:* Thou wast shown forth as a spiritual paradise, O worthy of glory, having the tree of life in thy midst, and ever scenting all things with the fragrance of divine flowers.

*Theotokion:* As a spiritual ark didst thou bear the Lawgiver, as a light-bearing lampstand didst thou give birth unto the Light, as an unwatered root didst thou sprout forth the Cluster of life, O Birthgiver of God!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):* Having followed the Master's saying, as an excellent shepherd thou didst lay down thy life for the sheep of Christ, O hieromartyr Athenogenes. Therefore we praise thee, and with thee the ten-numbered choir of thy disciples, who suffered, guided by the fear of God and by thy teaching. Therefore the Master crowned you, who pleased Him, with His life-originating right hand; Whom entreat for all of us.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The Chaldean furnace, kindled with fire, was bedewed by the Spirit at the presence of God; the Children sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* By the streams of thy blood having quenched the flame of deception, by the fire of thy struggles thou didst burn up the matter of malice, O blessed one, crying aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* Having consumed the wantonness of the flesh by the pains of abstinence, and the service of deception by torrents of blood, let the martyrs be praised as soldiers of Christ!

*Troparion 3:* By the severing of heads at the unlawful command, and by the scraping of flesh in manifold tortures, like unshakable pillars ye were not shaken, O venerable martyrs.

*Theotokion:* Delivered because of thee from the first ancestral fall, O all-immaculate one, we cry aloud unto thee: "Rejoice!" and in faith we glorify Him Who through thee delivered us from corruption.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The God-speaking Children in the furnace, trampling upon the flame with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion 1:* Looking unto the divine illuminations, Athenogenes destroyed the deep darkness of idol deception, and became light.

*Troparion 2:* The choir of angels and of martyrs, of hierarchs and venerable ones, rejoices at the memory of the honorable passion-bearers, glorifying the Lord.

*Troparion 3:* Thou wast numbered among the martyric flocks with thy ten disciples, going forth unto martyrdom, O hierarch: with whom we faithfully call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:* Entreat the Word Whom thou didst bear beyond word, O Mary, Mother of God, for us who faithfully hymn thee and call thee blessed, O most pure one!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* That which was revealed on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush, the childbearing of the Ever-Virgin for our salvation as faithful, with unceasing hymns we magnify.

*Troparion 1:* Behold, today is the radiant day of the martyrs, flashing with grace and with divine brightness illumining the hearts of the faithful with brightness: come, let us draw forth enlightenment!

*Troparion 2:* As pure offerings and blameless lambs were ye brought, O martyrs, unto Him Who was slain for our sake, receiving from Him crowns of victory as from the Arbiter of the contest.

*Troparion 3:* Thy pains drop sweetness, O sufferer, and drive away every bitterness of passions from those who run with faith unto thy temple, and who celebrate thy saving memory.

*Theotokion:* Thou bearest in thine arms God Who upholds all things, thou feedest the Nourisher Who became like unto us, O pure one: Whom entreat to grant unto thy people deliverance from all dire afflictions.

*Be it known that on this 16th day, if Sunday occur, the service of the Holy Fathers is sung on this Sunday. If on Wednesday or Tuesday, their service is sung on the preceding Sunday. If on Thursday, their service is sung on the coming Sunday.*[^380]

*We celebrate the memory of the Holy Six Ecumenical Councils. But the saint that occurs is sung at Compline.*

*On Saturday at Little Vespers: Stichera of the Resurrection, and of the Birthgiver of God according to custom.*

*At Great Vespers: After the customary Psalm, we sing: "Blessed is the man," the entire Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, 4; and of the Fathers, 6. Tone 6:*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Ѿча́ѧнныѧ):*[^381]

*Sticheron 1:* Out of the womb wast Thou born before the morning star, from the Father without mother before the ages, even though Arius glorifies Thee as a creature and not God, audaciously confounding Thee, the Creator, with creatures, storing up for himself the fuel of eternal fire. But the Council which was in Nicaea proclaimed Thee, O Lord, as the Son of God, enthroned together with the Father and the Spirit. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:* The torn garment of Christ, rent by dogs' jaws, did ye wisely sew together, O honorable Fathers: being by no means able to endure to look upon His nakedness, as Shem and Japheth covered their father's nakedness of old.[^382] Ye put to shame the senseless Arius, named after wrath,[^383] and those of like mind with him. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 3:* The Macedonians and Nestorians, the Eutychians and Dioscorians, Apollinarians and Sabellians, and Severians—grievous wolves who came in sheep's clothing—ye drove far away from the Savior's flock like true shepherds, stripping the thrice-wretched ones bare of their sheepskins. Therefore we call you blessed. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Tone 6, Automelon (Самогла́сенъ):*
Let us praise today the mystical trumpets of the Spirit, the God-bearing Fathers, who sang in the midst of the Church a harmonious hymn of theology: the one unchanging Trinity, essence and Godhead; the overthrowers of Arius and champions of the Orthodox, who ever pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... The first Theotokion of the Tone.*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*
*Readings, three.*

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 14):*[^384]
When Abram heard that his nephew Lot had been taken captive, he numbered his own household servants, three hundred and eighteen, and pursued after them as far as Dan. And he fell upon them by night, he and his servants with him, and smote them, and pursued them unto Hobah, which is on the left hand of Damascus. And he brought back all the horses of Sodom, and he brought back his nephew Lot, and all his goods, and the women, and the people. And the king of Sodom went out to meet him, after his return from the slaughter of Chedorlaomer and of the kings that were with him, at the valley of Shaveh; this was the king's vale. And Melchizedek, king of Salem, brought forth bread and wine, and he was the priest of the Most High God. And he blessed Abram, and said: "Blessed be Abram of the Most High God, Who created heaven and earth; and blessed be the Most High God, Who hath delivered thine enemies into thy hands!"

*Reading from Deuteronomy (Chapter 1):*[^385]
In those days, Moses said unto the sons of Israel: "Behold, I have delivered the land before your face; go in and inherit the land which the Lord swore unto your fathers, unto Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, to give it unto them, and unto their seed after them. And I spoke unto you at that time, saying: I am not able to bear you alone. The Lord our God hath multiplied you, and behold, ye are this day as the stars of heaven for multitude. May the Lord God of our fathers add unto you, that ye may be a thousandfold more, and bless you, as He hath spoken unto you! And I took from you wise and knowledgeable and understanding men, and appointed them to rule over you, rulers of thousands, and rulers of hundreds, and rulers of fifties, and rulers of tens, and officers over your judges. And I commanded your judges at that time, saying: Hear the causes between your brethren, and judge righteously between a man and his brother, and the stranger that is with him. Ye shall not respect persons in judgment; ye shall hear the small as well as the great, and ye shall not be afraid of the face of man, for the judgment is God's.

*Reading from Deuteronomy (Chapter 10):*[^386]
In those days, Moses said unto the sons of Israel: "Behold, the heaven and the heaven of heavens belong to the Lord thy God, the earth also, with all that is therein. Only the Lord had a delight in your fathers to love them, and He chose their seed after them, even you, above all nations, on this day. Circumcise therefore the foreskin of your heart, and be no more stiff-necked. For the Lord our God is God of gods, and Lord of lords, a great God, mighty and terrible, Who regards not persons, nor takes reward. He doth execute the judgment of the stranger, the orphan, and the widow, and loves the stranger, in giving him food and raiment. Love ye therefore the stranger, for ye were strangers in the land of Egypt. Thou shalt fear the Lord thy God; Him only shalt thou serve, and to Him shalt thou cleave, and swear by His name. He is thy praise, and He is thy God, Who hath done for thee these great and glorious things, which thine eyes have seen."

*At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple.*

*Glory... Tone 3:* Sure guardians of apostolic traditions have ye been, O holy Fathers: for having piously preached the consubstantiality of the Holy Trinity, ye overthrew the blasphemy of Arius in council; with him having also refuted Macedonius the fighter against the Spirit, ye condemned Nestorius, Eutyches, and Dioscorus, as well as Sabellius and headless Severus.[^387] Entreat that we may be delivered from their deception, and we pray that our life may be preserved undefiled in the faith.

*Both now... Theotokion:* From the seed of the divine...

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 4:* The prayerful memory today of the God-bearing Fathers, who gathered from the whole universe in the radiant city of Nicaea, the assembly of the Orthodox, piously honoring, in faith do we celebrate. For these wisely and piously cast down the godless doctrine of fierce Arius, and in council expelled him from the Catholic Church; and clearly they taught all to confess the Son of God as consubstantial and co-eternal, existing before the ages, setting this forth clearly and piously in the Symbol of Faith.[^388] Therefore we also, following their divine commandments, firmly believing, minister unto the Son with the Father and the all-holy Spirit in one Godhead, the consubstantial Trinity.

*Both now... Theotokion:* Look upon the supplications...

*At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion:* Birthgiver of God and Virgin... *(Twice)*

*And of the Fathers, Tone 8:* Most glorified art Thou, O Christ our God, Who didst establish our Fathers as luminaries upon the earth, and through them didst guide us all unto the true Faith: O most Compassionate One, glory to Thee! *(Once)*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion, twice.*
*Glory... of the Fathers. Both now... Theotokion:* He Who for our sake was born...
*After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos.*

*Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Birthgiver of God on 2; and of the Fathers on 4. The composition of the holy Patriarch Lord Germanus.*[^389]
*Tone 6.*

*Canon 1, Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having walked as on dry land, Israel, with steps upon the abyss, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion 1:* Those who thundered forth everywhere from Nicaea as from a heaven, the Word of the living Father, showing His enemies stricken as by thunder: let them be honored with sacred words!

*Troparion 2:* Christ drove far away from His Church the evil spirit of the fighters against the Spirit by the Holy Spirit, having gathered them by the second action of the Council.[^390]

*Troparion 3:* Cyril, the president of the Council, drove away the deception of the fighter against Christ, Nestorius, clearly confessing the Virgin Mary to be the pure Mother of God.[^391]

*Theotokion:* Thou givest birth in two natures and wills, O pure one, unto Christ, the One of the uncreated Trinity, Who for thy sake wrought the union of men and angels.

*Another Canon of the Holy Fathers [Canon 2], Tone 8.*

*Canon 2, Ode 1. Heirmos:* He plunged the armed Pharaoh into the deep: of old the wonder-working rod of Moses, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, saved Israel the fugitive traveling on foot, as he sang a song unto God.

*Canon 2, Ode 1, Troparion 1:* The pious assembly of the Fathers, having truly gathered against Eutyches of old, preached the Savior in two unseparated natures, following and clearly remaining in the teaching of the divine Father Cyril.

*Troparion 2:* The number of six hundred and thirty most pious men,[^392] having cast down the deception of Eutyches and the heresy of Severus, attained to sing thus: For we preach Christ in two essences, following the saying of blessed Cyril.

*Troparion 3:* Whosoever preaches not the Word, the Son of the Father, in two natures and operations, shall receive anathema! For the Fourth Council of holy Fathers with one mind thus proclaimed: let us all therefore call them blessed!

*Theotokion:* Most glorious things were spoken of thee throughout generations of generations, who didst contain God the Word in thy womb, yet remained pure, O Virgin Mary, Mother of God! Therefore after God do we all honor thee as our protectress.

*Katavasia:* I will open my mouth...

*Canon 1, Ode 3. Heirmos:* There is none holy like unto Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession!

*Troparion 1:* Senseless Arius was shown forth as a servant of the creature, and again Macedonius was shown forth as abominable; equally with the pagans are they tormented in the fiery Gehenna.

*Troparion 2:* By the seven sacred Councils of the holy Fathers hast Thou made radiant the honorable Church, O Christ, as with the light of seven luminaries, driving far away the darkness of deception.

*Troparion 3:* The manifold fury of heretics destroys Thy flock, O Word; but the shepherds of Thy spiritual sheep changed this by their teaching.

*Theotokion:* Thou gavest birth, O all-pure one, unto the One of the uncreated Trinity, in two essences and wills: Whose honorable image we venerate, being filled with grace.

*Canon 2, Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Lord, Builder of the vault of heaven in the beginning, and Founder of the earth upon the waters: do Thou establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments, for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 1:* Those who shamelessly yielded unto the words of Severus, which were filled with death-bearing poison: be ye ashamed forever, having separated yourselves from the whole Church, like ravenous wolves and dogs!

*Troparion 2:* Confessors and faithful witnesses of the Creator and Savior, we honor Him as twofold in two unseparated essences and wills, and in operations we honor Him: therefore we utterly turn away from the deception of Severus.

*Troparion 3:* Let us manifestly reject the deception of Orindites,[^393] Severus, and unfortunate Jacob, and with them Theodosius and Dioscorus. But the Fourth Council of pious Fathers let us hymn with divine songs!

*Theotokion:* Higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim wast thou shown forth, O Birthgiver of God: for thou alone didst receive the uncontainable God in thy undefiled womb; therefore all we faithful with hymns call thee blessed, O pure one!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Resurrection; then:*
*Sessional Hymn of the Fathers, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Most radiant luminaries of Christ's truth did ye appear unto the world upon earth, in truth most blessed and divinely-speaking Fathers, having dried up the heresies of blasphemous babblings, and quenched the flame-like confusions of the ill-believing: therefore as saints of Christ, pray that we may be saved! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* Thou hast renewed by thy divine childbearing, O pure one, the mortal essence of those born of earth, which had corrupted in passions; and thou hast raised all up from death unto the life of incorruption: therefore as is meet do we all call thee blessed, O all-glorious Virgin, even as thou didst foretell!

*Canon 1, Ode 4. Heirmos:* "Christ is my power, God and Lord," the honorable Church sings in godly manner, crying out from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion 1:* Thine enemies raged, and they that hate Thee lifted up their head for a little while, O Savior; but straightway they fell, not enduring the voice of spiritual trumpets.

*Troparion 2:* Suns of the Sun, the Son and the Spirit doubly illumined from the Father, uncreated, co-unoriginate: the Father alone is believed to be the Cause of Both.

*Troparion 3:* Seven spirits indeed rested upon Christ, as Isaiah said;[^394] and upon seven Councils Christ rested, with the Father and the divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:* Thou didst bear God Who wore flesh from thine honorable blood, O pure Maiden: Whom the Fathers proclaimed as twofold in nature, yet one in hypostasis.

*Canon 2, Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the Father's bosom didst visit our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Canon 2, Ode 4, Troparion 1:* Say unto us, O senseless and snare-caught Severus: Is the light, the Father's Word and Son, one composite essence without beginning? For if thou sayest thus, thou hast designated another nature also. For the flesh and the Word are not one essence, but existing as twofold, O wretched one!
