*Troparion 2:* Having said "one nature of the Word," he brought in the incarnate nature of mankind, wholly without alteration and confusion. The teacher and bishop of the Alexandrians[^395] clearly taught two natures and wills unto those who desire to think piously.

*Troparion 3:* In two natures of Christ do we all preach Him without confusion, O ye faithful, straightway trampling down all the impiety of Eutyches and of senseless Dioscorus: we follow the definition of the holy Fathers, and the sayings of divine Cyril.

*Theotokion:* The Cherubic chariot art thou, O pure Mother of God; thou art the receptacle, thou the container of the Word and God of the Father, Who was clothed in flesh from thine all-pure womb: therefore, worshipping Him Who was incarnate of thee in two essences, unceasingly we glorify Him!

*Canon 1, Ode 5. Heirmos:* By Thy divine light, O Good One, do Thou lovingly illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as true God, calling them from the darkness of sins.

*Troparion 1:* The divine vineyard of Christ, which was brought out of Egypt,[^396] the beasts of dark separation and destruction devoured at first; but by the sling of the holy Fathers they were driven far away.

*Troparion 2:* Having enlightened their mind with the divine, thrice-radiant illumination, the honorable Fathers proclaimed Christ the Lord as the One of the honorable Trinity, twofold in natures and wills.

*Troparion (Triadikon):* In material things, as among the Seraphim, the Church is adorned by the wise Fathers with a threefold song, ever singing unto Thee a hymn of the Trinity: Thy Trinitarian one Godhead gathers it together.

*Theotokion:* Mother and handmaid of thy Son wast thou, O pure one: for He Who was from thee was before thee, as thy Creator: Whom we recognize in two essences, united by the hypostatic Word.

*Canon 2, Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Canon 2, Ode 5, Troparion 1:* O Severus, who grievously blendest together the sleep of Christ's natures, O transgressor of the Law: for all the beholders and most blessed teachers, having proclaimed Him in two essences and in one person of Christ, set this forth unto all.

*Troparion 2:* The unoriginate Word of the Father manifestly assumed human nature, as the Lover of mankind, desiring to show mercy upon us who had perished: therefore I proclaim Him in two essences and wills.

*Troparion 3:* The Fourth Council cast down Severus and Dioscorus, who blasphemed Christ; confirming exceedingly well the Tome of Leo, the primate of Rome,[^397] which indivisibly revealed the two natures of the Savior.

*Theotokion:* Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, despise not, we pray, a kin-like providence concerning us, since we Christians favorably offer thee alone unto the Master as a propitiation.

*Canon 1, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion 1:* "The God of gods, the Lord," David sang and spoke beforehand, and called from the east, from the ends of the whole earth even unto the west,[^398] prefiguring the Ecumenical Councils of the Fathers.

*Troparion 2:* The Wisdom of God established seven pillars,[^399] even Thy Church, O Master, guarded unswervingly from all heresies by the sevenfold Councils of the sacred Fathers.

*Troparion 3:* Let shame cover the face of Eutyches and Dioscorus, who prated of the confusion of Christ's natures: for not in phantasm, but in deification did He assume the nature of those born of earth.

*Theotokion:* Let Nestorius be taken away unto the dark assemblies of the Jews, and let his blasphemous tongue be cut off: for the Virgin Mary gave birth unto God, Who wore flesh for our sake!

*Canon 2, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion 1:* The two epistles of Cyril, which were sent of old unto the syncellus,[^400] when the East was prevailing, refute the entire deception of Severus, piously proclaiming Christ.

*Troparion 2:* Cyril proclaims Christ in two natures and twofold operations, cutting off the heresy of senseless Severus: therefore let us all remain in his teachings let us all remain.

*Theotokion:* As Virgin and pure and most glorious Mother of God, O Mary, do the pious truly proclaim thee: stopping the shameless mouth of Nestorius and the malice of Dioscorus.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As the first-fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
The preaching of the apostles and the dogmas of the Fathers sealed the one Faith for the Church: which also wearing the garment of truth, woven of the theology from above, uprightly defines and glorifies the great mystery of piety.

*Ikos:* With a lofty proclamation let us hear the Church of God crying aloud: "Whosoever thirsts, let him come unto Me and drink; the cup which I hold is the cup of Wisdom![^401] This drink of truth have I drawn forth with the word, pouring forth water not of contradiction, but of confession; drinking which, the Israel of today sees God speaking: 'Behold, behold, that I Myself am, and I change not; I am God the First, and I am after these things, and beside Me there is no other at all!'[^402] From hence they that partake shall be satisfied, and shall praise the great mystery of piety."

*Canon 1, Ode 7. Heirmos:* An angel made the furnace dew-bearing for the venerable Children, but the command of God burning the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Daniel indeed crushed the Babylonian dragon of old;[^403] but by the prayers of the Fathers the fierce Egyptian dragon burst asunder, even Arius manifestly devouring Christ's flock.[^404]

*Troparion 2:* With unpardonable impiety the wolf Macedonius was indignant, rushing against the Spirit Who deifies men and refashions all the faithful unto the first beauty by the laver of baptism.

*Troparion 3:* Dioscorus, Eutyches, and Severus, a triad of lekythoi,[^405] confounding and mingling the essences of Christ by intellectual confusion, rushed with audacity against the honorable Trinity.

*Theotokion:* The image of Thy bodily form do we piously kiss, O Master, and of Thy Mother, and of all the saints: knowing with righteous thought that the honor passes worthily unto the Prototype.[^406]

*Canon 2, Ode 7. Heirmos:* The fire was ashamed of the condescension of God in Babylon of old; therefore the Children in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Confessing Christ in two natures and operations, unconfusedly and unalterably, the deception of Severus we overcome: therefore unto Him Who endured suffering by the assumption of flesh, we cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* As One we understand Him Who was upon the Cross and in the Father's bosom: as God in the highest, and in the tomb as united with flesh; unto Whom we sing, crying out in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:* Arius's diminution and cutting off, who diminishes God, O ye faithful, and straightway Sabellius's confounding into one: having hated these enemies of the Trinity, unto the Trinity let us cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion (Triadikon):* The Lord indeed of all, but Father of the only Only-begotten Son, piously theologizing Thee, we proclaim Thee; and knowing Thy one proceeding Spirit: upright, connatural, and co-ever-existing.

*Canon 1, Ode 8. Heirmos:* From the flame Thou didst pour out dew for the venerable Children, and with water didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one;[^407] for Thou dost all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* As from one root, God-planted offshoots, the Son and the Spirit shone forth: for the Father is the sole Cause,[^408] timeless, equally honored with the timeless Persons.

*Troparion 2:* From an immaterial womb timelessly didst Thou shine forth, the Word consubstantial with the Father and the Spirit; but for our sake Thou didst enter in time into the material womb of the only Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion 3:* Arrows kindled with spiritual fire were plunged into the shameful hearts of the heretic foes, and choked them unto death, by the assemblies of the sevenfold-numbered[^409] and divine Fathers.

*Theotokion:* Not a duality of persons in Christ do we glorify, nor by blending of essence do we unite Him: for the one Son is the very same Person, being twofold in natures,[^410] Thy Son and Creator.

*Canon 2, Ode 8. Heirmos:* Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously heat the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* Let the faces be put to shame, and let the mouths be stopped of those who do not proclaim the one Son in two indivisible, unalterable, and unconfused natures; for we the pious glorify Christ as acting and willing in one way and another, not in persons, but in two natures.

*Troparion 2:* Ye who bear the name of Jacob of the Patchwork Cloak,[^411] inscribing his name upon yourselves: answer us, whether it was in his name that ye were baptized in the font of old? Therefore for the sake of these things ye have in truth departed from the grace of Christ, willingly living in false security with this liar!

*Troparion 3:* In Chalcedon the Fourth Council cast down Dioscorus, Eutyches, and Severus,[^412] the fierce ones; unto the end it cut off their thorn-like deception, which mingled the essences of the Savior, from the Church of Christ and Master: with which orthodoxly believing, let us hate them!

*Troparion (Triadikon):* The thrice-radiant, single-shining beam of the Godhead, from the one tri-hypostatic nature: the unoriginate Begetter, the connatural Word of the Father, and the co-reigning, consubstantial Spirit: ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* All-pure Parent, cleanse the wounds and sinful stumbling-blocks of my soul, washing them with the fountain from the side of thy Son, and with the streams that flowed therefrom: for unto thee do I cry, and unto thee do I flee, and thee do I call upon, who art full of God's grace!

*Canon 1, Ode 9. Heirmos:* It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was manifested unto men, Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparion 1:* It is not fitting for the Orthodox to add or to leave out anything of the sacred tradition of our Faith, wherein we were faithfully baptized; and they that alter this Faith shall justly be delivered over to the threat of anathema.[^413]

*Troparion 2:* With joy of heart let us all leap for joy, having assembled the sacred councils of the Fathers: for through them we have seen the light of Orthodoxy; for they appeared as luminaries, instructing all toward the right findings of teaching.

*Troparion 3:* Let us ask cleansing for our souls, and strive to pass our life piously: so that we may become partakers with the holy Fathers, who laid up the wealth of upright teachings for us, their children.

*Theotokion:* God, received from thy womb, O Mother of God, deified the human race, and made them worthy of His glory, and showed all to be heirs who truly and ever proclaim thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Canon 2, Ode 9. Heirmos:* Heaven was astonished *Canon 2, Ode 9. Heirmos:* Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God was manifested unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion 1:* Having transgressed against Christ's glorious Church, deceitful Severus justly received rejection[^414] by the council of the pious Fathers because of his blasphemy and crafty speech: and being condemned, he is cast out from the assembly of teachers.

*Troparion 2:* Why dost thou confound the natures of Christ, introducing mixture and confusion, attaching suffering on the Cross and burial unto the impassible Godhead of the only-begotten Word of God, O wretched Severus? Therefore we abhor thy great blasphemy!

*Theotokion:* Cast down, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, the haughtiness and audacity of enemies together, and the counsels of those who blaspheme the Creator; and establish the assembly of the faithful unshaken: as God Who hast exalted our horn and strengthened us in faith, that all we may magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ꙋ҆слы́шите):*
Celebrating today the memory of the divine Fathers, by their supplications we pray Thee, O All-merciful One: deliver Thy people, O Lord, from every harm, and vouchsafe all to glorify the Father, the Word, and the All-holy Spirit!

*Theotokion, Podoben:*
Rejoice, palace of God! Rejoice, overshadowing mountain! Rejoice, unburnt bush! Rejoice, throne of glory! Rejoice, divine table! Rejoice, golden urn! Rejoice, all-radiant lampstand! Rejoice, light cloud, Virgin Mother Mary!

*At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the Fathers, 4. Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^415]
Having gathered together all spiritual skill, and having examined together by the Holy Spirit the heavenly and honorable Symbol of Faith, the honorable Fathers traced it out as written by God; wherein most clearly they teach that the Word is co-unoriginate with Him that begot Him, and all-truly consubstantial: manifestly following the apostolic teachings, the glorious and most wealthy, truly and divinely-wise ones! *(Twice)*

*Verse: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages!*

Having received all the intellectual radiance of the Holy Spirit, the supernatural blessing, in brief sayings and with great understanding, they divinely inspired proclaimed as preachers of Christ, champions of the Gospel teachings, the blessed ones blessed ones; and of pious traditions having received from on high their revelation manifestly, and having been enlightened, they set forth the Faith taught by God.

*Verse: Gather together unto Him His venerable ones.*

*Sticheron 3:* Having gathered together all pastoral skill, and having now stirred up most righteous indignation, avengingly they drove away the grievous and destructive wolves, casting them out with the sling of the Spirit from the fulness of the Church, fallen as unto death and incurably diseased: the divine pastors, as truest servants of Christ, and most sacred initiates of the divine preaching!

*Glory... Tone 8. By George of Nicomedia:*[^416]
The choir of the holy Fathers, having run together from the ends of the universe, taught the one essence and nature of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit; and clearly handed down unto the Church the mystery of theology: praising whom in faith, let us call them blessed, saying: O divine army, divinely-speaking armaments of the Lord's encampment, most radiant stars of the intellectual firmament, invincible towers of the mystical Zion, fragrance-breathing flowers of Paradise, all-golden mouths of the Word, boast of Nicaea, ornament of the universe: diligently pray ye for our souls!

*Both now... Most blessed art thou, O Birthgiver of God...*

*The Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion only. Litanies, and the Dismissal. And the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite;*[^417] *and the Procession into the narthex. Glory... Both now... The Evangelical Sticheron. Tone 1, and the Final Dismissal. At the Hours, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... Of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion of the Tone. After "Our Father", the Resurrectional Kontakion.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos, on 6; and of the Fathers, Ode 3, on 4. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Fathers. Then the Resurrectional Kontakion; Glory... Of the Fathers; Both now... Of the Temple of the Birthgiver of God, or: Steadfast Protectress of Christians. The Prokeimenon of the Tone. And the Song of the Fathers, Tone 4: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages! The Epistle of the day, and of the Fathers to the Hebrews, Pericope 334;*[^418] *the Alleluia of the Tone. And of the Fathers, Tone 1: The God of gods, the Lord hath spoken; Verse: Gather together unto Him His venerable ones. The Gospel of the day, and of the Fathers, John, Pericope 56. Communion Verse: Praise the Lord from the heavens; and of the Fathers: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous!*

*In the same month, on the 17th day: The Holy Great-Martyr Marina.*[^419]

*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6. Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^420]
O most marvelous wonder! The exalted haughtiness of him who said he would consume the earth and the sea, hath a young maiden and all-undefiled one brought down to the earth, having conquered his craftiness! O the power of the Cross and of grace, which hath manifestly strengthened all our weakness!

O all-lauded martyr Marina, neither the fire of tortures, nor the delight of food, nor worldly joys, nor the beauty of youth separated thee from the love of Christ, desiring to obtain the beauty above understanding of thine honorable Bridegroom: of which thou wast vouchsafed, O divinely-blessed one!

O all-glorious martyr Marina, having worthily taken up thy dwelling in the heavenly mansions, with the virgin ranks and the martyr hosts: save by thy prayers those who in faith keep thy memory and faithfully approach thy protection; and ask of God forgiveness of transgressions, and deliverance, and great mercy!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
In the city of our God, in His holy mountain, there did the holy one dwell, having kept her lamp unquenched. Let us give ear to the praise of virgins: O virginity, Church of God! O virginity, glory of martyrs! O virginity, companion of angels!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the wood of the Cross, O Jesus, she that knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone that bare Thee, O Light unapproachable of the unoriginate Father? Make haste and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 2:*
With the voice of rejoicing and with psalms of shouting, let us sing unto the martyr Marina: for she cast down to the earth the deception of idols, and manfully placed the adversary foe beneath her feet; therefore having finished her course, unto the heavens she took flight, bearing a crown upon her head and crying aloud: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I desire, and having love of desire, for Thee I gave my flesh to the fire, that I may dwell in Thine eternal mansions, where is the habitation of those that rejoice!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb drawn willingly as a man to the slaughter, weeping she said: Dost Thou hasten now, O Christ, to make me childless who bare Thee? What is this that Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all things? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine extreme grace which is above mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb, O Jesus, Marina, calls out with a great voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering, and am crucified and buried with Thy baptism, and I suffer for Thy sake that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee that I may also live with Thee: but receive me as a spotless sacrifice, offered with love unto Thee! By her supplications, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos without the Martyr troparia; and of the Saint, on 6. Whose acrostic is: Christ's virgin martyr and bride with songs do I sing.*[^421] *Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!

*Troparion 1:* Rejoicing in God, O martyr, and being filled with rays from Him, grant me a light-bearing beam by thy prayers, O good virgin!

*Troparion 2:* The fleeting, unstable, and corruptible things, being girded with a wise mind, O martyr of Christ, hast thou wisely despised; and thou wast vouchsafed the supernal brightness of the righteous.

*Troparion 3:* As a servant I come unto thee, O holy one: by thy supplications save me from the tribulations of life, O passion-bearing martyr, and calm the storm of my passions!

*Troparion 4:* Having been enlightened with faith and grace, in thy mind, O Marina, thou wast not afraid of the tormentors' threats, O martyr of the Savior, being strengthened by divine power!

*Theotokion:* Thou hast shown forth the path of life, having conceived Him Who is Life, and having in the flesh given birth unto Him, O Mother of God, and thou hast rent asunder the bonds of death!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, Whom all creation sings: there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 1:* Neither fire nor wounds, nor the cutting of the sword, nor the cruelty of the tormentors, nor death, nor the fierceness of beasts, separated thee from divine love, O all-lauded one!

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast unshaken and unbending in the time of tribulation, an all-glorious pillar, O all-blessed one, the foundation of the faith of piety laid upon the rock!

*Troparion 3:* Good wast thou in tortures, an invincible martyr wast thou among virgins, having betrothed thyself unto Christ, Who bestows a twofold crown upon thee, O all-undefiled one!

*Troparion 4:* The streams of thy holy blood poured forth, O good virgin, quenched all the idol deception; and thou didst bring an assembly of martyrs unto thy Bridegroom!

*Theotokion:* Strengthened by grace, with one accord we glorify thee, O Birthgiver of God: thou didst bear Him Who took flesh, Who gives being unto all, and thou hast renewed the world!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^422]
A radiant festival hath arisen for feast-lovers, the memory of the glorious passion-bearer of Christ, gladdening all things, shining with the rays of wonders in the world, bringing ceaseless grace unto men: by her prayers, O Christ, save our souls! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O all-undefiled Virgin who didst bear the transcendent God: with the bodiless hosts unceasingly pray Him to grant us remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end, unto us who with faith and love hymn thee as is meet, O only all-hymned one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she spoke: What new and strange wonder is this, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death? Desiring to make alive the dead, as Thou art merciful!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the Father's bosom didst visit our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 1:* Truly God-befitting was thy life, it appeared beloved unto the angels, but unto the host of demons and unto the fierceness of their servants it showed itself unapproachable; for unto Christ thou didst cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 2:* Having contended upon the earth, O all-wealthy one, and having endured temporary afflictions, in the heavens now dost thou receive recompense from the Master, O unconquerable martyr, unto endless ages, with the angels crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 3:* Now does the choir of virgins rejoice over thee, now the assembly of martyrs makes merry, now also the multitude of the pious, having gathered together, dance with joy, crying aloud together with unceasing voices: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 4:* All fair art thou, O virgin, and undefiled; an all-spotless bride hast thou appeared, lover of the soul's beauty. By the burning heat of tortures, O all-blessed one, thou didst appear more radiant, singing unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:* Alone pure didst thou remain also after childbearing: for thou alone, O Bride of God, didst bear incarnate from thee the hypostatic Word, the Son Who shares the Father's throne; and thou bearest in thine arms Him Who cannot be contained by all, and Who holds all the ends of the earth!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion 1:* In the judgment hall of sufferers, firmly didst thou run thy course, O maiden; for divine desire strengthened womanly weakness, manifestly showing thee, by the providence of suffering, to be a self-governing martyr!

*Troparion 2:* The drops of thy blood trickling forth, O martyr, wove a costly purple robe and a great vesture of incorruption; arrayed wherein now, O divinely-wise one, in the heavens, thou intellectually standest before the Bridegroom!

*Troparion 3:* With the bonds of thy prayers, O maiden, having bound the boastful serpent,[^423] thou didst humble unto the earth the pride that before was exalted; for as God Who knows all things, He knows how to fulfill the desire of them that fear Him!

*Troparion 4:* With divine crowns of highly exalted glory did the Bridegroom-Word truly adorn thee, deifying thee who didst suffer for His sake, O glorious one, having patiently endured the flame of fire and the stripes of wounds!

*Theotokion:* Beholding the nature of earthborn men cleansed from the first curse by the childbearing of thy virginity, O all-undefiled one: we glorify Him Who was born of thee, the Only-begotten for His tender mercy; Whom do thou beseech, O Virgin, to save us!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion 1:* A fair and undefiled vine-branch didst thou appear unto Christ, and as a pure fountain and a fruitful orchard, O all-glorious Marina, and a near bride, and an unplundered paradise!

*Troparion 2:* An all-comely maiden followed after Thee, running unto the fragrance of Thy myrrh, O Master, conforming herself by endurance unto Thy most pure and most honorable passion.

*Troparion 3:* Manfully didst thou endure struggles, O much-suffering martyr; and him that before boasted exceedingly in intellectual pride didst thou destroy with a crash by the power of the Cross, O Marina named of the Lord!

*Theotokion:* O pure Mary and most pure vessel of the Creator of all: grant me tears that cleanse the soul, and give ear unto my supplication, O all-undefiled one!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*[^424]
Adorned with the beauties of virginity, O virgin, with incorruptible crowns wast thou crowned, O Marina; and crimsoned with the blood of martyrdom, enlightened with the wonders of healings, piously, O martyr, hast thou received the prize of victory of thy suffering!

*Ikos:*
Having been kindled from infancy with longing of thy heart for the Bridegroom Christ, O honorable virgin-martyr, thou didst run as a thirsty hind toward the gushing streams! And having by suffering kept all thy body pure, into the incorruptible bridal chamber of thy Creator in truth, O glorious bride, didst thou arrive, adorned and embellished: a victorious crown-bearer, a flourishing lamp-bearer, having attained the incorruptible bridal chamber, and having received like gold the prize of victory of thy suffering!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The Hebrew Children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion 1:* The unapproachable Light, O Marina, worthily enlightened thy soul, and with radiant tapers illumined those who sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast lifted up unto divine height indeed, for high understanding didst thou acquire, and into a radiant bridal chamber didst thou take up thy dwelling, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion 3:* A near bride unto the Master wast thou shown forth, O God-bearing Marina; and as an unconquerable martyr thou cryest unto Him: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:* A prince hath no longer failed from Judah,[^425] for from thee, the Master, the Expectation of the nations, has ineffably shone forth: blessed is the all-pure fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* When the musical instruments sounded together, and the countless peoples worshipped the image in Dura,[^426] the three Children, not submitting, hymned the Lord and glorified Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* Having acquired a steadfast character, steadfastly didst thou endure sufferings, and wast crowned with crowns of victory, singing: Sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 2:* A mystical bridal chamber for thee, O Marina, did God the Word, the Bridegroom of souls, prepare in the heavens; Whom beholding, thou dost cry aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 3:* Adorned with various virtues, thou wast vouchsafed manifold gifts, beholding the Summit of desires and crying aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 4:* Thou wast shown forth, O passion-bearer, as the cause of salvation unto many, and didst bring assemblies of martyrs unto Christ, crying aloud in unison: Sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* Cleansing was given unto us through thee, O Mother of God: for thou alone didst pay the debt, having conceived the Lord of all, O Virgin; Whom we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Every ear trembled with fear at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from the virgin womb; therefore the all-pure Birthgiver of God do we faithful magnify!

*Troparion 1:* O Marina, martyr of Christ, eternal and incorruptible life in the delight of Paradise hast thou diligently and truly found; for thou hast passed unharmed by the turning sword,[^427] shining from afar with the blood of martyrdom, O good virgin!

*Troparion 2:* Divine desire burned up every defiled and worldly thought, O virgin, and showed forth a most steadfast martyr, previously radiant with virgin brightness, O all-wise Marina; therefore all we faithful call thee blessed!

*Troparion 3:* Loose for me, O chosen bride, the fetters of my transgressions by thy prayers, and tear asunder the handwriting of my sins, O martyr, standing comely before Christ, thine all-merciful Bridegroom, and dispel the gloom of my passions!

*Troparion 4:* Ceased is the fierce raging of the deceiver, for behold, the maiden, forgetting the weakness of woman's nature, suffered, and radiant victory over him hath she taken; and now our first-mother's fall hath she manifestly restored.

*Theotokion:* Ripe life do I reap, unharmed by the tree of knowledge; for thou, O all-pure one, didst bud forth Christ, the Tree of Life, Who showed the entrances of life unto all; therefore thee, O all-pure one, do we piously proclaim as the Birthgiver of God.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ꙋ҆слы́шите):*
Having been strengthened, O fair virgin, by the Cross of thy Lord, thou didst destroy the pride of the tormentors, having suffered manfully, O martyr, and from Christ didst thou receive the honors of victory, O all-wise Marina, named of the Lord, praying for us who honor thee with love.

*Theotokion:*
Standing around thee, the God-wise virgins, O Mother of God Mary, as unto the Queen of our race, were brought unto thy Son, in bridal beauty, O all-pure one, as unto the King of all; Whom do thou, O Virgin Mother and Maiden, beseech for us.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 4:*
As upon a firm rock of the divine confession, having been established, O all-praised Marina, thou didst drown the murderous enemy into the abyss, and worthily didst thou receive a crown of victory.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льніи мч҃енницы):*
When the ewe-lamb and undefiled Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor beauty, alas for me! weeping she said: Whither hath Thy beauty departed, O Sweetest One? Where is Thy majesty? Where is the radiant grace of Thy countenance, O my most beloved Son?

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos, on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 3, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^428]
*Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.*
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 181.*[^429]
*Alleluia. Tone 1: With patience I waited for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 33.*[^430]
*Communion Verse: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.*

*In the same month, on the 18th day: The Holy Martyr Hyacinth, who was in Amastris; and the Holy Martyr Emilian.*[^431]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^432]
*Sticheron 1:* The Hellenic services hast thou crushed like dust, and hast set thyself up as a conspicuous pillar of the knowledge of God by the grace of Christ our God, O martyr Emilian; and a temple of sanctification and an unshakable foundation of piety, for those who honor thy valiant struggles, in God-wise manner hast thou become.

*Sticheron 2:* Consumed with fire, and wounded with stripes, and shut up in prisons, O glorious one, and in manifold ways afflicted, thou didst not sacrifice unto graven idols, thou didst not deny Christ, but didst remain unconquered; therefore the Master bestows upon thee the crown of victory, as a good Contest-Arbiter, as an unchanging King.

*Sticheron 3:* In the purple dye of thy blood hast thou dipped thy robe, arrayed wherein, O glorious one, thou hast entered into the supernal kingdoms, rejoicing; and before God, the King of all, unto the ages dost thou stand, asking peace and health, and speedy deliverance from evils, for those who honor thee with faith.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
With the showers of the All-holy Spirit, O all-pure one, bedew my mind, O thou who didst bear the Drop, Christ, Who washed away ineffably the measureless transgressions of men by His compassions: dry up the spring of my passions, and vouchsafe me unto the river of living delight evermore, by thy prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When she beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, Thine ewe-lamb and Mother was filled with wonder, and what is this sight? she cried aloud, O my longed-for Son: hath the faithless and lawless assembly repaid this unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders? Yet glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord, Emilian, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors, and crushed the feeble audacities of the demons. By his supplications save our souls!

*Canon, the composition of Joseph.*

*Whose acrostic is: Emilian the martyr do I sing gloriously.*[^433] *Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall appear radiantly celebrating, and I shall sing rejoicing in her wonders.

*Troparion 1:* Today let us praise God Who is over all, and this martyr let us praise, who lawfully suffered, and received the crown of victory by spiritual power.

*Troparion 2:* Clothed with the strength of Him Who by grace was clothed with our weakness, O glorious martyr, thou didst strip bare the vain raging of the weakness of idols, O divinely-blessed one.

*Troparion 3:* The tri-named Unity, united in the selfsame nature, O glorious one, rejoicing didst thou proclaim; and the polytheistic deception didst thou destroy, having firmly suffered, O divinely-blessed one.

*Theotokion:* Christ the Lord is my strength, my song, and the illumination of my heart, O all-undefiled one; Whom thou didst bear, understood in two essences, but in one Hypostasis.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish thy singers, O Birthgiver of God, thou living and plentiful fountain, who have assembled a spiritual choir, and in thy divine glory vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*Troparion 1:* A lamp unto thy feet and a light unto thy paths was truly the law of our God, whereby thou didst trip up the steps of the lawless, being martyrologically adorned.

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst direct the journeyings of thy mind unto the divine will, O wise one, as unto a quiet haven; and from the surging sea of polytheism and the spirits of malice hast thou fled.

*Troparion 3:* At Emilian's divine suffering, and faith unto the end, and courage, the heavenly powers were astonished: how in the flesh he humbled and cast down the bodiless foe?

*Theotokion:* Escaping the laws of nature by a supernatural childbearing, a Virgin after childbearing, O Virgin Mother, hast thou remained; for the Creator of all creation, O pure one, didst thou bear.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^434]
Strengthened by divine power, the gods of the godless didst thou deliver over unto final oblivion, O valiant sufferer; and having entered into the judgment hall, firmly didst thou endure the suffering of scraping, and manfully didst thou endure the flame. Therefore didst thou finish the divine contest, and rejoicing didst thou receive the crown of victory, O Emilian all-praised Emilian: entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who with love honor thy holy memory.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As a Virgin and the only one among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all we generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead took up His dwelling in thee, and as an infant dost thou nurse the Creator and Lord with milk. Therefore we, the angelic and human race, worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who with faith bow down before thy most holy childbearing!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting she called out: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender love of mercy, O long-suffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain: take pity, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who with faith hymn Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the transcendent God came with an incorruptible hand, and saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion 1:* Divine desire gloriously kindled thee; therefore didst thou endure the burning heat of bitter wounds, and burned up the gods of the godless, and bedewed the souls of the faithful, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst despise temporal things for the sake of the enduring, and didst bind all thy soul unto divine love; therefore didst thou destroy the dominion of impiety, O passion-bearer Emilian, worthy of wonder!

*Troparion 3:* Piously glorifying one nature, one glory of the thrice-radiant Godhead, O Emilian, as a valiant soldier didst thou enter into the judgment hall, and didst cast down to the earth the mighty serpent!

*Theotokion:* As the place of sanctification of Him Who sanctifies us, O most holy Maiden, we glorify thee who alone wast found; for whose sake we who live upon earth have been deified, and have been vouchsafed true life!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all that hymn thee!

*Troparion 1:* With the sprinklings of the warm drops of thy blood, O Emilian, didst thou quench the fire of godlessness; therefore a fountain of wonders hast thou poured forth, drying up the lakes of passions, and drowning diseases, and engulfing the demons!

*Troparion 2:* Stretched out, the wicked one commanded thee to be beaten; therefore, when thy body was torn and wounded with stripes, thou didst represent the most blessed holiness of Him Who was wounded upon the Cross, O sufferer!

*Troparion 3:* Being full of spiritual gifts, thou healest the incurable diseases of the sick, thou stayest burning fever, thou drivest away evil spirits, and unto all in afflictions dost thou give aid, O worthy of wonder!

*Theotokion:* Wounded by the weapon of sin, heal me with thine effective remedy, O thou who didst bear Christ the Savior, Who was wounded with a spear for my sake, O all-pure one, and pierced the serpent's heart!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I am come into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of my many sins has overwhelmed me; but as God, lead up my life from the depth, O Greatly-merciful One!

*Troparion 1:* Rich and full of divine glory, by martyrdom, O blessed one, didst thou become, and with the angels in the heavens dost thou dwell: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst not spare thy mortal body; therefore hast thou inherited abiding immortality, having died for the love of Him Who put passions to death, O glorious one!

*Troparion 3:* A myrrh of healings does the shrine of thy relics pour forth, O most wealthy, valiant martyr, and ever dispels the foul-smelling diseases of men!

*Theotokion:* Eve indeed partook from the tree of deadly fruit; but thou, O all-pure one, didst give birth unto the Tree of Life, the taste whereof hath made alive all who were dying!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^435]
Kindled with divine zeal, thou wast not afraid of the fellow-serving fire; but having gone up dauntlessly of thine own will, consumed as a whole burnt offering by the kindled fire, thou didst offer thyself as a sacrifice unto the Master, O glorious martyr Emilian: entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The God-wise ones did not serve the creature rather than the Creator; but manfully trampling upon the threatening of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Enlightened by the grace of the divine Spirit, O blessed one, thou didst pass through the kindling of the furnace, and didst appear unburned, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* Kindled with longing for thy Beloved, Christ, O blessed one, thou wast not terrified of the fire; wherein, bedewed by the Spirit, thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:* By the beauty of sufferings thou wast likened unto the bodiless hosts, O wise one; standing with them before the all-comely Christ, rejoicing thou dost sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* O God-named Bride of God, with faith we cry aloud unto thee, Rejoice! For thou didst bear the ineffable Joy, Christ the Lord; unto Whom we cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious Children in the furnace: then indeed prefigured, but now in reality, it stirs the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* Having destroyed the worship of deception, thou didst release him who was held in bonds instead of thee, O all-wise one; and didst surrender thyself voluntarily unto martyrdom, crying aloud, O all-praised one: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 2:* Behold, I was found by them that sought me not, and unto them that asked not for me was I made manifest, dost thou cry, O all-glorious one: Understand, ye lawless ones, for as a harmless lamb have I hastened to be slaughtered, crying: Bless the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 3:* The great Emilian hath summoned us unto a banquet, setting forth his struggles as food; let us, the faithful, be spiritually fed, and let us sing: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* Rain down upon me drops of mercy, O thou who didst bear the Fountain of mercy, and dry up the floods of my sin, and calm the fierce waves of my soul, O Virgin Mother of God Mary, that I may glorify thee unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed, by the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding forth of thy pregnancy, didst blossom blessing for the world; therefore all we magnify thee!

*Troparion 1:* Desiring to see God's ineffable gladness and brightness, all the fair things of life didst thou diligently despise, O glorious one, and joyfully and rejoicing didst thou finish martyrdom; therefore with faith we call thee blessed!

*Troparion 2:* As an offering baked by fire, O all-praised one, unto Him Who was slaughtered like a lamb, unto the divine table didst thou offer thyself, preserved by the sweet fragrance of the Spirit, and vouchsafed true glory; therefore with love we call thee blessed!

*Troparion 3:* Today thy memory hath shone forth upon us, O passion-bearer, brighter than the sun, gladdening the hearts of all the faithful, enlightening minds, and driving away the darkness of diseases; which we prayerfully celebrate!

*Troparion 4:* The chosen choir of firstborn hath acquired thee as a fellow-citizen; and all the martyrs, beholding thee dancing with them, rejoice; with whom remember also us who remember thee, O martyr of great renown!

*Theotokion:* With thy light enlighten me, O all-hymned one, who walk in darkness; reach me a helping hand, drive away the clouds of my soul; calm the tempest of my passions, O refuge of the despairing!

*In the same month, on the 19th day: Our Venerable Mother Macrina, sister of Basil the Great; and our Venerable Father Dios.*[^436]

*Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^437]
*Sticheron 1:* Incorruptibly and undefiledly didst thou preserve thy virginity, having lived purely; and thou didst distribute thy wealth, fulfilling Christ's lawgiving, Whom thou didst follow, having forsaken the earth and the things upon the earth. Therefore unto thee the heavenly things and heaven hath Jesus, the Lover of mankind and the Savior of our souls, granted!

*Sticheron 2:* Philosophically didst thou show forth the soul's nature as self-moving and immortal; therefore also thou didst strive not to mingle with sinful defilement, and pure from all mire to exchange the bodily bonds, having neither spot nor wrinkle, O God-acceptable one, but pure and all-undefiled to stand before thy Bridegroom!

*Sticheron 3:* With a bright beam was thy light-bearing face enlightened, even when dead, O all-praised one, for Christ glorified thee as His God-like virgin, as merciful and prayerful, as compassionate and speaking of God; for having lived an equal-to-the-angels life upon earth, thou didst please God by the brightness of thy life!

*Other Stichera of the Venerable Father, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
*Sticheron 1:* As a universal star intellectually shining upon the firmament of the Church, we praise thee, O blessed one, ever enlightened by thy divine rays, and with soul and mind honoring thy light-bearing day prayerfully, O sacred wonderworker, ornament of monastics!

*Sticheron 2:* As a second Aaron by the budding of the rod, O father, thou becamest a divine sanctification; as Moses, water freshly from the rock didst thou bring forth; as Elijah, a dead man didst thou raise by thy prayer;[^438] admired for great wonders, and enlightened by divine callings, O God-wise, most wealthy Dios!

*Sticheron 3:* A spiritual radiance, manifestly by sweat didst thou drive away the spirits of malice, and didst establish a sacred care for souls: wherein multitudes are ever saved, who honor thy memory, thy manly struggles, and the marvel of thy wonders with faith, O Dios, boast of the fathers!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
O thou who didst hold in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who in love for mankind became man, and received our mixture from thee, and manifestly made it alive: despise me not, O all-pure one, who am now in sorrow, but speedily show compassion, and deliver me from manifold hostilities and the harm of the evil one!

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When she who bare Thee beheld Thee upon the Tree as the Lamb and Shepherd, the ewe-lamb wept, and maternally cried aloud unto Thee: O my longed-for Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 8:*
In thee, O mother, was that which is according to the image preserved with certainty; for having taken up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thine actions didst teach: to despise indeed the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, an immortal thing. Therefore also with the angels thy spirit rejoices, O venerable Macrina!

*Troparion of the Venerable Father, Tone 4:*
A dweller of the desert, and an angel in the body, and a wonderworker didst thou appear, O our God-bearing father Dios; by fasting, vigilance, and prayer having received heavenly gifts, thou healest the sick, and the souls of those who run unto thee with faith. Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who works healings for all through thee!

*Canon of the Venerable Mother. Whose acrostic is: As a pure bride unto Christ do I crown thee, O Macrina.*[^439] *Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall appear radiantly celebrating, and I shall sing rejoicing in her wonders.

*Troparion 1:* The all-pure Bridegroom, the Beauty of souls, O all-glorious Macrina, Whom having desired thou didst love with all thy heart: entreat Him to enlighten those who hymn thee!

*Troparion 2:* Being an abyss of goodness beyond mind, O Christ, Thou hast glorified Thine undefiled bride, who was nailed by desire for Thee, the spotless and all-glorious Macrina!

*Troparion 3:* O God-wise Macrina, boast of virginity, ardently didst thou preserve thy life unharmed by temperance, having subjected the flesh unto the spirit!

*Theotokion:* Light-bearingly hath Christ shone forth in the hearts of those who with faith honor thee as the Mother of God, O all-undefiled one; and the light-bearing day did He illumine, Who shone forth from thy womb, O Bride of God!

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, the composition of Joseph. Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having traversed the watery deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion 1:* Ever adorned by nods toward God, thou didst take up thy cross and follow Christ, having put to death the mind of thy flesh by temperance, O Dios!

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst rise early at dawn unto the glory of the never-setting Sun, O venerable one; an all-radiant star wast thou, and didst enlighten the faithful by the brightness of thy virtues and wonders, O blessed one!

*Troparion 3:* Thou didst alienate thy thought, O all-wise one, from the fair things in the world; a true lover of transcendent glory wast thou, having conquered the bodiless one with the flesh by divine grace!

*Theotokion:* A dwelling of the spiritual Sun wast thou, O all-undefiled one, Who with divine brightnesses hath piously illumined us and driven away the mist of ignorance, O Ever-virgin!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ: for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Ode 3 (Canon 1). Troparion 1:* Adorned with God-given beauty and grace, and deriving from an honorable race the glory of men, thou didst emulate their divine character, O pure one!

*Troparion 2:* Submitting unto the divine law, O virgin, thou didst leave behind the turmoil of life, and didst escape rumors, exercising thyself in fasting, and prayers, and longing!

*Troparion 3:* That which is from tender infancy didst thou love, and guarded by maternal eyes, thou didst remain incorrupt and undefiled, O Macrina!

*Theotokion:* We know thee as the earth, O Virgin, all-undefiled Maiden, that didst bud forth the Ear of grain of life without seed for us, strengthened whereby we call thee blessed!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 3. Heirmos:* Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment!

*Troparion 1:* Shining with divine rays, thou becamest a lamp, guiding with faith those who come unto thee, O blessed one!

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst guide unto life those who follow well, O venerable one; for the passions of the flesh hast thou put to death by divine power!

*Troparion 3:* Thou wast united in God-befitting manner with the heavenly hosts, for upon earth didst thou live an angelic life, O Dios, our God-bearing father!

*Theotokion:* He Who dwells in the heavens took up His dwelling in thee, O pure one; Whom do thou diligently beseech to save the souls of those who hymn thee!

*Kontakion of the Venerable Father, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (Тве́рдыѧ):*[^440]
Divinely armed with purity of soul, and having powerfully taken up ceaseless prayer as a spear, thou didst cut down the demonic hosts, O our father Dios, wonderworker: unceasingly pray for all of us!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Thy divine memory, O Macrina, having shone forth today, appeared unto the world like the sun, making known thy life; for having destroyed the carnal motions by temperance, thou didst betroth thyself unto Christ from infancy: therefore deliver from all evils those who praise thee!

*Glory... Of the Venerable Father, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having been dedicated unto God from infancy, and having well-pleased Him even unto the end, O our venerable father, thou didst obtain gifts, and by grace didst drive away a multitude of demons; unto the praise of the Lord didst thou build a monastery; therefore also most gloriously wast thou vouchsafed to raise the dead, O all-praised one, with undoubting [with undoubting] prayer. Therefore we cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love honor thy holy memory!

*Both now... Theotokion:* The heavenly gate and ark, the most holy mountain, the radiant cloud let us hymn, the unburnt bush, the rational paradise, the recall of Eve, the great treasury of the whole universe; for salvation was wrought in her for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions. Therefore we cry aloud unto her: Pray to thy Son, to grant remission of offenses unto those who piously worship thy most holy birthgiving!

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and bitterly wailing cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O Long-suffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain! Have compassion and grant remission of offenses unto those who with faith hymn Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Seated in glory upon the throne of Godhead, in a light cloud Jesus the transcendent God came with His incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion 1:* Having put thyself to death unto the world and worldly things, to live unto God alone by divine longing didst thou achieve, desiring the beauty of thy Bridegroom, Who saved thee incorrupt and pure, O all-honorable one!

*Troparion 2:* Having emulated honorable Thecla the first woman-martyr,[^441] and truly being likened unto her divine life, by the inspiration of the divine from on high, thou didst even figuratively receive her name!

*Troparion 3:* Guiding virgins as a bridesmaid, O Macrina, thou didst bring them unto the virginal Word Who shone forth from the Virgin; by passionless adoption and likeness, thou didst manifestly show forth dispassion!

*Theotokion:* Streams of divine gifts and fountains of healings, distributions of divine wealth are given by the life-originating hand of the Pantokrator, O Bride of God, Who was born of thy womb!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 4. Heirmos:* Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion 1:* Enlightening thy mind with the immaterial candle of the Spirit, thou didst account carnal deceit as a dream, O God-bearer, keeping watch and looking forward unto the city on high!

*Troparion 2:* Like a cloud bearing rain didst thou appear unto the imperial city:[^442] thou didst water it with the waterings of the Holy Spirit, and didst excellently raise up care for souls!

*Troparion 3:* Having put thyself to death unto sin by temperance, thou didst grant life unto the dead by divine assistance; and water didst thou pour forth for us of compunction, O venerable one, who celebrate thy memory!

*Theotokion:* As a light-bearing lampstand bearing the divine radiance in thy womb did we come to know thee, O Virgin; therefore we pray thee: With thy light illumine the souls of all who hymn thee!

*Canon 1. Ode 5. Heirmos:* The ungodly will not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, rising early at dawn from the night, hymn Thee, the Only-begotten One, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 1:* To be defiled by passions, unto the utmost soul-like and God-like beauty, thou couldst not endure, O all-wise one: having perceived the ineffable beauty of thy honorable Bridegroom, O good maiden!

*Troparion 2:* Having been enriched with the strong consolation of Christ, both firmly and unshakably didst thou put to death the great turmoil of the passions, rising early at dawn from the night and glorifying the Lord!

*Troparion 3:* A firm understanding didst thou preserve unto Christ, for unto Him from the womb didst thou dedicate thyself, O undefiled one; unto Whom also thou didst commit thy soul and body, fasting from thy youth!

*Theotokion:* Thou didst bear Christ, the Redeemer of all, Who fashioned us who by our will had fallen into corruption, and lifted us up unto ineffable glory, O Mother of God!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou, my Lord, camest as a light into the world, a holy light turning back from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith!

*Troparion 1:* Standing upon the sacred mountain of life, by the ascent of thy heart thou didst destroy the passions, and drew near unto God!

*Troparion 2:* As the rod of Aaron by a marvelous budding wast thou, O God-bearer, serving the Master purely and divinely!

*Troparion 3:* O divine gifts bestowed upon thee by God! For by the pickaxe of thy prayers, living water didst thou bring forth from the earthly sides, O father!

*Theotokion:*

Neither angelic nor human mind can tell, O all-undefiled one, the dreadful and unfathomable depth of thy birthgiving!

*Canon 1. Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side!

*Troparion 1:* The desire of Christ increasing more manifestly, all Thy mind bore virtue proper to it, delighting in it, and in pious divine teachings!

*Troparion 2:* Hymning Christ with unceasing prayers, thou didst draw firm help from Him, O virgin, and didst preserve the divine grace of virginity!

*Troparion 3:* With a crown of gifts Christ crowned thee as a pure virgin, and the leader of the virginal choir; and in the heavenly bridal chambers, O God-wise one, did He settle thee!

*Theotokion:* By union He brought together those things that were formerly separated; for having united two natures, thou didst ineffably give birth unto God the Word truly incarnate, O all-undefiled one!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side!

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst raise unto God the desire of thy heart, and humbled the passions; and the ferocity of demons didst thou cast down, O God-bearer, immovable foundation of monastics!

*Troparion 2:* With temperance thou didst strike Egypt with wounds, O father; from its passionate servitude didst thou free a multitude of unmarried and married who piously follow thee, O venerable one![^443]

*Troparion 3:* Being rich in virtues and filled with the brightness of wonders, thou didst pass over unto the city on high, O God-wise Dios, and thou entreatest that thy flock be delivered from tribulations!

*Theotokion:* Put to death the passions of my soul, O all-undefiled one, who by the ineffable Word gavest birth unto the Life of all, and pacify my troubled mind, O Maiden!

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
The good God with all thy heart didst thou love, O venerable Macrina; and having taken His honorable Cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst fervently follow Him: whence thou hast found forgiveness of offenses!

*Canon 1. Ode 7. Heirmos:* He Who saved Thy young men in the fire of Abraham, and slew the Chaldeans whom justice righteously snared: Blessed art Thou, O praised Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Desiring Christ alone, and wounded with perfect love wounded manifestly, she cried aloud: I run after Thee: Blessed art Thou, O praised Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* Having cast away passions and tumultuous motions, thou didst clothe thyself in the radiant garment of dispassion, crying: Blessed art Thou, O praised Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:* Having cast down the beast-like, exalted pride of the malice-originating enemy, thou didst receive the prize of victory, O praiseworthy one, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* Break the bonds of offenses, by thy maternal boldness, for those who piously and faithfully hymn thy birthgiving, O Virgin!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 7. Heirmos:* In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite children, consumed by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion 1:* A dwelling of the Trinity didst thou make thy heart; therefore for monastics didst thou raise up a most necessary care, O God-bearer, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion 2:* Having made swift thy marvelous fountain, O God-wise father, thou didst pour forth the water of salvation for those who faithfully celebrate thy falling asleep, O marvelous Dios, most radiant luminary of monastics!

*Troparion 3:* Having adorned thy life with virtues, thou wast all light-bearing, and a pillar visible as light, a guide unto those who sing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:* A wall broader than the Cherubim did the holy God manifestly make thee, O Maiden, shining upon those who sing: Blessed art thou among women, O all-undefiled Sovereign Lady!

*Canon 1. Ode 8. Heirmos:* The pious children in the furnace the birthgiving of the Mother of God saved: then indeed prefigured, but now in act, it moves the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* A divinely-befitting life and pure life didst Thou lead; an unstealable dedicated offering, a hidden beauty, a God-like adornment didst Thou appear, O God-inspired one, crying: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion 2:* Clothed in the mortification of passions, thou didst pass over unto divine immortality, having taught excellent and philosophical things unto the immortal and self-moving soul, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion 3:* Having enlightened thy mind and face with the beam of the thrice-radiant light-shedding, thou didst fall asleep, O most wealthy one, with a blessed falling asleep, beholding with joy Him Whom thou desiredst, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* Thee, the most holy ark overshadowed by the divine Spirit, who gavest birth unto the pre-eternal and co-eternal Word, Who through ineffable tender mercy became man, we hymn as the Mother of God, and exalt above all unto all ages!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 8. Heirmos:* Having stretched out his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the power of fire they quenched, girded with virtue, the children lovers of piety, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion 1:* By the budding of thy rod, God manifested thy fruitful soul, O all-wise one, delighting fruitful and rational gardens, by divine beckoning, in the paradise of delight, the multitude of monastics, crying: All ye works, bless the Lord!

*Troparion 2:* Thy mind shining with divine beauties remained fair; and now thou hast reached the highest of good things, as one pure, being deified continually by communion, and singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion 3:* That with joyful voices celebrating we may honor thee, O Dios: ever save thy flock that celebrates thy memory, and guide unto the entrances of life those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:* Thou didst appear broader than the heavens, having contained God, O all-pure Mary; Whom do thou beseech to save me from the passions that hold me, and deliver me from future dreadful condemnation, who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Canon 1. Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed by the sickness of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, by the budding of the bearing in thy womb, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee!

*Troparion 1:* Truly emulating the rich mercy of God, thou didst finish thy course, feeding all who were consumed by poverty, O all-praised one; therefore the Merciful One glorified thee, who didst show thyself merciful!

*Troparion 2:* Before the face of God hast thou stood, O all-praised one, for thou wast light-bearing, adorned in life and word, and with all praises, and shining with the nobility of race by most high ascents!

*Troparion 3:* Into the most radiant bridal chamber hast thou entered, and into the temple, O heavenly Macrina where thou abidest, O virgin, with thy holy kinsfolk;[^444] with boldness entreat the Lord, O good one, to save those who hymn thee!

*Theotokion:* Fair in beauty and radiant above all was the Fruit of thy womb; for thou, O Virgin Mother of God, didst bear the incarnate God, Who appeared to save man: therefore we all magnify thee!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 9. Heirmos:* A stone cut without hands from an uncut mountain, which art thou, O Virgin, was cut off as the cornerstone, even Christ, Who joined together the separated natures: therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God!

*Troparion 1:* Thee who by the comeliness of virtues, O father, didst appear as an angel and flew up unto heaven having gilded wings,[^445] joyfully do we honor, and piously praise!

*Troparion 2:* Like a many-lighted sun having shone forth from the East, O Dios, everywhere didst thou richly extend the beam of thy virtues and wonders, enlightening the ends of the world!

*Troparion 3:* Unto the never-setting Light, O father, having become radiant-looking by the brightness of virtues, thou didst pass over rejoicing, and didst settle where the choirs of the venerable dwell, praying for thy flock!

*Theotokion:* Spare me, O Christ, when Thou comest to judge the world with glory, having to entreat Thee, O Word, Thy Mother, the Ever-virgin, and the angelic orders, and the assemblies of the venerable!

*In the same month, on the 20th day: The Holy and Glorious Prophet Elijah.*[^446]

*Note: Be it known, that in Greek printed Menaia, and manuscript Typika: likewise also in Slavonic manuscript, and in printed Typika, the service of the Prophet Elijah is appointed as a sixfold service with the Oktoechos.*[^447]

*At Vespers, the customary kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ); and Idiomela, Tone 2; Glory... Tone 6: "Come, ye of the Orthodox..." Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion. At the Aposticha of the Oktoechos: Glory... Tone 6: "O prophet, preacher of Christ..." Both now... Theotokion. Troparion, Tone 4: "An angel in the flesh..." Glory... Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

...or Stavrotheotokion.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the prophet, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion. Customary kathisma, and Sessional Hymns of the Oktoechos. Both canons of the Oktoechos, and of the saint on 6. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn. After Ode 6, Kontakion, and Ikos, and Prologue. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the prophet. At the Praises, stichera on 6; Glory... Tone 8: "The summits of the prophets..." Both now... Theotokion. At the Aposticha of the Oktoechos: Glory... Tone 4: "Upon a chariot of fire..." At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the prophet from the canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: "Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek."*[^448] *Verse: "The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand."*[^449] *Epistle: The Catholic Epistle of James, Pericope 57.*[^450] *Alleluia, Tone 5: "Moses and Aaron among His priests..."*[^451] *Gospel: Luke, Pericope 14.*[^452] *Communion Verse: "In everlasting memory shall the righteous be."*[^453] *Note: In some Typika there are also found three readings at Vespers, and the Great Doxology at Matins.*

*If it be the temple of the Prophet Elijah, or if the superior wish, we celebrate a Vigil:*

*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 4. Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron 1:* With the thrice-radiant light of the divine radiance, through purity of mind, thou didst with righteous judgment rebuke the ungodly king as a transgressor of the law; and thou didst shut up heaven, and feed the widow with her son: pray, O prophet, that our souls may be saved! (Twice)

*Sticheron 2:* With divine vision of God, worthily honored with prophetic dignity and understanding, O divine prophet, thou foretellest things afar off as though they were near at hand; thou didst rebuke the king as unrighteous, and didst slaughter the shameful priests with a knife: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron 3:* The ungodly king, not enduring thy rebuke, said unto thee, O divine prophet: Art thou he that troubles and perverts Israel? But thou didst say: Not I, O king, but thou and thy father's house, since thou hast forsaken thy God Who made thee, and hast brought in strange gods! But by the prayers of Thy prophet, O Christ, deliver us from such deceit, and save our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:* O divine prophet Elijah, having boldly rebuked the king who acted unjustly in the murder of Naboth, and in the seizure of the vineyard, thou didst say: Because without justice thou hast slain an Israelite man, thus shalt thou also thyself be slain, and the dogs shall lick thy blood, and harlots shall wash in thy blood![^454] But by the prayers of Thy prophet and pleaser, O Christ, deliver us together from such threatening, and save our souls as the Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*

*Sticheron 1:* O divine prophet, being zealous for the Lord Almighty, by prayer thou didst shut up heaven, saying: Rain and dew shall not come down upon the earth, save by the voice of my mouth!

*Verse: Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His name.*[^455]

*Sticheron 2:* O divine prophet, by prayer and mercy thou openest the heavens again, and richly bestowest rain upon a thirsting people!

*Verse: Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek.*[^456]

*Sticheron 3:* O divine prophet, thou didst feed the widow with her son, and by prayer didst multiply the handful of meal and the cruse of oil: according to the word of the Lord, the handful of meal and the cruse of oil failed not!

*Glory... Tone and podoben the same:* O divine prophet, by thy prayer thou didst bring down fire from heaven, and didst persuade the king to say: Truly, the God preached by Elijah is the one God!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O good Maiden, lift up thy holy hands unto thy Son, the Creator Who loves the soul, to show compassion unto thy servants!

*At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 8. Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron 1:* O Word, Who by a fiery chariot didst mercifully translate Elijah the Thesbite from the earth: by his prayers save us who faithfully glorify Thee, and who joyfully celebrate his divine and saving memory!

*Sticheron 2:* In a reed and thin whirlwind didst thou behold the coming of God, O Elijah, divinely blessed one,[^457] which enlightened thee of old; and sitting upon a four-horse chariot, thou didst pass through heaven in a marvelous manner, being admired, O God-inspired one!

*Sticheron 3:* The shameful priests didst thou slaughter with a knife; heaven by thy tongue didst thou restrain from raining upon the earth, being ignited with divine zeal; and thou didst fill Elisha with the double gift of the mantle, O wise Elijah, of divine grace!

*Other Stichera, Tone 2. Idiomela. By John the Archbishop:*

*Sticheron 4:* Two luminaries shone forth upon the universe, all-radiant Elijah and Elisha: the one indeed by the divine word shut up the drops of heaven, and rebuked the king, and was taken up into the heavens upon a fiery chariot; while the other healed the barren waters, and received a double portion of grace, and divided the streams of the Jordan: and now dancing with the angels, they pray for us that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron 5:* The fiery chariot bearing thee on high as in a whirlwind unto heaven gave thee fire-inspired grace of wonders, O Elijah the Thesbite, making thee incorrupt, so that thou shouldest not see death, until thou hast preached the consummation of all things; therefore come, granting us instruction in thy righteous deeds!

*Sticheron 6:* O never-setting luminary of the Church, being ignited with divine zeal, O prophet Elijah, thou didst shut up the drops of heaven; and being fed by a raven, thou didst rebuke the king, and put the priests to death; thou didst bring down fire from heaven, and having slain two captains of fifty, thou didst feed the widow with oil and a small handful of meal; and by prayer didst raise her son; thou didst kindle fire in water; thou didst cross the streams of Jordan on foot; and wast taken up into heaven by a fiery chariot, granting a double grace unto Elisha: unceasingly pray unto God, that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 6. By Byzantios:*[^458]
Come, ye assembly of the Orthodox, having come together today in the most honorable temple of the God-speaking prophets, let us sing a fitting psalm-tune unto Christ our God Who glorified them, and with gladness and joy let us cry aloud: Rejoice, earthly angel and heavenly man, Elijah of great renown! Rejoice, O all-honorable Elisha, who didst receive a double grace from God! Rejoice, fervent defenders, protectors, and physicians of the souls and bodies of the Christ-loving people: from every assault of the adversary, and hardship, and all tribulations deliver those who faithfully celebrate your all-festal memory!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Who would not bless thee...

*The Reading from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 17):*[^459]
The word of the Lord came unto Elijah the prophet, and he said unto Ahab: As the Lord God of hosts lives, the God of Israel, before Whom I stand today, there shall not be dew or rain in these years, save by the words of my mouth! And the voice of the Lord came unto Elijah, saying: Go hence eastward, and hide thyself by the brook of Cherith, that is before the Jordan; and it shall be that thou shalt drink of the brook; and I have commanded the ravens to feed thee there. And Elijah went, and did according to the word of the Lord; and he sat by the brook of Cherith, that is before the Jordan; and the ravens brought him bread in the morning, and flesh in the evening; and he drank water of the brook. And it came to pass after some days that the brook dried up, because there had been no rain upon the earth. And the word of the Lord came unto Elijah, saying: Arise, and go to Zarephath of Sidon, and dwell there; for behold, I have commanded a widow woman there to sustain thee. And he arose and went to Zarephath of Sidon; and he came to the gate of the city, and behold, a widow woman was there gathering sticks; and Elijah called after her, and said unto her: Bring me now a little water in a vessel, that I may drink. And she went to fetch water; and Elijah called after her, and said unto her: Bring me now also a morsel of bread in thine hand, that I may eat. And the woman said: As the Lord my God lives, I have not a cake, but only a handful of meal in a waterpot, and a little oil in a cruse; and behold, I am gathering two sticks, that I may go in and prepare it for myself and my children, that we may eat it, and die. And Elijah said unto her: Be of good courage, go in, and do not according to thy word; but make me thereof a little cake first, and bring it out unto me; and make for thyself and thy children afterward.
