For thus says the Lord God of Israel: The waterpot of meal shall not fail, neither shall the cruse of oil diminish, until the day that the Lord gives rain upon the earth! And the woman went, and did according to the word of Elijah; and she gave unto him, and he did eat, and she, and her children; and from that day the waterpot of meal failed not, and the cruse of oil diminished not, according to the word of the Lord, which He spoke by the hand of Elijah. And it came to pass after these things, that the son of the woman, the mistress of the house, fell sick; and his sickness was so severe, until there was no breath left in him. And the woman said unto Elijah: What have I to do with thee, O man of God? Art thou come unto me to call my sins to remembrance, and to slay my son? And Elijah said unto the woman: Give me thy son! And he took him out of her bosom, and carried him up into an upper room, where he himself abode, and laid him upon his own bed. And Elijah cried unto the Lord, and said: Alas for me, O Lord, the witness of the widow with whom I dwell, Thou hast afflicted her by slaying her son! And he breathed upon the child three times, and called upon the Lord, and said: O Lord my God, let the soul of this child return into him! And it was so. And he cried out Elijah, and the Lord heard the voice of Elijah, and the soul of the child returned, and he revived. And Elijah took the child, and brought him down from the upper room into the house, and gave him unto his mother. And Elijah said unto her: See, thy son is alive! And the woman said unto Elijah: Now by this I know that thou art a man of God, and that the word of the Lord in thy mouth is true.

*The Reading from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 18):*[^460]
The word of God came unto Elijah the Thesbite, in the third year of the reign of Ahab, saying: Go and show thyself unto Ahab, for I will give rain upon the face of the earth. And it came to pass when he saw Elijah, that he said unto him: Art thou he that troublest Israel? And Elijah said: I do not trouble Israel, but thou, and the house of thy father, in that ye have forsaken the Lord your God, and have followed after Baal. And now send, and gather together unto me all Israel, unto Mount Carmel, and the shameful prophets of Baal, three hundred and fifty,[^461] and the prophets of the abominations, four hundred, who eat at Jezebel's table. And according to the word of Elijah, Ahab sent unto all Israel, and gathered all the prophets unto Mount Carmel, and brought Elijah unto them all. And Elijah said unto them: How long will ye halt on both your knees? If the Lord is God, follow after Him; but if Baal is god, then follow after him! And the people answered him not a word. And Elijah said unto the people: I only remain a prophet of the Lord, while the prophets of Baal are three hundred and fifty men, and the prophets of the abominations four hundred. And Elijah said: Give us two bullocks; and let them choose one bullock for themselves, and cut it into pieces, and lay it on wood, but put no fire under. And I will dress the other bullock, and lay it on wood, and put no fire under. And call ye on the names of your gods, and I will call on the Name of the Lord my God; and it shall come to pass, the God Who answers by fire, He is God. And all the people answered, and said: Good is the word that Elijah spoke; let it be so! And Elijah said unto the shameful prophets: Choose ye one bullock for yourselves, and prepare it first, for ye are many; and call on the names of your gods, but put no fire under. And they took the bullock, and prepared it so; and called on the name of Baal from morning even until noon, and said: Hear us, O Baal, hear us! But there was no voice, nor any to answer. And they leaped around the altar which they had made. And it came to pass at noon, that Elijah the Thesbite mocked them, and said: Call with a loud voice, for there is leisure for your god; lest when he is arranging something else, or he is asleep, that ye may awake him! And they called with a loud voice, and cut themselves after their custom with knives. And many beat themselves with whips unto the pouring forth of their blood, and prophesied. And it came to pass until evening, when it was time for the sacrifice to ascend, and there was no voice. And Elijah spoke unto the shameful prophets, saying: Step aside now, that I also may offer my sacrifice. And they stepped aside, and were silent. And Elijah said unto the people: Come near unto me. And all the people came near unto him. And Elijah took twelve stones, according to the number of the tribes of the sons of Israel, as the Lord spoke unto him: Israel shall be thy name. And he built the stones into the Name of the Lord, and repaired the altar that had been dug down. And he made a sea containing two measures of seed round about the altar. And he laid the logs upon the altar which he had made. And he cut the whole burnt offering into pieces, and laid it upon the logs, and went around the altar. And Elijah said: Bring me four waterpots of water, and pour them upon the whole burnt offerings and upon the logs. And they did so. And he said: Do it a second time; and they did it a second time. And he said: Do it a third time; and they did it a third time. And the water ran round about the altar, and the sea was filled with water. And Elijah cried aloud unto heaven, and said: O Lord God of Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, hear me, O Lord, hear me this day by fire; that all these people may know that Thou art the one Lord God of Israel, and I am Thy servant, and for Thy sake have I done all these works; and do Thou turn back the hearts of these people after Thee! And fire fell from the Lord out of heaven, and consumed the whole burnt offerings, and the logs, and the stones, and the dust, and the fire licked up all the water that was in the sea. And all the people fell upon their face, and said: Truly the Lord is God, He is God! And Elijah said unto the people: Take the prophets of Baal, let not one of them escape! And they seized them; and Elijah led them down to the brook of Kishon, and there slaughtered them. And after this Elijah said unto Ahab: Behold, there is the sound of the coming of rain; harness thy chariot, and go down, lest the rain overtake thee. And Elijah went up to Mount Carmel, and bowed down to the earth, and put his face between his knees, and prayed unto the Lord. And the heaven grew dark with clouds and wind, and there was a great rain; and Ahab wept, and went to Jezreel. And the hand of the Lord was on Elijah; and he girded up his loins, and ran before Ahab to Jezreel. And Ahab told Jezebel his wife all that Elijah had done, and how he had slaughtered the prophets with a knife. And Jezebel sent unto Elijah, saying: Tomorrow at this same hour will I make thy life as the life of one of them! And Elijah heard and feared, and arising, he went for his life, and came to Beersheba of the land of Judah. And he left his servant there, and went into the wilderness a day's journey. And coming, he sat under a juniper tree, and requested for his soul that he might die, and said: It is enough for me, O Lord; take now my soul from me, for I am not better than my fathers! And he lay down and slept under a plant. And behold, an angel of the Lord touched him, and said unto him: Arise, eat and drink! And Elijah looked up, and behold, at his head was a cake of barley, and a vessel of water. And he arose, and ate, and drank, and turning back, he fell asleep. And the angel of the Lord turned back a second time, and touched him, and said unto him: Arise, eat and drink, for the journey is great for thee. And he arose, and ate, and drank; and he went in the strength of that food forty days and forty nights unto Mount Horeb. And he entered there into a cave, and lodged in it. And behold, the word of the Lord came unto him, and the Lord said unto him: What art thou doing here, Elijah? And Elijah said: With zeal have I been zealous for the Lord God Almighty, because the sons of Israel have forsaken Thee; they have dug down Thine altars, and slaughtered Thy prophets with the sword, and I only am left, and they seek to take my life! And the angel said: Go forth tomorrow, and stand on the mountain before the Lord; and behold, the Lord will pass by. And a great and strong wind tearing the mountains, and breaking in pieces the rocks before the Lord; but the Lord was not in the wind. And after the wind an earthquake, but the Lord was not in the earthquake. And after the earthquake a fire, but the Lord was not in the fire. And after the fire the voice of a gentle breeze; and there was the Lord. And it came to pass, when Elijah heard it, that he wrapped his face in his mantle, and went out, and stood at the entrance of the cave. And behold, there came a voice unto him, and said: What art thou doing here, Elijah? And Elijah said: With zeal have I been zealous for the Lord God Almighty, because the sons of Israel have forsaken Thy covenant; they have dug down Thine altars, and slaughtered Thy prophets with the sword, and I only am left, and they seek to take my life! And the Lord said unto him: Go, return on thy way, and thou shalt go by the way of the wilderness of Damascus; and thou shalt anoint Hazael to be king in Syria; and Jehu the son of Nimshi shalt thou anoint to be king over Israel; and Elisha the son of Shaphat shalt thou anoint to be prophet in thy room.

*The Reading from the Fourth Book of Kings (Chapter 2):*[^462]
It came to pass in those days, Elijah found Elisha the son of Shaphat, and he was plowing with oxen. And Elijah went unto him, and Elijah cast his mantle upon him. And Elisha left the oxen, and ran after Elijah, and ministered unto him. And it came to pass, when the Lord was about to take up Elijah in a whirlwind as into heaven, that Elijah and Elisha went to Gilgal. And Elijah said unto Elisha: Tarry here, I pray thee, for the Lord has sent me unto the Jordan. And Elisha said: As the Lord lives, and as thy soul lives, I will not leave thee! And they both went on. And fifty men of the sons of the prophets went, and stood opposite afar off, and they both stood by the Jordan. And Elijah took his mantle, and wrapped it together, and smote the waters, and the water was divided hither and thither, and they both went over on dry ground. And it came to pass, when they were gone over, that Elijah said unto Elisha: Ask what I shall do for thee, before I be taken away from thee. And Elisha said: Let a double portion of the spirit that is in thee be upon me! And Elijah said: Thou hast asked a hard thing; nevertheless, if thou seest me when I am taken from thee, it shall be so unto thee; but if thou seest me not, it shall not be so. And it came to pass, as they went on, and talked, that behold, there appeared a chariot of fire, and horses of fire, and parted them both asunder; and Elijah was taken up by an earthquake as into heaven. And Elisha saw it, and he cried: My father, my father, the chariot of Israel, and the horsemen thereof! And he saw him no more; and Elisha took hold of his own clothes, and rent them in two pieces. And Elisha took up the mantle of Elijah that fell from upon him, and Elisha turned back, and stood upon the bank of the Jordan. And Elisha took the mantle of Elijah that fell from upon him, and smote the waters, and they did not divide. And Elisha said: Where then is the Lord God of Elijah, even He?[^463] And Elisha smote the waters a second time, and the waters were divided, and he went over on dry ground.

*At the Litiya, the sticheron of the temple, and of the saint, idiomela, Tone 4:*

*Sticheron 1:* A partaker of incorrupt union wast thou, O blessed prophet! In God my Savior, having divided the Jordan by prayer, thou didst pass over on dry ground; and upon a fiery chariot as unto the heavens wast thou caught up, leaving a double grace unto Elisha. Therefore we pray thee: though thou wast caught up in the body, yet part not from us in spirit, and ever pray unto Christ that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron 2:* A light-radiant cloud, O divine prophet, didst thou appear, distilling the rain of the knowledge of God unto all the faithful, watering richly with the wine of gladness all who with faith flee unto thy divine temple! Therefore we pray thee, O divine prophet and seer Elijah: grant victories over enemies unto our King,[^464] and unto our souls great mercy!

*Sticheron 3:* With sovereign mind didst thou subdue the flesh, O divine prophet Elijah, zealously placing ascents as steps in thy heart; therefore, having also rebuked the unjust king, thou didst proclaim the ban of God's wrath; and the shameful priests, being unrighteous, thou didst destroy with a knife; and thou didst teach all clearly to cry aloud: Truly, one is God, Whom Elijah preached!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O decree of an ungodly king! O fast full of blood and iniquity![^465] He commanded a fast that he might commit murder, and immediately Naboth the Jezreelite was slain. What then, O prophet, didst thou say unto these things? For thus says the Lord: Because without justice thou hast slain a man of Israel, so also shall thy blood be shed, and harlots shall wash in thy blood! But by the prayers of Thy prophet and preacher, O Christ, deliver our souls from such inhumanity, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the vine...

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Elijah the marvelous prophet, having enlightened his mind with radiance, became wholly divine; and beholding the unrighteous judgment of the ungodly king, he was indignant; therefore by the judgment of God he sent a sentence against him, and likewise the queen, being unmerciful and avaricious, he delivered over unto the judgment of God. But by the prayers of Thy prophet Elijah, O Christ, save us all, as Thou art compassionate!

*Verse:* Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His Name.[^466]

*Sticheron 2:* How long, O Lord, shall the way of the ungodly prosper?[^467] didst thou say, O prophet; Thou hast planted them, and they took root, and brought forth the fruit of iniquity; truly they themselves also perished evilly in their ungodliness. They will cry aloud unto Thee, and Thou wilt not hear. Why hast Thou shown me iniquity, to judge the lawbreakers?

*Verse:* Thou art a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek.[^468]

*Sticheron 3:* From perils and tribulations and grievous assaults deliver by thy prayers, O honorable and great Elijah among the prophets, those who celebrate thy venerable ascent; that we, the Christ-named people, may ever honor and call thee blessed, O glorious prophet!

*Glory... Tone 6:*

O prophet and preacher of Christ, from the throne of majesty thou departest never, and to every suffering one thou art ever present; serving in the heights, thou blessest the universe, glorified everywhere: entreat cleansing for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Creator and Redeemer...

*Troparion (Troparion), Tone 4:*
An angel in the flesh, the foundation of the prophets, the second forerunner of the coming of Christ, glorious Elijah: having sent down grace from on high unto Elisha to drive away diseases and cleanse lepers; therefore unto those who honor him he pours forth healings!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from before the ages...

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the troparion twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
Having mounted a fiery chariot as upon wings of the wind, O divine prophet, thou dost make today a most glorious journey unto the heavens, having left from on high a double grace and mantle unto Elisha. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We all entreat thee as Mother of God, who truly wast revealed as Virgin even after childbearing, who with love flee unto thy goodness: for thee do we sinners have as advocate; thee have we acquired as salvation in tribulations, the only all-immaculate one!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Thy divine temple pours forth healings without grudging unto all, O most marvelous prophet; with marvelous Elisha praying together unto Christ, to send down peace upon the world, and unto the people who honor you great mercy! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Whithersoever one is saved, thither also he rightly flees; and what other refuge is such as thou, O Mother of God, who shelterest our souls?

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Having been a lover of wisdom, O all-honorable prophet, thou didst openly rebuke the unjust king acting unrighteously; and didst persuade men to cry aloud: Truly one is God, Who is preached by Elijah! From whence also Jezebel regarding rapacity and regarding love of gold thou didst rebuke. O prophet and forerunner of the coming of Christ, Elijah of great renown, entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who faithfully glorify thine ascent!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As Virgin and only one among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all we generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead took up His abode in thee, and as an infant with milk dost thou nourish the Creator and Lord! Therefore the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy birthgiving, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of offenses unto those who with faith bow down before thy most holy birthgiving!

*Then the gradual hymns, the First Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Thou art a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek.[^469]
*Verse:* The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet.[^470]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 14.*[^471]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 4:*
Upon a fiery chariot, upon a cloud wast thou taken up, and into a radiant realm wast thou translated, O Elijah the Thesbite, having put to shame the shameful prophets! O thou who by a word didst bind the heavens, so also loose our offenses by thy prayers unto the Lord, and save our souls!

*Canon of the Mother of God with the heirmos on 6, Tone 8: "Having traversed the water..." And of the prophet, two canons on 8.*
*First Canon, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Let us sing unto the Lord, Who of old guided the people in the sea, and plunged Pharaoh with all his army into it, a song of victory, for He has been glorified!

*Troparion 1:* Desiring to see the fire-breathing power of the wonders of Elijah, the strength of the Spirit of the fire-radiant and honorable tongue, we are worthily gathered together!

*Troparion 2:* Open my lips, O God-bearer, and the tightness and halting tongue of a perplexed speech, by the operation of the Spirit that is in thee, and make it clear for the singing of thy wonders!

*Troparion 3:* Above nature dost Thou grant, O Word, unto those who obey Thy honorable commandments, and Thou subjectest the doors of the rain by the Spirit, causing the word to be confirmed!

*Theotokion:* Let us sing unto the Lord Who was born of the Virgin, and Who renewed the world, and for her sake granted us divine life, a song of victory, for He has been glorified!

*Another Canon (Canon 2), the work of the Hieromonk Pachomius. Its acrostic: "I sing praise unto great Elijah."[^472] Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having traversed the water as on dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion 1:* An instrument didst thou appear, struck by the divine plectrum,[^473] O most marvelous prophet, hymning the all-knowing God unto the Avenger Who renders righteous vengeance unto all according to their works!

*Troparion 2:* Not enduring deceit, O marvelous prophet, thou didst rebuke the king acting unrighteously in transgression; therefore the judgment of God, full of wrath, didst thou bring upon him as condemnation!

*Troparion 3:* O prophet and forerunner of grace, divine Elijah, having rebuked the lawbreaking Ahab, the ungodly king, thou didst destroy the shameful priests as unhallowed!

*Theotokion:* Let us, the faithful, worthily hasten to hymn in songs the Mother of Christ, as having born the Savior of all, Who reigns over life and death!

*Katavasia: "I will open my mouth..."*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit rejoiced when singing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous more than Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 1 (Canon 1):* An excellent provider art Thou, O prophet, unto Whom the ravens brought thy food, the only One Who fills every living thing with favor; unto Whom we all cry: Thou art our God, and there is none holy more than Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 2:* Thanks be unto the Benefactor and Ruler, Who nourished the widow and the prophet with ineffable providence; unto Whom we all cry: Thou art our God, and there is none holy more than Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion 3:* He Who closed the cloud-bearing heavens from raining, made the remnant of food in destitute Zarephath to dew forth continually; and thou didst cry aloud: There is none holy more than Thee, O Lord!

*Theotokion:* Thou alone above all who have lived from of old wast accounted worthy of great things, and things surpassing nature: for God, Whom all creation cannot contain, thou didst receive in thy womb, and didst incarnate. Therefore we all honor thee as Mother of God!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Lord, Maker of the vault of heaven, and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 1:* All dost thou mercifully instruct, O prophet, and all dost thou have mercy upon who with faith flee into thy temple; and they who ask help from thee have by no means failed!

*Troparion 2:* Ahab the ungodly king didst thou rebuke, O divine prophet; and as a transgressor of the law, by the judgment of God didst thou condemn him!

*Troparion 3:* Shameless queen Jezebel sought to kill thee, O most divine prophet, because upon her unhallowed priests thou didst worthily bring the sentence of death thou didst bring upon them!

*Theotokion:* The place of the radiance of glory, and the spiritual table, O pure one, which received the Bread Christ, the Life of all, do we hymn, O Sovereign Lady!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^474]
The fountain of wonders, the adornment of the prophets, let us all the faithful hymn in song, Elijah the Thesbite; for being yet immortal in the flesh, he confirms the resurrection of the dead as one dead; therefore having boldness toward God, he grants healings unto those who ask with faith. And he fervently prays unto Christ, to grant remission of offenses unto those who with love celebrate his holy memory! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having fallen into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of mine innumerable offenses: and as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O all-immaculate one, pray fervently unto Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants, who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-immaculate Mother of God; ever entreating Him: to grant remission of offenses unto those who worthily hymn thy glory!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion 1 (Canon 1):* A root-cutter of malice truly didst thou appear, O wise prophet, and a planter of virtue; therefore we call thee blessed!

*Troparion 2:* The widow upbraided thee with words regarding the death of food, O prophet, having assured the resurrection of the child!

*Troparion 3:* Plainly did he prefigure the Trinitarian glory: by breathing three times, he restored the child alive unto his mother!

*Troparion 4:* How wast thou kindled, burning with divine fire and with zeal, O prophet, rebuking the king committing iniquities!

*Theotokion:* We pray thee, O pure one, who didst conceive God without seed, ever to pray for thy servants!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, Thy report, and I was afraid, for by an ineffable counsel, being the ever-existing God, Thou didst come forth from the Virgin, having become incarnate. Glory to Thy condescension, O Christ; glory to Thy power!

*Troparion 1:* The Lord crowned thee as a most wise prophet, and granted thee grace to foretell things to come as though present; therefore now beholding the fulfillment of thy words: with praises continually do we call thee blessed as a true prophet!

*Troparion 2:* Inasmuch as the soul of Thy prophet was abundantly adorned with humility of mind, O Christ God, in so much did it burn with zeal above fire, destroying the shameful priests!

*Troparion 3:* With face uncovered as in a mirror dost thou stand before the face of Christ, O most marvelous prophet; from thence do thou mercifully visit us, that we all with praises may call thee blessed as a prophet!

*Theotokion:* Having acquired thee, O cause of our salvation, as our hope and confirmation, and an indestructible wall of salvation, we are delivered from every malice!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, in the light of Thy commandments guide us: for beside Thee we know no other God!

*Troparion 1 (Canon 1):* The paternal law showed thee forth as an all-true champion, a worker of wonders, turning back the elements of nature, O Elijah, and consuming the soaked sacrifice with waters!

*Troparion 2:* Excellently didst thou put to shame the prophets of foul shame, as an honorable minister of truth, O all-blessed Elijah, plainly outlining the Trinitarian power!

*Troparion 3:* Officiating as priest by the word of grace, O Elijah, thou didst slaughter the shameful priests with thine innocent hands, as one burning with zeal like fire!

*Theotokion:* Unto thee who didst bear Christ the Creator of all, we cry aloud: Rejoice, O pure one! Rejoice, thou who didst cause the light to shine forth upon us! Rejoice, thou who didst contain the uncontainable God!

*Another Canon (Canon 2). Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee!

*Troparion 1:* Having truly acquired the never-setting radiance that shone forth of divine grace, and a most divine mind, O prophet Elijah, thou dost stir up the pious unto song!

*Troparion 2:* Who will not marvel at thy divine zeal, O all-honorable prophet? Or who will not hymn Christ, Who granted thee such strength?

*Troparion 3:* By God, O prophet, wast thou adorned with prophecy, O most marvelous Elijah, being entirely united from thence unto God; rebuking deceit, thou didst bring the Israelites unto the knowledge of God!

*Theotokion:* Having born the Sun never-setting, O Mother of God, illumine me who am wholly darkened by passions, and fill me with divine light.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The final abyss of sins hath encompassed me; but like the Prophet Jonah, O Lord, O Lord, lead up my life from corruption.

*Troparion:*
A pillar of piety and of pure life, a planter of purity wast thou, and a peer of the angels, O marvelous, God-bearing Elijah.

*Troparion:*
The wrath of the prophet-slaying woman[^475] having terrified thee, who hadst received power to bind and to loose the flow of the rains, O marvelous Elijah, causes thee to flee.

*Troparion:*
Bending thy knees, thou didst hold the elevated eye of the mind, working a loosening by thy distinguished supplication, and with showers thou didst satisfy the furrows of the earth.

*Theotokion:*
The bush on Sinai, approaching the fire unconsumed, prefigured the ever-virgin Mother, O all-immaculate Mother of God Mary.

*Second Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for my iniquities are many, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparion:*
Bringing suppliant singing unto God, O all-honorable prophet, on behalf of all those fleeing unto thy divine temple, that we may ever honor thee as a great protector.

*Troparion:*
The mind of man will not attain to praise thy life worthily, O prophet; for thou wast magnified above the noetic ranks, beholding the Transfiguration of Christ.[^476]

*Troparion:*
The Lord and Creator of all appeared unto us according to thy prophecy, O blessed Elijah, calling all men unto His knowledge, as the Lover of mankind.

*Theotokion:*
O pure Mother, vessel of virginity, cleanse my mind from the darkening of passions, and fill my heart with grace and righteousness, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
O prophet and foreseer of the great works of our God, O Elijah of great renown, who by thy word didst halt the water-flowing clouds: pray on our behalf unto the only Lover of mankind.

*Ikos:*
Beholding the great lawlessness of men, yet the great love of God toward mankind, the Prophet Elijah was troubled, being provoked to wrath, and he stirred up unmerciful words unto the Merciful One: "Be Thou provoked to wrath," he cried, "against those who have rejected Thee, O righteous Judge!" But he in no wise moved the compassion of the Good One to torment those who reject Him; for He ever awaits the repentance of all, being the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The bush unconsumed by fire on the mountain, and the dew-bearing Chaldean furnace, clearly prefigured thee, O Bride of God; for thou didst receive in a material womb the divine immaterial Fire unconsumed. Therefore unto Him Who was born of thee we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Most gloriously clad with divine, prefigured power by forty days' sustenance,[^477] O marvelous Elijah, thou didst journey a long way. Therefore on Horeb thou didst say: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
A gentle and delicate breeze revealed the Lord unto thee, O Elijah, who wast zealous for God the Almighty: not a stormy wind, nor an earthquake, nor fearful fire.[^478] Therefore unto the gentle Jesus thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast accounted worthy of theophany and prophecy, even as great Moses, anointing prophets and kings by the Spirit, O marvelous Elijah. And having become a spectator of the glory of Christ on Tabor, thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Without seed thou dost bring forth the unoriginate and eternal Lord, the Son of the Father, His Wisdom and Power, in the last times, O all-pure one, beyond nature in the flesh, for the renewal of those on earth. Therefore unto Him Who was born of thee we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Second Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths having arrived from Judea in Babylon once of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of the fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
The men of Israel who came unto thee with the captain of fifty,[^479] O divine Elijah, thou didst consume with fire from heaven as being unworthy.

*Troparion:*
Illumined by the vision of God, thou wast worthily a most divine prophet, O all-honorable Elijah, and thou didst utterly abolish the sacrifices of Baal.[^480]

*Troparion:*
As the Holy Spirit granted thee to prophesy, O all-honorable Prophet Elijah, thou didst foretell concerning the coming of the Son of God, and thou erredst not, O all-blessed one, unto Whom we sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Upon the shameful priests who unrighteously ate from Jezebel's table,[^481] truly by the judgment of God and by the prophet's hand, the poison of death was deservedly vomited forth.

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O Mother of God, held fast by the depth of sins, and snatch me from all malice of enemies, that being saved, I may magnify thy protection.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The fire was affrighted at the pious youths of congruent soul and immaculate body, and the nurtured, untroubled fire withdrew in boundless matter; while the flame continually died down, an eternal hymn was chanted: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Ahab found an extraordinary and all-destructive rebuff of his line through the rebuke of the prophet, a vile torment for foul murder.[^482] But the Tishbite, from a fire-inspired mind, sang a hymn unto the Giver of life: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Fire rains down from heaven for thee, consuming, O Elijah, twice fifty possessed men,[^483] as an extraordinary minister of God. For unto Him Who holds ever-living life, in a manner befitting God thou didst cry aloud in song: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ revealed thee as an initiate of mysteries, as a planter of purity and a pre-divine sprout of virginity, having shown forth unto thee on Tabor the incomprehensible light of Godhead in His flesh at the divine Incarnation, as thou didst cry: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
All-rich became the womb of the Mother of God, for having received the Word made dense with flesh, compounded of earthly form from her according to hypostasis, she appeared as the city of God wherein the Most High was well-pleased to dwell. Unto Whom we sing: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

*Second Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn, praise ye and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
He Who knows all things as God, the understanding of things future and about to come to pass, He entrusts unto thee, O Elijah, as being faithful, and shows forth things far off as being near.

*Troparion:*
Having given wings to thy mind with the boldness of piety, O blessed prophet, thou foretellest things distant as being near, O all-honorable prophet.

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed thy mind from all defilement, O divine prophet, thou wast thereby entirely holy, guiding all unto the divine light.

*Theotokion:*
The Word Whom thou didst bear beyond word, O pure Mother of God, entreat on behalf of us who faithfully honor thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Him Who shone forth before the sun as God the Illuminator, Who came unto us bodily from the virginal loins, ineffably incarnate O blessed all-immaculate one, thee do we magnify as the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Having lived an untroubled life among men upon earth, the Tishbite crossed the stream of the Jordan with his mantle;[^484] raising the charioteer, he accomplished an unreckoned, strange journey by the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with the zeal of piety, the Tishbite is taken up in a fiery chariot; and he cast down his mantle,[^485] which could not be hidden, wherewith Elisha having clothed himself, received divine grace.

*Troparion:*
In a tabernacle the God-seer Tishbite with Moses beholds what eye hath not seen and ear hath not heard, and hath not entered into the heart of earth-born man: the incarnate Lord, the Almighty, on Tabor.

*Theotokion:*
Removal of the ancient sudden condemnation, and rectification of the foremother, who was the cause of our race's adoption unto God, the bridge unto the Creator: thee, O Mother of God, do we magnify.

*Second Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly we confess thee as the Mother of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless hosts.

*Troparion:*
Deified by the immaterial radiance of the life-originating Trinity, O Prophet Elijah: therefore do we all bring thee unto God as an intercessor for peace, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
We have known thee as an angel upon earth, O divine prophet, and in heaven entirely as a man of God; even as the Lord Himself said: "Since thou, Elijah, art a harsh man, and canst not bear with Israel when they sin, come thou up unto Me, and I will go down below."[^486]

*Troparion:*
Upon earth, O divine prophet, thou didst live as one bodiless; and now being borne up unto the heavens, thou makest chorus with the angelic choirs: look down from above upon us who sing of thee, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou bearest in thine arms God Who bears all things, and thou feedest the Nourisher Who became like unto us, O pure one: Whom entreat to grant unto thy people deliverance from all dire evils.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "O Light Immutable" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*
The light upon the fiery four-horse chariot, which revealed thee, O armed Elijah, as traversing the heavens, did not consume thee utterly; for by a fiery tongue thou didst draw fire downwards, and didst dry up the rain-clouds.

*Glory:*
Thou didst ascend unto the celestial spheres by a fiery chariot: now looking down upon those who faithfully celebrate thy memory, as being near the Light, give Thou unto us noetic light.

*Both now, Theotokion:*

Thou, O Mother of God, wast the cause of the good things given by God unto the world: but even now pray for the common salvation unto God Who is easily reconciled.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 6, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ вы́съ нарече́мъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
When thou, O marvelous prophet, didst mingle thyself with God through virtue and pure life, receiving authority, thou didst measure creation according to understanding, and by thy will thou didst shut the gates of rain, and from on high thou didst bring down fire, and didst destroy thine enemies: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron 2:*
Inflamed with the zeal of the Lord, thou didst openly rebuke lawless kings; thou didst ardently put the shameful priests to death; thou didst kindle fire in water, O blessed one; thou didst pour forth food not worked by man; and the waters of the Jordan thou didst part with thy mantle: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron 3:*
When thou, O prophet, didst show forth upon earth the life which is truly in heaven, enriched with the hypostatic life abiding in thee, thou didst raise a dead boy by thy breathing;[^487] and apart from death thou abidest for better years: having mounted a fiery chariot, lifted up on high, pray that our souls may be saved.

*Other Stichera, Tone 1, Automela by Germanus:*

*Sticheron 4:*
The zealous Elijah, master of the passions, was borne today in the air, initiate of universal salvation, beginner of mysteries! O what honorable glory wherewith the blameless prophet and distinguished adornment of prophets was accounted worthy! For he, an angel in the flesh and a bodiless man, was shown forth by his achievements. Praising him, let us say: Protect us, O wise one, in the Day of Judgment.

*Sticheron 5:*
With spiritual hymns let us all praise the prophets of Christ: for Elijah the Tishbite was a heaven-traverser, and by his mantle Elisha received double grace from God; and radiant luminaries they appeared unto the universe, praying unceasingly on behalf of our souls.

*Sticheron 6:*
Today, ye faithful, let us honor Davidically with hymns the prophet of the Lord, Elijah the Tishbite, and the most radiant zealot. For he with his tongue shut heaven like a skin, and made the fruitful earth unfruitful. O most marvelous wonder! An earthly man gave not the heavens to rain down rain! O wonder! A corruptible man clothed himself in incorruption, and ran up unto heaven in a fiery chariot: and having granted double grace unto Elisha by his mantle, rebuking the king and the disobedient people he wasted with famine; he put all the shameful priests to shame, and raised the widow's son by his word. By his supplications, O Christ our God, preserve in peace our Orthodox king,[^488] and grant him victories of conquest over enemies.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
The leaders of the prophets and all-radiant luminaries of the universe, Elijah and Elisha, let us ye faithful honor in hymns, and joyfully let us cry out unto Christ: O compassionate Lord, grant unto Thy people, by the supplications of Thy prophets, remission of sins and great mercy.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O Lady, receive the prayer of thy servants...

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy:*
*Beatitudes from the first canon, Ode 3, and from the second, Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Thou art a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek.
*Verse:* The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet.
*Epistle:* Epistle of James, Pericope 57 (James 5:10–20).
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His name.
*Gospel:* Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 14 (Luke 4:22–30).
*Communion Verse:* In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.

*July 21: Our Venerable Fathers Simeon, the Fool for Christ's Sake, and John His Fellow-Faster; and the Holy Prophet Ezekiel.*

*Stichera of the Venerable Ones, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Ye settled in the wilderness with one mind, O blessed ones, and bodily movements by fasting and prayers and radiant visions ye clearly withered away; and ye illumined your hearts, having made them acceptable by spiritual tradition, O Simeon and John, foundation of monastics.[^489]

*Sticheron 2:*
By foolish simulations, thou didst infatuate the sage of malice, O blessed one, performing most glorious deeds, working signs, driving away demons, enlightening those who are in the sinful night, O father: and an unwavering mind in the midst of tumults thou didst preserve thou didst preserve, O Simeon, having received dispassion from God.

*Sticheron 3:*
Humble in mind, merciful, God-befitting, and filled with love, thou wast revealed, O God-inspired one, contrite and gentle, walking about upon earth as an angel, having a heavenly life: therefore there rested upon thee, as upon purity, O blessed Simeon, the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.

*Other Stichera of the Prophet, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
O marvelous Ezekiel, the lightning surpassing the mind and the ineffable glory of the Lord, O all-wise one, borne by divine Cherubim, ascending and hymned by myriads of mouths, by purity of mind thou didst noetically behold,[^490] having pre-purified thy soul and body, O God-blessed one.

*Sticheron 2:*
O God-received Ezekiel, as an imitator of Christ, thou didst endure the torment of another's debt,[^491] being grievously examined, prefiguring the salvation and deliverance about to come unto the world for the sake of the precious Cross, O God-revealed one: Whom entreat that we all may partake thereof, who sing of thee.

*Sticheron 3:*
O God-inspired Ezekiel, having received the scroll from the hand of the Almighty, clearly sweeter than honey unto thy mouth,[^492] a sacredly-revealed God-seer and God-beholding theologian wast thou, imparting divine understanding unto the assembly that provoked God to wrath, O glorious one.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, enlightenment of souls, forgiveness of those who sin, correction of the negligent; rejoice, consolation and joy, helper of the afflicted; rejoice, O good one, healing of infirm souls; rejoice, reconciliation unto God and mediatrix, who art holier than all the Seraphim, O pure one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
"What is this spectacle seen by mine eyes, O Master? Thou Who holdest all creation art lifted up upon the Tree, and diest, granting life unto all," the Mother of God weeping said, when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and man, being lifted up upon the Cross.

*Dismissal Troparion, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, take not Thy mercy away from us, but by their supplications direct our life in peace.[^493]

*Troparion of the Prophet, Tone 2:*
Celebrating the memory of Thy Prophet Ezekiel, O Lord: therefore we pray Thee, save our souls.

*Canon of the Venerable Ones, the Composition of Theophanes, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The ancient Israel walked dry-shod through the Red Sea deep, and by Moses' arms outstretched crosswise, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
By communion with God, O venerable one, thou becamest light, and unto Him didst thou attain, O Simeon, by extreme desire; the fulfillment of thy good hopes, O father, hast thou received.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with desire for God, thou didst leave the world, having found as fellow-traveler, O father, the glorious John, with whom rejoicing thou didst traverse the saving path.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to be enriched with inexhaustible things, O wise ones, temporary wealth and glory, passing food, and worldly dignity, ye valiantly rejected.

*Theotokion:*
Having perceived thee to be fair and chosen and all-honorable, O all-immaculate one, the Son of God became thy Son: make them sons by grace, who honor thee as the Mother of God.

*Second Canon of the Prophet, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
Unto me who desire to praise Ezekiel Thy prophet, grant a gift, O Benefactor, and spiritual grace.

*Troparion:*
Having divine ascents, sacred Ezekiel, spectator of ineffable things, is illumined with the glory of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having directed thy life, and having subjected bodily wantonness unto the mind, thou wast revealed as a God-received prophet.

*Theotokion:*
Wholly enlightened, thou dost prophesy of the Virgin, O God-inspired one, as the much-hymned door turned toward the east.[^494]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and firm foundation.

*Troparion:*
Zealously didst thou strip thyself for noetic activity, O Simeon, conquering the hostile hosts by the divine assistance of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
With wounds of prayer and blows of self-control, O venerable father John, thou didst wound the Egypt of passions.

*Troparion:*
Thy fasters put the enemy to death, O Jesus, having joyfully desired Thy life-bearing mortification, O Master.

*Theotokion:*
Him Who delivers men from the dire fall of disobedience, the Cause of all, O pure and all-immaculate one, thou didst bear all-immaculate one, thou didst bear.

*Second Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the firm foundation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired passionate mortification, thou seest, O glorious one, the resurrection of the dead prefigured, as a prophet.[^495]

*Troparion:*
Being merciful unto His creation, God revealed Thee, O God-revealed one, as divine understanding unto the errant.

*Troparion:*
The radiance of Godhead, seated upon the Cherubim, which appeared unto Thee, O speaker of God, illumined Thee, O glorious one.[^496]

*Theotokion:*
A field thy prophet, O pure one, with clairvoyant eyes beholds, preserved for virginity, an entrance for Thee, O Benefactor.

*Kontakion of the Prophet, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Thou wast revealed as God's prophet, O marvelous Ezekiel; thou didst announce unto all the Incarnation of the Lord, of this Lamb and Creator, the Son of God Who hath appeared unto the ages.

*Sessional Hymn of the Venerable Ones, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (Собезнача́льное сло́во):*
Illumined with divine concord, ye walked in the paths leading unto divine splendors, faithfully, O venerable fathers; and having escaped the assaults of enemies, ye were revealed as sons of light and day, O John and Simeon, God-blessed ones.

*Glory, of the Prophet, Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the beauty" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
The Incarnation of the Word thou didst prefigure, illumined by the Divine Spirit, O great among the divine prophets, God-inspired Ezekiel: therefore as a prophet we sacredly bless thee, and we celebrate thy memory today, crying unto thee with one accord, O wise one: Pray unto Christ to save our souls.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Inconceivable and incomprehensible, O Lady graced of God, is the fearful divine mystery accomplished in thee: for having conceived the Seedless One, thou didst bear Him, clothed in flesh from thy most pure blood: Whom ever pray, O pure one, as thy Son, that our souls may be saved.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The unwedded pure Virgin and Thy Mother, O Christ, seeing Thee hanging dead upon the Cross, wept maternally and said: "What hath the lawless and ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I hymn Thy divine condescension."

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Seeing Thee lifted up upon the Cross, the Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Canon of the Venerable Ones. Troparion:*
Having joined yourselves unto the path of righteousness, entering into the divine sheepfolds of the God-bearing Nikon,[^497] O venerable ones, ye were enriched with the saving radiance of his instructions.

*Troparion:*
Having seen them of one form and piously minded, the God-bearing and venerable one adorned them with divine discourses and with divine monastic habit.

*Troparion:*
Finding thy heart a purified tablet, O father, the grace of the Holy Spirit inscribed perfectly dispassion, faith, and unhypocritical love.

*Theotokion:*
Seated upon the exalted Cherubic throne, He had thee as a throne, resting in thine arms, O Mary, Bride of God, our highly-glorified God.

*Second Canon of the Prophet. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:*
Upon thee was the hand of thy Master and Almighty,[^498] teaching ineffable things, and enlightening thee with divine commands.

*Troparion:*
Having been accounted worthy to behold dread divine visions, thou didst fall upon thy face, and heardest the voice of the Holy Spirit.

*Troparion:*
The scroll given unto thy mouth from the hand of the Lord, sweetening thy heart extraordinarily, accomplished thee as a divine prophet.

*Theotokion:*
Ezekiel beheld the radiant divine gate, through which the Lord passed: keep this shut.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, didst come as light into the world, the holy Light that turns from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.

*Canon of the Venerable Ones. Troparion:*
Having received the most radiant beam, O John, thou didst appear as the sun, driving away demonic darkness, O richly-renowned one.

*Troparion:*
Winged by desire for Thee, O Christ, the venerable ones wisely shook off bodily desires like the foam of the sea.

*Troparion:*
Having strengthened their souls by divine power, ye settled in the wilderness, putting passions to waste, O God-wise fathers.

*Theotokion:*
Corruptible flow ceased, for the Virgin incorruptibly brought forth God the Word, beyond nature and word, remaining a Virgin.

*Second Canon of the Prophet. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know none other.

*Troparion:*
O blessed, God-received Ezekiel, [calling] all sinners to turn thou didst instruct by thy words.

*Troparion:*
In all things thou didst resemble the Prophet of the Lord;[^499] for thou didst pray concerning the transgressions of others.

*Troparion:*
The Egyptian tongues cannot praise thee; for thou hast been adorned with super-celestial brightness.

*Theotokion:*
As a divine door Ezekiel beheld thee, through which the Giver of light and the Lord shone forth unto us.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demons' blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
Stretching thy whole mind unto God by unceasing meditations, thou didst not spare carnal love, mediating for thyself the adoption of good things, O Simeon.

*Troparion:*
Neither defilement nor blemish didst thou receive, O blessed one, dwelling in the midst of the world; but rather by honorable prayers thou cleansest the soul's defilements of those who run unto thee.

*Troparion:*
Being higher than carnal thoughts, thou wast not wounded in thy mind when stripping thyself naked in the midst of women;[^500] for thou hadst clothed thyself with the garment of dispassion, O Simeon.

*Theotokion:*
With lips and thoughts and heart, O all-immaculate one, we proclaim thee as the Mother of God; for through thee were we reconciled unto God, who were cast away before by the transgression of our forefather.

*Second Canon of the Prophet. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly-merciful Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast accounted worthy to behold God, O marvelous prophet, having cleansed thyself, O all-blessed one, from every material apparition.

*Troparion:*
Having become above the flesh, thou wast lifted up by the transcendent power of the Spirit, and wast revealed full of pure light.

*Troparion:*
Having an all-honorable life, sending forth heavenly choral singing, thou beholdest the Cherubic Almighty.[^501]

*Theotokion:*
A Mother and Virgin in truth, in an image thou didst behold as a most radiant door, a mediatress unto God and men.

*Kontakion of the Venerable Ones, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm and divinely-inspired preachers" (Тве́рдыя):*
Him Who appeared bodiless in the flesh, and shone forth supermundanely with virtues, Simeon equal-to-the-angels and God-bearing, let us praise, and with him John ever-memorable, in faith and love: as praying unceasingly unto the Lord on behalf of us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite children, with love of piety purer than when burned by flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Held fast by Christ-like divine love to save others, O father, gladly of thine own will didst thou surrender thyself wholly: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Thou receivest the food brought unto thee, O father; this food thou providest unto all who obediently receive thy commands and abandon the sin that drags downward, O blessed one.[^502]

*Troparion:*
Following the traditions of the fathers, O wise father, thou didst command those who cry to flee the impious ruin of Origen:[^503] Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, sanctified divine habitation of the Most High! For through thee joy was given, O Mother of God, unto those who cry: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady.

*Second Canon of the Prophet. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The children coming from Judea in Babylon of old trampled underfoot the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
An animate temple of God wast thou revealed, O God-bearer, O all-blessed one; and the measure of church buildings thou wast noetically taught,[^504] singing in the Spirit: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
Being wholly in ecstasy, thou wast once translated into the Holy City;[^505] and thou wast instructed by the revelation of divine mysteries, learning to sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as a priest of the Most High God, O worthy of marvel, thou offerest sacrifices and supplications unto the Master, as the people stand by and sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Theotokion:*
The transcendent Word, wishing to save human nature, having passed through the virginal door and clothed Himself with flesh from her: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Having stretched out his hands, Daniel stopped the gapings of the lions in the den; and the children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
A radiant star wast thou, leading unto life those who are in the darkness of passions; for thou makest impure women to live chastely; and thou drivest away grievous infirmities, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Sound in mind, covered with the veil of wise wonders,[^506] thou didst capture many to become fools for the Lord's sake, knowing that it is to be scoffed at to be scoffed at, crying with a purest soul: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Truly the only Son, the Lord of glory, worthily honors thee, O venerable one, when thou art carried forth and committed unto the tomb, with angelic radiant praises,[^507] as thou criest: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord Who delivers us shone forth from thee, O pure Virgin; Whom pray, O Lady, to enlighten those who hymn thee, and to deliver from tribulation us who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Second Canon of the Prophet. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The King of heaven, Whom angelic hosts hymn: praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Adorned sacredly with prophetic priesthood and prophecies, thou shinest, O prophet, singing of Christ the Giver of life unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Accept, as good, Ezekiel who bends his knees in prayer for the world, the beholder of Thy glory, that we may hymn Thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Inflamed with divine desire, thou wast instructed in mysteries beyond the mind, O Ezekiel, crying: Thee, O Christ, we exalt above all unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
Beholding from the night the dawn of forgiveness, the virginity rising early, Ezekiel cried unto the Lord: Thee we exalt above all unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The cornerstone was cut out without hands from the unhewn mountain that art thou, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the parted natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Having dedicated yourselves fervently with your whole heart unto the Trinity, and having graciously received beams therefrom, ye became light-bearing, and dance with the angels.

*Troparion:*
United by divine desire and enlightened by self-control, ye have already come into communion with the spoken-of good things, and have found blessed delight, O God-blessed ones.

*Troparion:*
Your soul received for toils and sweats a rejoicing that hath no end, wherein feeding yourselves, O venerable Simeon and glorious John, remember us unceasingly.

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O thou who didst bear the Deliverer and Benefactor of all! Dispel the clouds of my soul, O holy Lady, cloud of light, and make me strong against the passions that wage war upon me.

*Second Canon of the Prophet. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The birthgiving of the Ever-Virgin revealed unto the lawgiver upon the mountain in fire and bush, for the salvation of us faithful, with unceasing hymns with unceasing hymns we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Shining with the beam of piety, O prophet, as of old He appeared unto Moses in the fire and the bush, thou also wast accounted worthy to behold Him, mystically teaching thee ineffable things.

*Troparion:*
A priest wast thou shown forth, and an initiate of mysteries, and a lawgiver, and an all-wise prophet, and an imitator of the Master; and having become a partaker of His passions, now thou art revealed as a co-heir.

*Troparion:*
O ineffable and divine bestowing of light, which now enjoying, O blessed one, from afar thou beholdest in the Father the Son and the Spirit, three Hypostases of the Godhead.[^508]

*Theotokion:*
Thou art our weapon and wall, O Mother of God; thou art the protection of those who hope in thee: thee even now we move unto prayer, that we may be delivered from our enemies.

*July 22: The Holy Myrrh-Bearer and Equal-to-the-Apostles Mary Magdalene; and the Return of the Relics of the Holy Hieromartyr Phocas.*[^509]

*Stichera of the Saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Bearing myrrh with tears, Mary Magdalene, having reached the divine tomb, thou also didst behold the angel of glory, proclaiming the divine rising of the Giver of life, and the deliverance of all; therefore thou didst hasten to announce unto the Eleven, joyfully saying: Leap for joy, Christ is risen!

*Sticheron 2:*
Serving, O all-praised one, Christ God Who for our sake became like unto us, and having kindled soul and mind with unceasing brightness, thou wast light; and seeing Him hanging upon the Cross, a strange spectacle, shedding tears thou saidst: How doth the Life now accept voluntary death?

*Sticheron 3:*
We celebrate thy sacred memory, disciple of Christ, who didst preach His commandments, O all-glorious one, for the salvation of souls; and in faith we kiss the shrine of thy relics, O Mary, which pours forth inexhaustible grace and enlightenment unto those who run unto thee in love in love, O God-blessed one.

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron 4:*
Thou didst surrender thyself wholly unto Him Who for thy sake endured the saving Passion, O all-praised one; by the blood of martyrdom thou didst hasten to be well-pleasing unto Him, and to cleave unto Him, O father; therefore thou wast glorified, and wast enriched with the working of miracles, O all-wise one, crowned by Him.

*Sticheron 5:*
The ragings of the lawless and the service of demons thou didst cast down by the steadfastness of thy sufferings, O Phocas, clearly proclaiming the Savior of all, and enlightening the minds of the faithful, O God-blessed one, and driving away the gloom of idol madness by the illumination of thy wonders and the brightness of thy struggles.

*Sticheron 6:*
Having thee as a saving haven in distress, O all-glorious Phocas, when held fast by the waves of life, by the depth of transgressions, and by the tempest of sorrows, we pray in faith: by thy prayers save us from all tribulations, who honor thy memory, entreating the Lover of mankind, both Master and Lord.[^510]

*Glory, Tone 6, by Anatolius:*
Having been the first to behold the divine Resurrection, O Mary Magdalene, of the Author of all good things, Who compassionately deified our nature,[^511] thou wast shown forth as the first herald of good tidings, crying unto the apostles: Putting away sorrow, take courage, and come, behold Christ Who is risen, and grants unto the world great mercy!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having set aside all the hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
When of old the unblemished ewe-lamb and immaculate Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Tree of the Cross, maternally exclaiming and marveling, she cried: O sweetest Child, what is this sight, new and all-glorious? How did the ungrateful assembly deliver Thee unto the tribunal of Pilate, and condemn to death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*

*Glory, Tone 8, by Byzantios:*
Unto Christ God Who of His own will became poor with my poverty, by His abundant compassion, Mary Magdalene, having faithfully served as a disciple, seeing Him stretched out upon the Tree and enclosed in the tomb, cried out, shedding tears: What is this strange sight? How is He Who quickens the dead accounted as dead? What myrrh shall I bring unto Him Who changed me from the stench of demons? what tears shall I pour forth unto Him Who delivered my foremother from tears? But O King of all, as Thou appearedst as a keeper of the garden, by the dew of Thy words having washed her faith,[^512] Thou saidst unto her: Go unto My brethren, and cry aloud the joy of the good tidings: For I ascend unto My Father and your Father, and to My God and your God, that I may grant unto the world great mercy!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Seeing Thee nailed to the Cross, O Jesus, and willingly accepting passions, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou endure wounds unrighteously, O Physician Who healest human infirmity and hast delivered all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 1:*
Unto Christ Who for our sake was born of the Virgin, honorable Mary Magdalene, thou didst follow, keeping His statutes and laws; therefore today, celebrating thine all-holy memory, through thy prayers we receive remission of sins.

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 4:*
Partaker in manners and successor on the thrones of the apostles, thou didst find action, O God-inspired one, as an ascent to contemplation; therefore, rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst suffer for the faith even unto blood, O hieromartyr Phocas: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*One Canon from the Octoechos, and two of the saints.*

*Canon of the Saint, the Composition of Theophanes, the Acrostic whereof is: "I sing of Mary Magdalene with love." Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with divine beauties and shining with divine outpourings of light, illumine my darkened heart by thy prayers, O Mary.

*Troparion:*
The Word of the Father sanctified thee, having clearly delivered thee from the spirits of malice;[^513] and having become His disciple, thou wast filled with the gifts of the Holy Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Filled with living waters from the unstinting fountain, the Master Who appeared on earth out of tender mercy, thou didst dry up the turbid streams of sin.

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother by nature of the Creator, we hymn thee, O Maiden, who didst reconcile fallen human nature unto God, O Birthgiver of God, all-immaculate Lady.

*Second Canon of the Saint, the same Tone. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who broke wars with His arm, and led Israel through the Red Sea, let us sing unto Him, as unto our Deliverer and God: for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Standing before the unapproachable Light, O Phocas, enlighten my mind and heart, I pray thee, O wise one, to praise today thy holy suffering.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast adorned from childhood, having divine understanding, O Phocas; and thou wast accounted worthy of grace to work signs, and to drive away evil spirits, O all-wise one.

*Troparion:*
Being full of grace, full of the understanding of God, Thou hadst living in Thee the power of the Divine Spirit, O God-wise one; therefore like the sun Thou didst shine forth, enlightening the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing man, in thy womb thou didst contain the uncontainable God, O Birthgiver of God, incorruptibly; Whom ever entreat on our behalf, O thou full of God's grace.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desire, firm foundation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having first loved the Author of good things, Who compassionately deified our nature, thou didst eagerly follow Him, O Mary, obeying His divine commands.

*Troparion:*
Weeping, thou didst reach the tomb of the Deliverer; first to see His divine Resurrection, O maiden; therefore thou wast revealed as a herald of good tidings, crying: Christ is risen, clap your hands!

*Theotokion:*
The Word incarnate from thy most pure blood shall save me, O all-immaculate one, by the wealth of goodness, loosing the condemnation of the first fall; Whom pray unceasingly to save thy flock.

*Second Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
My heart is established in the Lord, my horn is exalted in my God; my mouth is enlarged over mine enemies, I have rejoiced in Thy salvation.

*Troparion:*
Thou hadst a sacred mind from thy youth, and even unto old age and advanced years, O God-bearer, thou wast well-pleasing unto God; therefore thou hast received glory.

*Troparion:*
The demon in the ship, not enduring thy great rebuke, O God-bearer, fled away;[^514] and Christ is magnified by thy deeds, O marvelous one.

*Troparion:*
Thy valor in cities, thy struggles and course, and thy good deeds: who can worthily praise them, O marvelous adornment and boast of martyrs?

*Theotokion:*
As higher than the angels, in thy womb thou didst receive, O all-immaculate one, the Master of angels and of all creation; therefore in faith we honor thee.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "In fulfilling the dispensation" (Е҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ):*
As a hierarch offering sacrifices, O father, at the end thou didst offer thyself as a living sacrifice, having lawfully borne witness to Christ God, strengthened by angels, and choosing death; being with those who cry unto thee: Come, O Phocas, be with us, and none shall be against us![^515]

*Ikos:*
Desiring heavenly life, and trampling underfoot food upon earth, let us cleanse our lips and tongues likewise, as we weave songs and hymns worthily for Phocas the priest. For having entered through the heavenly door, unhindered he found entrance within, and gazes upon the Desired One, Who is glorified by angels; for thence also he can pray for us unto the only Merciful One, the Healer of the soul's infirmities: since he endured torments for His sake, let us hymn him, and cry aloud unto him: Look upon us! Come, O Phocas, be with us, and none shall be against us!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Unto the Word Who became poor by His abundant mercy, Mary Magdalene, clearly as a disciple thou didst truly minister; and seeing Him lifted up upon the Cross, and laid in the tomb, weeping thou didst shed tears; therefore we honor thee, and celebrate thy festival with faith, O glorious myrrh-bearer: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory in love.

*Glory of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
A radiant festival hath arrived, O ye lovers of feasts, the memory of God-wise and holy Phocas, rejoicing the ends of the earth, shining with beams of wonders in the world, bringing unstinting grace unto mankind: for he prays unto the Lord to save our race.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
By thy divine birthgiving, O pure one, thou hast renewed the mortal nature of earthborn men that was corrupted in passions; and thou hast raised up all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore as is due we all call thee blessed, O all-glorified Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.[^516]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
O Virgin all-immaculate, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thine all-holy soul when thou beheldest Thy Son and God willingly crucified Thy Son and God: Whom, O blessed one, cease not to entreat, that He grant us forgiveness of transgressions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:*
Having a mind undisturbed by worldly glamour, thou didst serve Him Who came to save the whole world from deception.

*Troparion:*
Washing thyself with tears, having reached the life-bearing tomb, thou didst behold an angel proclaiming the Resurrection of Christ, O Mary.

*Troparion:*
Thy spotless heart was set upon the statutes of Christ; and Him alone didst Thou desire, Who is fair in beauty, O praiseworthy one.

*Theotokion:*
By the slumber of slothfulness sinful sleep hath overtaken me, O Maiden: by thy vigilant prayer, raise me up unto repentance.

*Second Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thee and was afraid, that by ineffable counsel, being ever-existing God, Thou camest forth incarnate from the Virgin: Glory to Thy condescension, O Christ; glory to Thy power!

*Troparion:*
We know thee as a luminary of righteousness shining with deeds and martyrdom, ever enlightening the hearts of the faithful with radiant beams, and driving away the gloom of infirmities, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Fair in the beauties of martyrdom wast thou shown forth, O Phocas: for the dove of God sat upon thy venerable head, signifying in the Spirit, O wise one, that thou wouldest be a partaker of the passions.[^517]

*Troparion:*
Having thy prayer ever as pilot, those at sea, O God-bearer, are delivered from grievous tempest, singing of Him Who for thy sake grants them saving deliverance.

*Theotokion:*
O unblemished and undefiled one, cleanse my heart that is wretchedly defiled with harmful thoughts and transgressions, by thy divine consolations addressed unto the all-good God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
The foremother, seeing him who of old seduced her with words and drove her out from Paradise now trampled underfoot by honorable women who acquired a manly spirit,[^518] rejoices with them eternally.

*Troparion:*
Wounded by love of Him Who died, and was shut in the tomb, and breathes life into all by His sweet love, thou bringest myrrh, O Magdalene, honorable Mary, and pourest forth the sweet fragrances of tears.

*Troparion:*
After the divine Passion, after the Savior's awesome Ascension, thou didst go about, proclaiming everywhere the honorable word, and capturing many who were deceived through ignorance, as the glorious disciple of the Word.[^519]

*Theotokion:*
He Who was well-pleased to be born of thee out of boundless mercy delivers me, who am fallen into complex sinful falls, O all-holy Maiden; Whom entreat that we who hymn thee in faith may be delivered from every harm.

*Second Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Lord our God, give us peace; O Lord our God, possess us; O Lord, besides Thee we know none other, we name Thy name.

*Troparion:*
Passing through as a precious stone, O venerable one, thou didst bring unto faith in God the people who served stones, by thy words and marvelous apparitions.

*Troparion:*
Venerable among venerable ones wast thou shown forth, O glorious one; and bright among martyrs, having suffered marvelously, and having conquered the adversary, O marvelous Phocas.

*Troparion:*
The angelic hosts sang praises, seeing thee suffer steadfastly, O martyr, and conquering the bodiless demons in thy corruptible body, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Save me tossed by the passions of life, O all-immaculate one, and pilot me unto the calm harbors of dispassion, O help of all Christians, full of God's grace.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and bring me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparion:*
The dew of thy words took away the heat of sorrow from the disciples, O honorable one, when thou didst cry aloud: Christ is risen, Life hath appeared, the bright Sun hath shone forth!

*Troparion:*
Lo, thy radiant memory hath shone forth, enlightening the faithful who hymn thee therein, and driving away the darkness of the temptation of evil demons, O worthy of marvel.

*Theotokion:*
The noetic and untouched mercy-seat of the priesthood, a light-bearing lampstand wast thou shown forth, and a bridge leading unto God for those who confess thee as the Mother of God, O all-immaculate one.

*Second Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Even as Thou didst deliver the prophet from the nethermost depth, O Christ God, deliver me also from my sins, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, and direct my life, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
A dove sent unto thee from on high was about to announce unto thee with human voice, O all-glorious one, saying that thou shouldest drink the cup of salvation.

*Troparion:*
After exceeding many sweats, after unmeasured bodily afflictions, thou didst bow thy holy head unto the sword, and didst sanctify the earth with thy blood.

*Troparion:*
Not having served dead idols, O exceedingly rich one, thou didst endure death, and livest after death; therefore we call thee blessed, O Phocas.

*Theotokion:*
He Who dwells on high made His abode in thy holy womb, appearing unto the world in the likeness of flesh; therefore we glorify thee, O pure one.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва днесь):*
Standing, O all-glorious one, by the Savior's Cross with many others, and suffering together with the Mother of the Lord, and shedding tears, she brought this as praise, saying: What strange marvel is this? He Who upholds all creation has been well-pleased to suffer: Glory to Thy power!

*Ikos:*
Christ the Master and King of creation, having left the heavens, came down even as He was well-pleased, and willingly took upon Himself corruptible flesh; Whom the immaculate Mother beholding nailed upon the Cross, standing by with two honorable women, Mary Magdalene also weeping—for she being there, and ever showing most fervent faith, followed Christ, and looked upon the tomb, waiting and crying out: Thou hast been well-pleased to suffer; glory to Thy power!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children boldly trampled upon the flame in the furnace and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst drive away diverse ailments, having the Word working together with thee, before Whom thou now standest, crying aloud, O myrrh-bearer: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou alone didst behold before others Christ our Life risen, yet thou didst suppose Him to be the gardener, crying aloud:[^520] Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst stay the flow of death, having conceived and given birth unto the immortal God, O pure one, unto Whom we all sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, unto the ages!

*Second Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who by an angel didst save the children from the fire, and didst change the rumbling furnace into dew: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having passed through the flame, even as the three youths, thou didst remain unconsumed, O martyr, by the power of Christ Who dwelt in thee beyond understanding.

*Troparion:*
Fervently kindled inwardly with the fire of divine love, O martyr Phocas, when mingled with material fire, thou didst remain unconsumed, O wise one.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the divine beauties of Thy wounds, thou didst ascend unto God, the Arbiter of Thy contest, receiving from Him the crown of victory.

*Theotokion:*
Delivered from all condemnation, O Virgin, by thy marvelous childbearing, with joyful voices we hymn thee, O ever-blessed one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant fiercely kindle the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried out unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Radiantly was thy life revealed, shining with the rays of virtuous deeds, O Magdalene, of divine preaching unto the will of God, and with the beams of love directed toward the Creator, unto Whom thou singest with the angels: Ye priests, praise; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Standing by the Cross, O glorious one, beholding the unjust slaughter of Him Who poured Himself out for ineffable mercy, and drenched with groanings and tears, what strange marvel didst thou cry aloud, O Mary: How is He put to death and dies, He Who put death to death, being Life by nature?

*Theotokion:*
