As the Mother of God with one mind we glorify thee, who art higher than creation, O pure one; for through thee the condemnation from Adam was consumed, and the human nature that had fallen away was reconciled, singing: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto ages!

*Second Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
He Who covers His upper chambers with waters, Who sets the sand as a bound for the sea, and Who upholds all things: Thee the sun sings, Thee the moon glorifies, unto Thee all creation brings a hymn, as the Creator of all unto ages.

*Troparion:*
From thy swaddling-bands God, the Creator of all, sanctified thee; and having attained unto the measure of the stature of Christ's sufferings, thou didst suffer, and didst conquer the principalities of the enemy's power, and as a crown-bearer didst reach the habitation of the abodes on high As an excellent pilot those who sail call upon thee unceasingly, O martyr, and are delivered from perils of the sea, O wise one, by thy favorable prayers, O glorious one, singing praises unto the King of all, and Creator, and God, unto ages.

*Troparion:*
As a gentle lamb the enemies of the Lord put thee to death, O Phocas, in deep and honorable old age, after thou hadst slain the enemy, the author of evil, O glorious one, with the sword of thy confession and boldness unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the cleansing for our fallen, corruptible nature, having given birth unto the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of men in His ultimate tender mercy; therefore we sing unto thee joyfully, O Virgin Mother of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast departed now unto divine joy, unto the spacious breadth of paradise, unto the noetic and supermundane abodes where are the ranks of the venerable, where is the pure voice of those who brightly celebrate, O God-bearing Mary; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst prefer nothing of the things upon the earth above the love of Christ God, but consumed by His beauties alone, and directly sent all-honorable beams, following in His steps thou didst cry aloud: I magnify Thee, O greatly-merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Having attained deification which makes godlike, and having drunk of the torrent of grace, and having received the rewards of thy afflictions from the divine Prince, and counted among the apostolic ranks, O Magdalene, disciple of the Word: preserve by thy prayers those who honor thee in love.

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother of Him Who suffered for us, deliver me from soul-destroying passions; as thou art merciful, loose the bonds of my transgressions; as thou art good, sweeten now my soul that is embittered by demonic temptations, O Virgin Mother of God, that I may hymn thee, the all-praised one.

*Second Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who has raised up a horn of salvation for us in the house of David His servant, wherein the Dayspring from on high has visited us, and has directed our feet into the way of peace.[^521]

*Troparion:*
As a starry heaven, adorned with the divine beauties of wonders, wast thou known, O martyr Phocas, ever enlightening the universe, and thou didst dispel the darkness of the passions and of all impiety; therefore in faith we hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst tread the narrow path on earth, O martyr, enlarged piously by hopes of everlasting beauties, O Phocas, and thou didst constrain all the fierce wiles of the enemy.

*Troparion:*
A sacred solemnity and thine honorable feast has now arrived, bringing unto us perfect joy, wherein thy valiant deeds and struggles are truly glorified; in which remember us, O passion-bearer and martyr.

*Theotokion:*
Thou bearest my likeness, having the Father's form, O my Son; for Thou willest manifestly to heal the fierce deformity of earthly ones, cried aloud the all-pure one bearing Christ in her arms, whom we hymn with unceasing voices.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "O Changeless Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*
The unsetting Sun, O Maiden, Who shone forth from the Father before the ages, the assembly of the Hebrews sealed in the tomb; but Mary, thou didst first behold Him risen, and didst proclaim Him unto the disciples.

*Glory, of the Saint:*
An adornment of hierarchs and defender of the poor wast thou revealed, O God-wise father, and a pillar of virtues; therefore remember all who hymn thee, O Phocas.

*Both now:*
Daniel depicted thee beforehand as the unhewn mountain, from which the precious Cornerstone was cut, and crushed the temples of the idols, O Mary, Mother of God.[^522]

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos:*

*Glory, Tone 4:*
From infancy thou wast a lover of the Lord, O all-blessed and wise Phocas; for taking the weapon of the Cross upon thy shoulder, without turning aside thou didst walk in the way of truth, whereby thou wast beloved of the angels, an adversary unto demons, and wast revealed as an excellent intercessor for the world.

*Both now, Tone 8:*
Unto Christ God, Who by His exceeding tender compassion willingly became poor with my poverty, Mary Magdalene, having faithfully served as a disciple, beholding Him stretched upon the Tree and enclosed in the tomb, cried aloud shedding tears: What strange vision is this? How is He Who quickens the dead contained as dead? What myrrh shall I bring unto Him Who delivered me from the stench of the demons? What tears shall I shed unto Him Who delivered my foremother from tears? But O King of all, even as Thou didst appear as the keeper of the garden, taking away the burning heat with the dew of Thy words, Thou saidst unto her: Go unto My brethren, proclaim the glad tidings of joy, cry aloud: For I ascend unto My Father and your Father, and to My God and your God,[^523] that I may grant great mercy unto the world.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Octoechos, and of the Saint, Ode 3 on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:36]. Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:27]. Epistle to the Corinthians, Section 141 [1 Cor. 9:2–12]. Alleluia, Tone 4: With patience I waited for the Lord, and He inclined unto me [LXX Ps. 39:2]. Gospel according to Luke, Section 34 [Luke 8:1–3]. Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous [LXX Ps. 32:1].*

*July 23: The Holy Martyrs Trophimus, Theophilus, and those with them.*[^524]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "All-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Thou didst enter boldly into the judgment hall, O martyr, and didst endure sufferings and lacerations, and wast wounded, having wounded the adversary, the wrestler, and didst carry off the victory against him, O Trophimus; therefore pray that He may grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*
Leading a choir of martyrs with thyself, O wise one, thou didst suffer, and rejoicing with them, thou didst worthily receive heavenly good things; with them remember us who praise thee in faith upon the earth, praying unto Christ that He may grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Sticheron 3:*
Let us the faithful praise Trophimus and Theophilus, the martyrs, with the remaining thirteen; for having already extinguished the fire of delusion with their precious blood, they dwell in the Kingdom on high, ever entreating that He may grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Woe is me! What shall become of me, having defiled mind, soul, and body with transgressions? What shall I do? How shall I escape the unbearable flame, and the eternal, unloosed bonds? But O all-immaculate one, before the end pray thy Son to grant me remission!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled Virgin, once beholding Him hanging upon the Tree Whom she had borne from her seedless womb, unable to endure the piercing of her maternal heart, tearing her hair, cried aloud: Thou Who by Thy nod upholdest all creation, how is it that as one condemned upon the Cross Thou art lifted up upon the Cross, willing in every way to save mankind?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received imperishable crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless audacity of demons: by their supplications save our souls!

*Canon, the composition of Andrew of Crete, Tone 4:*[^525]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou didst bring out Thy people from the bondage of Egypt, and didst cover Pharaoh's chariots and might.

*Troparion:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast made to dawn upon us a day of gladness, destroying delusion: the memory of Thy martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shine forth as a sun in the world, appearing in the firmament of the faithful, O martyr, wherein thou didst illuminate the ends of the earth by thy sufferings.

*Troparion:*
The homeland that reared thee was watered with thy blood as with golden streams, O blessed Trophimus, feeding with unfailing nourishment upon thy sufferings.

*Triadikon:*
Let us orthodoxly glorify the Trinity, with the Father the Son and the Holy Spirit, one Godhead, a tri-hypostatic Unity.

*Theotokion:*
An angel brought thee from heaven the news of thy translation unto God, O Virgin, and gathered the world unto the joy of the Annunciation.[^526]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
My heart is established in the Lord my God; therefore the weak have girded themselves with strength.

*Troparion:*
In the judgment hall the martyr, wounded together with his fellow-sufferers, rejoiced, foreseeing the ineffable recompense.

*Troparion:*
Thy shed blood burned up godlessness, kindling in all the warmth of the knowledge of God, O Trophimus.

*Troparion:*
Having the martyr in their midst as the sun among stars, Thy sufferers, O Lord, darkened delusion.

*Triadikon:*
Unto the tri-hypostatic nature, the Godhead of the one Kingdom, let us bow down: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Among mothers thou wast a virgin, and among virgins a mother, and by both thou astonishest every mind, O pure one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́ггелскїй):*
Having passed through tortures with valiant mind and received victory over them, O blessed one, thou didst turn the wolf into a lamb by divine grace,[^527] and didst persuade him to suffer with thee in faith; therefore we all honor thee with joy, O all-glorious Trophimus.

*Theotokion:*
O Hope of Christians, all-holy Virgin, Whom thou didst bear as God beyond understanding and word, unceasingly pray together with the hosts on high to grant remission of all our sins and amendment of life unto those who ever glorify thee in faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd suspended dead upon the Tree, weeping spoke, maternally crying aloud: How shall I endure Thy condescension which is beyond word, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O all-good One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O God, Thy report and was afraid; I understood Thy works and was astonished, O Lord, for the earth is full of Thy praise.[^528]

*Troparion:*
I heard, O God, of the martyrs' steadfast courage, which in their sufferings they showed for Thy love, and marveling I glorify Thee, the only Savior.

*Troparion:*
Shielded with the shield of faith, the great martyr mentally drew the spiritual sword and cut off the heads of the impious.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the beauty of virtues, O great martyr, and crowned with glory, thou didst put the persecutor to shame, accepting fire and slaughter for Christ's sake.

*Triadikon:*
In one essence I sing of the three Hypostases of the Godhead: Father, Son, and Spirit, as one radiance of the three-sun nature, one God.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin, how dost thou give suck? How dost thou give birth according to maternal laws? For He Whom thou hast borne is God incarnate, renewing nature, as He Himself knows.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Dispel the darkness of my sin when Thou risest, the bright morning of Thy commandments, O Lord, unapproachable Light.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst dispel the darkness of godlessness by radiant preachings; by thy God-illumined confessions thou didst put enemies to shame, O Trophimus, sufferer of Christ.

*Troparion:*
As thou didst love God, O God-loving martyr, thou wast fittingly shown forth according to thy name,[^529] driving away god-hating destruction, having become an excellent martyr of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Having clothed yourselves with the purple robe dyed in your blood, ye martyrs reign in heaven together with the God of all, as His imitators.

*Triadikon:*
Equally-glorious, equally-honorable Trinity, unoriginate unbegotten Father, begotten Son, and Holy Spirit: Thou art my God, O Lord, unapproachable Light.

*Theotokion:*
Her who gave birth among virgins, and thee a pure virgin among mothers we sing, the undefiled vessel of God, from which the Lord, the Savior of all, came forth.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Overwhelmed by the tempest of sins, as though held fast in the whale's belly, with the prophet I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Lord, and save me![^530]

*Troparion:*
Crushed with stones and clubs, ye did not fall away from the faith in any way, but became blessed destroyers of idol madness, receiving the prizes of victory.

*Troparion:*
Lifted up on high and scraped, Trophimus the theologian exposed the idol delusion; for he watered the hearts of the faithful with worthily dispensed teachings.

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth as stars by your sufferings, ye destroyed the gloom of unbelief; therefore, having illuminated the saving day wherein we may be saved, pray ye, O martyrs of the Lord!

*Triadikon:*
Consubstantial, unoriginate Trinity of one form, equally-honorable and tri-hypostatic, indivisible Unity, transcendent in Godhead: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, all creation sings unto Thee!

*Theotokion:*
Thy childbearing is beyond nature, for seedlessly, maternally and fittingly thou dost give birth again according to the law of nature, truly uniting both, so that foul heresies concerning thee may be silenced, O pure Mother of God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The burning fire in the furnace was afraid; the children were bedewed, and singing they said: Blessed art Thou Who alone art God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
When Thy sufferers despised the flesh, the angels marveled, and earthly ones cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, the one God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The high arm of Thy might crushed these Thy enemies, O Lord, while Thy martyrs finished their course in the faith amid bitter torments.

*Troparion:*
Some of Thy passion-bearers indeed were stoned with stones, and others were burned with fire, with whom Trophimus finished his life, singing unto Thee, the existing God: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Triadikon:*
Three-sun Godhead, one Kingdom, unoriginate, ever-existing: Father, Son, and Spirit: Blessed art Thou Who alone art God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O awesome mystery! O strange marvel! How she was revealed as a mother, and remained a virgin, the only blessed one, who gave birth unto the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
All works of God and all creation, bless the Lord; ye venerable and humble of heart: praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Extinguishing the fire by divine nod and strengthening the youths, thou wast revealed mighty, O Trophimus, being filled with inspiration divinely and with the faith of Christ; Whom supremely exalt unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast adorned with a sacred crown, O sufferer, and wast a chastiser unto the consecrated tyrants of the lawless dominion, whom thou didst wound, O Trophimus, supremely exalting Christ unto ages.

*Troparion:*
As a far-shining star, the God-chosen assembly of the sufferers, shining threefold fourfold in the world through faith in Christ:[^531] Whom supremely exalt unto ages!

*Triadikon:*
Let us the faithful sing of the unoriginate Father and the Son, together with the Holy Spirit, consubstantial, one Principle and Godhead, praising and singing Him unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
Every tongue of the earth-born hymns thee, O Virgin, for thou didst cause to shine forth the unapproachable Light, Christ God Who enlightens the world; Whom we supremely exalt unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, the unwedded Mother of God, the true bridal chamber, who gavest birth unto the Author of our salvation, with hymns we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Thee, who didst laudably pass through the torrents of suffering, and wast at the end revealed as a conqueror from Christ God, O sufferer, with hymns we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Thee, the all-lauded sufferer, who wast revealed as the leader of those who suffered with thee unto unfading life in a manner worthy of martyrs, with hymns we magnify.

*Troparion:*
The choir of martyrs worthy of God receives the prizes as having conquered; having received radiant brightness from God, pray ye that we may be saved!

*Triadikon:*
Thee, the tri-hypostatic Unity and indivisible Essence: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, with hymns we magnify.

*Theotokion:*
Thee, the bush which Moses foresaw, burning and unconsumed,[^532] having received the fire of the Godhead, with hymns we magnify.

*July 24: The Holy Martyr Christina; and the Holy Martyrs Boris and Gleb, named in Holy Baptism Roman and David.*[^533]

*Their service is written after this service.*

*Stichera of the Saint, on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Я́кѡ до́бля):*

*Sticheron 1:*
He Who disposes all things by providence worthily accounted thee of the name that bears Christ's name; for thou hadst an excellent end of both: deeds corresponding unto thy calling, having betrothed thyself unto Christ piously by faith, and having become the daughter of the heavenly King: thou rejoicest with Him, O martyr, praying for us.

*Sticheron 2:*
Having desired the heavenly Father, O glorious one, thou didst despise an impious parent; and having loved the Jerusalem on high as mother, thou didst cast away the attachment of maternal love, and didst become rich in Christ instead of all things; therefore thou didst lay down thy life, O martyr, not fearing torments.

*Sticheron 3:*
Neither attachment unto parents, nor enjoyment of food, nor possession of wealth, O glorious one, nor threats nor torments, nor fire nor sword, nor depth nor the wheel, nor the onrush of beasts, were able to separate thee from the love of the Creator:[^534] O virgin martyr Christina, glory and boast of martyrs!

*Glory, Tone 2:*
As costly myrrh thou didst bring thy blood unto thy Bridegroom Christ, O Christina, unconquerable martyr and passion-bearer; and thou didst receive from Him an imperishable crown as recompense, O worthy of marvel. Therefore, him who had died from the bites of poisonous serpents thou didst raise alive by thy word through the invocation of the Holy Spirit; therefore Jesus the Lover of mankind, the Savior of our souls, accounted thee worthy of the heavenly bridal chambers.

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда́ ѿ дре́ва):*
Having endured many sorrows at the crucifixion of thy Son and God, O all-pure one, groaning, shedding tears, and crying aloud: Woe is me, my sweet Child! How dost Thou suffer without cause, willing to deliver those born of Adam [Thou suffer unjust death, wishing to deliver] the earthborn? Therefore, O all-pure Virgin, we pray thee in faith: make Him merciful unto us.

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*

*Glory, Tone 6, by Anatolius:*
Unto the calling named after Christ, thou didst show forth a twofold work, as is truly meet: by the purity of thy virginity espousing thyself unto Christ, through the good pleasure of the Father and the operation of the Spirit; and by the steadfast struggles of martyrdom thou didst shine brighter than the rays of the sun. Therefore, as a pure and blameless sacrifice, thou wast offered upon the heavenly table, rejoicing with the choirs of virgins and martyrs unto ages of ages; with them, O Christina, who bearest Christ's name, entreat that peace and great mercy be granted unto those who honor thee.

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда́ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the lawless people lifted Thee up upon the Tree, O Savior, the Life of all, then Thy pure and all-immaculate Mother, standing by and weeping, cried aloud: O my sweet Child, Light of mine eyes, woe is me! How hast Thou endured to be nailed upon the Cross between evildoers, Thou Who didst hang the earth upon the waters?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Christina, O Jesus, cries with a great voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee do I suffer, and am crucified and buried with Thee in Thy baptism; and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may live with Thee; but as a blameless sacrifice accept me who have offered myself unto Thee with love. Through her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls.

*Canon of the Saint, to 6.*
*Whose acrostic is: "I sing of thee, the maiden named after Christ."*[^535]
*A composition of Joseph. Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped from the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!

*Troparion:*
Having been established in good hope, O Christina, thy mind took wing, and toward God, O martyr, it was lifted up from passing things, departing unto the abiding mansions.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the wicked one burst asunder and lying before the feet of the passion-bearing maiden, let us all give praise unto the Savior Who hath shown her forth as victorious.

*Troparion:*
Purity didst thou desire, that thou mightest behold the comely beauty of the Bridegroom and King; therefore, O passion-bearer, by divine sufferings thou didst radiantly adorn thyself.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst conceive beyond understanding, O pure Mother, the Father's Word hypostatically united unto flesh from thee; Whom Christina having desired, she gained the glory of martyrdom.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit doth sing of Thee.

*Troparion:*
Having lifted up thine eyes and thy mind unto heaven, O glorious one, through creation thou didst recognize thy Creator.

*Troparion:*
An inalienable wealth toward God didst Thou possess, O honorable one: faith; therefore Thou didst forsake the poverty of idols.

*Troparion:*
A song of thanksgiving didst thou sing unto Christ the Creator, O martyr, when cruelly bound unto a tree and enduring lacerations.

*Theotokion:*
Settle the turbulence of my thoughts, O pure Lady, and calm every sorrow of my soul, O thou who hast given birth unto Christ.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^536]
Thy precious blood, O honorable one, like an alabaster box of myrrh didst thou offer with love unto Christ thy Bridegroom; and thou didst receive from Him a reward, O most marvelous martyr: a crown divine and incorruptible. Therefore thou hast received the grace of healings by the power of the Spirit.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O unwedded pure Virgin Birthgiver of God, sole mediatress and protection of the faithful, deliver from tribulations, sorrows, and all cruel circumstances those who place their hope in thee, O maiden, and save our souls through thy divine prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, the all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Merciful One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast nourished by wounds, delighting in divine visions of thy Bridegroom, O martyr named after Christ, and gazing upon His beauty.

*Troparion:*
I magnify Thee, O Lord, and I praise Thy name, Who dost strengthen those who love Thee!—cried the martyr when bound unto wheels.

*Troparion:*
I have cleaved unto Thee, being wounded by desire for Thy love; show me victorious in suffering!—cried the martyr.

*Theotokion:*
Among the prophets, the glorious Isaiah of old named thee a rod that bears the flower, even the Lord, O Virgin Mother, Ever-Virgin Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early from the night we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, and we know no other beside Thee.

*Troparion:*
When thou becamest as an angel in the flesh, O martyr, heavenly angels nourished thee with angelic food.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not reject the Rock of life, O Christina; therefore the enemies, having bound thee to a stone, cast thee into the water.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast caught up to the heights, having flown up like a turtledove on the wings of the divine Spirit, and thou didst find rest with the Creator.

*Theotokion:*
Bring me to life, who am dead through sin, O Virgin Mother who didst give birth unto the immortal Life.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn nigh unto Hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Thy heart was sweetened by the beauty of the most sweet Lover, O passion-bearer; and toward the contest of His passions thou didst hasten eagerly, crying: O my Bridegroom, for the sake of Thy love am I slaughtered now!

*Troparion:*
When thy father saw thee loving the Heavenly Father, O martyr, he showed forth a barbarian disposition and encompassed thee with manifold tortures; for the wicked one acknowledged not the boundary of his own nature.

*Troparion:*
Like a lily in the valleys of martyrdom, like a sweet-smelling briar rose hast thou blossomed, distilling myrrh-like grace; and thou hast anointed the hearts of the faithful, O honorable martyr named after Christ, companion of the holy angels.

*Theotokion:*
Having comprehended the depth of thy mystery, O Virgin, the divinely-speaking prophets prophetically foretold, being enlightened from afar by the divine Spirit, that thou art truly the Mother of the Master of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся):*
A light-radiant dove wast thou shown to be, having golden wings, and thou didst fly up unto the heavenly height, O honorable Christina; therefore we celebrate thy glorious feast, bowing down in faith before the shrine of thy relics, from which there abundantly flows divine healing unto all, both for souls and bodies.

*Ikos:*
Having piously depicted the Holy Trinity, O honorable virgin martyr[^537] O virgin martyr, thou didst show unto those then darkened by the gloom of cruel wrath, how thy radiance enlightens the faithful. And thou didst escape from the tormentor's hand, like a second Thecla escaping from the lawless,[^538] and didst pass through the midst of the snares. Therefore, singing in truth, we honor thy departure, giving thanks unto the one God Who for thy sake hath granted divine healing unto both souls and bodies.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trod upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Approaching the bubbling fire, thou didst cry out a hymn unto the Benefactor of all, Who bedewed thy heart: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Desiring to attain unto the one God, thou didst not care for thy burning limbs; thou didst not reject Christ, O virgin, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparion:*
With the torrents of thy blood thou didst dry up the streams of idol-madness; and now thou dost rain down a sea of healings, O virgin martyr, and hast quenched the flame of passions.

*Theotokion:*
Transcending the laws of nature, O Virgin, by thy divine conception thou didst beyond nature give birth unto God, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Redeemer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The surging sea of tribulations and tortures thou didst safely traverse by divine piloting, having drowned the dragon, O martyr, in the depth of thy struggles. Therefore thou hast reached the heavenly haven of paradise, crying: Ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst slay the cruel dragon by thy life-giving struggles, and by the standing of thy prayers thou didst lull to sleep the untamed beasts, and didst remain free from harm from them, singing unto the Creator, O Christina: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
By the living word thou didst raise up him who had died from the bite of the serpent's poison, O passion-bearing martyr; for God, Who trampled down death by His divine burial, gave ear unto thy prayers, O Christina, unto Whom thou criest unceasingly:

Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
O firm helper of the faithful, Ever-Virgin, deliver me from the delusion of the subtle dragon who cruelly fights against me and strives to bring me low; for thou art an unerring guide unto those who trust in thee, who cry unceasingly: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, who through thee have been saved, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast counted worthy to behold thy Bridegroom, O Christina, much-suffering martyr, being adorned and shining with the garments of martyrdom.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thy comely beauty which thou didst love, Christ, Who is more beautiful than all beauties, made thee worthy of the heavenly bridal chambers.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast joined unto the bodiless choirs, O glorious one, and wast numbered with the assemblies of the suffering, entreating the All-Merciful on our behalf.

*Troparion:*
Thy memory is shown forth brighter than the sun, enlightening those who honor thee, O Christina, with the gracious splendors of the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin who lovest goodness, make happy my soul that is embittered by sins, and deliver me from eternal flame through thy prayers.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Like a most fair briar rose hast thou blossomed from a thorny root, O virgin Christina; having reddened thyself with the sprinkled blood of sacred suffering, O martyr, deliver from tribulations those who now perform thy divine memory with love.

*Theotokion:*
Standing round about thee, the divinely-wise virgins, O Birthgiver of God Mary, like queens by birth, have been brought unto thy Son, O all-pure one, in bride-befitting manner, as unto the King of all; Whom, O Virgin Mother and Maiden, entreat on our behalf.

*At the Praises, Stichera, to 4.*

*Tone 4. Automela [Samoglasny]:*
Holding the Cross with thy hands as a mighty weapon, O martyr Christina, faith as a shield, hope as a breastplate, and love as a bow, thou didst courageously conquer the tortures of the tormentors; divinely didst thou abolish the knaveries of the demons; and having been beheaded, thou dost dance with Christ, praying unceasingly for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Forsaking thy paternal wealth, and desiring Christ in truth in truth, the martyr found glory and heavenly wealth; and being protected by unceasing graceful prayer, by the weapon of the Cross she trampled down the tormentor. Therefore the angels, marveling at her struggles, said: The enemy hath fallen, being vanquished by a woman; the martyr hath appeared crowned; and Christ reigns unto ages of ages as God, Who grants great mercy unto the world.

*Sticheron:*
We glorify Thy great compassion, O Christ, and the goodness that hath come upon us, in that even women have abolished the delusion of idol-madness by the power of Thy Cross, O Lover of mankind; they feared not the tormentor, and trampled down the deceiver; and they were able to follow after Thee, running in the fragrance of Thy myrrh, praying for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
The power of Thy Cross hath worked wonders, O Christ, so that Christina the martyr also strove with the struggle of suffering; whence, casting aside the weakness of nature, she valiantly opposed the tormentors; therefore, having received the crown of victory, she prays for our souls.

*Glory, Tone 5:*
Desiring the beauty of thy virginity, Christ, the King of glory, betrothed thee unto Himself as an immaculate bride in incorruptible union; for by His will He gave power unto thy beauty, and showed thee invincible against enemies and passions, having endured bitter wounds and fierce torments. With a twofold crown He blessed thee, and set thee at His right hand as an embroidered queen. Entreat Him, O honorable and much-suffering martyr Christina, to grant salvation and life, and great mercy, unto those who sing of thee.

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O life-bearing Cross" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ):*[^539]
As a ransom for our sake and a great price, Thou didst deign to give Thy most pure blood, O my sinless Christ, wishing all to obtain salvation. Therefore, seeing Thee nailed, Thy Mother, weeping, tore her hair, saying: O Child, all-immaculate Lamb, Who willest to redeem the world by Thy precious blood: how hast Thou set from mine eyes, O Savior, Thou unsetting Sun, Who grantest unto all enlightenment, peace, and great mercy?

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*

*Glory, Tone 4:*
Thou hast been numbered with the hosts on high, O glorious one, having forsaken the world and desired Christ, O all-honorable Christina; ask great mercy for us.

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His longsuffering, the pure Mother, weeping, said: Woe is me, my sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos, to 4; and of the saint, Ode 3, to 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:36]. *Verse:* In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:27]. *Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 181 [2 Cor. 6:1–10].*[^540] *Alleluia, Tone 1:* With patience I waited patiently for the Lord, and He inclined unto me [LXX Ps. 39:2]. *Gospel according to Luke, pericope 33 [Lk. 7:36–50].*[^541] *Communion Verse:* In everlasting memory shall the righteous be [LXX Ps. 111:6].

*The Service of the Holy Martyrs Boris and Gleb, Named in Holy Baptism Romanus and David.*[^542]

*If there be a temple [dedicated to them], or if the superior so desire, we perform a Vigil.*

*At Small Vespers, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Most holy, radiant, and richly-festal hath your memory shone forth today upon the lands of Rus,[^543] O glorious Romanus with the gentle David, enlightening the souls of those who in faith honor your sufferings. Therefore pray, O most blessed ones, that cleansing and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Come, all ye newly-chosen flock of Christ, spiritually coming together let us praise the holy offshoot of both a pious and royal root, Romanus with the valiant David today, who loved Christ, and for this reason endured an unjust fraternal slaughter, and ever pray unto Christ that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
Let all mankind rejoice today with joy, and let the Church of Christ sing dancing in choir with divine songs, praising the newly-enlightened princes and martyrs; for for Christ's sake, having forsaken the passing earthly kingdom, they loved the Lord from their whole soul, and pray that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, Tone 2. Automelon [Samoglasen]:*
Overcome by desire for things to come, and still more by the love of Christ, they hated the earthly kingdom and glory, and loved purity, and endured unjust murder, in no wise in no wise opposing their brother who was slaughtering them. Therefore Christ also enriched you with gifts of wonders, O sacred pair, O radiant host, O divine yoke-fellows: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion:*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Having cleansed yourselves as a dwelling place for the Holy Spirit, make us also His abode, O holy ones, by your prayers.

*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [LXX Ps. 33:18].*
O divine and marvelous pair, destroyers of delusion and sowers of piety, pray that our souls may be saved.

*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, but the Lord shall deliver them out of them all [LXX Ps. 33:20].*
As most radiant stars have ye appeared, O invincible sufferers; illuminate us who honor your sacred sufferings in faith.

*Glory, Tone 2:*[^544]
The shrine wherein lie your honorable and sacred relics, O most lauded pair, abundantly pours forth divine healings unto those who draw near, driving away the ancient gloom of demon-madness and causing the light of piety to shine forth. But we entreat you: pray, O holy ones, to preserve your fatherland without harm, and that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion:*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Righteous passion-bearers and true hearers of the Gospel of Christ, chaste Romanus with guileless David, ye did not oppose the enemy who was your brother when he was killing your bodies, though he could not touch your souls; let the wicked lover of power weep, while ye rejoice with the angelic choirs, standing before the Holy Trinity: pray for the realm of your kinsmen, that it may be well-pleasing unto God, and that the sons of Rus may be saved.[^545] And the Dismissal.

*At Great Vespers:*

*Blessed is the man:*

*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, to 8. Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O most marvelous wonder! A divine and honorable pair of all-lauded uterine brothers hath shone forth upon us today, convoking the new people to praise the great martyrs: Romanus, in that he suffered zealously, and David, in that he was slaughtered together with him, unto the unblemished Lamb Who was sacrificed for our sake, the Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron:*
First indeed, O all-lauded ones, having submitted the purple of royalty unto Christ, and having recognized Him as true God and King of all, ye turned away from carnal, worldly gods. Therefore Christ also enriched you with gifts of wonders, and in place of corruptible things granted you eternal things, He Who is glorified in His saints, the Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Blessed is the fatherland and the city wherein ye were reared, and the honorable temple that received your bodies was adorned as with a royal crown! O acceptable protectors, who terrify enemies and drive them far away from your fatherland, O glorious Romanus and most marvelous David, pray that our souls may be saved.

*Other Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "With what crowns of praise" (Кі́ими похва́льными):*
With what crowns of praise shall we crown those who are praised, who are divided in bodies and united in spirit: the fervent protectors of the faithful people, the adornment of the land of Rus, and the delight of the whole universe; who by their manly-minded understanding destroyed the power of the demons, through the assistance of Christ Who grants great mercy unto the world?

*Sticheron:*
With what virtues of praise shall we adorn those who are praised: Romanus who valiantly had power over the passions, and David likewise a zealot, both luminaries shining together, illuminating all with the light of pious virtue? For having learned the commandments of Christ, they were gloriously glorified, entreating Him Who grants great mercy unto all.

*Sticheron:*
With what spiritual words shall we compose an honorable feast for the most glorious martyrs, who for Christ's sake left corruptible earthly glory? For the one received a piercing in the ribs, and the other was slaughtered like a lamb; who were worthily glorified by Christ, and received the gift of healings, worthily asking great mercy for all.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Come, let us praise the wonderworkers and martyrs; for these, having suffered lawfully, conquered the opposing enemy; and now, radiantly adorned, they stand before Christ rejoicing. Therefore we also joyfully praise their memory in songs with love, crying: Rejoice, protectors of the universe, and champions against enemies; rejoice, physicians of the sick, and expellers of demons; rejoice, O beloved pair, most fair brothers, glorious Romanus and marvelous David, beloved of Christ, who entreat the Holy Trinity on our behalf to bring peace unto the world and save our souls.

*Both now, Theotokion:*

*Then, the Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Readings, 3.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Isa. 43:9–14]:*
Thus says the Lord: All the nations are gathered together, and princes shall be gathered out of them. Who will declare these things among them? Or who will make heard unto you the things from the beginning? Let them bring their witnesses, and let them be justified, and let them hear, and let them speak the truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I also am a witness, says the Lord God, and the servant whom I have chosen; that ye may know, and believe Me, and understand that I am He: before Me there was no other God, and after Me there shall be none. I am God, and beside Me there is no savior. I have declared, and have saved, and have brought reproach, and there was no strange god among you. Ye are My witnesses, and I am the Lord God, and even from the beginning I am He; and there is none that can deliver out of My hands: I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus says the Lord God, the Holy One of Israel Who redeems you.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wis. 3:1–9]:*
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for affliction, and their going from us to be utter destruction, but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly benefited, for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace hath He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering sacrifice. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge the nations, and have dominion over the people, and the Lord shall reign in them unto ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy are in His saints, and He hath visitation in His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wis. 4:7–15]:*
Though the righteous happen to die early, yet shall he be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor that is measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him; so that living among sinners he was translated. Yea, he was caught up, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of naughtiness obscures things that are honest, and the wandering of concupiscence undermines the simple mind. Being made perfect in a little while, he fulfilled long years; for his soul was pleasing unto the Lord, therefore He hastened to take him out from the midst of wickedness. And the peoples saw, and understood not, neither laid they such a thing in mind: that grace and mercy are in His holy ones, and visitation in His elect.[^546]

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 1. Idiomela:*
*Though a new Cain, a hater of God and brother-hater, murderously deprived you of an earthly kingdom, yet Christ granted you an imperishable and endless Kingdom; standing before Him with the angelic hosts, pray to save those who in love sing of your all-honorable and much-celebrated memory.*

*The earth has been sanctified by your all-honorable blood, and mankind has been enriched by you, receiving healing unstintingly, O divinely-wise pleasers of Christ, glorious Roman with gentle David! Pray to the Lord, to Whom you cleaved from your youth, to save those who praise you in faith.*

*Glory, Tone 6:*
*With joy let us all feast today the memory of the great-martyrs, glorifying with hymns and songs Christ the Savior, Who has made His saints marvelous on earth through wonders and various gifts of healings; crying out to them, let us say joyfully: Rejoice, having received grace against various afflictions! Rejoice, for by your blood you entered into eternal life! Rejoice, speedy hearers of those in afflictions, delivering your homeland from calamities, O divinely-pleasing Boris with Gleb! Pray to the Lord that He have mercy upon our souls.*

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera to the Saints, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
*Come, lovers of chastity, let us honor the venerable pair who loved Christ, the Ruler over all, with pure heart and contrite soul: the glorious passion-bearer Roman and gentle, praiseworthy David, who were pure in souls and bodies, and crushed the demonic ranks.*

*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [Ps. 33:18a LXX].*

*From a venerable root you sprouted, glorious and noble brethren; and having loved nobility of end, you yearned for imperishable glory, choosing life and the indestructible Kingdom; and having suffered for righteousness, you received crowns of victory, O blessed passion-bearers and intercessors for our souls.*

*Verse: Many are the tribulations of the righteous, but out of all of them the Lord will deliver them [Ps. 33:20 LXX].*

*Fulfilling by deeds and teachings the commandments of Christ and His precepts, you bore no enmity toward enemies who came unrighteously to slay you; but as imitators of Stephen the Protomartyr, praying you said: Lay not this sin to their charge, O Lover of mankind, Jesus our God, and Savior of our souls.*

*Glory, Tone 8:*
*Come, newly baptized assemblies of Rus',[^547] and behold how without guilt the martyr Boris receives judgment: with a spear they pierced his side and shed his blood, from the instigation of the devil; while Gleb, by the same brother Sviatopolk, was slaughtered as a lamb. But these have been crowned, whereas he perished without remembrance; and these are glorious, whereas he is tormented in Gehenna; and these pray to Christ God for our souls.*

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
*Troparion, Tone 2:*
*Written at Little Vespers (see fol. 124r).[^548]*
*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*

*At Matins, after the first stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃лскїй):*
*Enduring your passion with your patience and courage, O Prince Boris, looking in Christ's love toward the merciful God and Lover of mankind, Who grants great mercy to the world.*
*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
*Him Who is without mother in heaven beyond thought and hearing, on earth without a father thou hast borne: pray to Him, O Birthgiver of God, for our souls.*

*After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃лскїй):*
*Having loved Christ together from youth, O venerable brethren, having acquired a dispassionate life, O glorious ones, you chose chastity and fasting from carnal and spiritual passions; and having received visitation by the grace of God, you heal those who praise you. (Twice)*
*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
*Sole mighty and fervent protectress of the human race, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: unceasingly pray with the prophets and martyrs, with the hierarchs, the fasters, and the monastics, to God the Word, Whom thou didst bear beyond nature, that He save all creation.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
*Having known the good commandments of Christ, having loved Him having loved them, O glorious passion-bearers, you took no heed for the slaying of your bodies, but surrendered your fair souls into the hands of Christ. The one, pierced with a spear, rejoiced; and the other, slaughtered without mercy like a gentle lamb, prayed. Therefore, having received the gift of healings, O natural-brother martyrs, pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love. (Twice)*
*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
*Cloud of the noetic Sun, golden lamp of the divine Light, undefiled, unblemished, all-immaculate Lady: illuminate my soul, darkened by the blindness of passions, with the beam of dispassion, I pray thee; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that in love I may cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin, pray to Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions, for I, thy servant, place my hope in thee.*
*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], First Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon:*
*The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [Ps. 33:18a LXX].*
*Verse: Many are the tribulations of the righteous, but out of all of them the Lord will deliver them [Ps. 33:20 LXX].*
*Let every breath:*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 106 [Lk. 21:12–19].[^549]*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 8. Idiomelon:*
*O fair brethren Boris and Gleb, your sufferings heal our illnesses and infirmities when we run to you in faith; freely you received, freely also give healing to the sick; but as having boldness, pray to Christ God for our souls.*

*Canon of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God, to 6, whose acrostic is this:*
*I sing praise to the life-bearing Maiden.[^550]*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the untrodden, unaccustomed sea-path with dry feet, chosen Israel cried aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified.*

*Troparion:*
*The immaterial ladder of old and the strangely congealed path of the sea revealed thy childbirth, O pure one, whom we all sing: for He is gloriously glorified.*

*Troparion:*
*The Power of the Most High, the perfect Hypostasis, the Wisdom of God, having become incarnate from thee, O all-pure one, conversed with men: for He is gloriously glorified.*

*Troparion:*
*Through the impassable door of thy shut womb, O pure one, passed the Sun of righteousness and shone forth upon the world: for He is gloriously glorified.*

*And two canons of the saints, to 8, Tone 4 Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by Moses' crosswise hands, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*

*Troparion:*
*O divinely-wise pair, great sufferers, standing before the Holy Trinity with the saints, pray that cleansing be granted unto me who praise you.*

*Troparion:*
*A divine wealth of healings and fragrant myrrh is your temple, O saints, in which we sing unto Christ God Who has glorified you.*

*Troparion:*
*Having acquired within yourselves the unwaning Sun, Christ our God, O most glorious Roman with David, sanctify us who honor your venerable memory.*

*Theotokion:*
*Knowing thee to be fair, chosen, and all-honorable, O all-immaculate one, the Son of God became thy Son; make us sons by grace, who honor thee as the Birthgiver of God.*

*Another Canon to the Saints, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the water as dry land, and fleeing the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*

*Troparion:*
*Grant me remission of my many transgressions, O Savior, bestowing wisdom upon me, that in songs I may praise Thee Who hast glorified Thy saints.*

*Troparion:*
*Let us, the pious, sing a hymn unto those glorified by chaste mind, glorifying together glorious Roman and pious David.*

*Troparion:*
*Both received the gift of grace from God, having loved nobility of soul above all, adorning themselves with chastity and virtue.*

*Theotokion:*
*Her who is chosen out of all generations, let us the faithful worthily praise: the Virgin who truly beyond word gave birth in the flesh to God the Word.*

*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth:[^551]*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty is broken by Thy strength, O Christ, and the weak are girded with power.*

*Troparion:*
*He Who is above all time, as the Creator of the times, was voluntarily fashioned as an infant from thee, O Virgin.*

*Troparion:*
*Let us sing of the womb more spacious than the heavens, through which Adam rejoices and lives in the heavens.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and foundation.*

*Troparion:*
*With joy Christ's Church rejoices in your memory, O saints, and cries to the Creator: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and foundation.*

*Troparion:*
*Let us all radiantly praise the crown-bearers of both, the sufferers of Christ, who taught us to cry unto Christ: Holy art Thou, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
*Neither the desire of a kingdom, nor the enjoyment of food, nor silver and gold did they desire, but unto Christ alone they cried: Holy art Thou, O Lord!*

*Theotokion:*
*Him Who delivers men from disobedience and grievous fall, the Cause of all things, hast thou borne, O pure and all-immaculate one.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thou art the foundation of those that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.*

*Troparion:*
*Having all your thoughts toward God, O wise ones, united by brotherly love you lived piously.*

*Troparion:*
*You hated corruptible and passing things, and were revealed as vessels of virtue, O glorious ones from youth.*

*Troparion:*
*Young indeed in body, yet together holy in souls as pious ones, therefore also you sincerely loved God.*

*Theotokion:*
*Thou alone ineffably, O incorrupt one, didst bear the Consumer of corruption, and didst save the world.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃лскїй):*
*Having loved Christ together from youth, O venerable brethren, having acquired a dispassionate life, O glorious ones, you chose chastity and fasting from carnal and spiritual passions; and having received visitation by the grace of God, you heal those who praise you. (Twice)*
*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
*O unwedded, pure Virgin Birthgiver of God, sole protectress and veil of the faithful: deliver from perils, afflictions, and all grievous circumstances those who place their hope in thee, O Maiden, and save our souls by thy divine prayers.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard, O Lord, of Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thy incomprehensible power, O Lover of mankind.[^552]*

*Troparion:*
*The divine mountain of the house of the Lord, having been exalted, is most manifestly higher than the celestial powers, O Mother of God.*

*Troparion:*
*Apart from natural laws, O Virgin, having alone given birth to the Master of creation, thou wast deemed worthy of the divine calling.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying:*

*...crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
*Shining forth as two luminaries, you enlighten your people, O God-bearers; but even now deliver us from the assault of enemies, that we may honor you, after God, as our protection.*

*Troparion:*
*Your reliquary has been revealed as an inexhaustible dispensary, O blessed ones, and a stormless harbor; for all of us who run unto it with faith find deliverance from evil tribulations.*

*Troparion:*
*Bound by love unto Christ, you were therefore deemed worthy of gifts to heal diverse passions; therefore we celebrate your feast, O divinely-wise ones.*

*Theotokion:*
*He Who sits upon the exalted Cherubic throne, having rested upon thine arms as upon a throne, O Mary, Bride of God, is the all-glorified God.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the bosom of the Father, and hast visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!*

*Troparion:*
*Striving youthful in the bloom of age and youth, O God-blessed ones, you had no care whatsoever for the pleasure of carnal love; but reading the divine books, and thereby enlightened by the divine commandments, you destroyed the prince of darkness.*

*Troparion:*
*Sagaciously following the paternal footsteps as a wise man, O blessed one, you had as a zealous imitator in good things, O divinely-wise Roman, the truly ever-memorable David; for as brethren together, piously united in souls, you were also revealed as one body.*

*Troparion:*
*Having loved the laws of God from the soul, foreseeing the life to come, stretching forth your minds, O saints, you hated earthly honor and power, and exchanged them, O spiritual-minded ones, for the incorruptible Kingdom and glory.*

*Theotokion:*
*Following thy words, O immaculate one, all Christian generations call thee blessed, as having truly and not in apparition given birth to God: perfect in both, in the nature of the Godhead and in the law of humanity.[^553]*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*The Sun revealed unto Isaiah as a live coal has shone forth from the virginal womb, bestowing the illumination of the knowledge of God upon those wandering in darkness.*

*Troparion:*
*Ye clouds, sprinkle the sweetness of joy upon those on earth, for a Child is given: the Son before the ages, having become incarnate from the Virgin incarnate, our God.*

*Troparion:*
*Light has shone forth upon my life and flesh, and destroyed the decrepitude of sin; for in these last days the Most High became incarnate without seed from the Virgin.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thou, O my Lord, didst come into the world as light, a holy light, turning from dark ignorance those who praise Thee in faith.*

*Troparion:*
*The all-honorable feast and sacred solemnity of glorious Roman with David has arrived, bringing us eternal joy, wherein we glorify the Lord Who has glorified them.*

*Troparion:*
*Adorned with the beauty of virtue, O radiant Roman with Gleb, having been crowned through suffering: having trampled upon the enemy and brother, you received slaughter for Christ.*

*Troparion:*
*Your venerable suffering has been heard in all the world, and every tongue is stirred to glorify Christ Who has glorified you.*

*Theotokion:*
*The corruption of man has ceased: for the Virgin incorruptibly gave birth to God the Word, beyond nature and word, and remained a Virgin again.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me, and direct my paths to the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.*

*Troparion:*
*As children of a pious father, bound by love and loving him, shining forth faithfully with the radiance of piety, O much-beloved brethren, therefore you were tried by the arrows of envy.*

*Troparion:*
*The fratricide was enraged, as Cain of old: the wretched Sviatopolk showed himself a transgressor of the law, and entwined murder with envy; he was seduced by the pleasure of the lust for power, therefore he did not escape righteous vengeance.*

*Troparion:*
*Wearing the purple of their own blood, O most glorious ones, and now holding the Cross as a scepter in their right hands, Roman and David, the unconquerable soldiers of Christ, were deemed worthy to reign with Christ.*

*Theotokion:*
*We glorify thee, O unwedded one, as higher than all and holier than the angelic ranks: as having joined earthly things with heavenly. But O Mother of God, visiting thy singers, put an end to the insolence of the pagans.[^554]*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Having heard the voice of suppliant words from an afflicted soul, O Master, deliver me from grievous things: for Thou alone art the Author of our salvation the Author of our salvation.*

*Troparion:*
*Human nature enslaved to sin, O pure Lady, obtained freedom through thee; for thy Son is slaughtered as a lamb for all.*

*Troparion:*
*We all cry unto thee, the true Mother of God: Deliver thy servants who have angered Him, for thou alone hast boldness toward thy Son.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice to Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood which flowed from Thy side for mercy's sake.*

*Troparion:*
*Pray, O blessed ones, for those who celebrate your radiant feast; and keep your homeland unassailed by enemies, and grant peaceful governance unto our King by your prayers.[^555]*

*Troparion:*
*Having become zealots of the eternal Kingdom, O blessed ones, having served Master Christ with a pure conscience, and standing before Him, pray to save those who praise you.*

*Troparion:*
*Having cleaved unto Christ and placed all hope in Him, O blessed ones, having therefore been slain, you reign with Him eternally.*

*Theotokion:*
*With lips, mind, and heart, O all-pure one, we proclaim thee as the Birthgiver of God: through thee we have been reconciled to God, having been cast off through the transgression of our forefather of old.*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depths of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!*

*Troparion:*
*The adversaries were called sons of night and darkness; in the night, as you were offering praise unto God, they pierced you with spears, having become the mediators of your divine crown, O Roman.*

*Troparion:*
*As a true imitator of the incarnate God, you fervently prayed for those who were slaying you, O holy one, like Stephen, Christ's second great martyr; therefore you have also been glorified with him.*

*Troparion:*
*Youthfully valiant, they manfully conquered the fierce adversary: the sacred Roman together with David showed themselves conquerors in deed and word, and the glorious ones received the crown of victory from God.*

*Theotokion:*
*Prophetic voices clearly designate the Mother of God: and we also sing of thee, beholding the fulfillment of honorable prophecies, O true receptacle of virginity and purity.*

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
*Today your all-glorious memory has shone forth, O noble passion-bearers* of Christ, Roman and David, calling us to praise Christ our God. Therefore, running to the shrine of your relics, we receive the gift of healing by your prayers, O saints: for you are divine physicians.*

*Ikos:*
*Leading a prudent life, O all-blessed one, adorned with a royal crown from youth, all-rich Roman, you were a great power to your homeland in all the land of Rus'.[^556] Therefore Christ God, beholding your advancement, called you by His judgment unto martyrdom: having granted you strength from heaven, that you might manfully conquer the enemy together with your brother David who suffered with you: for you are divine physicians.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Radiant were the youths revealed, most philosophic of old, for from a God-receiving soul, theologizing with their lips they sang: O God of our fathers and our God, surpassing in divinity, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
*Jacob of old beheld God in the night as in an enigma; but having become incarnate from thee, He appeared in radiance unto those who sing: O God of our fathers, surpassing in divinity, and all-glorified!*

*Troparion:*
*Manifesting the signs of the ineffable salvation wrought in thee, He wrestled with Jacob, through which He was voluntarily united with men, O pure one: O God of our fathers, surpassing in divinity, and all-glorified!*

*Troparion:*
*Abominable is he who does not proclaim thee to be the Mother of the one Son of the all-hymned Trinity, with undoubting mind and crying out with tongue: O God of our fathers, surpassing in divinity, and all-glorified!*

*Another Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
*Your divine and most radiant memory has appeared as a light-bearing heaven and as a radiant sun, enlightening those who undoubtingly cry unto Christ: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
*Who will not marvel, and who will not sing and faithfully glorify the most glorious martyrs of Christ, Boris with Gleb, who for the sake of Christ's humility hated the diadem and kingdom?*

*Troparion:*
*Having driven away the gloom of godlessness, they radiantly taught their people to honor one God in Trinity, and fervently to sing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Theotokion:*
*Rejoice, sanctified divine dwelling of the Most High! For through thee joy was given for through thee joy was given to those who cry unto the Birthgiver of God: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady!*

*Another Heirmos:*
*The youths having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
*Divine grace has gloriously glorified you, O all-praised ones, as truly a bond of brotherly love and piety, and radiantly taught us to cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
*O courage of the passion-bearers! O strength of purity! O steadfastness of mind! For severely wounded, and slaughtered without mercy, you were revealed as great passion-bearers, Roman and David, whom we the faithful praise, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
*You chose to suffer for Christ's sake, and therefore found reward from God: to drive away passions and diseases from earthly beings, who praise and cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!*

*Theotokion:*
*O divine-flowing drop that didst bring forth from thee Him Who sanctified the world, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: pour forth unto me a drop of mercy, sanctifying my soul and earthly thoughts.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The thrice-blessed youths, taking no heed of the golden image, beholding the immutable and living image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all creation, having been brought into being, sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
*He Who is incomparable in goodness and power was seen on earth through thee, and dwelt with men; singing unto Him, all we faithful cry: Let all creation, having been brought into being, sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
*Proclaiming thee truly as pure, we glorify thee, O Birthgiver of God: for thou didst bear the One of the Trinity incarnate; unto Whom with the Father and the Spirit we all sing: Let all creation, having been brought into being, sing unto the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Another Heirmos:*
*Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^557]*

*Troparion:*
*Having shone forth today, your most radiant memory, O blessed ones, summons the faithful to celebration, to glorify your endurances, and to cry unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Divinely-manifest and new passion-bearers, you loved Thee alone, O Christ, and directed the people to sing unto Thee, the Creator of all: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Shining forth as luminaries of righteousness, adorned with the beauty of martyrdom, enlightening the hearts of the faithful, they drive away diseases from those who cry in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord, O pure Virgin, shone forth from thee, delivering us; Him do thou beseech, O Sovereign Lady, to enlighten those who sing of thee, and to deliver from tribulations those who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant madly kindled the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Not satisfied with murder, the wretched ones, even having cast away your bodies uncared for, were not ashamed of your noble birth; but the grace of God preserved you, and by the keeping of angels, with whom you dance unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Men who were then seeking game in the mountains saw a light shining from thy body, O divinely-inspired David; what vision is this? they said to one another; and having seen the miracle, they proclaimed it unto all, praising Christ Who preserved thee.[^558]

*Troparion:*
Your most holy memory, sanctifying the world, has arrived now, O all-praised passion-bearers; wherein we pray to obtain your protection, O Roman and David, for those who sing unto Christ unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
Having become a vessel of light, O pure one, enlighten me: hasten to grant me, O all-immaculate one, to walk in the commandments of the Light Who shone forth from thy womb and enlightened the hearts of the faithful; Whom we highly exalt unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thou art all desire, all sweetness, O Word of God, Son of the Virgin, God of gods, O Lord, Most Holy of the holy; therefore with her who bore Thee we all magnify Thee.[^559]

*Troparion:*
A rod of strength was given unto corrupt nature, the Word of God in thy womb, O pure one; and It raised up this nature, which had slipped down unto hades; therefore, O all-pure one, we magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
O Sovereign Master, accept maternally as an intercessor for us Thy Mother, whom Thou didst choose, and all things shall be filled with Thy goodness: that we all may magnify Thee as Benefactor.

*Another Heirmos:*

A stone cut without hands from an uncut mountain was cut off from thee, O Virgin, even Christ the cornerstone, Who joined together the parted natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.[^560]

*Troparion:*
Adorned with divine beauties, by your sufferings you ran unto God, receiving the crowns of victory from Him; therefore remember your singers, that we may unceasingly magnify you.

*Troparion:*
O divine pair, true brethren and radiant, pray that cleansing of transgressions be granted, and amendment of life before the end: that we may unceasingly magnify you.

*Troparion:*
From early youth God the Creator sanctified you, and showed you to be excellent helmsmen for those sailing in the sea of life; therefore with one accord we honor your memory.

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O thou who didst bear the Deliverer of all: scatter the clouds of my soul, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, cloud of light, and make me strong against the passions that war against me.

*Another Heirmos:*
Heaven was struck with awe at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Though you were laid in the earth as dead, yet in the heavens you are glorified with the servants of God; therefore, having obtained boldness from Him, O glorious ones, by fiery fear you drove away the unclean one who stood above your tomb, O blessed ones.[^561]

*Troparion:*
You work health for the pious who are sick, and grant running unto the lame, and he who was made whole at your shrine bears witness, O most marvelous pair; therefore you become helpers unto all of us, speedy from on high, putting an end to diseases and the insolence of the pagans.

*Troparion:*
A truly divine and radiant pair, Roman and David, victorious passion-bearers, you now stand in heaven before the almighty Trinity, asking deliverance from grievous transgressions for those who faithfully celebrate your memory on earth.

*Theotokion:*
O wonder surpassing understanding! O truly great and most glorious marvel, how did God, Who cannot be contained in any way, fit into thy womb? Beseech Him, O Virgin, for those who piously praise thee, to deliver them from perils and from lawless nations always.

*Exapostilarion:*

*Tone 3, Podoben: "Thou hast visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны́):*[^562]
As truly most radiant luminaries, you enlightened the whole universe by faith, O God-welcoming Roman with David; therefore in hymns we praise Christ our God Who magnified you. *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
With unceasing hymns we piously sing unto the Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, holy mountain! Rejoice, fiery throne of the King of all! Rejoice, joy of the angels and glory of the martyrs!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^563]
The radiant and holy and beauteous all-festal memory of the Martyrs Boris and Gleb has shone forth: enlightening the whole earth, and driving away the gloom of idolatry, they pour forth the grace of healings. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron:*
With the precious drops of your blood you dyed your robes, O blessed Roman and David; therefore celebrating your memory in faith, we beseech you: pray to bring peace unto the world and to save our souls.

*Sticheron:*
As one light in two bodies, you enlighten the world with marvelous radiance, O passion-bearers of the Lord, driving away the gloom of ignorance; therefore we sing joyfully, praising your memory.

*Glory, Tone 4:*
Today let the assemblies of feast-lovers, having come together with joyful countenance and pure heart, spiritually form a choir: not with Jewish cymbals, but with contrite hearts, praising Christ our true God, Who glorifies His saints, the noble Roman with David; standing now around whose multi-healing and wonder-working reliquary, we lovingly embrace it, saying: Rejoice, you who kept Christ's commandments according to His image! Rejoice, you who received Christ's humility, and in no way resisted your brother and enemy who pitilessly slew your bodies! Rejoice, Roman most marvelous, and guileless David, who direct your people to the true faith, most radiant luminaries to your homeland, and bold intercessors for our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
*Great Doxology and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of both canons, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* To the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath made wonderful all His desires[^564] in them. *Verse:* I foresaw the Lord always before me.
*Epistle to the Romans, pericope 99 (Rom. 8:28–39).*
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them.
*Verse:* Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord shall deliver them. *Gospel of John, pericope 52 (John 15:17–16:2).*
*Communion Hymn:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise becomes the upright.

*July 25: The Dormition of the Holy Anna, Mother of the Holy Birthgiver of God; and the memory of the holy women Olympias and Euphraxia.*

*At Vespers, having recited "Blessed is the man", the first Kathisma glory. At "Lord, I have cried", we set 8 stichera, and sing stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^565]
Keeping the statutes of justification unharmed, and serving God the Almighty blamelessly, thou wast deemed worthy, O Anna, to be mother to the pure Maiden of God who according to the flesh gave birth to Christ; with her we call thee blessed, and celebrate thy holy dormition today with joyful mind.

*Sticheron:*
Having given birth to the palace of Life, O divinely-wise Anna, thou hast truly passed over unto incorruptible life, and joined the choirs of the righteous, who feed thee richly with the Master's joy; therefore we celebrate thy departure, O ever-remembered one, and faithfully honor it today, O divinely-wise ones.

*Sticheron:*
Having given birth to the heavenly gate, O divinely-wise Anna, thou didst ascend to the heavenly dwelling, O glorious one, and now dost enjoy divine glory; whereof do thou also vouchsafe us who celebrate thy honorable dormition by thy prayers, scattering the darkness of passions, and driving away the gloom of grievous circumstances.

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ ди́внагѡ чудесѐ):*[^566]
O radiant solemnity, and bright day, and joyful to the world: the honorable and all-praised dormition of all-blessed Anna, from whom was born she who gave birth to Life; the living ark, she who contained the uncontainable Word, the dissolution of sorrow, and the mediatress of joy: who grants great mercy unto all the faithful.

*Sticheron:*
O most glorious wonder! The fountain of life, having most gloriously given birth to having given birth to her who is alone blessed and pure among women: the all-glorious Anna passes over from temporal life unto endless life, taken from earth to heaven, to rejoice today with the angelic assemblies; whose holy solemnity we now celebrate.

*Sticheron:*
Today a radiant solemnity, ye choirs of the faithful, we honor thy honorable dormition with all reverence in the Divine Spirit; for upon us has arrived the shining grace of healings, burning up the assemblies of aerial evil spirits, and directing with light the minds of those who sing in faith of thy honorable departure, O most marvelous Anna.

*Glory, now and ever, Tone 8, by Anatolius:*[^567]
They who from barren loins sprouted the holy rod, the Birthgiver of God, from whom Christ God shone forth as salvation unto the world: the blameless spouses and holy pair, Joachim and Anna; for having passed over unto the heavenly dwellings, with their daughter, the all-pure Virgin, and with the angels they dance, offering prayer for the world; whom we also, having come together, piously singing, address: Ye who for the sake of the Maiden of God and all-pure Mary were the ancestors of Christ, pray for our souls!

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings:*
The righteous live unto ages *(Wisdom 5:15ff)*.
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God *(Wisdom 3:1ff)*.
Though the righteous happen to die early *(Wisdom 4:7ff)*[^568]: *(printed in this month on the 5th day)*.
*Then Litiya, if it be the temple feast.*
*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 1:*
She who brightly gave birth to the beginning of our salvation today passes over from the earth, the all-honorable Anna. Come therefore, ye feast-lovers and Christ-lovers, taking up flowers of hymns, let us cry unto her: O chaste Anna, blessed is thy womb which bore the Mother of God the Word, and fair are thy breasts wherewith thou didst nourish her; for she, having seedlessly given birth to the Giver of life, was deemed worthy to reign with Him; and He Who has now translated thee unto unceasing and divine life has deemed thee worthy to dwell with His Mother and rejoice together. Therefore we pray thee who faithfully celebrate thy memory, to pray together with her, that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 2:*
