Come, all ye lovers of virginity and seekers of purity: come, let us celebrate Anna's honorable dormition; for she bore beyond nature the fountain of life, Mary the Maiden of God; from whom was born the Deliverer, Who enlightens and sanctifies our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Why has the assembly of those who came together approached reverently into the all-honorable temple of the ancestors? Rejoice together, crying aloud, ye generations of men today: for Anna passes over from earth unto the Lord; standing before Him, and asking that cleansing be granted unto us who celebrate her dormition in faith.

*Tone 4:*
Come, all ye faithful, let us brightly celebrate today the memory of the righteous ones, the ancestors Joachim and Anna: for they gave birth unto us to the Savior's Mother, Mary the all-immaculate. Therefore let us cry aloud unto them: O divinely-chosen pair, holy and God-honored companionship, who from your loins did cause Christ God to shine forth: pray Him that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, now and ever, Tone 5:*
O blessed pair! You surpassed all parents, because you truly brought forth her who is exalted above all creation. Blessed art thou, O Joachim, having become the parent of such a Maiden! Blessed is thy womb, O Anna, because thou didst bring forth the Mother of our life! Blessed are the breasts wherewith thou didst suckle her who nourished Him Who nourishes every breath! Beseech Him, O all-blessed ones, we pray you, that our souls may be shown mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дуйсѧ):*[^569]
Rejoice, spiritual swallow, that didst announce the spring of grace unto us: having lived blamelessly and well in chastity, and a treasure of virginity: having honorably travailed with the immaculate Birthgiver of God, O pure lamb, who didst bear the heifer that bore by the Word the Word Who takes away the sins of the world, the only unwedded one, O Anna, ancestress of the Lord, who hast removed thee from the earth; Whom do thou now beseech, to grant great mercy unto our souls!

*Verse:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise becomes the upright.
Rejoice, pair beloved of God, chosen by the holies of the radiances of the law, O honorable couple: who by divine grace rightly offer salvation, having given birth to Christ the Prince of our life: having born her, Joachim welcoming God, and divine all-honorable Anna, luminaries who caused the unwaning lamp to shine forth, directing grace, the all-pure Birthgiver of God: with her do ye pray, to grant great mercy unto our souls!

*Verse:* Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways.
Rejoice, blessed earth, that didst blossom for the world the divinely-sprouted blessed earth, that didst blossom for the world the divinely-sprouted earth, who didst unceasingly meditate upon the divine law, and didst subscribe grace unto all: having escaped the bond of barrenness by thy birth-giving, and rendering incorruption by death; and having passed over unto divine radiance, O divinely-blessed Anna, ancestress of Christ God, who didst bear the light-bearing lampstand, the Birthgiver of God; with her do thou ask great mercy to be granted unto our souls!

*Glory, now and ever, Tone 8:*
Come, all creation, having assembled with cymbals psalm-like, let us praise divinely-wise Anna, who brought forth from her womb the divine mountain, and who today has passed over unto the spiritual mountain and paradise dwellings; and let us cry unto her: Blessed is thy womb that bore her who truly bore within her womb the Light unto the world; and fair are thy breasts which suckled her who suckled Christ, the Food of our life; Whom beseech, to deliver us from all affliction and assault of the enemy, and to save our souls!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^570]
Thou didst bear in thy womb her who bore Life, the pure Mother of God, O divinely-wise Anna; therefore hast thou now passed over in glory, rejoicing unto the heavenly reception, where is the dwelling of those who rejoice; asking cleansing of transgressions for those who honor thee with love, O ever-blessed one.

*At Small Compline, the Canon of the Holy Women, the acrostic whereof is: "I sing an honorable hymn to the honorable women. Joseph." Tone 8.*[^571]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having drowned Pharaoh the charioteer, the rod of Moses, working a wonder of old, by striking the sea cross-wise and dividing it, saved Israel the fugitive wayfarer, as he sang a hymn unto God.

*Troparion:*
By carnal weakness you cast down the mighty enemy, and joined yourselves unto God, O venerable women, praying that saving power be granted unto all of us.

*Troparion:*
You were wounded by the beauty of the Bridegroom, and cleaved unto Him psalm-like, following His life-bearing footsteps; and for this reason you cast down the serpent enemy.

*Troparion:*
Having withered carnal beauties with fasting labors, you adorned your souls, and entered with Christ the Bridegroom into the radiant bridal chambers, O venerable ones.

*Theotokion:*
A radiant bridal chamber was revealed, even thy womb, O Virgin, for the sake of mercy bodily taking flesh from thee taking flesh from thee, the Lord, Who enlightened all things with the rays of the knowledge of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst despise carnal beauty, O all-honorable one, and didst count fleeting glory as a dream; but in humility and fasting, O Olympias, thou didst seek God, O divinely-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Infant in years, O Euphraxia, thou didst love with faith the Word Who came as an infant upon earth for our sake, and bound thy soul to His image, remaining steadfast.

*Troparion:*
You rejected the mind of the world and the flesh, and by temperance and labors acquired Christ the all-pure Bridegroom and the heavenly bridal chamber, Who grants divine enjoyment unto you.

*Theotokion:*
The sacred trumpets of the sacred prophets of old proclaimed thee: the gate that bore the Light, and the living scroll, upon which beyond words the Word was written not by hands, O all-pure Virgin.[^572]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparion:*
A most zealous receiver of strangers, giving rest to the assembly of the sacred: and in this cheering Christ, Who for mercy's sake became a stranger.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the all-pure beauty of thy beloved Bridegroom, O Euphraxia, thou didst wither the beauty of the flesh through temperance.

*Troparion:*
Having cast away slumber from the eyelids of the soul, O sacred women, you lulled carnal movements through temperance.

*Theotokion:*
Having loved Thee, O Lord, Who wast incarnate from the all-pure Virgin, the venerable women ran after the fragrance of Thy myrrh, wounded by Thy divine love.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me, and direct my paths to the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
Watered by the fountain that watered the universe with the all-golden teachings of the sacred shepherd, thou didst sprout ears of good deeds, O Olympias, wherewith thou nourishest the hearts of us who honor thee.[^573]

*Troparion:*
Having strengthened thy glorious soul with blessed hopes, and by poverty and nourished with immaterial food in purity of mind, for many days patiently fasting, O Euphraxia, thou didst endure, rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
Having put yourselves to death unto the world, you inherited immortal life, and were deemed worthy of the divine bridal chamber, O divinely-blessed ones: having kept your lamps unquenchable with the oil of fasting, O all-honorable ones.

*Theotokion:*
God the Word voluntarily dwelt in the womb that knew not wedlock, and was seen as man, Who kept thee as a Virgin, O all-pure one; desiring Whom, the maidens ascetically followed Him with radiant life.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and to Him will I proclaim my griefs, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near to hades; and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Being young, O Euphraxia, by zeal of sufferings, with pious mind she trampled upon the ancient deceiver, in the weakness of a woman's body; for Christ strengthened her, Who clothed Himself in our weakness.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired temperance and a humble heart, vigilance with prayer, understanding and upright faith, and perfect love, O glorious Olympias, thou wast a temple of God and an inexhaustible fountain of miracles.

*Troparion:*
Having estranged yourselves from worldly pleasures, through endurance of ascetic sufferings, for the sake of Him Who descended upon earth and voluntarily lived as a stranger for our sake, O honorable ones, you therefore acquired in the heavens Him Who received you as strangers, O most marvelous ones.

*Theotokion:*
The Creator, having found thee, O Maiden, to be a pure vessel of purity and honorable virginity, by the Word dwelt in thy most pure womb, and drove away from man all the malice of the enemy.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The mystery commanded" (Повелѣ́нное):*[^574]
Keeping vigil in secret prayers, and delighting in the divinely-inspired Scriptures, glorious Olympias and Euphraxia took upon their shoulders the Cross of the Lord, and following Him temperately, cast down all the deception of the serpent, crying unto Christ: O heavenly Bridegroom, be Thou our confirmation!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Having exchanged corruptible glory for incorruptible through love, O venerable women, continuously enjoying which, rejoicing and making merry and making merry, they sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
O how didst thou endure standing and hunger for many days, O Euphraxia, crying unto Christ the Bridegroom: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Beholding what a vessel of the Holy Spirit thou wast, O Olympias, the great Chrysostom among the Patriarchs richly fills thee with his great teachings, O blessed one.[^575]

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone didst dissolve the curse of the first mother, having given birth to Christ, Who crowns all with blessings, O Virgin; unto Whom we sing: O Lord God, blessed art Thou unto ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Having become conquerors of the tyrant and of the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the youths cried: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst subject irrational passions to reason, O Virgin, and wast betrothed unto the Word as Bridegroom, O blameless and pure one, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having cast down the dark prince by your labors, you obtained crowns of incorruption for us, and the entrances of paradise, O maiden, and divine enjoyment, in the joy of the soul unto ages.

*Troparion:*
Crucified unto the world and wounded with divine love, O sacred virgins, with arrows of temperance you wounded him who wounded Eve by the sweet tasting, singing unto Christ unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
Having born Jesus incarnate from thy pure blood, thou didst gather all virgins, O all-pure Virgin Mother; and with them crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly we confess thee as the Birthgiver of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparion:*
Beauteous doves, and divinely-speaking turtledoves, and radiant and purity-loving swallows, you were led unto heavenly beauties.

*Troparion:*
You were joined unto the celestial choir-ranks, and dwell in the assemblies of the elect, praying with love for us unto the compassionate God.

*Troparion:*
The memory of Euphraxia and Olympias has shone forth as a sun unto the world, enlightening the minds of the faithful who ever magnify Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Being the receptacle of Light, O Virgin, enlighten my soul darkened by passions, and deliver me from the outer darkness by thy supplications.

*July 25.*
*Stichera of the Holy Women, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^576]
Let us worthily honor in hymns with one accord Olympias, disciple of the mystical speaker, prophet, and our teacher; and praising with her the chaste and honorable Euphraxia, as they pray unto Christ that we may be saved.

Offspring of piety and adornment of monastics, with hymns let all of us, the faithful, worthily honor with one accord the chaste Euphraxia, as she prays to the Creator on our behalf, that our souls may be saved.

*Glory...*
Having well stripped off earthly beauty, thou didst choose the good part, O divinely-wise one, which Mary found; and having immediately forsaken all temporal things, by virtue thou didst receive the pearl of great price.

*Both now...*
It is truly meet...

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of Saint Anna, thrice. We do not say the Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
We celebrate thy dormition, O righteous Anna, for Christ God glorified thee with ineffable glory as His foremother; therefore pray to Him Who was translated,[^577] to grant peace unto us through thy prayers, covering our souls from adversaries.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbirth, O pure one, thou hast renewed the mortal nature of the earthborn which had decayed in passions, and raised up all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore as is meet we all bless thee, O most glorified Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ їѡ́сифъ):*
Having surrendered thy soul into the hands of Him Who was incarnate from the Virgin for our sake, for thy Creator and God translated thee unto the life of incorruption; therefore now we honorably bless thee, as the holy mother of the truly only honorable and immaculate Birthgiver of God, and with one accord we cry aloud: Pray to thy Creator and God, Christ our Savior, that our souls may be saved. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Joseph marveled, beholding that which was above nature, and considered in his mind the rain upon the fleece, in thy seedless conception, O Birthgiver of God, the bush unburned by fire, Aaron's rod that budded; and bearing witness, thy betrothed and guardian cried unto the priests: A Virgin gives birth, and after childbirth remains again a Virgin!

*After the Polyeleos After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (Собезначáлное сло́во):*
The multitudes of angels glorify thee, and the human race faithfully sings of thee, for thou hast departed unto Him Who created and saved thee, O righteous Anna; and now having prayed earnestly, deliver from perils those who celebrate thy dormition in faith.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Being a protection for those who possess unwavering hope in thee, O all-pure one, deliver us from diverse circumstances and grievous perils, praying to Thy Son with His Mother,[^578] to save all who sing of thee.

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them.
*Verse:* Many are the afflictions of the righteous.
*Let every breath...*
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 56 [Mt. 13:44–54].*[^579]

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Come, all ye ends of the earth, let us form a spiritual choir, and let us praise the foremother of Christ, saying: Rejoice, O righteous Anna, honored by God! Rejoice, thou who didst well receive righteousness! Rejoice, equal in honor with the righteous, and kin of the saints! With them pray that our souls may be saved.

*Canon of the Birthgiver of God, to 6, Tone 8,*
*And of Saint Anna, to 6, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen Mother, and I will be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I will sing her wonders.

*Troparion:*
Now ask remission of transgressions, and deliverance from evils, and amendment of life for me who honor thy light-bearing memory today, O all-praised Anna.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst give birth in a manner fitting God to her who bore Life; therefore thou hast departed unto ever-living life, enjoying ineffable joy and the unwaning light, O divinely-called Anna.

*Troparion:*
Divine grace translated to joy her whom this pure daughter bore without seed; and she stands with great boldness, praying unto the Lord that all may be saved.

*Troparion:*
Having lived righteously and venerably, O all-praised one, thou hast now departed unto the righteous Lord, to rejoice with the righteous of old; therefore coming together, we honor thee in faith.

*Theotokion:*
From thee the righteous Sun shone forth upon us, and illuminated all the earth with the knowledge of God, and dispersed the gloom of ignorance, O pure, all-immaculate, and all-blessed one.

*Katavasia:* I will open my mouth...

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
We boast not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy besides Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst conceive and give birth to her who conceived Him Who upholds all things, even to her who bore Christ beyond speech; therefore we honor thy dormition, O Anna.

*Troparion:*
Thy glorious memory is accomplished with praises, for thou didst bear for us the pure Mother of God, who is higher than praises, O God-welcoming Anna.

*Troparion:*
As the sun uniting with the moon, glorious Joachim uniting with Anna gives birth to the dawn of virginity, for whose sake the ray of the Godhead shone forth.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bring forth the divine mountain from thy loins, and hast passed over unto the noetic mountains and the dwelling places of paradise, O divinely-blessed Anna.

*Theotokion:*
We have acquired thee, O Parent of God, as a firm protectress; placing our hope in thee we are saved, and fleeing unto thee we are fortified.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^580]
Thou wast mother of the Mother of the Master and Creator, O all-praised Anna, ever keeping His commandments; for this cause having died, thou hast truly departed unto immortal life, and unto the unwaning light. Therefore we celebrate thy light-bearing and holy memory, ever enlightened by the Spirit, and with one accord we cry unto thee: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine, came on a light cloud with an incorruptible hand, and saved those who cry: Glory, O Christ, to Thy power!

*Troparion:*
Learning the things written in the Law, thou didst fulfill them, and wast mother of the Mother of the Lawgiver; therefore all creation rightly makes thy joyful memory today.

*Troparion:*
Blessed is thy womb truly, which bore within its womb her who bore the Light of the world; and fair are thy breasts, which suckled her who suckled Christ, the Nourisher of us all, O Anna.

*Troparion:*
Having lived blamelessly, thou didst blamelessly give birth to her who the Virgin Maiden, who gave birth to the Father's Word; and unto Him hast thou settled with glory, truly deified by honorable communion.

*Theotokion:*
The Virgin's womb truly appeared as a heap of wheat on the threshing-floor, holding the untilled ear of grain, wherewith every nature is nourished, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Christ, the All-powerful!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The ungodly will not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, rising early from the night, sing of Thee, O Only-begotten One, the radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
For on earth thou didst truly bear her who bore the Creator of heaven, Who translates thee today unto the heavenly realms with glory, O most glorious Anna.

*Troparion:*
With immaterial choirs, with an immaterial mind, thou makest merry with joy, being filled with the radiance of the Generous Giver; yet remember us who praise thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly sprouted from the root of Jesse as a fair-flowering rod, O glorious one, thou didst put forth the pure one, who put forth the unfading flower, Jesus our Deliverer.

*Theotokion:*
Unto thee do I flee, the only sovereign protectress of the faithful; upon thee do I place the hope of my salvation: O most holy Sovereign Lady Birthgiver of God, despise me not.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
Let Joachim and Anna, the ancestors of the Lord Jesus God, adorned with righteousness, be worthily praised today in songs.

*Troparion:*
Anna surpassed all praises, for she gave birth to her who surpasses all praise; therefore she is established in the assembly of the righteous.

*Troparion:*
Thy memory is light-bearing and full of brightness, sending forth saving rays into the world; O chaste Anna, fill us with all manner of gifts.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst carry in thy womb the jar that contained the divine manna, O Anna; and living with her now in the heavens, thou prayest for all, O all-praised one.

*Theotokion:*
From Anna thou wast born into the world, O Queen, having given birth to the King of all, and remaining a virgin after childbirth, higher than the cherubim, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^581]
We celebrate the memory of Christ's ancestors, [faithfully asking...] faithfully asking for help, that all may be delivered from every tribulation, crying out: Our God is with us, Who has glorified them as He pleased!

*Ikos:*
Let us all gather prophetically, worthily to sing the blessed translation of the foremother of Christ; for today she has departed from temporal life, rejoicing as she makes her journey in the heavens with joy, as being the mother of the truly existing Birthgiver of God. And she cries aloud in faith: My soul magnifies the Lord, for I have borne His Mother on earth, Who has glorified her as He pleased!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Having saved Thy Abrahamic youths in the fire and slain the Chaldeans whom justice righteously snared, O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Having borne the Mother of the true Life, O Anna, thou hast departed unto the true Life and unto the light, crying aloud: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Anna, being the mother of the Mother of the Deliverer, ascended from earth to heaven, adorned with virtues, crying in praise: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Unto unending life, unto the spacious expanse of the delight of paradise, unto the unwaning light hast thou gone, O God-bearer, crying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Varied with virtues, adorned with the beauty of divine gifts, she passed over unto God, unceasingly crying: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Adorned with the beauties of virtues, O all-immaculate one, thou didst give birth to the Word, Who is fairer than the sons of men, Who adorns with fair comeliness those who sing of thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The childbirth of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious youths in the furnace; then indeed prefigured, but now in reality, it raises up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Mother of the only Birthgiver of God, who was barren before, but now the foremother of Christ: having stripped off the garment of barrenness even as that of mortality, she cries aloud in the land of the living: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ opened unto thee the doors on high and received thee joyfully, for thou didst bear the door through which He Himself passed, and showed it closed after His departure, O divinely-wise *(Duplicate exposure — Fol. 137r: Duplicate photographic exposure of Fol. 137r / Page_0275.jpg)*[^582]
...faithfully asking for help, that all may be delivered from every tribulation, crying out: Our God is with us, Who has glorified them as He pleased!

*Ikos:*
Let us all gather prophetically, worthily to sing the blessed translation of the foremother of Christ; for today she has departed from temporal life, rejoicing as she makes her journey in the heavens with joy, as being the mother of the truly existing Birthgiver of God. And she cries aloud in faith: My soul magnifies the Lord, for I have borne His Mother on earth, Who has glorified her as He pleased!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Having saved Thy Abrahamic youths in the fire and slain the Chaldeans whom justice righteously snared, O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Having borne the Mother of the true Life, O Anna, thou hast departed unto the true Life and unto the light, crying aloud: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Anna, being the mother of the Mother of the Deliverer, ascended from earth to heaven, adorned with virtues, crying in praise: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Unto unending life, unto the spacious expanse of the delight of paradise, unto the unwaning light hast thou gone, O God-bearer, crying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Varied with virtues, adorned with the beauty of divine gifts, she passed over unto God, unceasingly crying: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Adorned with the beauties of virtues, O all-immaculate one, thou didst give birth to the Word, Who is fairer than the sons of men, Who adorns with fair comeliness those who sing of thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The childbirth of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious youths in the furnace; then indeed prefigured, but now in reality, it raises up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Mother of the only Birthgiver of God, who was barren before, but now the foremother of Christ: having stripped off the garment of barrenness even as that of mortality, she cries aloud in the land of the living: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ opened unto thee the doors on high and received thee joyfully, for thou didst bear the door through which He Himself passed, and showed it closed after His departure, O divinely-wise O divinely-wise, right-worthy-of-song Anna; therefore we the faithful honor thee unto ages.

*Troparion:*
Having finished thy life, thou wast holy mother of the Ever-Virgin, who bore the Most Holy Word, Who grants us cleansing and deliverance, O divinely-wise and most marvelous Anna; therefore we the faithful ever sing of thee unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
Held fast by many tribulations and perishing in the tempest of sin, direct me now, O Sovereign Lady Birthgiver of God, unto the haven unbuffeted by the breath of the Spirit; for thou art the haven of Christians; therefore we sing of thee unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding of thy gestation didst blossom forth blessing unto the world; therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
From the earth of thy womb truly was born the holy land, having sprouted without seed the untilled ear of grain, the Nourisher and Lord of the world, Who in divine wisdom translated thee unto inexhaustible nourishment, O Anna.

*Troparion:*
Thy memory has shone forth upon us today brighter than the sun, with the radiance of rich gifts, enlightening us, O all-honorable one, and taking away the gloom of passions; preserve those who celebrate it.

*Troparion:*
Divine Joachim and Anna, divinely-wise and all-glorious, the honorable and light-bearing couple, divinely summon us to make merry at their feast, which the Lord, the Giver of light to all, has made radiant.

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, Lady of all, deliver me now who am enslaved to all manner of transgressions; for thou thyself didst bear the Deliverer of all, Who delivered us from the servitude of sin by His divine nod.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "With the disciples" (Со оу҆чени́ки):*[^583]
As is right and meet let us sing of divinely-called Anna; for having beyond hope given birth to the Virgin Mother Birthgiver of God, she was shown to be kin according to the flesh to Christ our Deliverer; Who divinely received her today on high, as she prays for us and for the pacification of the world. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Podoben: "Heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^584]
Having given birth to the new heaven, Mary, from whom the Sun of glory shone forth, O Anna: today thou passest from earth unto the heavenly realms, rejoicing with Christ, O most glorious one.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Celebrating the honorable memory, we sing unto Thee, O Christ, Who hast gloriously translated Anna from temporal life unto unending glory, as the holy mother of the Mother of the Birthgiver of God and Ever-Virgin, who bore Thee seedlessly above nature. *(Twice)*

Celebrating the holy memory of Christ's ancestors Joachim and Anna, honorable and blameless: we unceasingly glorify the Deliverer and most good God Who translated them unto unaging and incorruptible life.

Unto the immaterial choirs, and the dwellings of the righteous, where are the angelic ranks, where is the assembly of the saints, the joy of those who keep festival is accomplished; and now He has settled the spirit of righteous Joachim and Anna, whom we bless, praising them with piety.

*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*
Come, all ye lovers of virginity and seekers of purity: come, let us celebrate Anna's honorable dormition; for above nature she bore Mary, the God-Maiden, the fountain of Life; from whom the Deliverer was born, enlightening and sanctifying our souls.

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel [Ps. 67:36 LXX].
*Verse:* In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel [Ps. 67:27 LXX].
*Epistle to the Galatians, pericope 210 [Gal. 4:22–31].*[^585]
*Alleluia, Tone 1:* The salvation of the righteous is from the Lord, and He is their defender in time of affliction [Ps. 36:39 LXX].
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 36 [Lk. 8:16–21].*[^586]
*Communion Hymn:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous [Ps. 32:1 LXX].

*July 26: The Holy Hieromartyr Hermolaus and those with him.*[^587]
*Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^588]
O martyr-sufferer Hermolaus, having once hidden thyself for fear of the lawless, thou didst rejoicing reveal the hidden mystery of piety and understanding unto Panteleimon the passion-bearer; and becoming manifest, thou didst zealously die for Christ, Who was pleased to suffer for the sake of goodness.

O martyr-sufferer Hermolaus, having sacredly desired a sacred life: having been a hierarch of Jesus the Almighty, by thy honorable words through grace thou didst save people from unclean darkness; and thou hast passed over unto the supermundane realms, [having washed thyself with...] having washed thyself with suffering blood, O divinely-blessed one.

Let us praise, sacredly glorifying, Hermippus, Hermolaus, and Hermocrates, who faithfully suffered marvelously the tortures of the Lord, and slew the evil one, and washed themselves with a bath, never being defiled by secondary pollutions; and celebrating their memory, let us keep festival.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Save me, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, who didst ineffably bear Christ the Savior; for thee alone have I acquired as protectress, and an invincible rampart, a shelter and rejoicing, and the divine consolation of my soul. Do thou therefore deliver me from the unsleeping worm and eternal fire, O Mother of Christ God.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting sufferings, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried out: Woe is me! O sweet Child, how dost Thou unjustly endure wounds, O Physician Who didst heal human weakness, and deliver all from corruption by Thy mercy?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received crowns of incorruption from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors, and crushed the powerless insolence of demons: by their prayers save our souls.

*Canon of the Saint, whose acrostic is: With songs I honor the labors of Hermolaus. By Joseph. Tone 8.*[^589]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
With songs let us praise the light-bearing, all-praised, and all-glorious memory of Hermolaus, magnifying Christ.

*Troparion:*
Great was thy glory, O all-praised Hermolaus; for thou wast shown to be a most great pleaser of the great God.

*Troparion:*
Having lawfully traversed the contest, O glorious one, thou didst trample the arrogance of the lawless, O all-glorious martyr Hermolaus.

*Theotokion:*
Make Christ merciful unto me, O Virgin, all-immaculate one, in the day of the dread judgment, delivering me from condemnation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who run to Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the spiritual power of the Creator of all, O wise one, thou didst by thy might cast down the malice of the strong tormentor.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast known unto the Almighty, O holy one, having made all-known by thy divine word unto Him.

*Troparion:*
Taught by thee from the vessel of thy spirit, O wise one, Panteleimon also suffered lawfully.

*Theotokion:*
Deliver me, O all-immaculate one, from the grievous tempest of sins and from thoughts assaulting my heart mercilessly.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^590]
Having sacredly finished thy life, thou wast shown to be a priest of the Deliverer, ever enlightening by faith those who approach thee; and at thy end, O blessed one, thou didst divinely shine forth with the radiance of martyrdom and illumined the world; therefore celebrating thy light-bearing and holy memory, we sing of thee and faithfully bless thee: O passion-bearer Hermolaus, pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and only one among women who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all human generations bless thee; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou dost feed with milk the Creator and Lord; therefore both the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbirth, and with one accord we cry unto thee: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who bow down in faith before thy most holy childbirth.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, cried weeping and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for mercy's sake, O longsuffering Lord, Mother! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:*
Directing thy ways by the Divine Spirit, the falling ways of the evil one and the right path were revealed unto many.

*Troparion:*
A sacred priest, a true martyr wast thou, O worthy-of-glory Hermolaus; therefore we all praise thee.

*Troparion:*
With a steadfast mind opposing the lawless one, thou didst receive the crown of martyrdom, having manfully suffered, O sacred one.

*[Fol. 137v]*
*Theotokion:*
As a fiery throne thou bearest the Master, O woman full of the grace of God; pray Him to save me, weighed down by the tempest of sin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst enlighten the ends of the earth with the knowledge of God out of the night of ignorance: enlighten me with the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Those held fast in deceitful night hast thou guided toward the light, and thou didst receive the recompense: to finish thy course by blood.

*Troparion:*
Let Christ's armed warriors—Hermippus, divine Hermolaus, together with Hermocrates—be glorified in sacred hymns.[^591]

*Troparion:*
Having suffered together for the venerable Trinity, the three martyrs now rejoice in the heavens with ten thousands of angels.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as protectress, refuge, and steadfast defense, all we, O Virgin, are delivered through thee from every affliction.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and to Him will I declare my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades; and like Jonah I pray: O God, lead me up from corruption.

*Troparion:*
Hermolaus, the noble warrior, together with Hermocrates and Hermippus, showed forth a single steadfast character: having proclaimed the one God over all and having died for Christ, they received heavenly crowns.

*Troparion:*
By the sprinkling of their shed blood, the sufferers dyed for themselves a royal robe of purple; and having clothed themselves radiantly, they stand before the King of the ages, praying for us together with all the hosts on high.

*Troparion:*
The great Panteleimon was instructed in the divine right faith by thee among the youths; and having suffered lawfully, he became for thee a mediator of martyrdom, O Hermolaus; with him do thou entreat Christ God on behalf of all.

*Theotokion:*
The tempest of measureless transgressions disquiets me and ever casts me down into the abyss of despair, O Virgin; but make haste, stretch forth thy hand to me, and grant me to reach the calm havens of repentance.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йся):*
Having lived piously as a hierarch, thou didst receive the crown of hieromartyrdom; having extinguished the idol-sacrifices, thou wast a good shepherd of Christ's flock, O most wise one, and a true teacher unto Panteleimon. Therefore we honor thee, crying out in hymns: Deliver us ever from perils by thy prayers, O Hermolaus, our father.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

*[Fol. 138r]*
The youths who arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trod down the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^592]

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the unwaning illumination of divine understanding, O venerable one, thou didst guide a multitude of the saved toward the light of the knowledge of God, to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Being brotherly of one mind, great Hermocrates and divine Hermippus drink with thee the cup of martyrdom, O all-blessed Hermolaus, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with love for the divine Trinity, O glorious martyrs, ye quenched the fire of godlessness by the sprinkling of your blood, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
O most pure Virgin, who gavest birth to God in the flesh: save thy people who cry aloud from every sin and sorrow: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud to the Creator and Redeemer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Sacred Hermolaus, being sacredly enlightened, offered a bloodless sacrifice unto Him Who for our sake was slaughtered as a lamb through tender mercy, and for His sake was slaughtered willingly, being offered as an acceptable sacrifice, crying aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Sacred Hermolaus, most great Hermippus, and Hermocrates, steadfast diamonds, spared not the flesh, nor did they sacrifice to idols; but they offered themselves as acceptable sacrifices to the Master, crying aloud: Ye priests, sing praises; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by Christ, godly-wise Hermolaus revealed the mystery hidden from generations unto the faithful, who were hidden for fear of the enemies; and having made it manifest, he openly proclaims God the Word, Who was slaughtered for all, and he dies, and lives with the martyrs unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee as the fair palace and radiant vessel of the King of all, O most pure Lady, praying: Show us to be temples of Him Who was born of thee and Who cleansed away all hostile malice, as we cry unceasingly: Ye people, exalt Christ above all unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

*[Fol. 138v]*
Every ear trembled at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, being made man from a virginal womb. Therefore, O faithful, we magnify the most pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand against the enemies, confessing Christ, O most glorious one, Who confessed the good confession before Pilate; and being led to the slaughter, thou didst sanctify the earth with thy divine blood, and with thy soul thou didst illumine the heavens.

*Troparion:*
Three-rayed torches appeared, kindled by the fire of the Divine Spirit: glorious Hermippus, with Hermocrates and Hermolaus, who illumined the souls of the faithful with divine grace and drove away the polytheistic gloom of impiety.

*Troparion:*
With our lips radiantly and with joyful thought let us all sing of Christ's passion-bearers, who are divinely clothed with divine glory as with a garment, who now dwell in the heavens and pour forth rivers of wonders.

*Troparion:*
O unwaning Sun, Christ: through the sacred supplications of Hermolaus, Panteleimon, and Hermocrates, and of those who suffered with them, cast not away from us Thy mercy, O Word, but having had compassion upon us as is Thy custom, deliver us from evils.

*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Savior, Who wast born and Who didst preserve incorrupt after childbearing her that bare Thee, when Thou shalt sit to judge all things, overlooking my sins and iniquities, as Thou art sinless, as Thou art a merciful and mankind-loving God.

*July 27: The Holy Great-Martyr and Healer Panteleimon.*

*At Vespers, we stichologize "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6, in Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
When thou didst extend loving care of souls unto all, showing forth healing of bodies, then wast thou called worthily Panteleimon,[^593] then wast thou enriched with the namesake calling, having found recompense for virtues and reward for piety through suffering, having shown thyself a crown-bearer and invincible warrior *[Fol. 141r]*[^594]
of Christ our God: pray Him to save and enlighten our souls. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron:*
Ever drawing grace from the supernatural springs of the Savior's divine healings, and pouring it out richly, and imparting it unto those who approach thee, O all-blessed one: with divine grace illumine those who faithfully celebrate thy glorious, all-sacred, and light-bearing festival; for having been anointed with a God-like name openly and with goodness, pray that it be granted unto all who sing of thee.

*Sticheron:*
By many torments was thy fiery love now tested toward Him Who is true desire, by sea and by fire, and assailed by envy; therefore, having cast down the author of evil, thou didst openly receive the life-bearing, richly-giving energy of the Comforter, through which thou, O godly-wise one, didst most gloriously show him that had died to be alive, and didst work diverse healings, O all-blessed one.

*Sticheron:*
Having received the grace of healings, when thou didst discern the piety of all, cleaving unto thy mother's piety and having abhorred thy father's impiety: then the abyss of illuminations, the Giver of light, the Word of God abundant in gifts, by divine foreknowledge foreknowing thy radiance, O most wise Panteleimon, bestowed upon thee a divine ray, which thou didst show forth unto all, living piously.

*Glory, in Tone 6, by Byzantios:*
Today the venerable memory of the unmercenary healer has shone forth, calling the faithful unto a mystical banquet and leading the gatherings of feast-lovers unto a festal celebration. For now there has arisen among us the wonder-working physician who heals the afflictions of all, Panteleimon, the steadfast sufferer; and he prays fervently unto the Lord, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
*Tone 6: Who will not call thee blessed...*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings:*
*Thus says the Lord: All the nations... (see fol. 124)*[^595]
*The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God...*
*Though the righteous happen to die early...*

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 1, by Byzantios:*
Thou didst fight the good fight and didst finish the course of thy suffering, O martyr Panteleimon; therefore thou dost also dance with the angels on high, worthily receiving the kingdom on high for thy sufferings. But pray, O all-blessed physician, that He grant us great mercy.

*Tone 2, by Anatolios:*
Freely *[Fol. 141v]*
Freely dost thou give grace unto men, O glorious martyr Panteleimon, and thou drivest away spirits by the invocation of Christ, and thou grantest the blind to see, as His true favorite. But pray, O all-blessed physician, that He grant steadfast peace unto the world, and great mercy unto those who love thee.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst cast away the deceit of thy father, O all-glorious one, and having prudently received thy mother's words, thou didst bring forth fruit more than gold, according to the Lord's word; and thou wast shown to be an excellent physician, O glorious martyr Panteleimon. Journeying along the path of grace, thou didst find a child lying low, bitten by a viper; and having made prayer unto God and prayed fervently, immediately thou didst raise the lad and tear the beast asunder.[^596] Therefore remember us who faithfully celebrate thy memory, that we may find mercy on the day of judgment.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst desire the faith of thy pious mother, and didst correct the impiety of thy father; for being confirmed in the teachings of Hermolaus, through him thou didst also accomplish baptism, O glorious martyr Panteleimon of the merciful God: O physician of the sick and expeller of passions, pray that we may be delivered from tribulations, who in faith celebrate thy most honorable memory.

*Tone 5, by Byzantios:*
Come, ye martyr-lovers, with one mind let us all praise the passion-bearer of Christ, who fought well for piety and clothed himself with the prize of victory, the luminary of the universe and the ever-shining star of the Church. Therefore unto him let us cry aloud: O glorious martyr Panteleimon, be thou salvation for our souls and bodies, and an unmoved defense, praying unto the Lord unceasingly for us, that He may save us.

*Glory, Tone 6, by Byzantios:*
Today the venerable memory of the unmercenary healer has shone forth, calling the faithful unto a mystical banquet and leading the gatherings of feast-lovers unto a festal celebration. For now there has arisen among us the wonder-working physician who heals the afflictions of all, Panteleimon, the steadfast sufferer; and he prays fervently unto the Lord, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, and faithfully glorify thee as is meet: an unshaken city, an indestructible wall, an unconquerable defense, and the refuge of our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Panteleimon *[Fol. 142r]*
O ye martyr-lovers, let us pay our debt with praises unto the memory of merciful Panteleimon; for having rivaled the marble[^597] of the One Who is unique in compassions and ineffable in mercy, from Him he received divine grace.

*Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.*

*Sticheron:*
Having philosophized according to understanding through grace, meditating upon action whereby thou didst find ascent, O blessed one, unto true visions: thou wast a benefactor unto those afflicted by all kinds of diseases, possessing the divine healing of Christ.

*Verse: Planted in the house of the Lord.*

*Sticheron:*
Who then can worthily recount the sufferings, O martyr Panteleimon, which thou didst nobly endure? Every kind of torment didst thou prove weak, praying for us unto Christ, the Savior and Redeemer, Who now glorifies thee.

*Glory, in Tone 8, by Byzantios:*
Having loved thy mother's piety and abhorred thy father's godlessness, having become a warrior of the Physician of souls, thou didst instruct thyself medically; and by divine grace being well-skilled in both, thou wast shown to be the all-destroyer of passions and the physician of souls. But having acquired steadfastness in sufferings and perseverance in prayers, O martyr of Christ Panteleimon, pray fervently that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
*O Lady, receive the supplications of thy servants...*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy passion-bearer and healer Panteleimon, pray the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The soldiers keeping watch at Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Thou didst preserve thy mother's piety, O blessed one, and didst piously correct thy father's impiety; therefore thou didst also receive grace from God the Ruler of all to heal the diseases of the infirm, O glorious martyr Panteleimon, who with warm faith hasten unto thee.

*Glory: the same. Both now, Theotokion:*
O most pure Virgin, the hope of Christians, Who barest God beyond mind and word: unceasingly pray with the hosts on high to grant remission of sins unto us all, and amendment of life, unto those who ever glorify thee with faith and love.

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йся):*
As an invincible soldier of Christ and a passion-bearer *[Fol. 142v]*
O ye faithful, let us all nobly praise the beauty of the martyrs; celebrating thy holy memory in faith, O all-praised martyr, we glorify thy venerable contests in hymns, O most wise one, and we magnify Christ the Savior.

*Glory: the same. Both now, Theotokion:*
Who can recount the multitude of my unclean ponderings and the tempest of perilous thoughts, O all-immaculate one? Who can declare the upsurging of my bodiless enemies and their deceits? But through thy prayers, O good one, grant me deliverance from them all.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having nobly borne witness for Christ and having proclaimed the faith unto thine own father, thou didst draw him out of the abyss of impiety, O all-praised one; and having not feared the godless philosophy of the tormentors, thou didst put to shame the weak cruelties of the demons. Therefore, having received grace from Christ, heal the diseases of our souls and bodies, O all-praised Panteleimon: pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*
Today the universe celebrates thy venerable contests, O sufferer Panteleimon, glorifying the Giver of life, Who showed thee forth as a fervent protector.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Having stretched forth thy most pure hands, O Virgin Mother, shelter those who hope in thee and cry unto thy Son: Grant Thy mercy unto all, O Christ.

*Anavathmoi, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.*
*Verse: Planted in the house of the Lord.*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 106.*[^598]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
O divine physician, entreat merciful Christ now, O Panteleimon, to deliver me from passions and to have mercy upon me.

*Canon of the Birthgiver of God, with the Heirmos, to 6; and of the Saint, to 8. A work of Theophanes, the acrostic whereof is: I sing of Panteleimon the most excellent martyr. Tone 2.*[^599]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The well-armed power of old laid flat the whole army of Pharaoh in the deep; and the Word made flesh destroyed all-evil sin. The supremely glorified Lord, for gloriously has He been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Having earnestly approached Christ, before thy end in Christ thou didst first raise a dead man; and now, O most wealthy one, enliven me by thy prayers, O Panteleimon, who am dead through the bite of sin.

*[Fol. 143r]*
Thou didst shine forth like a star, bearing an elder's and godly-wise understanding in thy youth; and having acquired the beauty of soul with the comeliness of body, thou didst appear exceedingly fair unto the supremely glorified Word.

*Troparion:*
Having died to the world and having clothed thyself in Christ through the bath of baptism, O blessed one, thou wast a God-bearing instrument and a receptacle of the Spirit's operation, pleasing all and healing the diseases of all.

*Troparion:*
Having made wisdom thy sister and being led as a partaker of life, thou wast honored by her and adorned with a crown of gifts, shining radiantly with divine rays, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
By thy blessings, O most pure and chaste one, having given birth to Christ, Thou didst crown our nature that had become alienated from the Creator through disobedience, and didst deliver it from corruption. Therefore, rejoicing, all we, the faithful, call thee blessed.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The wilderness, the barren church of the nations, has blossomed like a lily at Thy coming, O Lord; in it has my heart been established.

*Troparion:*
Having heard the words of the Spirit, thou wast like good and fruitful ground, receiving the seed of great price, and bringing forth salvation for souls, O thrice-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst deaden the serpent's bitten wisdom, and having been divinely regenerated, thou didst enliven the soul, standing before the King of all.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst put to shame the godless command of the tormentors and didst hasten unto Christ, having acquired Him instead of all things; and thou wast a great merchant, O godly-wise one.

*Troparion:*
Having loved the piety of thy mother, O glorious one, thou didst abhor the much-turbulent godlessness of thy father; for possessing understanding, thou didst choose the better part.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord Who upholds all creation by His divine nod is held in thy hands, O Virgin; entreat Him now to deliver from perils those who sing of thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся їѡ́сифъ):*
Let us, the faithful, radiantly praise the noble sufferer of the piety of Christ, Panteleimon, with psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, through whom the tyranny of the unseen enemy was trodden down by divine power, and healing of diseases was richly granted from the Lord unto those who faithfully celebrate his divine *[Fol. 143v]*
and venerable feast.

*Glory, another, in the same tone and podoben (Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (*Оу҆диви́ся їѡ́сифъ*)):*
O martyr, thou didst listen to the words of wise Hermolaus, and immediately thou didst forsake earthly craftsmanship as vile, weak, and corrupting; having the holy teachings of Christ's life in thy heart, thou didst grant health unto the incurably suffering, O Panteleimon. Therefore pray for those who celebrate thy memory with love.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Tossed about by the tempests of passion, I, senseless one, O pure Lady, call upon thee fervently: despise me not, wretch that I am, to perish, for thou hast given birth to the abyss of mercy, and besides thee I have no hope; let me not appear as a rejoicing and laughingstock unto my enemies, having placed my hope upon thee; for thou art able to do whatever thou desirest, since thou art the Mother of God Who is over all.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou camest from the Virgin, not an intercessor nor an angel, but Thou Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and didst save all me, mankind; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Following the Master in love, thou didst distribute thy wealth to the poor, stripping thyself for suffering, which thou didst endure, foreseeing eternal hopes.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst offer a sacrifice of praise unto God, openly abhorring idol-sacrifices; and all the insolence of the impious didst thou trample down, O passion-bearer.

*Troparion:*
Unto those consumed with envy and battling with wrath didst thou oppose an invincible power, O martyr, and armed with it, thou didst conquer impious torture.

*Troparion:*
He Who was held in the hands of the elder Simeon disposed thee, O Panteleimon, by the words of an elder unto divine understanding and unto the salvation and deliverance of many.[^600]

*Theotokion:*
Young maidens and virgins ran after thee, seeing thee as a Virgin, a Maiden, and a Mother; for thou alone didst unite both in one, O ineffable Bride of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The illumination of those lying in darkness, the salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: unto Thee do I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy radiance, for besides Thee I know no other God.

*Troparion:*
Joyfully didst thou undertake the contests of martyrdom, protected by divine power; and enduring torments steadfastly with patience, rejoicing thou didst cry aloud: For besides Thee *[Fol. 144r]*
we know no other God.

*Troparion:*
Possessing youthful resistance and having acquired elevation by steadfastness of soul, thou didst nobly endure every wound, strengthened with manly mind by the grace of the divine Cross, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
By heavenly teaching the sufferer cast away the discordant utterance of the deceived, and became a most glorious cause of salvation unto many, strengthened by Thy power, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
The sayings of the most wise, and all the most glorious riddles, and the prophecies of the prophets prefigured thee to be manifestly the Parent of God in the end of times; for besides thee we know no other pure one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: O God, lead me up from corruption.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst spit upon deceptive idols and didst abolish exalted deceit, working marvelous wonders and performing healings, O godly-wise one.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be a man of fire, O most excellent one; for thou didst pass through fire and water,[^601] and being stretched on the wheel, thou didst most gloriously destroy the senseless ones.

*Troparion:*
Having cast down the cruelty of the tormentors, thou didst suffer in bodily beatings cast downwards with fortitude of soul, O godly-wise one, through grace.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord Who upholds the ends of the earth as God is held by thy hands, O pure one, in the circumscription of the body, He Who is uncircumscribed in divine form.

*Kontakion, Tone 5:*
Being an imitator of the Merciful One and having received from Him the grace of healings, O passion-bearer and martyr of Christ God: by thy prayers heal our spiritual infirmities, ever driving away the stumbling-blocks of the tempter from those who cry aloud in faith: Save us, O Lord.

*Ikos:*
Let us, the Christ-lovers, piously sing of the memory of the unmercenary healer, the good sufferer, the faithful physician, that we may receive mercy, and especially those of us who have defiled our temples as I have; for he grants beloved healings unto souls and bodies. Let us make haste, therefore, O faithful brethren, to have his steadfastness in our hearts, which delivers from deceit those who cry aloud: Save us, O Lord.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The most wise children did not serve the golden image, but went themselves into the flame and mocked their gods; they cried aloud in the midst of the flame, and the angel bedewed them: The prayer of your lips has already been heard *[Fol. 144v]*
...of your lips has already been heard.[^602]

*Troparion:*
Having suffered lawfully and conquered the enemy, thou wast a calm haven unto those tossed by tempests upon the sea of life, O glorious one, and a light unto those in darkness; and thou didst teach them to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Blessed art thou, and well is it with thee now, O all-blessed one, who hast received thy most rich and blessed hope, prepared through faith for those who cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
The souls of the saints, the choirs of the venerable, and the bodiless angelic ranks received thee as a fellow dancer, O blessed one; for being beheaded with the sword, rejoicing thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
The sword that formerly separated us from the tree of life is now turned back,[^603] receiving those marked with the blood that came forth from the side of thy Son, O all-immaculate one. Blessed art thou, who barest God in the flesh.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew: sing, ye works, as the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The acceptable nature of thy skill heals the blindness of souls, O father, granting light through faith unto those who hasten unto thee, O all-blessed one, and guiding them unto Christ the Savior.

*Troparion:*
Being slain for Christ, Thou hast passed over unto eternal life; and Thou wast shown to be called of God by divine naming, O godly-wise one. Therefore we honor thee, and we exalt Christ above all unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Having cast aside the idle talk of those who are wickedly wise,[^604] by the invocation of Christ thou cuttest down the roots of painful passions, healing those who exalt Christ above all unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
We know thee, O Birthgiver of God, as the fountain and radiance of immortality, as having given birth unto the Word of the immortal Father, Who delivers all from death, who exalt Him above all unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, having taken flesh of the Virgin, has appeared unto us, to enlighten those in darkness, to gather together those that were scattered. Therefore we magnify the all-praised Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, thou hast passed over unto the ultimate desire, where the crowns are, O all-blessed one; truly wast thou counted worthy to receive a blessed end, being with thy Master unto endless ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast obtained thy desire and the fulfillment of thy love, while thy warm blood yet dripped, poured out for Christ; from Whom rejoicing Thou didst receive the crowns of thy contests.

*[Fol. 145r]*
*Troparion:*
In deed[^605] thou didst bridle the mouths of the lions and the gapings of the wild beasts, even as Daniel of old;[^606] for irrational nature also knows how to reverence virtue, O martyr. Therefore, coming together, O most glorious one, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Christ grants Thee bounteous grace, giving us a treasure of healings; and by His all-merciful purpose offering Thee unto everyone who is afflicted, as a calm haven, and a protector, and a defender.

*Theotokion:*
As the fleece, O all-immaculate one, having conceived the heavenly rain in thy womb, thou didst bear Him for us, Who grants tranquility unto those who sing of Him as God, and who proclaim thee the all-praised Birthgiver of God.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "With the disciples" (Оу҆чн҃кѡ́мъ):*
God, foreseeing thy merciful soul, proclaimed thee Panteleimon;[^607] for gratuitously dost thou pour forth healing unto those in need, O holy one, who by thy patience didst conquer the savagery of the tormentors.

*Theotokion:*
With unceasing hymns we piously sing unto the Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, O holy mountain; rejoice, O fiery throne of the King of all; rejoice, O praise of angels and glory of the saints.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Greatly was the providence of the Most High that was upon thee shown forth brightly; for being trained in the art of healing bodily passions, O Panteleimon, thou wast found to be an excellent physician of souls, healing all by the word of grace, and delivering from dark deceit those who hasten unto thee.

*Sticheron:*
Emulating the mercy of God, he was openly named Panteleimon,[^607] doubly receiving the name and that which corresponds to the fact; for as one full of compassion, thou showest mercy unto all, doubly granting healing unto them, nourishing and curing, and guiding them, O glorious and most wealthy one, unto divine and most radiant understanding of Christ.

*Sticheron:*
O godly-wise one, with bodily comeliness thou didst magnanimously exchange the beauty of the soul, and spiritual splendor for carnal beauty; and thou didst astonish the beholders, O martyr, shining with signs and wonders, having received the understanding of the aged in the flower of youth, and being enlightened by faith and grace.

*Tone 5, Automelon, by Germanos:*
The most radiant memory of the martyr shines forth like the sun unto the ends of the earth, and unceasingly grants healings unto the faithful, driving away diseases and curing passions; for Panteleimon ever prays unto Christ, that He grant great mercy unto our souls.

*[Fol. 145v]*
*Glory, Tone 4, by Byzantios:*
Today the memory of the passion-bearer has shone forth; come, ye faithful, let us rejoice spiritually and crown him with hymns: for by the power of the Cross he manfully conquered the unseen enemy, and having not feared the measureless torments of the torturers, he lawfully received the prize of the high calling; and now he rests eternally with the angels. But O martyr of Christ Panteleimon, physician of the ailing and haven of the tempest-tossed, cease not to pray unto the merciful God, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
From all perils preserve thy servants, O blessed Birthgiver of God, that we may glorify thee, the hope of our souls.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Octoechos, on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 3, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel. Verse: In the congregations bless ye God...*
*The Apostle to Timothy, pericope 292.*[^608]
*Alleluia, Tone 2: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.*
*Gospel according to John, pericope 52.*[^609]
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be...*

*July 28: The Holy Apostles and Deacons Prochorus, Nicanor, Timon, and Parmenas.*[^610]

*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
By the divine election ye were preferred to minister unto the needs of the saints, as being full of divine wisdom, illumination, and grace, O glorious ones; and now ye dwell in the heavens, ever rejoicing with the angels.

*Sticheron:*
Shown forth unto the world as luminous candles through the grace of the Spirit, ye richly flashed forth the radiance of understanding and dispelled the gloom of malice; and having passed away, O apostles, ye took up your abode in the unwaning light.

*Sticheron:*
Let Nicanor the most sacred be praised, and Parmenas, and Timon, and with them let godly Prochorus be honored: fulfillers of the commandments of the Word, enrichers of the destitute, and most fervent intercessors unto our God *[Fol. 146r]*
...most fervent intercessors unto our God.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having slipped through corrupt ways, I lie prostrate, and I hasten unto thy tranquility, O Virgin; from the tempest of the adversaries and from manifold temptations deliver me, that I may unceasingly hymn thy grace, O Birthgiver of God, Mother Ever-Virgin.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the immaculate Virgin saw the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, weeping she cried aloud: O my sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How art Thou, Who holdest all things in the hollow of Thy hand, nailed in the flesh upon the tree?

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Canon, whereof the acrostic is: I sing of the rays of the noetic Sun. Joseph. Tone 4.*[^611]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Israel of old traversed the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, and by the cross-shaped hands of Moses conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
Having become partakers of the divine light, O most wise ones: deliver from the gloom of passions those who celebrate your light-bearing and bright festival, and count them worthy of illumination.

*Troparion:*
Ye were drawn by the sweet desire of Christ, O blessed ones; and having piously despised the fair things of corruptible life, ye followed Him, having been instructed in true things.

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as luminaries of the light-giving Orient, ye were light-guides of souls: Parmenas, and Prochorus, and marvelous Timon, and Nicanor the most great.

*Theotokion:*
The goings of God, O all-immaculate one, were made known in thee, having taken place by a strange birth and an ineffable union; therefore we glorify thee as the Sovereign Lady of all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my foundation.

*Troparion:*
Being spiritual clouds, O glorious ones, ye dropped down the water of true understanding for the sanctification of those upon the earth.

*Troparion:*
By election, as being full of His grace, O most wise ones, ye were appointed to minister unto the needs of the saints.

*Troparion:*
Praise glorious Parmenas, and wise Timon, and venerable Prochorus, and Nicanor lofty in mind.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto the Good Lord, Who drops down sweetness, the deliverance from evils *[Fol. 146v]*
...the Good Lord, O pure and all-immaculate Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*
As divine trumpets of truth, honorable vessels of piety, and saving guides of those that go astray were ye shown forth, O apostles of the Lord; rejoice, and entreat Christ God, asking that He grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
He was not separated from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained what He was, Who after childbearing preserved thee, His Mother, a Virgin, even as before childbearing, all-immaculate, the one Lord: Him do thou entreat fervently, to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him hanging upon the Cross Who without pain sprouted forth from her, lamenting as a mother, cried aloud: Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, wishing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth upon the heights of the Church like unwaning stars, the apostles of the Benefactor dispelled the gloom of impiety and enlightened the world.

*Troparion:*
Ministering by grace unto the Gospel, thou wast an excellent teacher in Nicomedia and a divine first-hierarch, O all-blessed Prochorus,[^612] shepherding the flock of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst inherit divine honor, O blessed Nicanor, being slaughtered as a sheep with two thousand that hoped in Christ, on the very day whereon Stephen the Protomartyr met his end.[^613]

*Theotokion:*
Without wedlock thou didst bear God the Word incarnate, O most pure one, beyond mind and word; therefore as is meet we glorify thee, O Virgin, and worthily call thee blessed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning back from dark ignorance those that hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
Thou, O Lord, didst send forth Thy disciples like trumpets, raising up from the graves of deceit the souls of those that hymn Thee.

*Troparion:*
Sacred Timon, having been pastor of the citizens of Bostra,[^614] received the blessed end of martyrdom, being consumed by fire.

*Troparion:*
Great Parmenas fell asleep before the eyes of the apostles,[^615] having completed his divine ministry with fervent diligence *[Fol. 147r]*
...having completed his divine ministry with fervent diligence.[^615]

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, through whom we were delivered from corruption, O all-immaculate one, and from hades, and from the first torturing malice of the deceiver.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demons' blood by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming the life to come, and the Cross and passions of Him Who for tender mercy's sake suffered in the flesh, Nicanor was put to death, being slain by the hands of the Jews.

*Troparion:*
Great Timon, inflamed with the divine desire of the Lord's love, by the commands of the lawless met his end in the midst of fire, having become a divine whole-burnt offering.

*Troparion:*
Your divine, light-bearing, and grace-filled memory, O apostles, sanctifies the thoughts of all who celebrate it with faith, O blessed ones.

*Theotokion:*
The ranks of bodiless angels honor thee, and the apostolic choirs, and the assemblies of martyrs, O Parent of God; for thou didst ineffably give birth unto the King of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 1, Podoben: "The virginal womb" (Оу҆тро́бꙋ дв҃чꙋ):*
Honorable deacons and eyewitnesses of the Word, and chosen vessels of faith were ye shown forth, Nicanor, and Prochorus, Parmenas, and Timon, O glorious ones; therefore today we celebrate your sacred memory, blessing you in gladness of heart.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic children, burned rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having loved Christ with your whole mind, Who alone is truly supreme in beauty, O most glorious ones, ye received the better portion, the Kingdom of heaven.

*Troparion:*
Honored with speech by the Word Who shone forth unto us from the Virgin, and by sacred deeds shown forth as saviors of the faithful, ye transformed their unreason, O divinely-blessed ones.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst suffer strangely, O blessed Nicanor, being slain by the savagery of the impious Jews and brought unto Christ like a lamb, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*
By a word thou didst give birth unto the Word of the Father, O Maiden, Who by a word made heaven and earth; and unto those who glorify Him, O most pure one, He grants a word to those who now purely believe in Him.

*[Fol. 147v]*
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching forth his hands, shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the fiery power, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Ye delivered from the mire of passions and from bondage those who submit unto your teachings, O most wise ones; and ye led them up unto the height of understanding, well-pleasing unto God, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Let godly Parmenas be honored with divine hymns, together with Timon, and all-praised Prochorus and marvelous Nicanor: as disciples of Jacob, and enrichers of the nations, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
The Lord received you as disciples with Peter and the rest of the chosen ones; numbered therefore, O glorious ones, among the named Seventy,[^616] and well chosen as being full of faith, to minister unto the needs of the saints, O all-praised ones.

*Theotokion:*
The voices of the God-proclaiming prophets received their fulfillment when thou gavest birth unto the transcendent Lord, Who was manifested by them for the salvation of the faithful, who cry aloud unto thee, O full of God's grace: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, an uncut mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Ye were shown forth as vessels of the Word, having contained all the radiance, being the best of honorable ministers, having served the needs of the saints; and with the Word, O most wise ones, ye enjoy ineffable glory.

*Troparion:*
Today all creation celebrates your memory, O Timon, Parmenas, and Nicanor, with Prochorus the preacher of God, worthily calling you blessed, O seers of God and all-blessed ones.

*Troparion:*
Ye were taken up unto great glory as disciples of the Word of God, and now ye rejoice with the apostolic and bodiless choirs; with them ever pray, O glorious ones, that we may find mercy.

*Theotokion:*
Joyfully we cry unto thee, O Virgin, the voice of the archangels: Rejoice, O joy of angels, honor of apostles, preaching of prophets, and crowning of martyrs.

*[Fol. 148r]*
*July 29: The Holy Martyr Callinicus.*[^617]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O godly-wise martyr Callinicus, desiring to die piously rather than to live, thou didst truly depict Christ's death; whence thou didst obtain immortality and unwaning blessedness, where the ranks of angels and of all the sufferers are, wherein rejoicing thou didst make thine abode, O glorious one.

*Sticheron:*
The shrine of thy relics is shown forth as pouring out the flower of healing and as a river filled with unfailing gifts, a gratuitous cure, an ark of sanctification; from which we who faithfully approach draw ever-living health, O glorious martyr, and for thy sake we truly glorify the marvelous God.

*Sticheron:*
O all-blessed martyr Callinicus, against the unseen enemies having the invincible help of the unseen God, thou didst set up trophies of immortality, having cast down by the struggle of thy sufferings him who boasted to destroy the earth and the sea, and having drowned him in the flow of thy blood.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
The champion of piety, the destroyer of the enemy, the converser with martyrs, who was the temple of the Holy Spirit, the soldier of Christ the King, the truly inexhaustible treasure of healings, the confirmation of the faithful: let us today honor marvelous Callinicus with hymns.
