*Both now, Theotokion, in the same tone and podoben (Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (*Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ*)):*
Having taken flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth as the mediatress for mankind, O pure one; therefore deliver thy servants from every affliction and distress, and from the snares of the crafty enemy, and make partakers of the radiance of the elect all who glorify and worship thee.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same tone and podoben (Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (*Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ*)):*
What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Dost Thou, Who upholdest all creation, lifted up upon the tree, die, Who givest life unto all? Thus weeping the Birthgiver of God said, when she beheld Him lifted up upon the Cross, Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and man.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Callinicus, O Lord, in his contest received an imperishable crown from Thee *[Fol. 148v]*
...our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers and crushed the demons' powerless insolence. By his prayers save our souls.

*Canon, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
He Who is mighty in battle cast Pharaoh's chariots and his power into the sea; let us sing a new song, for He is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
Having mounted upon a noetic chariot, in truth thou didst drown the captains of noetic Egypt[^618] in the streams of thy blood, O praise of martyrs, Callinicus.

*Troparion:*
Having clothed thyself with divine vesture, O wise one, thou didst strip off earthly garments, O all-blessed one; and thou didst run unto the contest and put the enemy to shame.

*Troparion:*
Having undone the intricately woven wiles of the enemy by the bonds of thy sufferings, thou didst cast him powerless unto the ground, O Callinicus, and didst trample him under thy feet.

*Theotokion:*
Whom the prophetic choir foresaw as the heavenly door and the unburned bush, thee, O pure Virgin Mother, we have known as having given birth unto God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou Who dost establish the thunder and create the spirit, establish me, O Lord, that I may truly hymn Thee and do Thy will; for there is none holy as Thou, our God.

*Troparion:*
Wounded with the love of Christ, enduring in the contests, the martyr cried aloud: In the fragrance of Thy myrrh I run, following Thy passion by the suffering of my blood.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast preserved without harm from the serpent's darts; for enduring his wounds upon thy heel, O wise one, thou didst crush his head in thy sufferings, O Callinicus.

*Troparion:*
Having cut down the thorns of deceit with the spiritual sword, without deceit thou didst bear forth a hundredfold fruit of the faith of Christ, O all-blessed one, in the midst of the pains of thy person.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone wast the Mother of an ineffable birthgiving: having conceived in thy womb God the Word Who was before thee, and thou didst nourish Him with milk, O most pure one, as an unoriginate Infant.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*
Having divine zeal in thy soul, O glorious one, thou didst patiently rebuke all the lawless; and having drawn near unto torments, thou didst traverse thy contest with noble mind, O martyr Callinicus: entreat Christ God, to grant us great mercy *[Fol. 149r]*
...to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
He was not separated from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained what He was, Who after childbearing preserved thee, His Mother, a Virgin, even as before childbearing, all-immaculate, the one Lord: Him do thou entreat fervently, to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him hanging upon the Cross Who without pain sprouted forth from her, lamenting as a mother, cried aloud: Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, wishing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard Thy report, O God, and was afraid; I understood Thy works, and was astonished, O Lord; for the earth is full of Thy praise.

*Troparion:*
Washing thyself in the streams of thy blood, O all-blessed one, thou wast shown forth purest in spirit, and thou didst attain unto the enjoyment of the bodiless ones, O martyr Callinicus.

*Troparion:*
A precious stone in truth wast thou built into the Church, and thou didst grind to powder the idol temples and cast them down unto the ground by the unyielding resistance of thy sufferings.

*Troparion:*
The deadly flatteries of the enemies didst thou in truth cast aside, O martyr, by spiritual words; and being strengthened in soul, thou didst set up a trophy of victory over deceit.

*Theotokion:*
The great Moses, who prefigured thee as the bush on Sinai, beheld it unburned, carrying the fire in thy womb, O pure one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Shine forth upon me, O Lord, the light of Thy commandments, for my spirit rises early unto Thee, and hymns Thee: for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace.

*Troparion:*
Having slipped into unstable mire, O Lord, and having fallen into the depth of my sins and into the tempest of my grievous deeds, I cry unto Thee: Be Thou my Helper.

*Troparion:*
Pierced with iron spikes through the soles of thy feet, O martyr, thou didst sing: In the way of Thy testimonies I have richly delighted, O Master,[^619] enduring wounds, O prize-winner, in the hope of recompense.

*Troparion:*
Having made a good merchandise of martyrdom through the oil of mercy, thou didst brighten thy lamp, O all-blessed one; therefore thou didst become wholly an immaculate sacrifice, an acceptable whole-burnt offering.

*Theotokion:*
Preaching of the prophets, praise of the apostles, and of the martyrs *[Fol. 149v]*
...adornment of the martyrs, art thou, O Birthgiver of God, having brought forth[^620] the divine Word, our praise, O most pure one, Who was before the ages from the Father.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Engulfed by the tempest of sins, as if held fast in the belly of the whale, with the prophet I cry aloud: Bring up my life from corruption, O Lord, and save me.

*Troparion:*
The fold of thy flock was preserved unharmed by the wild beasts; for holding fast, O glorious one, the sturdy staff of thy sufferings, it was shown forth as a driver-away of wolves.

*Troparion:*
By thy suffering, the fire of deceit was quenched, and the beauty of faith shone forth in thy confession, O most glorious one; therefore, O imitator of Christ, thou hast shone forth like the sun unto the ends of the earth.

*Troparion:*
As a faithful preacher of the faith, ready to rebuke the lawless, he cried unto those who threatened him: Weaken not the steadfastness of the soul, for the arrows of the godless are light.

*Theotokion:*
Abiding after childbirth unwedded, even as before childbirth, a Virgin, and incorrupt; for the mystery that is in thee, O Bride of God, is known to be beyond all mind and word.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Thou hast worthily inherited the highest delights, O Callinicus; for being exceedingly kindled with the love of Christ, thou didst courageously dare to face the fire; standing before Him now, cease not to pray for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The burning fire was quenched in the furnace, bedewing the children,[^621] who sang and said: Blessed art Thou, Who alone art the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Unto me, fearful torments are the occasion of delight, causing laughter; for neither fire nor wounds can separate me from my true Lover, the martyr cried aloud.

*Troparion:*
By a bitter life, O glorious one, thou didst cast down the adversary unto the earth, and wast shown forth as true to thy name, a noble victor over the torturer.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst put the devil to shame, and didst make the angels glad by thy sufferings, O glorious one, and didst cry aloud unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, Who alone art the God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
By thy childbearing, O all-pure Virgin, thou didst loose the curse of our first-mother Eve; for thou art the Mother of all good things, having obtained joy for all.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
By Thy creative wisdom, O Master, Thou didst establish the heavens, and didst again establish the earth *[Fol. 150r]*
...and didst again establish the earth, as Thou knowest its depth, having founded it upon the boundless waters. Therefore we all cry aloud, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, unceasingly.

*Troparion:*
Rejoice, delight of true sufferers; rejoice, adornment and embellishment of the faith; rejoice, most radiant light-bearer of martyrs, who by thy suffering didst destroy deceit and shine forth light unto the world, O all-wise Callinicus.

*Troparion:*
The torturer was put to shame by the struggles of thy resistance; the evildoers were vanquished by thy wounds, O blessed one; thy soles were pierced with nails,[^622] but all deceit was burned up, and the idol temples were abolished, O godly-wise one, by the fire of thy suffering.

*Troparion:*
With what boldness thou hast toward God, O Callinicus, bow down and pray unceasingly for those who honor thee; stand before thy flock, delivering it from the diverse snares of the crafty one, and establish it in faith; for through thee it grows and is strengthened.

*Theotokion:*
In the Spirit the prophets prefigured thee, O Virgin, as the tabernacle, and the table, and the jar of manna, and the lampstand, and the most holy mountain, and the cloud, and the tablets, and the ark, and the rod, and the divine gate, through which the ancient paradise was opened unto all men.[^623]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
With unceasing hymns we magnify the seedless childbearing of Christ our God from thee, O pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
The vesture of faith was woven for thee from on high, wherewith thou didst strip the enemy naked at the judgment seat, O glorious martyr.

*Troparion:*
The torturer did not succeed in beguiling the martyr with flatteries, but he was established in the faith.

*Troparion:*
A pure whole-burnt offering and an acceptable sacrifice, O glorious Callinicus, thou wast offered unto God by the fire of thy struggles.

*Troparion:*
Beautiful are thy feet, O sufferer Callinicus, pierced with nails, and walking toward the divine proclamation.

*Theotokion:*
We have gained thee as a protection, and a harbor, O pure one, and a weapon, and a rampart, and a steadfast hope in afflictions evermore.

*[Fol. 150v]*
*July 30: The Holy Apostles Silas, Silvanus, and Those with Them.*[^624]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Illumined with divine outpourings of light, O apostles, like winged eagles ye traversed the world, driving away the gloom of polytheism and making all men sons and heirs of the day; therefore we honor you, and celebrate your holy and auspicious festival, venerating your relics.

*Sticheron:*
Silas and Andronicus, Silvanus and Crescens, we sacredly call blessed, and with praises let us today with joyful heart honor marvelous Epenetus, the disciples and preachers of the Lord; through whom we were delivered from the idolatrous lure, and were piously taught the splendor of the Trinity.

*Sticheron:*
Christ sent you forth into all the world, O apostles, cleansing the suppurations of malice, renewing the souls of those wounded by soul-corrupting venom, and leading them unto perfect health; therefore we honor you as all-praised benefactors, most wise physicians, and spiritual instruments.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, sun-like ray, throne of the unsetting Sun, who hast shone forth the incomprehensible Sun! Rejoice, O mind illumined with divine radiances, lightning-bright dawn that hast enlightened the ends of the earth! True glittering of gold, beautiful in form and all-undefiled, who hast shone forth the unwaning light unto the faithful!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Christ put to death, Who puts to death the deceiver, the all-pure Lady cried aloud, weeping bitterly; and marveling at the longsuffering of Him Who came forth from her womb, she cried: O my most beloved Child, forget not Thine handmaid! Tarry not, O Lover of mankind, my Consolation!

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He grant our souls remission of transgressions.

*Canon, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who by His divine command dried up the impassable, surging sea, and guided the Israelite people to pass through on foot,[^625] let us sing unto the Lord; for He is gloriously glorified.

*[Fol. 151r]*
*Troparion:*
Ever standing before the divine throne, and enlightened by the unsetting radiance, O holy ones, ask spiritual illumination for all, that it may be sent down from the Father of lights unto us who faithfully honor you.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the Sun of righteousness that shone forth from the Virgin, Maiden of God, O glorious ones, ye cleaved unto Him by divine communion, having become light, and drove away all the gloom of deceit.

*Troparion:*
Bearing the divine law upon your tongues, ye traversed all the earth, taking away its iniquity, O wise Crescens and Andronicus, Silas and Silvanus, and sowing the saving word.

*Troparion:*
Let us praise glorious Epenetus, who was the divine boast of Carthage, a disciple of the Word, a fountain of healings, a great preacher of things beyond the mind, and the confirmation of the faith.

*Theotokion:*
Ineffably thou didst bear the all-hymned God, Who was clothed in flesh from thee for goodness' sake, O all-hymned and pure one; therefore with grateful voices we hymn thee and call thee blessed, even as thou didst prophesy of old.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth over mine enemies; for my spirit rejoiced to sing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous more than Thee, O Lord.[^626]

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming the Master's Incarnation and loosing the bond of malice, ye loosed those who of old were bound by unbelief, O wise and great preachers, and brought them unto God through faith.

*Troparion:*
Having become beacons of unsetting light-pouring, ye drove away the darkness of godlessness, showing unto men the light of knowledge, O all-praised ones; therefore ye are ever called blessed.

*Troparion:*
Into all the earth went forth the divine apostolic proclamation, as David sings, teaching the Savior's passions and Resurrection, whereby we have all risen from the tombs of corruption.

*Theotokion:*
Finding thee purer than the angels, the unapproachable Light entered into thy holy womb, O Birthgiver of God, and was manifested as man, taking flesh from thee, known in two natures.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*
Having become the divine coal of the Comforter, kindled with warmth, O all-praised apostles, ye dispersed the darkness of godlessness and sacredly warmed the faithful; therefore, honoring your memory today, we ask to receive great *[Fol. 151v]*
...mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine dwelling of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the angels; cleanse me, who beyond all others have become dust and am defiled by bodily transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him hanging upon the Cross Who without pain sprouted forth from her, lamenting as a mother, cried aloud: Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, wishing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I sing of Thee, for with hearing, O Lord, I have heard Thy report and was afraid;[^627] for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who am gone astray. Therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O most merciful One.

*Troparion:*
By the power of Him Who made all things, having strengthened the world like winged eagles, ye saved those who were in distress in the deep of cruel afflictions, O God-bearing ones, and guided them unto a calm haven.

*Troparion:*
Strengthening the enfeebled thoughts, O glorious Silas, by the word, together with Paul the preacher, thou didst traverse the whole world, showered with many afflictions, and wast manifested as a savior unto the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with shining graces, thou didst journey from the east unto the west, like a radiant dawn alongside Paul, O all-wise Silas, being as it were a sun, and ye enlightened the hearts of the nations.

*Troparion:*
Let Crescens and Andronicus be praised, and together with Silas let Silvanus be magnified today, and let Epenetus be lauded; for they are clusters of Christ's vine, dropping forth saving sweetness.

*Theotokion:*
A superabundance of wonders is the miracle that was wrought in thee; for beyond the laws of nature thou givest birth unto the Lawgiver Christ, Who looses the transgression of the first-created, O all-undefiled one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Illumination of those lying in darkness, salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: early in the morning I awake unto Thee, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy radiance, for other than Thee I know no God.

*Troparion:*
The magnificent assembly of Thy disciples was on earth, preaching Thy glorious coming, Thy passions, and Thy Resurrection, O Word of God, all-good Jesus.

*Troparion:*
The radiant and most bright vessel of the Comforter, the guide of the errant, the habitation *[Fol. 152r]*
...most glorious praise, the adornment of the apostles: now let us praise wise Silvanus.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened according to thy name by the help of the Word, O all-wise Andronicus, thou dost pass through, establishing the hearts weakened by the temptations of the wrestler, and guiding them into the knowledge of God.

*Theotokion:*
David described thee beforehand as a curdled mountain, the mountain of our God, a fertile mountain;[^628] for in thee, O Maiden, we have recognized the journeyings of Him Who impoverished Himself in the flesh, and led us back unto our first blessedness.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
From the whale Jonah cried aloud unto the Lord: Bring me up from the depth of hades, I pray Thee, that as unto the Deliverer, in the voice of praise and the spirit of truth, I may sacrifice unto Thee.[^629]

*Troparion:*
By pure beckonings lifting men up unto the Lord, ye drew unto the heavenly height of sublime contemplation and action those who were bent downward into the pits of perdition, O wise ones.

*Troparion:*
Having preached unto the ends of the earth the Unity in essence and Trinity in Persons, O divinely-blessed ones, ye utterly dispersed the mist of polytheism, and were shown forth as beacons unto souls.

*Troparion:*
Having anointed thy mind, O all-wise Crescens, with the oil of gladness, Christ showed thee forth as the first-shepherd of Chalcedon, as His disciple, piously pointing out the path unto those that have gone astray.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee beautiful in goodness, the Creator made His abode in thee, and taking flesh was manifested as beautiful beyond all the sons of earth-born men, O most holy and all-undefiled Virgin.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Ye were shown forth as vines of Christ's vineyard, bearing clusters in virtues, O wise ones, pouring forth for us the wine of salvation; receiving which, we are filled with gladness, celebrating your all-honorable memory; wherein pray for the remission of our sins, O apostles of the Lord.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Radiant were the youths shown forth, most philosophical of old; for from a God-receiving soul, theologizing with their lips, they sang: O supremely divine God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
Let us harmoniously hymn Silas and Silvanus, the vessels that contained all the grace of the Comforter, who destroyed the former receptacles of deceit and made them acceptable unto God.

*Troparion:*
Who is sufficient to praise marvelous Epenetus? For he was conformed unto Christ, having endured sufferings for His sake, and of the Kingdom *[Fol. 152v]*
...is become a partaker of His kingdom, having fought the good fight.

*Troparion:*
Let Crescens be sung with divine hymns, who showed the hidden ways of Christ, and in His flesh condemned the condemned sin, showing many to be uncondemned.

*Theotokion:*
Loose the bonds of my transgressions by thine intercession, O pure one, and bind me unto the perfect love of thy good Son, who am ever drawing away from Him by unseemly pleasures.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Disobeying the torturer's decree, the all-hymned three children, cast into the furnace, confessed God, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^630]

*Troparion:*
They gave no sleep to their eyes, the vigilant ministers of the Word, until they delivered the faithful from the heavy slumber of demonic deceit, and showed them forth as sons of light and of the day.

*Troparion:*
Having become the chariot of God, ye possessed Him Who guided you unto the heavenly journey, O glorious apostles, and He shattered all the weapons of deceit.

*Troparion:*
Let all-wise Silas, as a minister of Christ, be worthily praised, and with him let Crescens and Andronicus be faithfully glorified: the beacons who have enlightened all nations.

*Triadikon:*
Let us sing of the life-originating Trinity, threefold in Hypostases, yet understood as one in essence, and singing let us say: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Having ineffably incarnated God, the Light Who shone forth before the sun, Who came unto us in the flesh from the virginal loins: O blessed and all-undefiled Birthgiver of God, thee do we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Ye became saving physicians of souls and bodies; with spiritual medicines ye cleanse the infirm, and doubly display health, O divinely-illumined apostles; therefore ye are worthily called blessed.

*Troparion:*
Ye were shown forth kindled by the warmth of the Spirit like coals; ye melted the ice of deceit, and warmed frozen hearts through faith, O all-wise ones, guiding them unto the saving springtime.

*Troparion:*
Let us form a choir in the house of our God, glorifying Silvanus and Silas, and Andronicus and great Epenetus, and all-wise Crescens, as true preachers and our sacred intercessors.

*Troparion:*
Holy and full of the gifts of the divine Spirit, sacred truly and praiseworthy is your memory, which with beneficent *[Fol. 153r]*
...rays, illuminating the fullness of the pious, commands us to sing of you, O wise ones.

*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Almighty One, Who art to come again in the glory of the Godhead to judge the universe, by the entreaties of her who unwedded gave birth unto Thee, O Master, and send me not into the fire, condemned by transgressions.

*July 31: Forefeast of the Procession of the Honorable and Life-Creating Cross of the Lord; and the Holy and Righteous Eudocimus.*[^631][^632]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Cross, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Let all human nature now joyfully dance, keeping the forefeast; for the Wood of the Cross is about to be set forth, an inexhaustible medicine for all the faithful, loosing diseases and passions and every affliction.

*Sticheron:*
Come, ye faithful, let us behold a strange wonder: how the Wood of the Cross relieves the burning of every disease, and quenches the fever-heat of passions in those who keep the forefeast with faith and honor its solemnity.

*Sticheron:*
Ye that live, and ye that are dead, keep the forefeast together! For the Tree of life, which put hades to death, the Guardian of mankind, the Resurrection of the dead, comes now to grant unto all men the ungrudging grace of healings.

*Other Stichera, of the Saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O blessed and godly-wise Eudocimus, thy pure soul, shining with orthodox understanding, shone with the splendor of virtues, and illumines the fullness of the faithful, driving away the gloom of demons; therefore, as a partaker of unsetting grace, we all piously honor thee, O richly-marvelous one.

*Sticheron:*
Having a soul devoted to prayer, a pure mind, a vigilant heart, unswerving faith, and love truly unfeigned, thou didst pass from earth unto heavenly things, and makest thine abode with the choirs of the venerable; therefore we honor thee with sacred hymns, and all piously call thee blessed, O blessed Eudocimus.

*[Fol. 153v]*
*Sticheron:*
Strengthened by divine teachings, thou didst drive away the fleshly passions from the soul, and wast a pure dwelling of the Holy Trinity; therefore thou didst depart unto divine enjoyment, while God now preserves thy body unconsumed, O blessed one, by divine judgments whereby He Himself knows, manifestly glorifying thee, O Eudocimus.

*Glory, Tone 4:*
How shall we not marvel at thy good understanding, or how shall we not praise, O venerable Eudocimus, thine angelic life, the chastity of thy mind, thy meekness, humility, and silence, and thine unceasing almsgiving? For thou wast adorned with all virtues, O blessed one; therefore ineffable joy and the Kingdom of heaven await thee.

*Both now, in the same tone (Tone 4):*
Today, as truly holy-spoken, David's word has received its fulfillment; for lo, we worship at the place, the footstool of Thy most pure feet,[^633] and hoping in the shadow of Thy wings, O all-bountiful One, we cry aloud unto Thee: Let the light of Thy countenance be signed upon us! Exalt the horn of Thy faithful people, O Christ, through the elevation of Thy precious Cross, O most merciful One.

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Both now...*
*Tone 2. Idiomelon:*
Thou art my mighty protection, O tripartite Cross of Christ; sanctify me by Thy power, that with faith and love I may venerate and glorify thee.

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 4:*
He Who called thee from earth unto the heavenly mansions preserves thy body unharmed even after death, O holy Eudocimus; for in chastity and a pure life, O blessed one, thou didst live, not having defiled the flesh; therefore with boldness entreat Christ God, that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... Both now...*
*Troparion of the Cross, Tone 4:*
Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting victories unto our pious King[^634] over adversaries, and by Thy Cross preserving Thy commonwealth.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Cross twice; Glory, of the Saint; Both now, of the Cross.*
*Canon of the Octoechos, one; and of the Cross, to 4; and of the Saint, to 4.*
*Canon of the Cross, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us all sing a triumphant song unto God, Who wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel; for He is glorified.[^635]

*[Fol. 154r]*
*Troparion:*
The divine and surpassing Cross sends forth the ray of grace, and enlightens all who venerate it and piously hymn the crucified Christ.

*Troparion:*
The veneration of the Cross exalts those who glorify Him with love and faith as God and the Lord Who suffered in the flesh and sweetened us with divine sufferings.

*Troparion:*
The veneration of the Cross pours forth the fragrance of gifts, ever wafts sweetness upon all who glorify Christ, and takes away all the stench of souls.

*Theotokion:*
Having known the Lord Who was incarnate of thee, O Bride of God, and Who endured the Cross and death, we hymn Him as Deliverer, calling thee blessed.

*Canon of the Saint, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the entire army of Pharaoh; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord; for He is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
Possessing an elevated life, and having become wholly radiant with divine ascents, ask illumination for me who praise thy venerable struggles; by which having lived well, O Eudocimus, thou hast obtained that which thou didst hope for.

*Troparion:*
Loving wise teachings, and exercising thyself in the reading of the sacred Scriptures, thou didst turn away from foul and dishonorable pleasures, O all-blessed one, and wast made fragrant, adorned with divine gifts.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst drive away the slumber of the eyes from the soul, O blessed one, being vigilant and uniting thyself unto God by all-night standings, unbent by the necessities of nature; therefore thou hast attained unto the unwaning day.

*Theotokion:*
As the animate bridal chamber of God, and the censer of the noetic and light-bearing coal, we call the ever-graced Birthgiver of God blessed, crying aloud: Rejoice, mediatress of the saving Resurrection!

*Ode 3 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*
Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who hast established the second heaven above the waters, and founded the earth upon the waters, O Almighty One.

*Troparion:*
Prefigured in types, working most glorious wonders, the Cross now venerated works most marvelous things, sanctifying and enlightening those who glorify the Creator.

*Troparion:*
The Cross of Christ, when venerated, is the divine victory in battles, [scattering] the encampments of the enemies *[Fol. 154v]*
...vanquishing the [encampments of the enemies], grants unto orthodox kings mighty victories.

*Troparion:*
The Cross, set forth today in the temples and habitations, preserves the foundations thereof unshaken, and vanquishes every assault and craft of the adversaries.

*Troparion:*
The Cross, venerated today with piety, fills those who worship it with divine grace and spiritual censing, and removes the clouds of temptations and afflictions.

*Theotokion:*
He Who was born of thee, O Birthgiver of God, delivered by the Wood of the Cross mankind that was driven out by the deceitful one, and exalted unto the knowledge of Himself those who piously glorify thee.

*Ode 3 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against my enemies; for my spirit was made glad when singing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous above Thee, O Lord.[^636]

*Troparion:*
Giving heed unto the life-flowing headwaters, thou didst drink the water of salvation, abhorring bitter sin, and singing thou didst say unto God, O Eudocimus: For there is none holy above Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst acquire mercy in imitation of Christ; thou didst distribute riches unto the needy, and didst obtain heavenly wealth that intercedes for thee, crying aloud, O Eudocimus: For there is none holy above Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Through divine love for the Creator of ever-living life, thou didst receive the heavenly inheritance, and art a partaker of the Tree of life, deified by communion, and singing, O glorious one, thou didst say: For there is none holy above Thee, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Higher and more honorable than the angels, thou didst bear in the womb the Creator of the angels, and didst give birth ineffably unto Him, to Whom we all sing: Thou art our God, and there is none holy above Thee, O Lord.

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4:*
Well-pleasing through good deeds,[^637] thou wast tried like gold in the furnace by afflictions, O glorious Eudocimus; therefore even after death thou pourest forth wonders richly like waters, and dost stay diseases, ever praying for us, that we may receive the remission of sins.

*Glory... Both now... of the Cross, Tone 4, Podoben: "Lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Radiantly it is shown forth brighter than the sun; with the light of the Cross it enlightens all creation by its dawn. Therefore, O Lover of mankind, Savior of all things, enlighten our mind and senses for its sake; for we possess it as a weapon, an invincible victory unto the world.

*Ode 4 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*

*[Fol. 155r]*
Foreseeing in the Spirit the incarnation of the Word, O Prophet Habakkuk, thou didst proclaim, crying aloud: When the years draw near, Thou shalt be known; when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown forth; glory to Thy power, O Lord![^638]

*Troparion:*
Depicted, the divine and precious Cross firmly drives away the prince of darkness from the air, extends the rays of grace unto those who depict it, and illumines them as they sing of Christ God.

*Troparion:*
Depicted in hearts, the divine and most pure Cross calms the sea of passions, stays the tempest of perils, and leads unto the calm of uprightness all who depict it with love.

*Troparion:*
Sending forth as it were burning coals, the precious Cross burns up all the operations of the enemy, drives away his power, and refreshes many in faith, who praise and sing with love of Christ Who was nailed upon it.

*Troparion:*
The all-honorable and divine Cross, venerated with faith, pours forth spiritual streams of gifts and waters of healings, and unceasingly waters those who worship and honor it.

*Theotokion:*
As Queen of all and our Sovereign Lady, we glorify thee with love, O most holy and pure one, having been enlightened by the passions of thy Son, and having known Him as the Benefactor of all; glory to His grace!

*Ode 4 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
Thou didst come from the Virgin, not an intercessor, nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and hast saved all of me, a man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Keeping the ancestral teachings unharmed with orthodox wisdom, from infancy thou didst possess an undefiled life and a merciful disposition, O richly-worthy of wonder.

*Troparion:*
Neither worldly turmoil, nor the holding of power, nor temporal glory turned away thy desire toward God, O Eudocimus; but truly thou wast well pleased by divine doing.

*Troparion:*
Elevated ever by divine meditations, thou didst humble the enemy, and received the working of healings, O Eudocimus, from Him Who directs all things by righteous judgment.

*Troparion:*
Distilling the sweetness of rich healings, the shrine of thy relics drives away the passions, O Eudocimus, from those who honor thee with faith, and burns up all the legions of demons.

*Theotokion:*
Sung by the hosts on high, thou didst give birth unto God Who truly became man; Whom entreating for thy servants *[Fol. 155v]*
...cease not to pray, O Ever-virgin!

*Ode 5 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*
Cause Thy radiant, ever-existing Light to shine forth upon us who early awake unto the judgments of Thy commandments, O Master, Lover of mankind, Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
As our mighty and immovable weapon lies set forth, now venerating the most holy Cross with faith, we receive invincible power against the enemies.

*Troparion:*
Holding now the figure of the cleft rock that made water pour forth,[^639] and venerating the precious Cross, we receive a fountain of gifts.

*Troparion:*
The golden-shining Cross of Christ, appearing unto us, grants gifts unto souls by grace, unto those who venerate it and ever sing of the Lord and God.

*Theotokion:*
Holding by faith the mighty weapon, the Cross of thy Son and our God, O Bride of God, we sing of thee as Queen of all, calling thee blessed.

*Ode 5 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
Illumination of those lying in darkness, salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: early in the morning I awake unto Thee, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy radiance, for other than Thee I know no God.

*Troparion:*
Enlightened purely with the radiance of the Holy Trinity, and walled about by Her law, thou didst pass through the night of this life without stumbling, winged with divine contemplations, O blessed one, and hast made thine abode in divine life.

*Troparion:*
Now a great wonder is beheld in thee, full of awe: how after burial truly for many years thy body lying in the tomb has been preserved intact, O blessed one,[^640] by judgments whereby He Himself knows Who glorifies thee.

*Troparion:*
Good was thy boldness toward God, and good the desire which Thou hadst in thy heart toward Him; having lived well according to thy name,[^641] Thou didst blossom forth most gloriously with divine splendors, and didst make clear the assemblies of the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
Ever entreat Him Who was incarnate of thee for our sake, O Virgin Mother, that those who piously call thee blessed may be delivered from perils, and from soul-destroying passions and griefs, and from the torment of the future flame.

*Ode 6 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*
Imitating the Prophet Jonah, I cry aloud: Deliver my life from corruption, O Good One, and save me, O Savior of the world, as I cry: Glory to Thee![^642]

*Troparion:*
The most pure Cross, now venerated, proclaims the joy of Christ's Resurrection, and the death of the prince of evil is heralded.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast set up the precious Cross upon the earth; it becomes a ladder *[Fol. 156r]*
...a spiritual ladder unto heaven for the faithful, and an ascent for those who worship and honor it.

*Troparion:*
Planted in the midst of the earth, the precious Cross also extends branches of gifts within itself, and overshadows all who worship it.

*Troparion:*
The Cross, which when prefigured divided the Red Sea,[^643] being now venerated, divides the sea of perils and passions, and saves those who worship it.

*Theotokion:*
Thy Son and God, having ascended the Cross, drew us up from the earth unto heavenly life, who truly honor thee with love as His Mother.

*Ode 6 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy tender mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Thy sweats and rejoicings, O blessed one, distilled sweet streams, the radiance of healings, unto the praise of Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst blossom forth in the courts of God, springing up as a fruitful vine, and offering grapes of virtues, an abundance unto the Husbandman of souls.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst desire the Kingdom of God, and having reigned over the passions, O Eudocimus, according to the better good pleasure, thou didst obtain the joy of the righteous.

*Theotokion:*
Having been chosen for God as a fair and spacious dwelling, Thou wast truly unto Him both a bridal chamber and a lampstand and a table.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Desiring things on high, joining thyself unto the highest, and having wrought thy soul as a fiery chariot through divine ascents of virtues, O most blessed Eudocimus; having lived on earth as one bodiless, thou didst well-please the Creator of all.

*Ikos:*
Thou didst show forth a twofold life through upright faith, O blessed one, well-pleasing God in both, O Eudocimus; therefore thou hast found the inheritance of the heavenly Kingdom, and now living with the saints, thou standest before the Master of all; and thy body thou didst preserve free from defilement, and hast left it intact unto those who honor thee with love; for having lived purely, O glorious one, thou didst well-please the Creator of all.

*Ode 7 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*
The fire touched not, nor afflicted Thy children in the furnace, O Savior. Then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The Cross of Christ, being soaked with His precious blood, pours forth rivers of splendors of spiritual gifts, and waters all who *[Fol. 156v]*
...venerate it, and sing of it faithfully.

*Troparion:*
The Cross of Christ, having overthrown death and the enemy, shattered the spiritual bars; and now unto all who venerate it with pure faith and love, it opens the gates of life and incorruptible food.

*Troparion:*
Those who venerate the Cross with love are walled about by its power; they drive away enemies spiritual and visible, and are preserved unharmed, hymning Christ.

*Theotokion:*
The Cross of Christ thy Son was given unto us as might and dominion against enemies through thy welcome and maternal boldness; by which, O Bride of God, we vanquish the assaults of the enemy.

*Ode 7 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew unto the pious youths:[^644] He Who is blessed and supremely glorified!

*Troparion:*
By the power of God the Life-giver, thou didst restrain the desires of the flesh, O richly-blessed one, and didst wing thy mind toward heavenly things, singing steadfastly: Blessed and supremely glorified art Thou, O God!

*Troparion:*
Having made thyself ignoble, yet having acquired noble ways through chastity of mind, and exercising thyself in the meditation of the divinely-inspired Scriptures, thou didst cast all thy desire upon God the All-seer, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Of the Kingdom on high, and of the City on high, the Paradise of God, the enjoyment of the righteous, the incorruptible food, and the riches that cannot be stolen, and the unwaning Light, thou wast accounted worthy, O blessed Eudocimus.

*Theotokion:*
Ever entreat Him Whom thou didst bear, O Sovereign Lady, to deliver me from the bondage of passions and circumvention, and from the cruel despair that afflicts me, and from the grip of those who afflict me, O pure one, full of God's grace.

*Ode 8 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*
Him before Whom the angels and all the hosts tremble as Creator and Lord:[^645] sing, ye priests; glorify, ye children; bless, ye people, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Beholding the Cross set up, which overthrew death, being now venerated, the spiritual regiments of the enemy hide beneath the earth, and those who worship it are renewed.

*Troparion:*
The faithful, now piously venerating the all-radiant Cross which Christ showed of old prefigured on the heavenly height,[^646] are exalted *[Fol. 157r]*
...and illuminated by its dawn.

*Troparion:*
The Cross which the prophets of old truly foretold should appear as Christ's footstool:[^647] worshiping it now in truth and faith, we enjoy its rich gifts.

*Theotokion:*
Thy Son and Lord, O Virgin, Who by His Passion poured forth passionlessness unto earth-born men, through thee ever renews us who sing of thee with love, as Benefactor.

*Ode 8 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
In the furnace of the children Thou didst prefigure of old Thy Mother, O Lord; and this image rescued from the fire those who entered without being burned:[^648] whom we sing, who was revealed through Thee unto the ends of the earth today, and exalt supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Pondering the dread fire that is to go forth before the throne of Christ, with compunction of heart thou didst pour forth warm drops of tears, extinguishing for thee the furnace of passions, O godly-wise Eudocimus.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst apply the whole spiritual eye unto God, O wise one, and clave unto Him, crying aloud in the words of the Psalm: O my Christ, deliver me from the mire and from deadly passions unto the ages![^649]

*Troparion:*
Patient in waiting and humble of heart wast thou; merciful unto the needy, offering a good demeanor and virtuous ancestral ways; having lived well in which, thou pourest forth the grace of wonders.

*Troparion:*
Possessing a divine understanding, thou wast shown forth as a doer of the Master's words, loving the right paths, and manifestly turning away from the left as one prudent and wise; therefore we honor thee, O godly-wise one, unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
The Creator of Adam is fashioned from thy pure blood, and is nourished with milk, He Who nourishes every breath, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; therefore we glorify thee as Mother of God.

*Ode 9 [of the Cross]. Heirmos:*
The light-bearing cloud, wherein the Master of all descended from heaven like rain upon the fleece,[^650] and was incarnate for our sake, becoming man, the Unoriginate: all of us magnify as the pure Mother of our God.

*Troparion:*
The precious Cross pours forth ungrudging drops of immortality and incorruption unto those who bow down before it; it quickens, cleanses, and renews, and accounts worthy of the rays of glory and goodness those who sing of Jesus Who was nailed upon it.

*Troparion:*
Sending forth operations like radiant beams, the all-honorable Cross [leads] those who worship it with love and pure faith unto the light *[Fol. 157v]*
...unto the unapproachable and immaterial light, shining, directs those who sing of the Creator of all.

*Troparion:*
Thou Who didst cleanse the Wood of Thy most holy Cross with Thy precious blood, O all-good Word: by its operation enlighten and sanctify us who worship it and sing of Thee, the Master of all.

*Theotokion:*
The grace that has shone forth brighter than the sun unto all drives away the clouds of perils and passions, O Virgin, and with the light of the knowledge of God enlightens those who glorify thee with love and unceasingly sing of thy Son as the Master of all.

*Ode 9 [of the Saint]. Heirmos:*
The Son of the Unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin has appeared unto us, to enlighten what was darkened, to gather together what was scattered; therefore we magnify the all-hymned Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to behold the glory of our God, thou didst adorn thyself with virtues of the knowledge of God, and bearing a lamp didst enter into the bright bridal chamber,[^651] living with the angels, and more clearly enjoying the vision of God.

*Troparion:*
Like the morning dawn, like the sun, thy memory has shone forth, enlightening the hearts of those that are in darkness; for truly thou wast shown forth as a son of light and of the day,[^652] O godly-wise Eudocimus; therefore we honor thee.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with wisdom, and enlightened with understanding, beautified with humility of heart, shining with height of life, thou didst come unto the spiritual courts, where remember us who honor thee, O Eudocimus.

*Troparion:*
Let us observe the festal and profitable day of thy precious translation from earth; for thou wast shown forth as the Lord's pleaser and an unashamed doer of His commandments, O richly-worthy of wonder, Eudocimus.

*Theotokion:*
Thy womb was a receptacle of Light that enlightened the world, O full of God's grace; therefore I, wretched, pray thee: grant me radiant beams through the entreaties of godly-wise Eudocimus, O Bride of God.

*Exapostilarion from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... of the Saint:*
Flashing like the sun, thou sendest forth beams of wonders, enlightening the hearts of all who faithfully observe thy glorious memory, O thrice-blessed one, and savest [us], O Eudocimus.

*Both now... of the Cross, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жєны̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
The Cross is kept as a forefeast, and the world is sanctified; the angelic ranks sing of Him Who was crucified for our sake *[Fol. 158r]*
...and they feast and rejoice with us, crying in the words of David: The Master has wrought salvation in the midst of the earth![^653]

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 4:*
How shall we not marvel at thy good understanding, or how shall we not praise, O venerable Eudocimus, thine angelic life, the chastity of thy mind, thy meekness, humility, and silence, and thine unceasing almsgiving? For thou wast adorned with all virtues, O blessed one; therefore ineffable joy and the Kingdom of heaven await thee.

*Both now... of the Cross, Tone 6:*
Prophetic voices proclaimed beforehand the holy Wood, whereby Adam was set free from the ancient curse of death; and creation today, as it is elevated, raises its voice together, asking of God rich mercy. But O Master, Who alone art boundless in tender-heartedness: be Thou our cleansing, and save our souls!

*At the Liturgy. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^654]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213.*[^655]
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.*
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43.*
*Communion Hymn: The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance.*

*[Full-page copperplate engraving preceding the Resurrectional Theotokia for the Eight Tones. Depiction of the Miraculous Icon of the Most Holy Mother of God of Pochaiv. The Most Holy Mother of God is crowned with an imperial crown surmounted by a cross, holding the Christ Child on her right arm. The Christ Child is likewise crowned with an imperial crown. Below, upon a ribbon cartouche amidst billowing clouds, is the Latin inscription:][^656]*
*Vera Effigies B.V.M. Poczajoviensis in Ecclesia PP. Basilianorum Provinciae Poloniae Coronata.*
*("True likeness of the Blessed Virgin Mary of Pochaiv in the Church of the Basilian Fathers of the Province of Poland, crowned.")*

*[Fol. 159r]*

### Notes

[^6]: The Holy Wonderworkers and Unmercenaries Cosmas and Damian of Rome (July 1), distinct from Cosmas and Damian of Cilicia (October 17) and Cosmas and Damian of Mesopotamia (November 1).
[^7]: The Church Slavonic text here abbreviates or truncates the vocative phrase to simply `мо́й?` ("mine?"), which in context represents `[Бо́же] мо́й?` ("O my God?"), corresponding to the canonical Greek automelon incipit `Τριήμερος ἀνέστης` / `ὁ Θεός μου`.
[^8]: "The pool of healing" (И҆сто́чникъ и҆сцѣле́нїй): An allusion to the Sheep Pool (Bethesda) in Jerusalem (John 5:2–4), where an angel stirred the waters and healed only the first invalid who entered.
[^9]: Cf. Mt. 10:1, 8.
[^10]: The feast of the Deposition of the Precious Robe (*Честна́я Ри́за*) of the Most Holy Theotokos in Blachernae commemorates the translation in 458 AD of the Marian maphorion from Palestine to Constantinople under the Byzantine Emperor Leo I the Thracian (457–474) and Empress Verina, and its enshrinement in the Blachernae basilica.
[^11]: Ps. 131:8 LXX.
[^12]: That is, Beer-sheba (*Студенец клятвенный*); cf. Gen. 28:10.
[^13]: Ps. 44:13 LXX (Masoretic Ps. 45:12).

[^15]: Ps. 131:8 LXX.
[^16]: The Greek acrostic composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer is: Τὴν ἐσθῆτα σέβω τῆς Ἁγνῆς ("I revere the robe of the Pure One").
[^17]: Jonah 2:10 LXX.
[^18]: Cf. Dan. 6:22 and Dan. 3:23–27.
[^19]: Ps. 131:8 LXX.
[^20]: St. Hyacinth of Caesarea in Cappadocia (d. 108 AD), a nineteen-year-old royal chamberlain (cubicularius) under Emperor Trajan, starved to death in prison after refusing to eat meat sacrificed to idols.
[^21]: The Greek name Hyakinthos (Ὑάκινθος) denotes both the flowering hyacinth and the precious gemstone (jacinth/sapphire, Rev. 21:20); the hymnographer plays on this lapidary imagery throughout the canon.
[^22]: The Church Slavonic acrostic reads: Тѧ̀ свѣ́тлый мꙋ́чениче хвалю̀ ка́мень ("Thee, radiant martyr, as a stone I praise"). In the Greek Menaion, the acrostic by Theophanes compares him to a sapphire (σάπφειρον), rendered in Slavonic by ка́мень ("stone").
[^23]: St. Andrew of Crete (c. 660–740 AD), Archbishop of Gortyna in Crete, born in Damascus and tonsured at the monastery of the Holy Sepulchre / Great Laura of St. Sabbas in Jerusalem (hence "the Hierosolymite"), renowned as the author of the Great Canon of Repentance and numerous festal triodia, heirmoi, and stichera.
[^24]: St. Martha of Antioch (d. 551 AD), the mother of St. Symeon Stylites the Younger of the Wonderful Mountain (feast day May 24).
[^25]: An allusion to the Great Canon of Repentance (*Вели́кїй Покая́нный Канѡ́нъ*), authored by St. Andrew of Crete.
[^26]: The Church Slavonic acrostic reads: Пѣ́снми восхва́лимъ мꙋ́жескими а҆ндре́а ("With manly songs let us praise Andrew"). In the Greek Menaion, the acrostic by an unknown author is likewise: ᾌσμασι ταινιώσωμεν ἀνδρείοις Ἀνδρέαν ("With manly songs let us crown Andrew").
[^27]: Cf. Exod. 15:1–3.
[^28]: Cf. Exod. 14:27–28; 15:1.
[^29]: St. Martha of Antioch prayed for a child and was granted St. Symeon Stylites the Younger (feast day May 24), who like the prophet Samuel (1 Sam. 1:20) was dedicated to God from the womb.

Five days after his death, his harsh elder and a neighboring great elder visited the tomb; the great elder called out: "Brother Acacius, art thou dead?" From the tomb, the obedient Acacius replied: "How is it possible, father, for an obedient man to die?"

[^141]: An allusion to the historical life of Saint Thomas of Maleon prior to his monastic tonsure, when he served as a military commander defending Byzantine territories against barbarian incursions.
